# Graphics review, 2026-11-22 (light)

Critics: one art-direction and one pixel-technician agent per area, findings merged per area. Two sceptics then checked most findings: one asked whether it really shows in the pixels or the code, the other whether it is intended, already settled or just taste. The run was stopped early, so the World, Effects and Pipeline findings and most Hero findings were never put to the sceptics. Images: scratchpad gfx-review/<area>/ (session only).

Status: **confirmed** = both sceptics agree; **rejected** = a sceptic refuted it; **half-checked** = one sceptic only; **unchecked** = critics only.

## heroes

Both critics (an art lens, A, and a technical lens, B) agree that the heroes are not pixel perfect yet, and that the cause is a handful of systemic delivery faults rather than weak art. At game scale the 22 heroes are rich, hue-shifted painted sprites with one body height (30-33 px; Ea-nasir's 45 px statue is deliberate) and one pixel density. Sorcerer, Paladin, Seraph, Arcanist and Witch Hunter stand out. The sheets are technically sound: no frame touches its cell edge, every sheet keeps foot row 55 (anchorY 56), the hero is drawn last over the horde inside his own light pool, and the Ranger, Berserker and Ea-nasir have perfectly crisp alpha. Seven things spoil the result. (1) Alpha fringe: 19 of 22 painted sheets carry invisible alpha 1-31 pixels (up to about 160 per frame). Anything that thresholds at alpha>0 picks them up, so the hurt and death flash is a blob 13-28% larger than the hero that swallows swords and staffs, the Shadow's menu rim floats off his body, and the pose boxes come out a row too tall. One palette clamp in approved-heroes.js plus an alpha-keeping silhouette fixes all of it. (2) World scale: the world is drawn at a non-integer 0.8×S (3.2, 2.4 or 1.6), which gives uneven pixels that crawl as the camera moves. Fixing it trades field of view, so it is the owner's call. (3) Menu scale: the stage, the roster and the departing soul are drawn at 0.8 on the low-res UI canvas, which drops every fifth art row and column. (4) The ult cut-in and the chest card scale the pose by fractions, the cut-in head overlaps its own banner rule, and the cut-in shows the idle stance. (5) A found that the new death falls rotate and stretch painted parts with nearest-neighbour sampling, which scrambles robes into speckle. B states the falls are authored frames, so verify this first. (6) The sheets are unquantised (hundreds of colours per frame), so the idles shimmer instead of breathing. (7) The Wraith's walk and attack frames use a paler palette than its idle, so it pops at every start and stop. Smaller items: Ea-nasir blends into the sand and looks like the mummies; the Ranger's values are compressed; the five darkest heroes rank below the brightened horde at night; several recruits read as recolours; the HP bar is snapped to the UI grid and ignores screen shake; the title campfire is drawn at 2x beside 1x heroes, with the Shadow hovering in the flames. Owner questions: the brief's style rule (plumed Knight, edges in each hero's own dark shades) does not match the shipped roster. Sir Aldric is the live Knight, Dame Seraphine the live Paladin, and 21 of 22 heroes use one shared near-black ink. Three art modules are dead code that still ships. The code fixes for the fringe, the scales, the cut-in, the chest card, the HP bar and the campfire are small. Re-quantising is one bake pass with no visible change (32 colours gives a mean error of 5.7-12.2 RGB). Files with another session's uncommitted edits are noted per finding (hero-selection.js, gamescreen.js, tools/animation-round/*, public/assets/heroes/*). Neither critic wrote in the repo. Extra evidence is in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/heroes/ (subfolders art-extra/ and tech-extra/).

### heroes-1 [confirmed] Hurt and death-blow flash is a white blob 13-28% larger than the hero, erasing weapons and adding ghost shapes (alpha fringe)
- bug / code - public/js/engine/pixels.js:74-78 (silhouette turns every pixel with alpha>0 white); engine/sprites.js:152-155 (Frame.white); game/game.js:3477 (hurt) and :3513 (death blow); ui/hero-showcase.js:105-108 (selection-lock silhouette). Root data: public/assets/heroes-approved/*.js palettes, unpacked at public/js/art/approved-heroes.js:7-18
- Evidence: [Both lenses] Cause: pixels.js:76 does `if (buf.d[i] >>> 24) out.d[i] = color`, so every alpha 1-31 fringe pixel becomes opaque white.

Visible pixels against flash pixels: Knight 435 vs 541, Sorcerer 631 vs 742, Druid 565 vs 725, Paladin 445 vs 565, Arcanist 710 vs 877, Seraph 578 vs 709.

Faint pixels per frame: Druid 161.6 (43.9 not touching the body), Arcanist 120, Shadow 110, Wraith 101, Footman 74, Knight 16.7. Ranger, Berserker and Ea-nasir have 0.

Images (gfx-review/heroes/):
- art-extra/hurt-flash-fix-x4.png: column 2 is today's flash, which loses the Knight's sword, the Sorcerer's staff top, the Druid's staff and the Arcanist's hat brim; column 3 keeps per-pixel alpha and restores them.
- tech-extra/fringe-alpha-x5.png: magenta ring of faint pixels plus orphan specks around every painted sheet.
- field-hurt-core-x3.png and field-hurt-recruits-x3.png: the flash is wider than the hero and spills onto the adjacent skeleton.
- lineup-core-x4.png, row 4 against row 3: the Knight's flash has a 'flag' above the shield.
- frames-druid-x4.png: the idle-0 flash swallows the staff, and the frozen tint shows the same haze in pale blue.
- The hurt flash fires on every hit and opens every death. On the 19 painted sheets it shows a puffy, noisy silhouette, bigger than the figure and with no weapon or hat, so it reads as a glitch. The same invisible fringe also feeds TINTS.frozen, the selection-lock silhouette, rimRing, bounds() and the _pose crop boxes, which is why the Knight's pose is 33 rows instead of 32.
- Fix: Two layers.

(1) At the source, clean the palette once at load in approved-heroes.js unpack (line 14), then use pal[color] instead of data.palette[color]:
`const pal = data.palette.map(c => { const a = c >>> 24; return a < 32 ? 0 : a >= 240 ? (c | 0xff000000) >>> 0 : c; });`
This fixes the flash, frozen tint, lock silhouette, rim, crops and pose boxes in one place, with no visible change to the normal draw.

(2) Guard the silhouette by keeping each pixel's own alpha:
```js
export function silhouette(buf, color) {
  const out = makeBuf(buf.w, buf.h), rgb = color & 0xffffff;
  for (let i = 0; i < buf.d.length; i++) {
    const a = buf.d[i] & 0xff000000;
    if (a) out.d[i] = (a | rgb) >>> 0;
  }
  return out;
}
```
Alternatively threshold at alpha>=128. Apply the same rule in hero-showcase.js:107: `c >>> 24 ? ((c & 0xff000000) | 0x201b29) >>> 0 : 0`.

Monster frames are opaque, so they are unaffected. Byte-exact art tests may need their hashes refreshed.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed this in the code and in the pixels. pixels.js:76 does `if (buf.d[i] >>> 24) out.d[i] = color`, so any pixel with alpha of 1 or more turns opaque white. Frame.white (sprites.js:154) is the only caller. It runs on hurt (game.js:3477, flash 0.12 s) and on the death blow (game.js:3513, D.flash 0.12 s). The approved sheets carry a large faint fringe. Palette index 1 is 0x01000000 (black at alpha 1). Faint palette entries (alpha under 32): Knight 154, Druid 178, Arcanist 191, Wraith 250. Ranger and Berserker have 0. My own probe (verify/probe.mjs) reproduces the counts exactly. Alpha 128 or more against flash pixels: Knight 435 vs 541, Sorcerer 631 vs 742, Druid 565 vs 725, Paladin 445 vs 565, Arcanist 710 vs 877, Seraph 578 vs 709. That is 13-28% growth; Ranger, Berserker and Ea-nasir show 0%. Most faint pixels are alpha 1-3. A dump of the Knight's idle 0 frame (verify/dump.mjs) shows a 5x4 block of alpha-1 black pixels at x45-49, y24-27, plus a column down x49. It cannot be seen in the sprite but becomes the white "flag" above the shield. verify/flashcmp-x6.png colours the fringe red: it fills the gap between the Druid's staff crook and hood and the gaps under the Arcanist's hat brim, so those shapes vanish into the blob. The hero-showcase.js:107 lock silhouette uses the same alpha>0 rule. Overstated parts: (a) The Knight's sword is drawn across his body in idle 0, so no silhouette would show it on its own. "Loses the sword" fits the Druid staff and the Arcanist brim, not the Knight. (b) TINTS.frozen (px.tint) keeps each pixel's own alpha, so the frozen state makes no opaque haze; mostly-alpha-1 pixels stay invisible. (c) Overlapping the nearby skeleton mostly comes from how the capture scene was set up. Severity: on every hit and at each death, ghost shapes that are not in the art flash for 0.12 s. That is a visible defect rather than a functional bug. None of the cited files are in the other session's uncommitted list. Better fix: Both layers work and are safe, but layer 1 claims too much and goes beyond what is needed. (1) The _pose box in approved-heroes.js comes from the packed frame rects (f[0..3]), not from pixel alpha, so cleaning the palette leaves it unchanged. Measured: Knight rects give 24x33, while pixels at alpha 32 or more span 22x32; Druid 31x38 vs 29x37. Fixing the pose box needs the box computed from the cleaned pixels, or a re-trim in tools/animation-round/build.mjs. (2) The step that forces alpha 240 or more to fully opaque is unrelated to this bug: most hero pixels sit at 240-254 and look fine. Drop it to keep frame data and tests churn-free. (3) Keeping each pixel's alpha in the flash, without the palette clean, still leaves a 6-12% white haze from the alpha 16-31 pixels; a cut at 128 matches what the eye sees. Minimal fix: in pixels.js silhouette, use `if ((buf.d[i] >>> 24) >= 128) out.d[i] = color;`. This covers hurt and death-blow flashes for all 19 painted sheets. Monster frames are opaque, and the Bone King's partial pixels are all alpha 128 or more, so enemy flashes don't change. Use the same cut in hero-showcase.js:107: `c >>> 24 >= 128 ? 0xff201b29 : 0`. Optionally also zero only alpha under 32 at unpack in approved-heroes.js (`pal = data.palette.map(c => (c >>> 24) < 32 ? 0 : c)`), so the fringe stops inflating rimRing, Frame.box and radius. Byte-exact art tests may need refreshing if that is done.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding is confirmed, and nothing marks it as deliberate. px.silhouette (pixels.js:74-78) is called only by Frame.white. It paints every pixel with alpha>0 opaque white. The painted hero sheets carry faint export fringe: alpha 1-31, on average per frame Druid 161.6, Arcanist 120, Shadow 110, Wraith 101, Footman 74, Knight 16.7, Ranger 0. My census is verify/alpha-census.mjs. The repo already treats this fringe as residue, not art: - test/hero-art.test.js:64-67 calls it "faint export fringe". - reviews/2026-11-11-knight-walk.md says a "detached export fringe" was removed as a defect. No settled call or earlier review covers the flash. My in-game-scale render (verify/flash-verify-x3.png) shows the problem. Today's 0.12 s hurt flash (player.js:710), which also opens the death blow, is a puffy blob. It swallows the Druid's staff, the Arcanist's staff and hat brim, and the Knight's sword, and it adds a 'flag' above the Knight's shield. Keeping each pixel's alpha restores the drawn shape exactly. The same alpha>0 rule in hero-showcase.js:107 gives locked heroes on the chooser a hazy dark halo. That one stays on screen, not just for a flash. Two parts of the description are overstated: - TINTS.frozen uses px.tint, which already keeps per-pixel alpha, so the frozen look has no visible haze. - The _pose crop box comes from the packed frame rectangles (approved-heroes.js:41-44), not from pixel content, so a palette clean would not change the 33-row pose anyway. None of the files involved (pixels.js, sprites.js, game.js, hero-showcase.js, approved-heroes.js) has the other session's uncommitted edits. The fix only changes drawing: no gameplay change and no Math.random. Fix layer (1) is not sound: - It breaks test/hero-skins.test.js:24-42, which checks that unpack rebuilds each frame exactly from data.palette. - It snaps alpha 240-254 to 255. Those are the majority of hero pixels (about 300-690 per frame), so the normal draw changes. - It is not needed for the visible defect. Layer (2) alone, plus the showcase line, fixes it. No test checks the white variant's pixels; ending.test.js and boneking-sweep only compare canvas identity. Better fix: Leave approved-heroes.js unpack unchanged, because the palette clean breaks the exact-reconstruction test in hero-skins.test.js and slightly changes the normal draw. Make only these two draw-side edits. (a) pixels.js silhouette keeps each pixel's own alpha: ```js export function silhouette(buf, color) {   const out = makeBuf(buf.w, buf.h), rgb = color & 0xffffff;   for (let i = 0; i < buf.d.length; i++) { const a = buf.d[i] & 0xff000000; if (a) out.d[i] = (a | rgb) >>> 0; }   return out; } ``` (b) hero-showcase.js:107 uses `c >>> 24 ? ((c & 0xff000000) | 0x201b29) >>> 0 : 0`. Keeping alpha makes the flash cover exactly what is drawn. Opaque monster frames are unchanged. The Bone King's translucent robe hem (about 23 semi-transparent pixels per frame) now flashes translucent as it is drawn. An alpha>=128 cut is an acceptable alternative.

### heroes-2 [confirmed] Shadow's menu rim line ('showcase-edge') floats off the body as broken segments
- bug / code - public/js/ui/hero-showcase.js:110-115 (rimRing drawn at dx - scale with scale 0.8); public/js/engine/pixels.js:184-190 (rimRing, via outline's alpha>0 test at :53); data: public/assets/heroes-approved/rogue.js (110 faint px per frame); heroes.js:92 (line: 1)
- Evidence: [Technical lens] tech-extra/rogue-stage-rim-x6.png, cropped from chrome-hero-stage-1-1600x900.png: the rim is a detached vertical bar right of the hood and loose L-shapes round the cloak. tech-extra/rogue-rim-faint-vs-clean-x6.png: traced from the sheet as is, the ring sits about 1 px off the visible edge with gaps (left); traced after dropping alpha<32 it hugs the figure (right).
- The only menu-only hero effect traces the invisible alpha fringe rather than the visible cloak, and is then decimated at 0.8 on the UI canvas. It reads as a stray wireframe beside the hero.
- Fix: The fringe clean-up in the hurt-flash finding fixes the shape. Also build the ring from alpha>=128 (pass a threshold to outline/rimRing), and draw it at the same integer scale as the figure (see the menu-scale finding) so the 1-px ring is never dropped.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed this myself in real Chrome at 1600x900 (S=3, view.zoom 0.8) on the running dev server. My script rimchrome.mjs takes the Shadow's HeroShowcase state and calls its draw onto fresh UI-size canvases several times from that same state: rim on, rim off (HEROES.rogue.line=0), a rim built from alpha>=128, and the same three at view.zoom=1. The images are under C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/heroes/verify/ (rim-zoom-0-on-off-x13.png for idle, rim-zoom-2-on-off-x12.png for walk, rim-zoom-0-clean-off-x13.png, rim-zoom-0-onZ1-cleanZ1-x11.png, rim-compare-0-x6.png). What shows in the pixels: - When idle, a vertical bar sits one background pixel to the right of the hood, with an L running out over empty background. Loose loops hang below and to the left of the cape tip, where the picture with the rim off shows nothing. - When walking it is worse: squiggles float clear of the cape tip and the dagger, and broken stubs sit by the feet. Both causes the critic gives are real: 1. The fringe. The rogue sheet carries a faint alpha fringe, about 110 pixels per frame with a<32 (5698 over 52 frames; rimstat.mjs). outline() counts anything with alpha>0 as solid (pixels.js:53). In idle frame 20, 76 of the 122 ring pixels touch only pixels with alpha<128, so the ring sits about 1 px off the visible edge. You can see this even at scale 1: there is a gap row above the hood and a gap column to its right. 2. The decimation. hero-showcase.js:113 draws the ring on the 534x300 UI canvas at scale 0.8 with nearest sampling. Whole ring rows and columns drop out, which breaks it into segments. The ring and the figure sample on the same grid, so the 1-px offset at dx-scale itself is correct; the gaps come from the dropped rows and columns. I also checked the cited line numbers and data, and they are correct. hero-showcase.js, pixels.js and rogue.js are not among the other session's uncommitted files. No earlier review settled this. Severity: I rate it a defect, not a bug. Nothing breaks; it is a visible art flaw on the Shadow's main hero-screen preview. The owner wants a faint 1-px line round him there (heroes.js:89-92) and it comes out as a broken wireframe. Fix: building the ring from alpha>=128 is correct and on its own fixes the 1-px float (clean-off and cleanZ1 captures). It does not fix the breaks: the clean ring at 0.8 still has gaps and loose strokes. The rest of the proposed fix leans on two other findings, the sprite fringe clean-up and an integer menu scale. The integer scale would change the size of every hero on the stage, which goes well beyond fixing this line. So the fix is not minimal as proposed. Better fix: Keep the whole change inside hero-showcase.js and build the ring in menu pixels, after the scaling, so it holds up at any stage scale. 1. Each frame, take p._rect = [dx, dy, dw, dh, frame, flip]. 2. Draw frame.get(flip) at exactly (dx - X0, dy - Y0, dw, dh) into a small offscreen canvas, with imageSmoothingEnabled false. That gives the same nearest sampling, and so the same dropped rows and columns, as the figure on the stage. 3. Read the pixels back and keep only alpha >= 128 as the mask. 4. Run outline(mask, '#d8e2f0' packed) once and clear the mask's own pixels, which is what rimRing does now at width 1. 5. Draw the result 1:1 at (X0 - 1, Y0 - 1) with globalAlpha 0.5. 6. Cache it in a Map keyed by frame, flip, scale and the fractional parts of dx/dy, so the readback happens once per pose. The ring is then always exactly 1 menu px wide. It is continuous and hugs the silhouette as it is actually drawn, with no change to the stage scale or to the sprite data. If you'd rather keep a per-frame variant, add an optional alpha threshold to outline() and rimRing(), defaulting to 0 so the current callers are unchanged, and pass 128 here. That fixes the float only; the breaks need the menu-pixel ring above.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): Confirmed. The line itself was the owner's request (heroes.js:89-92: "a faint 1 px light line round him on the menus... drawn round him"), but on the hero screen it does not hug him. No earlier review in reviews/ covers it, and the git log shows nothing that makes the broken shape deliberate: the line dates from f416b0e and the 0.8 menu scale from 281bd5c. I checked it on my own in verify/rimcheck.mjs. The approved rogue sheet has 94-125 pixels at alpha 1-31 per frame (average 109.6 over 52 frames). outline() counts any alpha above 0 as solid (pixels.js:53, :58), so rimRing traces that invisible fringe. Across all frames, 69.1% of the ring's pixels touch no pixel of alpha 128 or more, so the ring sits 1 px off the visible cloak. Tracing from alpha 32 and up brings that to 5.8%, and from 128 and up to 0%. The ring is then drawn at scale 0.8 on the UI canvas with nearest-neighbour, which drops every fifth row and column. That leaves the dotted gaps and the bar that floats right of the hood (verify/rogue-rim-08-x7.png, column 1). hero-showcase.js is not one of the other session's work-in-progress files. The fix only touches a menu effect: no gameplay, no Math.random, no change to the hero's look. The critic's fix is not fully sound, for three reasons. (1) "Draw it at the same integer scale as the figure" goes against a deliberate, tested call. 281bd5c (2026-11-21) made the menu figure gameplay-sized via heroMenuScale, and test/hero-selection.test.js:115 checks "the showcase draws the selected hero at gameplay size". (2) Passing a threshold only to outline's solid() test leaves most of the ring blank. outline() still skips any pixel with alpha above 0 (`if (src[...] >>> 24) continue`), and rimRing then clears every pixel with inner alpha above 0. That is exactly where the fringe lies. (3) Relying on the hurt-flash finding's sheet clean-up (a re-bake) is broader than this needs. Better fix: Change only public/js/ui/hero-showcase.js. Leave pixels.js, the sheet and the figure's scale alone. Smallest fix (cures the floating), clean the buffer before tracing, in the same way as the 'selection-lock' variant two lines above: `frame.variant('showcase-edge', b => rimRing({ ...b, d: b.d.map(c => c >>> 24 >= 128 ? c : 0) }, '#d8e2f0', spr.hires || 1), flip)`. To also remove the dotted gaps from the 0.8 decimation without changing the figure's size, build the ring on the destination grid. Call g.placePlayer(camera) first. Then draw a light silhouette variant ('showcase-sil': alpha>=128 → #d8e2f0, else 0) at the four whole-UI-pixel offsets (dx±1, dy) and (dx, dy±1), at globalAlpha 0.5 or in a pre-mixed colour. Then let g.drawPlayer paint the figure over it. Both sample on the same grid, so the 1-px ring always touches the visible edge (verify/rogue-rim-08-x7.png, column 3).

### heroes-5 [confirmed] Ultimate cut-in: fractional pose scale gives 6/9-px pixels, the head pokes over the band's rule, and it shows the idle stance
- defect / code - public/js/game/hud.js:830-846 (ps = (bh - 6) / pf.height; pose drawn at by + 3; rule at by + 4 drawn first; band top sloped from by+10 to by); pose crops built in art/approved-heroes.js:41-48 from idle frames whose boxes include the faint fringe
- Evidence: [Both lenses]
- ult-cutin-full-knight-1600x900.png: device-pixel run lengths along row 560 are 6,2,9,6,6,6,6,9,6,12…, so art pixels are 6 or 9 px wide.
- tech-extra/cutin-knight-face-x5.png: at 2.18x the face mixes 6- and 9-px pixels.
- menu-poses-x4.png: scales 1.39-2.13 at 1080p, 1.57-2.40 at 1600x900, 1.91-2.93 at 1280x720; none is an integer.
- ult-cutin-core-1600x900.png: every hero, Ea-nasir included at 1.57, has uneven pixels.
- tech-extra/cutin-berserker-top-x4.png: Ylva's hair covers the pink rule.
- tech-extra/cutin-full-knight-head-x4.png: at x = W*0.18 the band's top is at about by+8, the rule (by+4) floats above it, and the helmet (by+3) sticks out into the dimmed strip.
- Each banner uses idle frame 0, so Whirlwind is announced by a Knight with his sword lowered.
- The cut-in is the hero's poster moment and the largest view of the hero art in play. It shows the sprite mixel-scaled, at a different pixel size per hero, in a passive stance, and not contained by its own banner.
- Fix: (1) Use one integer scale for every hero. Either `ps = Math.max(1, Math.round((bh - 6) / 33))`, giving 2 at 1080p and 1600x900 and 3 at 1280x720, or floor((bh - 14) / h). Better, `view.overlay(pf, x + off, by + 10, boxW, bh - 14, { fill: null })`, which floors to whole device multiples; overlays ignore ctx.translate, so add off to x.

(2) Contain or deliberately break the frame. Start the pose inside the band (by+10 at that x) and draw the top rule after the pose or along the slope. Or bottom-align the figure (y = by + bh - 3 - pf.height * ps) and let tall heads (Sorcerer 40, Ea-nasir 46) break above as a deliberate flourish.

(3) Add an `${id}_strike` sprite cut from data.frames[attackRelease] in approved-heroes.js, and have the cut-in prefer it over `_pose`.

h should come from the fringe-cleaned pose.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): Confirmed in the code and by measurement, with one part that is a design choice, not a defect. (1) Uneven pixels: real. gamescreen.js:182-184 passes view.uctx, the W×H UI canvas with smoothing off, to hud.draw. hud.js:842-843 draws the pose at ps = (bh-6)/pf.height, which is not a whole number, so nearest-neighbour gives 2 or 3 UI px per art pixel, and S then multiplies that. field.mjs draws the production Game and HUD headless, so the capture shows what the game draws. I measured device-pixel runs in ult-cutin-full-knight-1600x900.png myself. Row 560 reads 6,2,9,6,6,6,6,9,6,12,6,6,9,…; columns 270, 290 and 310 also read 6,6,6,6,9,… Every 4th to 6th art pixel is 1.5 times wider. My own recomputation of the pose boxes (knight 24x33, mage 27x40, berserker 32x30, …) gives ps 1.60-2.13 at 1080p, 1.80-2.40 at 1600x900 and 2.20-2.93 at 1280x720. None is an integer for any hero. (2) Containment: real, but small. The pose is drawn at by+3, after the 1-px rule at by+4. Berserker is the only hero with opaque pixels in pose row 0 (4 px at alpha 255), so in cutin-berserker-top-x4.png the dark top of her hair is drawn over the pink rule. For the other heroes the solid head begins about 2 art rows down, at roughly by+7. That is below the rule but above the band's sloped top, which is about by+8.2 at x = 0.18W, so the helmet's top outline row sits in the dimmed strip. At night it is dark on dark and barely visible. The level rules crossing a slanted band look like the intended style. (3) Idle stance: true but not a defect. The comment at hud.js:814-815 says the cut-in shows "his menu pose" on purpose. A strike pose would be new art direction, not a fix. Exposure is short: the band holds about 0.3 s of real time (ULT_LAUNCH slideIn 0.15 to slideOut 0.45). Still, it is the largest view of the hero in play, and the owner asked for pixel-perfect, so I rate it a defect at the low end. Neither hud.js nor approved-heroes.js has the other session's uncommitted edits. Why the proposed fix is not sound: - (a) A shared ps = round((bh-6)/33) gives 3 at 1280x720. Sorcerer and Arcanist would then overflow the band by 32 UI px and Ea-nasir by about 50. - (b) floor((bh-14)/h) gives ps = 1 for nearly every hero at 1080p and at 1600x900 (64/33 = 1.94). That halves the figure. - (c) The "better" view.overlay(pf, …) does not work. overlay reads img.naturalWidth/naturalHeight, and pf is a canvas from Frame.c (bufToCanvas), so k is NaN and nothing is drawn. It would also paint after the whole UI, so it would sit on top of the HUD drawn later. - (d) Point (3) is a design addition, outside the defect. Better fix: Keep the pose on the UI canvas and use one whole-number scale per hero, worked out from the fringe-cleaned height. Measuring the height has to happen at runtime. In approved-heroes.js, store the rows with alpha >= 128 across the idle frames, e.g. pose.solidTop/solidBottom, or crop the _pose box to them. My measurements: knight 32, archer 30, mage 39, druid 37, berserker 30, arcanist 39. In hud.js: `const ps = Math.max(1, Math.round((bh - 6) / hClean)), dw = pf.width * ps, dh = pf.height * ps;` `ctx.drawImage(pf, Math.round(W * 0.18 - dw / 2), by + bh - 3 - (solidBottom + 1) * ps, dw, dh);` This bottom-aligns the figure, so the feet stand on the lower rule. Berserker then sits below the top rule (by+7 at 1080p, by+15 at 1600x900). Robed casters rise at most about 14-16 UI px above the band, 19 for Ea-nasir at 1440p, which reads as the figure deliberately breaking the frame. Draw the top rule before the pose, as now, so a head that crosses it reads as intentional. If any overflow is unwanted, cap it: ps = Math.max(1, Math.min(Math.round((bh-6)/hClean), Math.floor((bh - 6 + 12) / hClean))). Leave the idle pose as it is; a strike-frame banner is the owner's design call, not part of this fix.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): I checked this against the code and two earlier sources: the reviews/ folder and commit dfaacd1, which added the cut-in. Parts (1) and (2) of the finding are real. Part (3) is not. (1) Uneven pixels, confirmed. hud.js:845 sets ps = (bh - 6) / pf.height and draws onto the W×H UI canvas with smoothing off. presentUI then scales that canvas up by S. A fractional ps therefore makes each art pixel 2 or 3 UI pixels wide, which is 6 or 9 device pixels at 1600x900. - I re-rendered it in verify/cutin/cutin-{1920x1080,1600x900,1280x720}.png. Current scales are Knight 2.18, Berserker 2.40, Ea-nasir 1.57 at 1600x900, and 1.94 / 1.60 / 1.39 at 1080p. The integer-scale rows next to them are visibly cleaner, the Knight's visor most of all. - No review settles this. The codebase's own rule is crisp, whole-multiple pixels: see the view.js header and the overlay comment "whole multiples of its own size". (2) Frame breach, confirmed but small. The pose starts at by+3, one row above the top rule at by+4. At x = W*0.18 the band's top edge is about by+8. - Most heroes' helmets poke 1-2 UI px into the dimmed strip. - Ylva's frame 0 has art in its top row, so her hair covers the rule. (3) Idle stance, not a defect. The commit says the cut-in shows "his menu pose" on purpose. Adding a _strike sprite is new scope. Severity is polish: the cut-in lasts 0.6 s (ULT_LAUNCH: 0.15 s in, 0.3 s hold, 0.15 s out) and gameplay is unaffected. Why the proposed fixes don't work: - ps = round((bh - 6) / 33) gives 3 at 1280x720. That puts Ea-nasir (46 tall) at 138 px and the Sorcerer at 120 px inside a 94 px band. - floor((bh - 14) / h) drops the Knight and every taller hero to 1x at 1080p and 1600x900. A 33 px figure in a 70-78 px band is a clear step backwards. - The "better" view.overlay route is broken. overlay() sizes the image from img.naturalWidth and naturalHeight, which a canvas (Frame.c) doesn't have, so the size comes out NaN and nothing is drawn. It would also paint after the whole UI canvas, so over any pause menu, and ignore globalAlpha. Nothing in the code calls overlay() today. - Part (3) adds an asset path for a choice the owner made on purpose. Also noted: the SIGNATURE moment in gamescreen.js:1365 uses ps = Math.min(2, 64 / pf.height), which is fractional (1.6-1.94) for heroes 33-40 px tall. That file belongs to the other session's uncommitted work. I edited nothing in the repo and ran no tests. My comparison images and script are in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/heroes/verify/cutin/ (cmp.mjs, cutin-*.png). Better fix: The smallest fix is two lines in hud.js:845-846. Use a whole-number scale per hero and stand the figure on the lower rule, where its feet are today: const hero = g.player.hero, pose = SPR[hero.pose] || SPR[hero.sprite], pf = pose.frames[0].c; const ps = Math.max(1, Math.round((bh - 6) / pf.height)), pw = pf.width * ps, ph = pf.height * ps; ctx.drawImage(pf, Math.round(W * 0.18 - pw / 2), by + bh - 3 - ph, pw, ph); What this gives: - Every pixel is the same size: 2x for almost every hero at 1080p, 1600x900 and 1440p, and 2-3x at 720p. - At 1600x900 the Knight, Ylva and the 30-34 px recruits sit fully inside the band. Ylva no longer covers the rule. - Taller heroes (Sorcerer, Druid, Arcanist, Ea-nasir) break above the band. That reads as a deliberate frame-break, a standard cut-in look, not a 1-px slip. If the owner wants every figure contained instead, compute ps and ph first, then grow the band to fit: bh = Math.max(Math.round(H * 0.26), ph + 14) The rest of the band and the name are already placed relative to bh, so they follow. Keep the idle menu pose; it is deliberate (dfaacd1). There are no random calls here and nothing in update changes. The same rounding applies to the SIGNATURE pose in gamescreen.js:1365: ps = Math.max(1, Math.min(2, Math.round(64 / pf.height))). That file is the other session's uncommitted work, so the change should wait for them.

### heroes-3 [half-checked] World is drawn at a non-integer 0.8×S, so art pixels are 1/2, 2/3 or 3/4 device px wide and crawl as the camera moves
- defect / owner - public/js/game/game.js:2787 (syncCam: c.S = Math.max(1, view.S * view.zoom)); engine/view.js:24 (zoom 0.8) and :56 (view.S); camera snapped to device px, not art px, at game.js:3233
- Evidence: [Both lenses] World scale: 1920x1080 = 3.2, 1600x900 = 2.4, 1366x768 = 2.4, 1280x720 = 1.6. Only 2560x1440 gets an integer (4.0).

Column widths per 5 art px:
- 3.2 gives 3,3,3,3,4 (20% fat)
- 2.4 gives 2,2,3,2,3 (40% fat)
- 1.6 gives 1,2,1,2,2 (60% doubled)

Images:
- chrome-run-forest-knight-1600x900-hero-x3.png and chrome-run-forest-necromancer-1600x900-hero-x3.png: mixed 2/3-px columns.
- chrome-run-forest-knight-1280x720-hero-x3.png and chrome-run-desert-knight-1280x720-hero-x3.png: 1-px outlines alternate thin and thick; the Knight's face, visor and the rats' outlines distort.

The pattern is tied to each sprite's origin, so the hero, the monsters and the tiles show different fat-pixel grids. The camera is rounded to device px (game.js:3233), so the mapping shifts every frame the camera moves, and a part moving 1 art px between walk frames changes width.
- This is the single biggest reason the game is not pixel perfect. Every hero, monster and tile is resampled nearest-neighbour by a fractional factor at every common desktop size; only the HUD is integer. It is worst at 1280x720. It is an owner call because an integer scale changes how much of the field each resolution shows.
- Fix: Snap the world to an integer in game.js:2787. Two options:

(a) `c.S = Math.max(1, Math.round(view.S * view.zoom))`:
- 1080p becomes 3: 640x360 view, now 600x338 (+7%).
- 1600x900 becomes 2: 800x450, now 667x375 (+20%).
- 1366x768 becomes 2: 683x384.
- 1280x720 becomes 2: 640x360, now 800x450 (-20%).

(b) Hold the view height near a target: `c.S = Math.max(1, Math.round(Math.min(view.pxW, view.pxH) / 360))` (B suggests /340). With 360 this gives 3 at 1080p and 900p, 2 at 768p and 720p, and 4 at 1440p.

heroMenuScale (hero-showcase.js:27-29) and the HUD's world anchors (z = cam.S/view.S) follow on their own. Device-pixel camera snapping is fine once S is an integer.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The main claim holds, but part of it is wrong and the proposed fix changes gameplay. What I checked: - game.js:2787 sets `c.S = Math.max(1, view.S * view.zoom)` with zoom 0.8 (view.js:24). The world scale is therefore 1.6 at 1280x720, 2.4 at 1366x768 and 1600x900, 3.2 at 1080p, and 4.0 only at 1440p. - I drew a 1-px stripe sprite with the same nearest-neighbour drawImage, using Skia (verify/widths.mjs). The widths of 10 art px came out as:   - 1.6: 2,1,2,1,2,2,1,2,1,2   - 2.4: 2,3,2,3,2,2,3,2,3,2   - 3.2: 3,3,4,3,3,3,3,4,3,3   - 2, 3 and 4: all equal - So art pixels are uneven at every common desktop size, and 1-px outlines double at 720p. This is the biggest reason the world is not pixel perfect. - It breaks none of the owner's settled calls, and no review in reviews/ addresses it (grep for zoom/fractional/integer). The only comments on it come from the bulk first commit 84963a5. game.js:3229-3230 says "a fractional scale keeps a steady pixel pattern", and view.js:7-8 says the zoom shows more forest. Whoever wrote the code knowingly traded pixel-perfect art for a wider view. That makes it the owner's call, as the critic says, not an accident. Since the owner is now asking whether the game is pixel perfect, it should be reported. The overstated part: art pixels do NOT crawl as the camera moves. The camera is snapped so that cam.x*S is a whole number. Ground chunks (forest.js:343-345) and props (forest.js:367) are drawn at round(constant - integer), so the pattern on static things stays put while the camera moves. Moving sprites keep their own pattern, because their origin is rounded. Only a part that shifts 1 art px between animation frames changes width. What you see is a fixed patchwork of uneven pixels that differs per sprite, not shimmer. Why the fix is not sound: 1. It changes gameplay. Spawn and despawn distances are measured from the visible field (cam.W/cam.H):    - forest.js:589-590 spawnPoint (the normal horde spawns at the screen edge)    - spawner.js:418 ring radius and :443 swarm start    - game.js:3164-3165 recycleFar    - game.js:2588 the warning arrows    - game.js:2568 soulDuration    Rounding S changes all of these: 1080p goes from 600x338 to 640x360, 900p and 768p get +20%, 720p gets -20%. Headless runs on the default view (S=4, zoom 0.8 = 3.2) get 640x360 instead of the 600x338 field that tools/headless.mjs:4-5 says bots and dps are tuned to, so every seeded test replay would change. 2. The critic says heroMenuScale "follows on its own". It does not: hero-showcase.js:27-29 returns view.zoom directly. The menu hero would stay at 3.2 while the game draws 3, and test/hero-selection.test.js "draws the selected hero at gameplay size" would fail at uiScale 4 (about 0.2*w px off, tolerance 4). 3. Option (b) with /360 rounds 900p to 3, giving 533x300: 20% less field than today. Better fix: Present this as an owner decision, not a quiet fix. If the owner accepts a different view size: 1. Use option (a) in Game.syncCam: `c.S = Math.max(1, Math.round(view.S * view.zoom))`. It gives 640x360 at 720p, 1080p and 1440p, 683x384 at 768p and 800x450 at 900p. That spread is actually narrower than today's 569-800 px wide. 2. Change hero-showcase.js heroMenuScale to `Math.max(1, Math.round(view.S * view.zoom)) / view.S`, so the menu hero matches the in-game size. 3. Keep the balance tuning: have tools/headless.mjs set its own view explicitly so the bots keep their 600x338 field (e.g. view.S=3, view.zoom=1, view.W=600, view.H=337.5). Alternatively, re-baseline the seeded tests and bot/dps numbers. 4. Tell the owner that the edges where foes appear move with the new field at 720p, 768p and 900p. Reject option (b) (/360): it shrinks 1600x900 to 533x300. Device-pixel camera snapping can stay as it is.

### heroes-4 [half-checked] Hero screen, roster and departing soul draw the sprite at 0.8 on the low-res UI canvas, dropping every 5th art row and column
- defect / code - public/js/ui/hero-showcase.js:27-29 (heroMenuScale = max(1/view.S, view.zoom) = 0.8), :84-85, :103 (stage via Game.drawPlayer); ui/hero-selection.js:249-252 (roster thumbnails; committed lines, though another session has uncommitted DEMO edits elsewhere in the file); game/hud.js:1246, 1254, 1267, 1277 (departing soul and Ea-nasir's walking spirit at c.S/view.S = 0.8)
- Evidence: [Both lenses]
- art-extra/menu-dropped-rows-1600-x2.png and tech-extra/menu-dropped-rows-zoom-x3.png: at 0.8 then x3, the Knight loses an eye column and part of the sword hilt, the Ranger loses her eyes, and the Witch Hunter's crossbow and face collapse. The in-run 2.4 draw and 1 UI px per art px keep them.
- chrome-hero-stage-demo-knight-x2.png: the 'walk 2.60s' cell has a reshaped helmet and face against 2.01s and 3.20s.
- chrome-hero-stage-demo-berserker-x2.png: Ylva's eyes and axes change shape frame to frame.
- chrome-hero-roster-top-1600x900-x2.png: Ranger, Paladin, Knight and Sorcerer thumbnails have doubled or missing rows and lose facial features.
- chrome-death-knight-x2.png at 2.01 s: the soul's coarse 3-px stepping sits beside the fallen body's 2.4 grid.
- The title campfire draws at 1 UI px per art px and is crisp.
- Drawing smaller than 1:1 on the W×H UI canvas is true decimation. About 20% of authored pixels never reach the screen. Which ones are lost depends on the subpixel phase, so the stage demo shimmers as the hero animates. This is the screen where players judge the hero art.
- Fix: Draw menu heroes at an integer scale.
- Stage: heroMenuScale() / HeroShowcase.draw use scale 1, which fits at 1600x900 and 1280x720 (stage about 103x67 UI px). Where it does not fit (1080p), draw the figure on the device canvas at Math.floor(0.8*view.S) or Math.round(view.S*view.zoom) device px per art px with smoothing off.
- Roster (hero-selection.js:249): `Math.max(1, Math.floor(...))`. Either clip the 39-45 px tall heroes (Sorcerer, Arcanist, Ea-nasir) to the 34-px row or raise ROW_H/ROW_STEP to about 44/46. Or route the thumbnail through view.overlay with clipping to the panel, since overlay floors to whole device multiples. Coordinate with the session editing hero-selection.js.
- Soul: draw the wisp inside Game.drawFallen with the world camera (c.S), so it shares the body's grid. Or use Math.max(1, Math.round(c.S / view.S)), or queue it on the device canvas after presentUI.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real. heroMenuScale() returns max(1/view.S, view.zoom), which is 0.8 at S of 2, 3 or 4. HeroShowcase.draw passes that scale to Game.drawPlayer on the W×H UI canvas (view.uctx). A nearest-neighbour downscale there is plain decimation. I checked this with @napi-rs/canvas (verify/decim.mjs): drawing 40 distinct columns at 0.8 keeps only 32 of them. At the in-run scale of 2.4 or 3.2 device pixels per art pixel, all 40 survive. The real-Chrome captures agree. The Knight demo at walk 2.60s has a reshaped helm and face compared with 2.01s and 3.20s, and the roster thumbnails lose facial rows. The hero screen is where players judge the art, so this counts as a defect, not taste. The intent check also points to an accident. Commit 281bd5c ('Rework the hero chooser...') added the comment 'Game.syncCam draws at least one device pixel per world pixel', and the 1/view.S floor copies syncCam's max(1, …). So the author wanted at least one device pixel per art pixel. They missed that a 0.8 draw on the UI canvas is resampled at UI resolution before the ×S blit. No review in reviews/ mentions or settles this. None of the owner's settled calls is involved, and nothing changes gameplay or Math.random. What 281bd5c did deliberately is the menu hero's size. It made the stage hero exactly gameplay-sized and tests that with 'the showcase draws the selected hero at gameplay size on phone and desktop displays', which checks |menuW*S - fieldW| <= S. The critic's main fix, drawing the stage at scale 1, would make the hero 25% bigger than in a run and break that test. It reverses a deliberate, tested choice instead of fixing the side effect. Math.floor(0.8*S) or Math.round(S*zoom) is 2 at S=3, so the hero would no longer match its in-run size either. The soul option 'draw it inside Game.drawFallen with the world camera' goes against hud.js's own comment 'Draw over the HUD so a rising wisp can cross the top edge'. Raising ROW_H/ROW_STEP for the roster changes the layout in hero-selection.js, which another session is editing right now (uncommitted DEMO/SHOWN_HEROES changes, though the thumbnail lines themselves are committed). So the fix as proposed is not sound. Its fallback idea of drawing on the device canvas is the right direction. On severity: the stage is the main defect. The roster thumbnails are smaller and already limited by the row size, so they are a secondary defect. The departing soul is a translucent wisp that is gone in about a second, so it is polish. Better fix: Keep the gameplay size and draw the stage hero on the device canvas at the run's own scale, cS = Math.max(1, view.S * view.zoom). That is lossless: 2.4 or 3.2 device px per art px, exactly as in a run. Use the presentBackdrop pattern the title screen already uses (engine/view.js presentBackdrop, screens.js:88). 1. Split HeroShowcase.draw into drawStage(ctx, box, locked), which draws the backdrop and plinth on the UI canvas as now, and drawFigure(dctx, box, locked). 2. In HeroScreen.draw, call presentBackdrop() right after the stage backdrop, then preview.drawFigure(view.ctx, stage, locked). 3. In drawFigure, clip with dctx.rect(x*S, y*S, w*S, h*S) and call g.drawPlayer(dctx, { S: cS, x: -center*S/cS, y: -foot*S/cS }). Draw the lock silhouette and the h.line rim the same way, with the rim offset at cS instead of scale. Keep using the UI-px `scale` from heroMenuScale/fit to work out foot, center and plinth, and multiply by S only for the figure. 4. The hint, identity, details and buttons that follow are drawn on the cleared UI canvas and composite over the figure, so the z-order stays the same. The MenuDialog path returns before the stage, so nothing changes there. 5. Update the gameplay-size test to compare the device-px _rect with fieldW directly (difference <= 1). Roster, as a follow-up and in coordination with the session editing hero-selection.js: queue the thumbnail frames during drawRoster. After the same presentBackdrop, draw them on view.ctx clipped to the roster viewport ×S, at min(cS, fit*S) device px per art px with smoothing off. ROW_H stays unchanged. Soul (polish, optional): keep it over the HUD. Have presentUI run an optional post-UI device draw (or give overlay() a crisp, no-smoothing stretch mode with a source rect) that draws the wisp at c.S device px per art px. Do not move it under the HUD and do not round it up to scale 1. No gameplay, update-path or Math.random changes, and no hero looks touched.

### heroes-6 [unchecked] Death falls rotate and stretch painted parts with nearest-neighbour sampling, scrambling robes and armour into speckle
- defect / art - public/js/art/hero-fall.js:46-60 (paint: arbitrary-angle inverse nearest-neighbour sampling) and :62-67 (segment: stretches parts by scaleY); used by art/martial-falls.js, caster-falls.js, recruit-falls.js, eanasir-fall.js; Paladin's fall sword is a hand-typed 18x6 template at martial-falls.js:37-47; fall key times at martial-falls.js:9 (i / 25)
- Evidence: [Art lens; B's verdict says the falls are authored frames, never a rotated sprite, so check hero-fall.js before acting] art-extra/fall-rotation-compare-x5.png:
- Sorcerer #30, #33, #40: the robe becomes speckle squashed to about 5 rows.
- Paladin #1: the thin sword pops into a fat white blade, and #40 is garbled.
- Seraph #26 and #40: broken outline and noise.
- Last column: a lossless 90° turn of the idle keeps every detail.

Full sheets:
- fall-mage-p1-x4.png rows 6-7.
- fall-seraph-p1-x4.png rows 4-5.
- fall-paladin-p1-x4.png row 1.
- fall-eanasir-p1-x4.png rows 4-5: the outline breaks into floating dark fragments.

Timing: fall-paladin-p1-x4.png alternates 'held 0.03s / 0.05s', a 2:3 judder from 1/25 s keys sampled at 60 Hz. In play: field-death-core-1-x2.png and field-death-recruits-*.png, columns 1.30-1.92 s.
- The slow-motion fall over a world drained to grey is when the player stares at the hero longest, and here the sprite is at its worst. Rotating detailed pixel art by 10-80° with nearest-neighbour sampling, and stretching limbs along one axis, cannot keep outlines or texture. The Knight's original fall and Ylva's fall (owner-approved 2026-11-21) are least affected, though Ylva's falling limbs are thin unoutlined sticks.
- Fix: Keep the timing and staging and change only the pixel operation:
- (1) For in-between angles, use RotSprite: Scale2x/EPX three times to x8, rotate, then sample back down by mode.
- (2) Build the landed pose from an exact 90° turn of the standing pose, with the face and hands touched up by hand.
- (3) Paladin: cut the real idle sword from the standing frame instead of the 18x6 template.
- (4) Key poses at multiples of 1/30 s (martial-falls.js:9: i / 30), or round each key to whole 1/60 s steps.

If RotSprite is too costly, hand-key 4-6 fall poses per hero.

### heroes-8 [unchecked] Wraith recruit changes palette between idle and walk/attack frames: it pops pale every time it moves
- defect / art - public/assets/heroes-approved/wraith.js: walk frames #41-#56 and attack frames #17-#24 (and #1) against idle #25-#40; regenerated by tools/animation-round/build.mjs; the switch is at game.js:3473

This combines naturally with the quantisation pass.

- defect / owner - public/assets/heroes-approved/*.js (live since ee1cc16, 2026-11-07; README names 'Sir Aldric'). None is imported under public/js; knight-plumed is used only by tools/animation-round/capture-review.mjs
- Evidence: [Both lenses] Edge-pixel colours (art-extra/edges.mjs):

### heroes-11 [unchecked] Chest signature card draws the pose at min(2, 64/h): 16 of 22 heroes at a fractional 1.39-1.94
- polish / code - public/js/ui/gamescreen.js:1364-1366 on disk (HEAD 1361-1363; another session's uncommitted hunk adds 3 lines above it, and these lines are untouched)
- Evidence: [Both lenses] menu-poses-x4.png, last number under each pose: Knight x1.94, Sorcerer 1.60, Shadow 1.88, Necromancer 1.83, Druid 1.68, Ea-nasir 1.39, Wraith 1.88, Arcanist 1.60, Seraph 1.88. Only Ranger, Berserker, Marksman, Templar, Trailblazer and Boltguard get exactly 2. The Knight misses 2 only because the fringe makes his pose 33 rows instead of 32.
- At 1.94 one art row in 33 is drawn 1 UI px tall instead of 2, leaving a visible seam. At 1.6-1.9 most heroes get a mix of 1- and 2-px rows.
- Fix: Use an integer, with h taken from the fringe-cleaned pose: `ps = Math.max(1, Math.floor(64 / h))`, or a fixed `ps = 2`. Or use view.overlay with a 64-UI-px box for whole device-pixel multiples. Coordinate with the session editing gamescreen.js.

### heroes-14 [unchecked] Hero HP bar sits on the UI grid with the unshaken camera: it ticks in 3-4 px steps and detaches during screen shake
- polish / code - public/js/game/hud.js:1003-1012 (x/y rounded in UI px from g.cam); public/js/game/game.js:3231-3234 (shake applied to c.x/c.y for the world draw only) and :3415-3416 (restored before the HUD draws)
- Evidence: [Technical lens] The hero is placed in device px (game.js:3484), but his bar is placed in whole UI px: 3 device px at 1600x900, 4 at 1080p. The camera damps toward the hero (game.js:3034, damp 9), so on start, stop and turn the bar jumps up to 2-3 device px off. A boss-kill shake (0.8) offsets the world by up to ±2.9 world px (about ±7 device px at 2.4). The hero shakes, but his bar, elite name bars and the soul stay still (chrome-death-knight-x2.png).
- The hero and his own HP bar are out of sub-pixel register: the bar jitters under him at every start, stop and heavy hit.
- Fix: Draw the hero bar in the world pass at the end of Game.drawHero, while c is still shaken, in device px: `const u = view.S, x = Math.round((p.x - c.x) * c.S), y = Math.round((p.y + 3 - c.y) * c.S);` then `fillRect(x - 9*u, y, 18*u, 4*u)` and so on. Or keep it in the HUD, add the frame's shake offset, and position it from p._rect.

### heroes-7 [rejected] Painted hero sheets are unquantised (hundreds of colours per frame) with alpha fringes, so idles boil instead of breathing
- defect / code - Bake tool tools/animation-round/build.mjs:125-143 (pack keeps every distinct RGBA) and :79-103 (warp/materialField idle, nearest-neighbour sub-pixel warp); runtime unpack in public/js/art/approved-heroes.js:7-18. Another session has uncommitted work in tools/animation-round/ (README, index.html, review.js) and in public/assets/heroes/* (hero gait), so coordinate before rebaking
- Evidence: [Art lens; root cause shared with the hurt-flash and pose-crop findings]

Colours in idle frame 0: Knight 417, Sorcerer 589, Arcanist 654. The cleaned sheets have far fewer: Ranger 51, Berserker 52, Ea-nasir 16. Whole sheets: 2063-6643 colours.

Alpha: the body sits at 250-254, plus 62-149 faint pixels per frame.

The art-extra/boil.mjs probe: between consecutive idle frames, 1-8.5% of body pixels change by 8 levels or less per channel (Shadow 8.5%, Wraith 7.9%, Necromancer 4.8%), against 0-0.1% on the cleaned sheets.

Images:
- art-extra/idle-step-change-x5.png: each idle step reshuffles an elliptical patch of cape or robe rather than moving a fold.
- art-extra/quantise-32-x5.png: one 32-colour palette per hero with snapped alpha looks identical at x5 (mean error 5.7-12.2 RGB).
- Near-duplicate colours shuffled by a sub-pixel warp make the breathing idles shimmer. With 5000+ colours per sheet, the owner's colour-tune workflow (def.palette exact '#rrggbb' keys, tools/bake-tune.mjs) cannot practically recolour these heroes, though it works on the 51-colour Ranger. Several palette-tune fixes below depend on this.
- Fix: In build.mjs pack(), before indexing:
- (a) Snap alpha: below 32 becomes 0, 224 and above becomes 255, and anything between is thresholded at 128.
- (b) Quantise each hero's whole sheet to one 32-48 colour palette (weighted k-means over all frames, as in art-extra/quant.mjs; use 48 for the Sorcerer and Druid flames).
- (c) In materialField, round each part's displacement to whole pixels so a cape moves as a unit.

Until the rebake, the runtime alpha clamp from the hurt-flash finding is the stopgap. After quantising, re-check the Sorcerer and Arcanist flame flicker, since flicker() picks from the palette.
- Reproduce sceptic (not real, taste): The numbers are right, but the conclusion drawn from them is wrong. Re-measured on the shipped frames, which load through public/assets/heroes-approved/* via spriteBuffers: - Colours in idle frame 0: Knight 446, Sorcerer 645, Arcanist 737. Ranger has 51, Berserker 52, Ea-nasir 16. - Whole sheets have 1550-9742 colours. - Body alpha is 250-254. None of that makes the idles shimmer. The changes the critic calls "boil" are steps of 8 levels or less, averaging 3.7-5.1 levels. That is below what the eye can see on a pixel. I ran a perceptual test (verify/quantdiff.mjs). It counts the share of pixels per idle step whose colour visibly changes (CIE Lab dE of 5 or more, and of 10 or more). Then it quantises each whole sheet to 32 colours (48 for the casters) with alpha snapped, the critic's own method, and counts again. Quantising barely changes the visible counts: | Hero | dE10 before | dE10 after | dE5 before | dE5 after | |---|---|---|---|---| | Knight | 11.67% | 11.53% | | | | Shadow | 12.38% | 12.27% | | | | Necromancer | 7.77% | 7.86% | | | | Wraith | 11.93% | 13.01% | 17.25% | 18.47% | The Wraith, the critic's second-worst case, gets worse. The clean 51-colour Ranger already has the highest visible change per step of the whole roster (19.55% at dE10). So the many near-duplicate colours are not the cause. What the eye sees comes from the warp itself. build.mjs warp() is an inverse nearest-neighbour warp, so every output pixel copies a source pixel. Each rig ellipse moves as a 1-px block. - verify/strip-knight.png: frame 1 is a solid orange (visible-change) ellipse. That is the "mantle" part at [33,43,8,10], which on this front-facing sprite sits over the belt and sword hilt, so the hilt area slides 1 px. Blue (invisible) pixels are a small minority. - verify/strip-rogue.png: shows the Shadow's dithered cape being displaced, not a colour shuffle. Fix (c) says to round each part's displacement to whole pixels, but every pixel already moves by a whole pixel. A rigid per-part shift would only trade the moving contour for a hard ring seam. Alpha: about 100 of the 80-186 faint pixels per frame are alpha 1-15. They are invisible in a normal draw and only matter to effects that read alpha, which is the separate hurt-flash finding. The tuner point is a guess. tune.js applies its sliders to every colour slot, and tuned.js is empty today. Quantising 2.5-7k painted shades to 32 colours also costs a mean error of 5.7-12.2 RGB. That repaints the owner's settled final looks, and it means rebaking files the other session is editing right now (tools/animation-round/*, public/assets/heroes/*). Better fix: Don't quantise or rebake to fix shimmer, because quantising doesn't remove any visible change. Two smaller options: (1) Alpha: if the hurt flash or another alpha-reading effect shows the fringe, apply a cheap alpha snap at runtime unpack in public/js/art/approved-heroes.js (or later at bake): below 16 becomes 0, 224 and above becomes 255. Normal drawing doesn't need it. (2) Idle motion: if the owner finds the idles wobbly, treat it as its own rig finding about build.mjs rigs.parts. Several ellipses sit on the front of the body rather than on cloth behind it (the Knight's [33,43] moves the belt and sword hilt). Move them onto actual cape pixels, or weight them by a cloth mask instead of an ellipse, so only the mantle sways. Verify that by viewing the animation, not by counting colour deltas. Either change touches the other session's tools/animation-round and public/assets/heroes work, so coordinate first.
- Intent sceptic (not real, taste): The data is correct, but the cause it blames is wrong, and keeping the colours is a deliberate, tested choice. 1) The colour counts and alpha values check out. Knight has 6349 palette entries, Wraith 9743 and Seraph 7862. Most body pixels sit at alpha 224-254. Ranger has 52 colours and Berserker 53. 2) Quantising does not calm the idle. My probe C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/heroes/verify/energy.mjs composites each idle frame over ground. It then sums the luminance change shown between consecutive idle frames, before and after the critic's own 32-colour k-means. The totals barely move: - Knight 4613 -> 4596 - Rogue 4792 -> 4847 - Wraith 4677 -> 4630 - Mage 6007 -> 6037 - Every hero is within about ±2%. Quantising only removes the deltas of 8 levels or less, which are near-invisible anyway. It turns them into fewer, larger jumps: the share of pixels changing by more than 24 goes up for 19 of 22 heroes. The image C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/heroes/verify/idle-change-orig-vs-quant-x4.png shows the change maps are practically the same either way. The "elliptical patch" is the rigs' deliberate cloth, mantle and robe ripple (materialField parts with per-part lag). It is not palette noise. The cleaned 52-colour Ranger also changes 17% of its pixels by large amounts per step. 3) Keeping the palette is deliberate. tools/animation-round/README.md says "each character's original palette, 80×64 canvas ... idles and stationary attack clips are retained". The 7 November pass "keeps every selected character model ... palette and equipment identity". The Knight walk "preserves existing colours". test/knight-gait.test.js asserts "travel must preserve the approved colour/alpha palette". Only Ranger, Berserker and Ea-nasir were moved to the master palette (hero-style-match-2026-11-15), and that was because their higher density made them look smoother than the rest of the roster. The other heroes were the roster those three were matched to. 4) Fix (a), the alpha snap at 128, would erase or harden the Wraith's partly transparent shroud (736 palette entries at alpha 32-223). hero-polish-graphics and hero-round2-graphics both record the Wraith's travel translucency as intentional. 5) Fix (c) is a motion redesign of idles the owner has already reviewed. Rounding a soft-weighted field to whole pixels per part creates seams at the patch edge. 6) The tuner argument is speculative. public/js/art/tuned.js TUNED is empty, so no production hero has ever been colour-tuned. 7) The rebake would mean rerunning build.mjs over 21 approved sheets. That would change every picked look (mean error 5.7-12.2 RGB) while another session has uncommitted work in tools/animation-round/ and public/assets/heroes/*. Better fix: No rebake and no quantising. The faint fringe pixels only matter where an effect turns every pixel with any alpha solid, which is the hurt flash. Handle that with the runtime alpha clamp proposed in the separate hurt-flash finding. If the owner later wants a calmer idle, lower the ripple strength of the cloth parts in the rigs table in tools/animation-round/build.mjs (columns 5 and 6 of each part: horizontal and vertical swing) for the heroes he names, and rebuild. That is a motion tweak he would approve, done after the other session's animation work lands, not a palette change.

## monsters

Both critics agree on the main point. The monster art is not pixel-perfect yet, but it is strong and clean at the pixel level, and most of the defects come from runtime compositing rather than from the art. The tech lens checked all 69 kinds' frames from the production builder. No frame is clipped, no pixel is semi-transparent, the feet anchors are consistent and the generated outline is clean. Several runtime details are also right: the camera snaps to whole screen pixels, the padded night layer lines up exactly with the lit body, and flyer hover, shadows, the y-sort by feet, whole-pixel death debris and the allies' green ring all work. The art lens finds the 2026-10-27 Darkwood set coherent: one ink weight, ENDESGA hue-shifted ramps, clear silhouettes and a sensible size ladder up to the 28 px treant and the 32 px Knight. Crowds y-sort cleanly, death bursts differ by material, and most map variants are good themed work (frost troll, sand and magma golems, treant, flameskull, myconid, snapjaw).

What stops it being pixel-perfect is mostly code. Champions, the foes the player is meant to read, lose their identity in several ways:
- the ink-to-gold swap gilds their faces and inner lines;
- the additive aura is drawn over their own bodies;
- by day on sand the gold replaces their only dark edge;
- frozen, burning or winding-up champions drop the gold entirely, and a winding-up one strobes it.

At night a hit flash makes a foe greyer and darker, not white, and merges a crowd under AoE into one mass. The foes nearest the hero are 25-34% dimmer than those further out. Risers come up unlit, then pop bright, and let the foe behind show through for 0.3 s. Status tints wash out the outline. The wind-up strobes at 10 Hz even under FLASHES: SOFT. Champion names collide and can cover the hero.

Behind all of this is one global gap: the world is drawn at a fractional scale (1.6, 2.4 or 3.2 screen px per art px), so art pixels come out uneven on every screen below 1440p. The x1.35, x1.5, flash and slime stretches add more uneven pixels on top.

On the art side:
- the x1.5 Cultist family breaks the shared pixel density;
- the Abyss horde is one red mass (Fiendling and Imp share colours);
- Anubite, Frost Witch and Thorn Witch fall short of the set's finish;
- the Cultist's rotated orb becomes a medkit cross;
- the night lift clips skeleton bone to pure white.

Almost all of the code fixes are small and local: edge-only outline swaps, the aura under the existing horde mask, an unlit flash that keeps its outline, a partial night hole, a cropped night layer for risers, and tints that skip the ink. Together they would lift readability more than any redraw. Two findings need the owner: the integer world scale, and whether monsters keep their uniform ink outline. One more needs the owner if the integer-scale fix is not taken: how champions are scaled. No finding touches the other session's uncommitted files. The whole review was done in memory and in the scratchpad (C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/art-extra/ and .../tech-extra/), and nothing in the repo was changed. The tech lens also measured draw-order swaps in a packed crowd, about 107 flip-backs a second. A sort-hysteresis mock removed none of them, so they are real jostling, not a sort bug, and are not listed.

### monsters-1 [confirmed] At night a hit flash turns foes grey and darker, not white, and merges a crowd under AoE into one blob
- bug / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:940-942 (flash draws fr.white in the lit pass, look -1), :962 (no night look, so the flash cuts itself out of the night layer), :146-150 (1.12x0.9 flash stretch), drawEmissive :1012-1036; public/js/engine/sprites.js:153-155 + public/js/engine/pixels.js:74-78 (the silhouette whitens the outline too). Both lenses (art + tech).
- Evidence: closeup-hit-death-forest.png, row 'hit' against row 'before'.
- Skeleton: bones #f8f8f7 (L 249) before; the flash peaks at #bbbbbd (L 202), top-10% luminance 249 to 200.
- Thief: 222 to 138 (rgb 129/139/160, dark slate).
- Slime: 252 to a mean of 157.
- Imp inside the hero's light: 143 to 254, the only one that works.

C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/art-extra/ab-night-hitflash.png: all eight flashing foes beyond the hero's light dull to grey-blue.

C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/tech-extra/flash-ab.png:
- panel A: 18 foes flashing together become one lilac-grey mass (max L 198);
- panel B: white body with the dark outline kept, drawn unlit; every foe stays a separate white figure (max L 255).

field-forest-aoe-crop-centre-x2.png: a white/grey blob right of the hero (x 480-600, y 200-330).
- The white silhouette is drawn into the lit scene before the lighting multiply. Out in the dark it becomes ambient-coloured, about 103-110/111-115/164-168 in the forest. With look -1 it also cuts itself out of the night layer and loses its lift, ring and halo, so the flash makes a monster darker than its normal lifted look. Hit feedback is reversed on 3 of the 5 maps, and worst at the screen edge, where the lift is strongest. The silhouette also turns the dark outline white, so flashing neighbours have no edge between them. Under AoE the crowd becomes one shape, and gaps drawn in outline (the crypt archer's bow) fill in. The fractional 1.12x0.9 stretch adds uneven pixels on top.
- Fix: (a) Keep the outline in the flash. In Frame.white, set non-ink pixels to WHITE and keep the pixels equal to b.ink ?? OUTLINE (new variant key 'white-ink').
(b) Draw the flash unlit. Either:
- add NIGHT_LOOKS[7] = ['night-flash', b => px.pad(whiteInk(b), NIGHT_PAD)] with no ring, and use look 7 instead of -1 in draw() (exempt it from the champion +3), so it composites in y-order at about 248; or
- in drawEmissive, when this.flash > 0 and the foe is not a boss or lord, draw the white frame over this._rect at this.alpha after drawNightEdge, keeping the lit-pass draw for the cut-out.
Either gives white at every distance and on day maps too. A night look alone (variant B in ab-night-hitflash.png) fixes far foes but leaves foes near the hero grey, so check near ones.
(c) Drop the 1.12x0.9 stretch (FLASH_BOUNDS/renderScale), or limit it to +2 art px wide and -1 art px tall.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, bug): Confirmed in code and in my own render of the production Game.draw. The finding is real but partly overstated, and one of its two fixes is broken. Code: draw() at enemies.js:940-942 draws fr.white (px.silhouette, every opaque pixel including ink set to WHITE) in the lit pass and sets look -1. lighting.render (lighting.js:142) then multiplies the scene by the light map, so beyond the hero's light the flash becomes about the ambient colour (forest 103/110/164). drawNightEdge gives a _look -1 foe no lift; it only cuts its shape out of the night layer behind it. The comment at :961 ("A white flash needs no lifting") shows the intent was white, so this is a logic bug, not taste. Files enemies.js, sprites.js, pixels.js and game.js have no uncommitted changes. My measurements (verify/flash-far.mjs): 1080p world scale (S 4 × zoom 0.8 = 3.2), camera centred on the hero, p90 luminance of each foe's silhouette, before → flashing. - Forest, +220/+260 px: skeleton 239→88 / 238→77, goblin 174→80 (+260), zombie 173→77 (+260), thief 223→90 / 196→76. - Hell, +220/+260 px: every kind lands at 78-94. - Meadow, +260 px: about 150, against 169-234 before. - Bone-white kinds already dim from about +100 px (skeleton 243→144). At 1080p the screen reaches about ±300 world px across, so most foes on screen flash dull slate-blue, darker than their lifted look (verify/flash-far-forest-a.png). By day (desert, frozen: sun 255/248/230 and 240/246/255) the flash stays about 250, so only the night maps are affected. That covers meadow, forest and hell, plus Ranked, Dungeon, crypts and Darkwood, which are also night. Overstated parts: - Inside about 100-140 px (the hero's light) most kinds flash brighter than before (goblin 168→255, zombie 167→255). The imp is not "the only one that works"; the reversal depends on distance. - The cited AoE crop is ambiguous: the white shape sits inside the hero's light and could be weapon VFX. - The crowd turning into one blob (a) is mostly the grey lit result. Keeping the outline in the flash is reasonable polish, not the bug. - (c) adds little. Sprite pixels at 1080p are already uneven because the base scale is fractional (3.2). renderScale's 1.12×0.9 also feeds body() and reach (the hitbox), so removing it changes hit geometry while a foe flashes. The two proposed fixes, tested in memory (verify/flash-far-forest-b.png): - Option 1 (a night look for the flash): white at every distance (p90 235-255 to +260 px) with occlusion kept. Foes behind trees stay hidden. - Option 2 (white redrawn in drawEmissive): white everywhere, but the white paints over trees and over foes standing in front. The skeleton at +180 and the goblin and zombie at +220 show through the trees. The finding offers it as an equal alternative ("Either gives white at every distance"); it is not one. Better fix: Use only option 1, in enemies.js only. That file is not in the other session's uncommitted work. 1. Append `NIGHT_LOOKS[7] = ['night-flash', (b) => nightBuf(px.silhouette(b, WHITE), 0)]`, with no ring (nightBuf with ring 0 is px.pad(b, NIGHT_PAD)). If the owner wants (a), build the look from a white-ink buffer instead: non-ink pixels set to WHITE, pixels equal to b.ink ?? OUTLINE kept, with Frame.white switched to the same 'white-ink' variant. 2. In draw(), make the flash branch set look = 7, and replace line 962 with `this._look = this.boss || this.lord || this.prop ? -1 : this.elite && look < 7 ? look + 3 : look;` so a champion's flash never becomes the nonexistent look 10, which would crash drawNightEdge. 3. Fix the comment at :961 and the drawNightEdge header comment, which still lists "a foe flashing white" among the things that cut the layer. That gives about 238-255 at every night distance with y-order occlusion intact. Near the hero, where the hero hole clears the night layer, the lit flash already measures 246-255. Day maps need nothing, since the flash there is white × sun ≈ 248. Do not use the drawEmissive redraw: it draws flashing foes through trees and over foes standing in front. Leave the 1.12×0.9 stretch (c) alone: it also shapes body() and reach (the hitbox), and the 1080p world scale is already fractional (3.2).
- Intent sceptic (real, bug): The finding is real, and nothing the owner or an earlier review decided covers it. The comment at enemies.js:961 ("A white flash needs no lifting", from db3987d) shows the author expected the flash to stay white without help. It doesn't, for two reasons. First, the flash is drawn before game.js:3338 applies the lighting with a 'multiply' composite and before the night vignette at :3349, so it picks up the ambient colour. Second, look -1 sends the foe to nightCut, so it cuts its own shape out of the night layer and loses its lift. I checked this in memory with the production Game (forest night, x4). The image is C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/flash-verify.png and the script is verify/flash-verify.mjs in the same folder. Top-10% luminance, before the hit and then flashing as shipped: skeleton 215 to 190, thief 191 to 168. Both get darker when hit. The goblin goes from 179 to only 195, a pale grey-blue blur. The bright night ring is lost, and the flashing shapes have no edges. None of the reviews in reviews/ settled this. They only touch the stretch: hero-round2-graphics-2026-11-14 kept the flash/squash draw and the body aligned on purpose. No settled call is involved. The bug shows on meadow, forest and hell, and in crypts, Ranked, Dungeon and the Darkwood. Desert and frozen are fine because nightFloor is 0 by day. The proposed fix is not sound as a whole: - Part (c) is wrong. renderScale() is shared with Enemy.body() (enemies.js:901, "Drawing and collision share the same squash/stretch"), and FLASH_BOUNDS feeds `reach`, which decides grid.big. Removing or capping the 1.12x0.9 stretch changes hit detection, which is a gameplay change. Changing it only in draw would undo the deliberate draw/body alignment from the 2026-11-14 repair. - The drawEmissive alternative in (b) breaks occlusion. The emissive pass draws after every lit body, so a flashing foe is painted over whatever stands in front of it. In my render, an orc behind a gravestone covers the gravestone, and a foe behind a tree would show through the canopy. - The night-look option in (b), together with (a), works. In the night layer, y-ordered with the existing cuts, the same foes read 247-253 at every distance, white with their ink edges. Things in front stay in front. A crowd stays separate figures. It is draw-only: no update code and no Math.random calls change. - Near the hero the light hole limits the lift: the skeleton at 30 px goes 215 to 220, never darker than before. That is the same as any white thing in his light, and acceptable. - Two cautions for (a). test/boneking-sweep.test.js:155 expects a boss's flash image to equal frame.white(false), so give the outlined white frame ('white-ink') to non-boss, non-lord foes only, or update that test. Leave Frame.white itself alone, because the hero's flash uses it (game.js:3477, :3513). - None of the touched files (enemies.js, sprites.js, pixels.js) has uncommitted work from the other session. Better fix: Make two changes, both draw-only: 1. In enemies.js, add an outlined white flash frame, a new variant key 'white-ink': fr.variant('white-ink', b => white everywhere except pixels equal to b.ink ?? OUTLINE, which stay ink, flip). Use it in Enemy.draw for non-boss, non-lord foes; bosses and lords keep fr.white, since they get their own white light (game.js:3321) and a test pins it. Do not change Frame.white, which the hero uses. 2. Add NIGHT_LOOKS[7] = ['night-flash', b => nightBuf(whiteInk(b), 0)], with no ring. In draw(), set look = 7 instead of -1 for the flash, and exempt it from the champion +3, for example: this._look = this.boss || this.lord || this.prop ? -1 : look === 7 ? 7 : this.elite ? look + 3 : look. The flash then joins the night layer in y-order, about 248 bright beyond the hero's light, cut by whatever stands in front, and unchanged by day. Do not add the drawEmissive redraw, because it paints over trees, props and foes standing in front. Keep the 1.12x0.9 stretch and FLASH_BOUNDS as they are: body() and the hit detection share renderScale.

### monsters-2 [confirmed] Risers come up unlit and pop bright a second later, and the foe behind shows through them for 0.3 s
- bug / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:984-990 (rise: _look -1), :1077-1082 (nightShare fades in over NIGHT_RISE 0.3 s), :1187-1192 (layer drawn at alpha a<1 over earlier layers without cutting first; `if (a < 0.02) continue` skips the cut), :1216 (nightCut skips foes still rising), :1250. Both lenses (art + tech).
- Evidence: closeup-rise-forest.png:
- at +0.6 to +0.8 s the risen skeleton, mummy and damned are dull grey-brown;
- at +1.1 s the skeleton jumps to pure white with a ring;
- the fade-in kinds (bat, wolf, cultist) are lifted from the first frame.

C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/tech-extra/riser-nightcut.png:
- bottom row (+0.8 s, risen, night layer fading in): the ogre's lifted orange body shows through the skeleton in front of it (x 150-280, y 380-560), the orc's through the zombie, the death knight's through the skeleton;
- top row (+0.33 s): a rising foe never cuts the layers behind it.
- For about 1.05 s a riser has no night colours, then brightens over 0.3 s, so a ring of 20+ skeletons visibly 'switches on'. drawNightEdge only cuts the night canvas for objects without a night look. A foe with a look draws its layer source-over at its nightShare, so during the 0.3 s NIGHT_RISE fade and the 0.25 s spawn fade the lifted foe behind stays visible at (1-a). Mid-rise foes are skipped by the cut entirely.

Seven kinds rise: skeletons, ghouls, mummies, frostbitten, myconids, snapjaws and the damned. Skeletons alone are about 14% of the steady horde on desert, frozen and hell, so in a dense horde some foe is ghosting at almost any moment.
- Fix: In draw(), record the visible rows of a riser (this._vis = vis). Then:
- in drawNightEdge, draw the riser's night look cropped the same way, source rect (0, 0, fr.w + 2·NIGHT_PAD, vis + NIGHT_PAD) and scaled the same, so it emerges already in night colours, and drop the NIGHT_RISE ramp;
- in the drawNightEdge loop, if a < 1, first destination-out the foe's own shape (o._img over its rounded _rect, alpha 1), then draw its layer at alpha a; for a < 0.02, still cut, just don't draw;
- make nightCut (and horde-over.js drawShape) cut and mask risers with the same cropped rows instead of skipping them.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, bug): The finding is real. I checked it in the code, in pixels and with a frequency count. Code: - In Enemy.draw, the rise branch (enemies.js:984-990) sets _look = -1. So a riser gets no night layer for its whole 0.75 s rise (spawnMax = 0.75 for def.rise, line 306). - It also gets no cut. A foe with _look -1 goes to nightCut, which returns early for `foe && o.spawnT > 0 && o.def.rise` (line 1216). horde-over.js:37 skips risers the same way. - After the rise, nightShare ramps a from 0 to 1 over NIGHT_RISE = 0.3 s (line 1080). drawNightEdge draws the layer source-over at alpha a without cutting first, and when a < 0.02 it does `continue` with no cut at all (line 1187). - The night canvas then goes over the lit scene at f, measured at 0.909 on a forest night. Pixels: verify/riser-ghost.mjs places a skeleton riser 5 px in front of an ogre and checks the night canvas with and without the ogre, at the riser's own opaque pixels. - +0.33 s (rising): the ogre's lifted layer covers 54% of the riser's visible pixels (mean night alpha 132). - +0.6 s: 75% (alpha 201). - +0.8 s, +0.9 s and +1.0 s (a = 0.17, 0.50, 0.83): 89%, 89% and 83%. - +1.1 s (a = 1): 0%. - In the frames, the rising skull reads as behind the orange ogre, then the ogre's body shows through the skeleton's ribs. - Image: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/riser-ghost.png The critic's capture (riser-nightcut.png) uses the production g.draw and only pins positions, so it is not an artefact. closeup-rise-forest.png also shows the dull, unlifted risers at +0.6/+0.8 s and the lifted ones at +1.1 s. Frequency: verify/riser-freq.mjs ran a real spawner on forest and hell for 120 game s, with the hero walking in circles. - About 400 risers were seen rising or ramping on screen. - About 200 of them overlapped a foe behind them, more than 40 px from the hero. - In forest, 12% of frames had one ghosting while rising and 9% while ramping. Overstated parts: - Ring and last-stand spawns are instant (spawner.js:426), so "a ring of 20+ skeletons switching on" doesn't happen. - The unlifted rise followed by a 0.3 s fade-in is the code's stated intent, not an accidental pop. - The spawn-fade half of the description is not a defect. During the 0.25 s spawn fade, and for Wraiths (def.alpha 0.78, phaser 0.58-0.78) and a boss burning at dawn, the lit body itself is translucent. So the foe behind showing through the night layer matches the lit scene. The real defect is the z-order inversion: a riser looks behind the foe it stands in front of for 0.75 s, then that foe ghosts through it for 0.3 s. It happens often in a night horde, so I rate it a bug. The fix as written is not sound: - Step 2 (when a < 1, destination-out the foe's own shape at alpha 1, then draw at a) would also run every frame for Wraiths, for every foe's spawn fade, and for dawn-burning bosses. Foes seen through those genuinely translucent bodies would show unlifted and dark. It is also unnecessary once the ramp is gone. - It leaves out nightShare's `spawnT > 0` factor.
