That factor would still fade a riser's cropped layer in over its rise, which brings back the (1-a) ghosting. Better fix: Keep the riser's night look and draw it cropped at full share. Leave the translucent cases alone. (1) In Enemy.draw's rise branch, drop `this._look = -1;`. Record `this._vis = vis;` and keep _rect as the full rectangle, as it is now. (2) In nightShare, change the spawn fade to `if (this.spawnT > 0 && !this.def.rise) a *= 1 - this.spawnT / this.spawnMax;` and delete the NIGHT_RISE ramp and the constant. A rising body is drawn opaque, so its layer goes at alpha 1. Drawing it source-over already hides the layers behind, with no extra cut. (3) In drawNightEdge, for an Enemy with `o.spawnT > 0 && o.def.rise`, set `k = h / fr.h` and `v = o._vis`, then draw `n.drawImage(fr.variant(key, fn, flip), 0, 0, fr.w + 2*NIGHT_PAD, v + NIGHT_PAD, X - ux, Math.round(r[1] + r[3] - v*k) - uy, w + 2*ux, (v + NIGHT_PAD)*k)`. This is the same crop Enemy.draw uses, plus the top and side padding for the ring and halo. (4) In nightCut, a riser hit while rising flashes white and gets _look -1. Instead of returning early, cut its cropped rows the same way (source 0,0,img.width,_vis to the rise rectangle). horde-over.js drawShape can mask risers the same way. That part is optional: it is about weapons drawing over a rising foe, not the night ghost. (5) Do not add a general 'a < 1, cut first' rule. Wraiths, the 0.25 s spawn fade and the dawn-burning boss are translucent in the lit scene too, so the layer behind should keep showing through them lifted.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real, and no settled call or earlier review covers it. enemies.js is not one of the files the other session has uncommitted work in. What the code does: - While a foe is rising, draw() sets `_look = -1`, so it has no night layer. nightCut and horde-over.js drawShape both skip it on purpose, because only some of its rows are drawn. - Once it has risen, nightShare() fades the night layer in over NIGHT_RISE (0.3 s). All of this came in with db3987d. The ramp only softens the pop that the missing night layer would otherwise cause. No comment, commit or review says risers should come up dull. Why it is a defect: 1. Every riser out of the hero's light comes up in dull ambient colours. After 0.75 s it brightens noticeably (grey to white for the skeleton in closeup-rise-forest.png). Bats, wolves and cultists that fade in have their night colours from the first frame. This goes against the owner's "monsters visible at night". 2. During the ramp the riser's body is fully opaque, but its night layer goes source-over at only a = 0.17 to 1. So at (1 − a), the lifted layer of the foe behind it shows through. riser-nightcut.png's bottom row shows it plainly: the ogre's orange through the skeleton. 3. While it rises, the foe behind is never cut, so that foe's whole layer covers the rows already above ground. Both 2 and 3 break drawNightEdge's own stated rule: "nothing shows through what stands in front of it … a foe through another foe". Each case lasts only about 1 s and needs foes to overlap, so this is a defect, not a bug. Part of the proposed fix is sound: draw the riser's night look cropped to the rows above ground, and drop the 0.3 s ramp. Nothing touches update(), Math.random or gameplay, and the hero looks are unchanged. Two parts are not sound: - The second step (when a < 1, cut the foe's full shape at alpha 1 before drawing its layer) breaks translucent foes:   - the Wraith (def.alpha 0.78, pulsing in the 'phaser' AI);   - bats and wolves fading in;   - bosses fading out in the sunrise (game.js:2708, burnBoss).   The lit scene shows the foe behind through these at (1 − a), but its night layer would be erased there, leaving a dull silhouette. When a foe's night share equals its body's opacity, drawing source-over at alpha a already blends correctly. The real fault is only the ramp, where the share is lower than an opaque body. - The fix leaves out that nightShare() also scales by `1 − spawnT/spawnMax` for risers. If their cropped layer were drawn during the rise, it would start nearly transparent and bring the ghost back. Better fix: Keep it to the riser path in public/js/game/enemies.js, with no other compositing changes: (1) nightShare(): `let a = this.alpha; if (this.spawnT > 0 && !this.def.rise) a *= 1 - this.spawnT / this.spawnMax; return a;` Delete the NIGHT_RISE line and its const. A riser's body is opaque, just cropped, so its layer uses its full alpha. (2) draw(), in the riser branch: keep `_img` and `_look` as computed (remove `this._look = -1`), and store `this._vis = vis`. Its `_rect` already holds the uncropped rectangle, whose bottom is the ground line. (3) drawNightEdge, Enemy branch: if `o.spawnT > 0 && o.def.rise`, let `v = o._vis` and `k = h / fr.h`. Draw `fr.variant(key, fn, flip)` with source rect (0, 0, fr.w + 2·NIGHT_PAD, v + NIGHT_PAD) to destination (X − ux, Y + h − v·k − uy, w + 2ux, (v + NIGHT_PAD)·k). At a = 1, source-over covers whatever is behind, so no extra cut is needed. (4) nightCut, and optionally horde-over.js drawShape: for a riser, instead of returning early, draw `_img` source rows 0..`_vis` at (r0, r1 + r3 − `_vis`·k, r2, `_vis`·k). That covers a riser flashing white mid-rise, and keeps a riser in front of the weapons. Leave the `a < 0.02` skip and the plain source-over at alpha a as they are. They are already right for translucent Wraiths, foes fading in, and bosses burning in the sunrise. All of this is draw-only: no update() or Math.random changes, no gameplay effect, no hero-sheet files touched.

### monsters-5 [confirmed] The Cultist's darkbolt rotates into a white plus on a magenta square: a health-pack icon
- bug / code - public/js/game/projectiles.js:408 (SHOT_LOOK.darkbolt scale 1.5), :523-527 (EnemyShot.draw: f.rotated(this.ang, 32)); public/js/art/items.js:195 (6x6 round orb). Art lens.
- Evidence: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/art-extra/darkbolt-rotations.png: 7 of 8 sampled angles turn the round orb into a white '+', and angles 3/32-5/32 give a square framing a white cross.

In the field: field-forest-aoe-crop-centre-x2.png (230-300,180-320 and 380,350) and field-forest-combat-hud.png (400-460,350-500) show rows of red squares with white crosses at the cultists.
- Nearest-neighbour rotation can't keep a radially symmetric 6x6 orb round. It snaps to a cross or a square, and the white core becomes a plus. The result is the universal 'heal' icon, on a hostile bullet, drawn unlit over everything. The size 1.875×S (1.25 × 1.5) is also non-integer, so its pixels are uneven.
- Fix: Add a 'round' flag to SHOT_LOOK.darkbolt (and check iceshard, which is also symmetric), so that EnemyShot.draw draws f.c unrotated at an integer factor such as size 2. Optionally make the core 'i' pink instead of 'w' white so it never reads as a cross.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): The defect is real, and I confirmed it in code, in pixels and in real Chrome. Code: `EnemyShot.draw` (public/js/game/projectiles.js:526) always draws `f.rotated(this.ang, 32)`. `px.rotate` (pixels.js:108) uses nearest neighbour into a 9x9 buffer with floor sampling. When I ran the production 6x6 darkbolt (items.js:195) through all 32 rotations, the 2x2 white core became a 5-pixel 3x3 plus in 28 of them. Only the 4 axis-aligned indices kept a square core, and indices 16 and 24 even come out lopsided (3 px). Around 45° (indices 3-5, 11-13, ...) the outline also snaps to a magenta square, giving a white cross on a pink tile. Real Chrome (localhost:3017, 1920x1080, view.S 4, cam.S 3.2): I placed 32 stationary darkbolts and ringed live cultists round the hero. The live shots show the pink square with a white plus beside the Knight. See verify/chrome-darkbolt-live-2.png, near (480,285), and live-1.png, near (240,328) and (530,125). Before/after image: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/darkbolt-before-after.png. Drawing the unrotated `f.c` keeps the round orb at every angle. An orb has no heading, so the rotation does nothing useful. Where the critic is wrong: 1) The non-integer size point is mistaken. What lands on screen is `cam.S` × size, and `cam.S` is itself fractional (view.S × 0.8). At 1080p, 3.2 × 1.875 is exactly 6 device px. The proposed size 2 would give 6.4 there, which makes pixels uneven at the most common resolution, not fixes them. 2) The iceshard does not have this problem. Its core is one white pixel at every angle, so it never turns into a plus. 3) The critic missed the Crypt fireball (SHOT_LOOK.cryptfireball, the 8x8 round 'fireball' sprite at scale 1.5). Its 2x2 core also turns into a 5-pixel plus in 28 of 32 rotations, so the same fix should cover it. Neither file is in the other session's uncommitted set. Better fix: Skip the rotation for round orbs and leave their size as it is. In SHOT_LOOK, add `round: true` to `darkbolt` and `cryptfireball`, and optionally to `iceshard` so its rime stays on the upper facets as its comment intends. In `EnemyShot.draw`, change the image line to `const img = this.look.spin ? f.rotated(this.rot, 16) : this.look.round ? f.c : f.rotated(this.ang, 32);`. `drawCentered` already centres the 6x6 canvas, so nothing else changes: not collision (`touches` uses x/y, not the angle, unless `reach` is set), not the glow, and not the seeded dice. Do not change `scale` to make the size "integer": `cam.S` decides that, and 1.875 is already exactly 6 px at 1080p. The core can stay white because, drawn unrotated, it is a 2x2 square heart and no longer a cross.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): I reproduced it. The darkbolt (items.js:195) is a 6x6 orb with a 2x2 white core. EnemyShot.draw (projectiles.js:526) always passes it through f.rotated(this.ang, 32), and px.rotate samples it nearest-neighbour into a 9x9 buffer. At 28 of the 32 headings the white core becomes a 5-pixel plus. At about 12 of 32 (3/32 to 5/32 and the matching angles in each quadrant) the outline also snaps into a magenta square around that plus: a health-pack icon. My image of the shot as it looks now beside the unrotated fix is at C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/darkbolt-now-vs-fix.png. The field crop shows rows of these crosses at the Cultists. Nothing marks the rotation as deliberate: - The only darkbolt commit, d7f4310, set scale 1.5 so the orb is "big, slow, easy to see coming". It never asked for a rotating look. - The comment calls it "a big, slow orb". - The hero-side Projectile already draws its round wisp unrotated (frames[..].c, line 367), so a precedent exists. - None of the reviews in reviews/ mention it. - None of the owner's settled calls cover it. Why it matters: an enemy bullet that reads as a heal pickup misleads the player in combat, which is more than taste. It's a visual readability defect, not a logic bug, so I rate it "defect". The core fix (draw the frame unrotated) is sound. It is draw-only: no RNG, no collision change (darkbolt has no reach; touches uses r), and no hero art is touched. projectiles.js and items.js aren't in the other session's uncommitted files. The two optional extras aren't needed and I'd leave them out: - Changing the drawn size to an integer 2 would override the owner's tuned 1.875. All enemy shots use the non-integer SHOT_GROW 1.25, so it isn't specific to this shot. If done via this.size, it would also change the glow and light size. - Recolouring the core from 'w' to 'i' changes the art. Unrotated, the 2x2 white core already reads as a round hot centre. Better fix: Smallest fix, in projectiles.js only. Add `round: true` to SHOT_LOOK.darkbolt (line 408). In EnemyShot.draw (line 526), use `const img = this.look.round ? f.c : this.look.spin ? f.rotated(this.rot, 16) : f.rotated(this.ang, 32);`. This keeps scale 1.5 (size 1.875), the glow, the light and the sprite art as they are. Optionally set `round: true` on iceshard too: its comment says the rime sits on its "upper facets", which only holds unrotated. It doesn't form a cross, though, so it's not required. cryptfireball (SPR.fireball, 2x2 core) also turns into a plus when rotated, but in yellow and orange, so it doesn't read as a medkit; it's a candidate for the same flag, not required.

### monsters-6 [confirmed] Champion aura is drawn additively over the champion's own body and its neighbours, washing out their detail
- defect / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:1100-1121 (drawGlow: radial glow h*2.6 centred at h*0.45, alpha 0.28-0.38); public/js/game/game.js:3365 and :3376 (drawn in the 'lighter' pass over the horde, after the night layer). Both lenses (art + tech).
- Evidence: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/tech-extra/champ-glow-ab.png, the same frame with and without drawGlow:

| Champion | Darkest-10% L with / without | Mean saturation with / without |
|---|---|---|
| skeleton | 107 / 56 | 0.31 / 0.38 |
| ogre | 108 / 60 | 0.44 / 0.59 |
| orc | 100 / 47 | 0.37 / 0.63 |
| cultist | 58 / 36 | 0.60 / 0.73 |

C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/art-extra/ab-champion-aura.png: with the aura cut out of the champion, bones, shading and face come back.

closeup-champions-thief-forest.png:
- Grimfang (160-245,215-320) loses its ribs to a cyan-white blob;
- Bonehide (1140-1265,190-320) goes flat peach;
- the Vampiric cultist (1430-1530,495-640) goes flat pink.

Other shots:
- field-frozen-horde-crop-centre-x2.png: the Swift rime knight (100-160,20-90) is a featureless cyan block and the Stoneskin yeti (180-290,110-200) is lavender-white;
- field-forest-horde.png (300-450,350-450): the Brutal aura dyes nearby cultists orange;
- closeup-crypt-lord-skeletonarcher.png: the lord is washed the same way.
- The aura's opaque centre lands on the champion's chest and head and adds about +64 per channel (Stoneskin #c0cbdc) on top of a sprite the night layer has already lifted. Interior shading, outline and eye sockets collapse to a flat pastel. The most important foe on screen becomes the least legible, its neighbours get dyed, and by day on snow the Swift aura erases ice kinds outright.
- Fix: Draw the elite and boss glow under the horde, as the weapon glows already are:
- in game.js, move the drawGlow loop (:3376) inside the keepHorde/putHorde bracket at :3364-3368;
- at :3309, build the horde mask when (armed || an elite or boss is on screen).
The aura then lights the ground and air round the champion, never its own pixels or its neighbours'.

Alternatives: draw the auras into a scratch canvas, destination-out each elite's shape (as nightCut does) and composite with 'lighter'; or draw them in the ground pass after the shadows, with alpha raised to make up for the light map.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): Confirmed in the code and in real Chrome, so it is not a capture artefact. In enemies.js:1115-1120, drawGlow draws glow(col), whose gradient is fully opaque at the centre (fxsprites.js:163-171), at 2.6h across, centred 0.45h above the feet, with alpha 0.28-0.38. game.js:3376 draws it in the 'lighter' pass after drawNightEdge and outside the keepHorde/putHorde bracket at :3364-3368, so it adds straight onto the champion's own pixels and onto any monster within about 1.3h. Since horde-over.js (27 Oct), the weapons' glows go under the horde, but the elite and boss glow never moved with them. I re-rendered it in real Chrome (localhost:3017, 1600x900, camS 2.4) and drew the same frame with and without Enemy.drawGlow: verify/aura/chrome-forest-ab-x3.png and chrome-frozen-ab-x3.png. With the aura: - the Swift skeleton turns pale cyan-white; - the Stoneskin orc's green and armour wash to lime/pastel; - the Brutal ogre goes flat peach; - the Vampiric cultist goes pink. Measured over each champion's box (forest), darkest-10% luminance with/without: 78/51, 80/58, 86/45, 56/39. Mean saturation: 0.31/0.37, 0.44/0.56, 0.40/0.60, 0.55/0.67. By day on frozen the same lift (141/108 for the skeleton) makes the Swift skeleton nearly melt into the snow. Neighbour dyeing also shows in the cited field-forest-horde.png: the plain cultist beside the champions is visibly orange compared with the untouched one (verify/aura/field-forest-horde-brutal-x3.png). The critic's wording "least legible foe" goes a bit far: the gold outline, name and bar still mark the champion, and its shapes stay readable. Its shading and its bone and face detail do flatten, so this is a real visual defect, not a bug. No earlier review or owner call settles it. The 11-14 note "colored rims and glow preserve enemy silhouettes at night" is about the night rim. enemies.js and game.js are not among the other session's uncommitted files. I also tested the proposed fix in memory in Chrome, using the game's own horde-over.js maskHorde/maskBosses/keepHorde/putHorde (verify/aura/chrome-forest-fix-abc-x3.png). The champions' pixels come out identical to the no-aura frame, and the coloured halo still shows round each one. Better fix: The direction is sound and minimal. Two details for the implementer: (1) Widening only :3309 is not enough. maskBosses at :3324 is gated on `armed` as well, so the mask would have no bosses or crypt lords and a boss's own aura would still wash the boss. Gate both on the wider condition. Keep the first bracket (:3310-3313, shots and blasts) on `armed` so frames with no shots skip a wasted full-screen copy. For example: ``` const champs = tops.length > 0 || list.some((o) => o.elite); let horde = (armed || champs) && maskHorde(c, list, p); if (horde && armed) keepHorde(ctx, c); /* shots */ if (horde && armed) putHorde(ctx); ... if (armed || champs) horde = maskBosses(c, tops, horde); ... if (horde) keepHorde(ctx, c); ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'lighter'; /* effects' and projectiles' drawGlow */ for (const e of this.enemies) if (!e.dead && (e.elite || e.boss) && onScreen(c, e.x, e.y, 60) && this.inView(e)) e.drawGlow(ctx, c); if (horde) putHorde(ctx); ``` Then delete the old loop at :3376. (2) Side effect: the sealed-guardian branch of drawGlow moves under the horde as well. Its small FROST sheen (c = 1.4h, centred on the body) would then be mostly hidden behind the guardian. That is acceptable, since the guardian already has its ice drawn over it and an icy outline. If the owner wants the sheen kept, call drawGlow for `e.sealed` foes after putHorde as now. Optional: the halo now shows only round the silhouette and reads a little softer. A small alpha bump (0.28 to about 0.34) would restore its presence without touching any monster pixels.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real and none of the settled calls or earlier reviews covers it. The champion and boss aura (enemies.js:1115-1120: a radial glow h*2.6, solid colour at its centre, alpha 0.28-0.38) has been drawn in the additive pass over the horde since the first commit (84963a5). That pass is game.js:3376, after drawNightEdge. When 6f26a8e (27 Oct 2026) put the weapons' shots and glows under the horde, following the owner's "monsters ... should be above weapons", it bracketed only the weapon glows (keepHorde/putHorde, game.js:3364-3368). The aura stayed on top, so the wash looks left over from before that change, not chosen. Nothing in the settled calls covers it. NIGHT_EDGE's halo and ring are a different layer. The pixel-pass exclusion of the big bosses is about sprite pixels, not this glow. No review in reviews/ mentions it either: hero-polish-graphics-2026-11-14 only says in general that "colored rims and glow preserve silhouettes at night". I checked it myself. I applied the critic's fix to game.js in memory only, through a Node loader hook; the repo was not touched. I then rendered the same seeded frames three ways, as shipped / with the fix / with no aura, in forest by night, frozen by day, hell by night, and a scene with the four bosses and a lord-like elite (lord: true). Images are in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/aura-skeptic/: - forest-champs-abc-x2.png - frozen-champs-abc-x2.png - hell-champs-abc-x2.png - forest-bosses-abc-x1.png As shipped: - The Swift Death Knight and Rime Knight become a flat cyan block. - The Swift skeleton loses its bone shading. - The Stoneskin orc and yeti go pale lavender; the orc loses its dark edges and eyes. - The imp beside the Swift knight is dyed cyan. - The Bone King's dark armour goes grey, and the Lich and Butcher come out pale. With the fix, every body matches the no-aura render pixel for pixel in tone. The aura still shows as a coloured halo on the ground round each champion (cyan, red, lavender, pink), with the gold outline on top, so champions stay clearly marked. Neighbours are no longer dyed. The fix is draw-only. It adds no Math.random call and changes no gameplay or hero looks. test/horde-over.test.js does not pin the order of the enemy aura. Side effects for the owner to know about: - The bosses lose the pastel wash and show their authored colours, with only a faint halo. - A sealed Dungeon guardian's ward and frost sheen also move under its body. Its comments already describe them as drawn "around" and "round it", and the ice tint itself comes from TINTS.frozen in draw. Better fix: Keep the critic's approach of moving the aura under the horde mask, but trim it: 1. Set the flag in the enemy loop that already runs at game.js:3281, not in a second pass over this.enemies. For example: let auras = false; ... if (e.elite || e.boss) auras = true; just before the push. (inView is already checked there; the aura loop's onScreen margin is also 60.) 2. Then use const masked = armed || auras; let horde = masked && maskHorde(c, list, p); 3. Keep the first bracket gated on armed: if (horde && armed) keepHorde(...) / putHorde. Otherwise a frame with a champion but no shots does two full-screen copies for nothing. 4. Change line 3324 to if (masked) horde = maskBosses(c, tops, horde); 5. Move the enemy aura loop (line 3376) inside the second bracket, just before the putHorde at 3368. The aura alpha needs no change: at night the halo still reads clearly. By day on snow it is faint, but the gold outline carries the champion mark there. Before shipping, show the owner the boss before/after (forest-bosses-abc-x1.png), because the bosses' look in game changes with this.

### monsters-7 [confirmed] By day the champion's gold outline vanishes on sand: a champion has a weaker edge than a plain foe
- defect / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:1124-1130 (eliteOutlineBuf swaps ink #181425 for gold #feae34), :959, :1293 (night-eliteo). Both lenses (art + tech).
- Evidence: closeup-champions-thief-desert.png:
- Bonehide (sand golem, 1130-1265,190-320) and Grimfang (mummy) fade into the sand, and the Stoneskin aura brightens the sand further;
- the plain mummy at (640,745) keeps a crisp dark edge.
Measured: sand next to it L 164-187, gold outline 184, so the champion's edge contrast is ΔL 0-20, against about 150 for a plain foe's ink (L 23). On snow (L about 225) the gold gives only ΔL ≈ 40. The same weak edge shows on orange kinds (magma slime, tormented soul, flameskull, jackal).
- Swapping the dark outline for gold removes the one dark edge that separates a sprite from bright day ground. On Sunscorch, and partly on Frostfell, a champion is outlined worse than the plain horde round it, and only its aura and name still single it out.
- Fix: Keep both rings: gold on the silhouette edge (the edge-only swap from the interior-ink finding) plus 1 px of ink outside it. Build it with px.outline(goldEdged, OUTLINE), which pads the frame by 1 art px a side, and draw it over the rect grown by one art pixel each way, as drawNightEdge already does with NIGHT_PAD (ux/uy). Do this in both the lit and the night champion looks. It reads gold on dark ground and dark-edged on sand and snow.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): Confirmed in the code, in the pixels and by measurement. The finding is not touched by the other session's uncommitted files: enemies.js and game.js are clean in git status. Code: eliteOutlineBuf (public/js/game/enemies.js:1126-1130) turns every ink pixel (b.ink ?? OUTLINE) into #feae34. It is used for the lit champion look at :959 and inside NIGHT_LOOKS[3] 'night-eliteo' at :1293, so a champion has no dark edge left at all. #feae34 has L = 184. Then Enemy.drawGlow (champion aura) is drawn additively ('lighter', game.js:3365-3376) over both the sprite and the ground round it, which brightens both and closes the gap further. Pixels (closeup-champions-thief-desert.png, production Game.draw; crops in verify/sandedge/bonehide-x4.png, grimfang-x4.png, mummy-plain-x4.png): - Bonehide's rendered gold is (242,197,87) to (248,209,93), L 198-210. The aura-lit sand beside it is (211,175,124) to (230,196,139), L 180-200, so ΔL is about 0-30. The gold is told apart only by saturation (the blue channel 87 vs 139), not by lightness. - Grimfang is badly washed out. - The plain mummy's ink is (30,26,41), L about 29, on sand at L 181, so ΔL is about 150. My own A/B renders (verify/sandedge/ab-desert.png, ab-frozen.png, ab-forest.png, 8 kinds per map, champion row over a plain row) show the same thing: - Desert: the mummy, jackal and sandslime champions blur into the sand. - Frozen: snow is L 200-222 and the aura-lit gold is L 200-226, so the edge is also weak there; the lemming champion all but vanishes. - Forest by night: the gold reads well. So by day a champion's edge is weaker than a plain foe's, exactly as reported. No earlier review in reviews/ settled this. The proposed fix works. Gold on the silhouette edge plus 1 art px of ink outside it gives crisp dark edges on sand and snow, and the gold still reads at night (ab-*.png, PROPOSED band, emulated in memory). night-edge.test.js:240-251 checks only that the 'night-eliteo' look draws and contains gold, so keeping that key keeps the test valid. The weak point is the plumbing it describes: "draw it over the rect grown by one art pixel" in Enemy.draw. If the lit _img is padded, everything that assumes _img matches the frame's _rect must change too: - horde-over.js drawShape/maskHorde and nightCut draw _img over _rect, so they would squash the padded picture by 1 art px; - drawEmissive eyes and drawRim use _rect for the unpadded frame; - drawNightEdge's ux = NIGHT_PAD*w/fr.w assumes the frame's own width; - the rise crop uses img.height; - Y is anchored at the feet and would need +1 art px. The fix works if all of those are handled; a smaller implementation is below. Better fix: Get the same look without changing _img or _rect. 1. Lit look: keep the 'eliteo' variant the frame's own size, and swap only the silhouette-edge ink for gold (ink 4-adjacent to transparent or to the frame border; interior ink stays). Right after ctx.drawImage(img, ...) in Enemy.draw, for a champion whose _look is 3, draw one extra variant, 'eliteo-ring'. It is px.outline(fr.buf, ink) with every pixel that is opaque in pad(fr.buf, 1) cleared, which leaves only the 1-px ring outside the silhouette. Draw it at r[0]-ux, r[1]-uy, r[2]+2ux, r[3]+2uy, where ux = r[2]/fr.w and uy = r[3]/fr.h. That is one drawImage per champion, and champions are few. Use the same alpha as the sprite, and skip it while it rises. 2. Night look: NIGHT_LOOKS[3] = ['night-eliteo', (b) => px.pad(px.outline(goldEdge(nightLift(b)), b.ink ?? OUTLINE), NIGHT_PAD - 1)]. The total padding stays NIGHT_PAD, so drawNightEdge needs no change. Optionally, to stop the day aura washing out the sprite (Grimfang): by day (world.daylight) draw drawGlow at a lower alpha or under the horde. That is a separate, smaller tweak.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding is accurate, and no review, commit or test settled the outline as deliberate. **The code matches the description.** At enemies.js:1124-1130, eliteOutlineBuf turns every ink pixel (#181425, L 23) gold (#feae34, L 184). It is used at :959 for the lit look and at :1293 for the night look. **History.** The gold outline came with the first commit (84963a5) and its night handling with db3987d, before Desert and Frozen became day maps (d2981ae). Nobody judged it against bright ground. The only test about it is night-edge.test.js:240, "champions keep their gold outline unringed". It checks the gold and the absence of a semi-transparent ring, and an opaque ink ring passes both. None of the owner's settled calls covers champion outlines, and none of the earlier reviews mentions them. **Measured on the closeups' own ground.** - Sand: median L 184 (p10-p90 179-195). That is exactly the gold's 184, so a champion's edge contrast is about 0, against about 160 for a plain foe's ink. - Snow: median L 235 (ΔL about 50). - Night forest: L 26, where the gold reads very well. **Rendered comparison.** C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/champ-day-edge.png, made by champ-day-edge.mjs in the same folder. It uses real ground patches and the production glow() aura. - The shipped champion jackal, anubite, mummy, frostbitten and magmaslime melt into sand or snow. Their plain versions keep a crisp edge. - The critic's option A (gold on the silhouette edge plus 1 px of ink outside) reads dark-edged on sand and snow and looks the same as now at night. On dark ground the ink is invisible and the gold carries. - Option B (keep the ink, gold outside) still blends into sand. The fix is draw-only. It does not touch gameplay, hitboxes (spriteExtent) or Math.random, and no hero art. **Severity: defect, not bug.** Champions are still identified by name tag, bar and aura. Their sprite just reads worse than a plain foe's on two of the five Survival maps. **The fix works in principle but needs care in the plumbing.** If the padded buffer simply replaced the 'eliteo' variant, Enemy.draw would work out w and h from img.width/height and anchor at the bottom. That causes four problems: - The champion would float 1 art px up. - Its size would jump by 2 px every time it flashes white (white() is unpadded, and the flash runs on every hit). - fr.em eyes (drawEmissive) would stretch over the grown _rect. - The night layer's ux/uy would be computed from the grown w. Also, horde-over.js drawShape and nightCut draw o._img over o._rect, so _img and _rect must stay a matching pair. Better fix: Keep _img and _rect the same size as the frame, and draw only the visible champion picture padded. (1) Change eliteOutlineBuf so gold goes only on ink pixels 4-adjacent to the flood-filled exterior. This is the edge-only swap from the interior-ink finding. If that finding is rejected, the plain ink-to-gold swap plus the ink ring below (option C in champ-day-edge.png) still fixes the day contrast. (2) In Enemy.draw, when `this.elite` picked 'eliteo', keep `img = fr.variant('eliteo', eliteOutlineBuf, flip)` as _img for the masks and night cuts, so w, h and _rect stay as they are. At line 993, draw the padded look in its place: `ctx.drawImage(fr.variant('eliteo-ink', (b) => px.outline(eliteOutlineBuf(b), OUTLINE), flip), Math.round(X - S*sx), Math.round(by - S*sy), Math.round(w + 2*S*sx), Math.round(h + 2*S*sy))` Draw the normal image for everything else. The rise clip and the white flash keep the unpadded frame, so nothing jumps. (3) Change the night look to `['night-eliteo', (b) => nightBuf(eliteOutlineBuf(nightLift(b)), OUTLINE)]`. px.outline adds 1 px and nightBuf's pad adds 1 more, so the result is still NIGHT_PAD = 2 and drawNightEdge's ux/uy stay correct. The night-edge test still passes: it is opaque, so there is no semi-transparent ring. The only loss is that horde-over's mask leaves out the 1 px ink ring, so a weapon blast can cover that ring. That is acceptable.

### monsters-8 [confirmed] At night the nearest monsters are the dimmest: they sit in the faded night-layer hole, and the hero's light doesn't make up for it
- defect / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:138 (NIGHT_EDGE.near 60), :1197-1202 (destination-out hole), :1236-1247 (nightHoleBuf, opaque at its centre). Art lens.
- Evidence: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/art-extra/night-distance-ramp.png shows the same kind at 12-170 px from the hero. Mean sprite luminance:

| Kind | Map | Near the hero | 60 px and beyond |
|---|---|---|---|
| goblin | forest | 105 at 12 px, 131 at 28, 149 at 44 | 157-161 |
| death knight | forest | 96 at 12 px | 128 |
| damned | hell | 90 at 12 px | 133-137 |
| fiend | hell | 71 at 12 px | 105-108 |
| lava golem | hell | 82 at 28 px | 109 |

The 'before' row of ab-night-hitflash.png shows the same: the skeleton next to the hero is grey, the goblins further out are vivid.
- Within 60 px of the hero, the lift, ring and halo fade to nothing, on the assumption that his light shows those monsters as well. It doesn't. They read 25-34% darker than the same kind further out and lose their edge ring. The monsters about to hit the hero are the least visible on screen, the reverse of the threat hierarchy.
- Fix: Keep part of the layer at the centre: scale nightHoleBuf's alpha to about 0.5·k so the hole removes at most half, and/or cut NIGHT_EDGE.near from 60 to about 28. Re-measure with art-extra/distance.mjs. The target is luminance at 12 px ≥ 0.95 × luminance at 100 px for every kind.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The effect is real, but the critic overstates how big it is. The code works as described. drawNightEdge (enemies.js:1197-1202) punches a destination-out hole through the night layer, using nightHoleBuf, which is fully opaque at its centre. Next to the hero his light multiplies the scene by about 1.0 (warm light plus 0.7 white saturates the light map), so foes there show their raw sprite colours. Further out they show the lifted, saturated night colours (lift 90, sat 40, contrast 25) at the 0.94 floor. A test documents this on purpose ('nothing of the layer left at the hero', test/night-edge.test.js:194). The 25-34% figure is mostly a capture artefact. distance.mjs and ab-fixes.mjs place the hero at the left edge of the screen (cam.x = p.x-8, or aim 0.06). That puts him inside the night vignette, which darkens the scene before the horde layer goes on top. The hole then exposes vignetted pixels. In the game the camera centres on the hero (game.js:3033), and the vignette is clear for about 120 world px around him. I re-measured at the real 1600x900 view (S 3, zoom 0.8): one foe per render, hero not drawn, ink pixels excluded (verify/nightnear/measure.mjs). Ratio of brightness at 12 px to far: | Kind | Ratio | |---|---| | skeleton | 0.96 | | wolf | 0.89 | | goblin | 0.88 | | deathknight | 0.85 | | fiend | 0.84 | | zombie | 0.84 | | damned | 0.79 | | lava golem | 0.71 | The critic's framing (mode 'leftedge') gives 0.58-0.67, which matches their numbers, so the vignette roughly doubles the dip. Near foes do lose some contrast against the lit ground: the damned is about the same brightness as the ground there, and the lava golem nearly matches the grey stone. You can see the dulling on the deathknight, damned and lava golem in verify/nightnear/ramp-shipped.png. The claim that the nearest monsters are 'the least visible on screen' does not hold at real framing. In verify/nightnear/crowd-shipped-vs-half.png the foes pressed on the hero still read clearly: full colour, ink outline, on lit ground. So this is a modest, real inconsistency: the night pop fades as a foe walks into the hero's light. It is polish, not a defect. The proposed fix is only half right. Halving the hole (mode 'half') gives 12-px/far ratios of 0.96-1.01, except the lava golem at 0.90, whose far samples sit near a cart's light; against 60-100 px it is 0.94. Cutting near to 28 alone fails the critic's own 0.95 target (0.79-0.98). Changing nightHoleBuf itself would also break its shape test. Better fix: Leave nightHoleBuf and NIGHT_EDGE.near (60) as they are. Add a constant, for example NIGHT_EDGE.hole = 0.5, and set n.globalAlpha = NIGHT_EDGE.hole for the destination-out hole draw in drawNightEdge (enemies.js:1199, currently n.globalAlpha = 1). Then update the 'nothing of the layer left at the hero' assertion in test/night-edge.test.js to match. Don't touch near alone. Trade-off to point out to the owner: the pale ring and halo, and allies' green ring, will then show at about half strength on foes pressed against the hero. That is visible as a faint halo in verify/nightnear/crowd-shipped-vs-half.png. If he wants the near zone free of rings, the larger alternative is to draw a lifted look without a ring inside the hole instead of erasing the layer. Re-measure with the hero at screen centre at real view scale (verify/nightnear/measure.mjs), not at the screen edge.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The effect is real, but the critic overstates how big it is. The hole itself is deliberate: commit db3987d (2026-10-16) added it "near the hero's own light", and test/night-edge.test.js:184-197 checks it. At that point the night layer was the monster's own unlit colours, so in the hero's light the hole hid nothing. Then 0c888f7 (2026-10-20, the owner's "make the monsters brighter") and 309b8e4 began lifting, saturating and adding contrast to the layer, and nobody revisited near: 60. Now a monster in the hero's light shows its plain colours, while the same kind in the dark shows the lifted ones. So monsters get duller as they walk into his light. Nothing deliberate covers that, and the owner's calls (2026-10-16 "monsters must stay visible at night", 2026-10-20 "brighter at night") point the other way. No earlier review in reviews/ mentions it. My re-measurement (C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/night-hole/measure.mjs) used the production Game.draw in the real 1600x900 view (S 3, zoom 0.8) with the hero at screen centre, where the game keeps him. Luminance at 12 px divided by luminance at 100 px came out at 0.97 for the skeleton, 0.92 goblin, 0.89 death knight, 0.83 damned, 0.88 fiend, 0.78 lava golem, and 0.94/0.90 for the meadow skeleton/goblin. The critic's 25-34% comes from framing the hero at the left edge, where the vignette darkens the area inside the hole but not the night layer laid over it. The critic also says the nearest monsters are "the least visible on screen". That doesn't hold up: the ground near the hero is lit (luminance 72-199 against 41-116 further out), so close monsters read clearly as dark ink on bright ground (ramp-shipped.png). What's left is a brightness flip of 3-22% and the ring disappearing as a monster closes in. That makes it polish, not a defect. The fix only touches drawing: no gameplay change, no Math.random. Scaling the hole to half meets the critic's own target. With the hole at half: 1.02, 1.00, 0.99, 0.97, 0.99, 0.96, 0.98, 0.98 (ramp-half.png). Cutting near to 28 alone does not: damned 0.88, lava golem 0.85, goblin 0.95 (ramp-near28.png). Removing the hole entirely gives 1.00-1.04 (ramp-nohole.png), but the monsters next to the hero lose all of his warm tint. Better fix: Keep nightHoleBuf and near: 60 as they are. Add hole: 0.5 to NIGHT_EDGE in enemies.js:138. In drawNightEdge (enemies.js:1200), set n.globalAlpha = NIGHT_EDGE.hole instead of 1 just before the destination-out draw of the hole, so the hole removes at most half of the layer at the hero. That is one constant plus one line. Leave out the near-28 part, which doesn't reach the target by itself. Don't bake the 0.5 into nightHoleBuf either: test/night-edge.test.js:194 asserts the buffer is opaque (>= 245) at its centre. Update that test to also assert the hole is drawn at NIGHT_EDGE.hole, and change the drawNightEdge comment from "to nothing at the middle of his light" to "to half". Measured with this change in the real view: luminance at 12 px is 0.96-1.02 of luminance at 100 px for all 8 kinds checked.

### monsters-10 [confirmed] The world is drawn at a fractional scale, so every art pixel is 1-2, 2-3 or 3-4 screen px wide
- defect / owner - public/js/engine/view.js:23 (zoom 0.8), :57 (S = round(min/300)); public/js/game/game.js:2787 (cam.S = view.S * view.zoom). Tech lens (the art lens notes the same base in the cultist finding).
- Evidence: World scale by screen:

| Screen | World scale | Screen px per art px |
|---|---|---|
| 1280x720 | 1.6 | 1 or 2 |
| 1366x768, 1440x900, 1600x900 | 2.4 | 2 or 3 |
| 1920x1080 (and 1536x864 at 125%) | 3.2 | 3 or 4 |
| 2560x1440/1600, 4K (dpr-capped) | 4.0 | 4 |

C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/tech-extra/chrome-skel-x8.png (real Chrome, 1600x900): the skeleton's left eye is 2 px wide and its right eye 3 px, and outline columns alternate 2 and 3. true1080p-forest-crop-1to1.png and tech-extra/tf-crop.png show the 3/4 alternation on every monster.
- This is the largest pixel-perfection gap, and it hits every monster. One art pixel is drawn 50% (at 2.4) or 100% (at 1.6) wider than its neighbour, so thin details (eyes, bowstrings, horns) change thickness from one sprite to the next. The camera sits on whole screen pixels, so the pattern doesn't crawl, but it is uneven on every screen below 1440p.
- Fix: Draw the world at an integer scale: cam.S = Math.max(1, Math.round(view.S * view.zoom)) (1.6→2, 2.4→2, 3.2→3, 4→4), or take it from the screen with Math.max(1, Math.round(Math.min(pxW, pxH) / 360)). The HUD's world-to-HUD factor (hud.js overHead z = cam.S/view.S) adapts by itself. The trade-off is the owner's: the field of view would then vary by about ±10-20% between resolutions, instead of always showing 25% more than the HUD scale.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real. I confirmed it in the code and in live Chrome pixels. Code: view.js:57 sets S = round(min(pxW,pxH)/300). syncCam (game.js:2787) sets cam.S = view.S * 0.8. enemies.js:993 then does drawImage(img, X, by, round(img.width*S*sx), ...) with smoothing off. That is nearest-neighbour at a non-integer factor, drawn straight onto the full-resolution canvas with no integer-scaled offscreen pass. The comment in game.js draw() admits it: "a fractional scale keeps a steady pixel pattern". The critic's resolution table is right. I recomputed it, including the 4K dpr cap (2698x1518 gives S=5, world scale 4.0) and 1536x864 at 125% (3.2). No review or memory note records the 0.8 zoom or a fractional world scale as an owner's call. My own Chrome measurement (verify/fracscale/measure2.mjs) read the live canvas in the same rAF frame. For monsters that overlap nothing, I predicted where each art-column edge lands under nearest-neighbour and checked for a colour change in that exact screen column. - 1920x1080 (cam.S 3.2): 5 of 5 scarabs (16x9 drawn as 51x29) put 42/42 edges exactly where predicted. Column widths are 3343333343333433 and row heights 334333433. - 1600x900 (cam.S 2.4): rats (17x8 drawn as 41x19) have column widths 23232232323223232. Three rats matched 26/26, 29/29 and 29/29 edges. Two partial matches were most likely a frame or flip change. - In clean-1920x1080-scarab-0-x10.png the red eye is 4 px wide next to 3 px features. - The cited chrome-skel-x8.png agrees: the skull's two red eyes are about 2 and 3 screen px wide. The pattern sits on rounded X, so it does not crawl. Corrections to scope: 1. Integer cam.S evens out only ordinary monsters drawn at their own size, and the ground drawn at cam.S. Cultists (x1.5), elites (x1.35), bosses (57/26 and similar), heroes with hires 1.5 (hero-sheets.js, in HEAD too; the file also has the other session's uncommitted edits) and the hit squash/stretch stay fractional. For example, a Cultist at 3x1.5 = 4.5 still alternates 4 and 5 px. 2. The proposed syncCam rounding also changes the headless camera. headless.mjs relies on view's default S=4 and zoom 0.8, which today gives a 600x338 camera. Rounding would make it 640x360. The spawner uses cam.W/H for its spawn rings (spawner.js:418,443), so seeded sims and tests would shift: test/ending.test.js and test/hero-selection.test.js set zoom .8 explicitly, and the owner relies on seeded runs. 3. The alternative round(min/360) gives 3 at 900p: a 300-art-px-tall view, 25% zoomed in. With round(S*0.8), the field of view changes as follows: - 1366x768, 1440x900, 1600x900: about +20% (a 900-tall screen goes from 375 to 450 art px tall, and sprites get 17% smaller). - 720p: -20%. - 1080p: +6.7%. - 1440p and up: unchanged. That trade-off is the owner's to make. Severity: a systematic, measurable failure of "pixel perfect" on every screen below 1440p. It is subtle at 3/4 px and clearly visible at 2/3 px on 1-px details such as eyes. That makes it a defect, not polish. Better fix: Make the browser view choose an integer world scale, and leave syncCam and headless alone. In view.js resize(), right after view.S is set, add: view.zoom = Math.max(1, Math.round(view.S * 0.8)) / view.S; Keep 0.8 as a named target constant. This turns 1.6, 2.4, 3.2 and 4.0 into 2, 2, 3 and 4. What this keeps the same: - syncCam still computes cam.S = view.S * view.zoom, now always an integer in the browser. - hud.js z = cam.S/view.S adapts by itself. - hero-showcase.js, which reads view.zoom, gets the same integer world scale. - headless.mjs and the tests that set zoom .8 keep today's 600x338 camera. Seeded runs and balance baselines do not move. Also update the comment in game.js draw() that says "a fractional scale keeps a steady pixel pattern". Tell the owner two things: 1. The field of view changes: +20% on 768/900-tall screens, -20% at 720p, +6.7% at 1080p. 2. Cultists (x1.5), elites (x1.35), the big bosses' non-integer scales, heroes with hires 1.5 and the hit squash stay fractional. Snapping those means rounding each figure's per-pixel scale, Math.round(S*drawScale)/S, which changes their size slightly. That is a separate owner call.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real. view.zoom 0.8 dates from the first commit (84963a5), and only its field-of-view purpose is written down ("shows 25% more each way"). Nothing in the code, the git log, reviews/ or the memory notes accepts the fractional scale that comes with it. The code points the other way: the view.js header says every art pixel is drawn S×S "so sprites stay crisp", and the syncCam comment guards only the floor ("never below one screen pixel per art pixel"). The table is correct. view.S = round(min/300), so cam.S is 1.6 at 720p, 2.4 at 768p and 900p, 3.2 at 1080p, and 4.0 at 1440p and above. Enemy.draw (enemies.js:967-993) draws img.width*S*sx to Math.round(w) with no smoothing, so every drawScale-1 monster gets uneven 1/2, 2/3 or 3/4 px columns. The critic's x8 Chrome skeleton capture shows it: the two red eye cells are 2 px and 3 px wide. None of the owner's settled calls conflicts with this. Two caveats: 1. Not literally every monster gets fixed. The Cultist's scale 1.5 is the owner's call, and the reforged bosses have hires 4.07-4.56, so both stay fractional at any cam.S. Hit squash/stretch is deliberate too. 2. The trade-off is stated backwards. Field of view is not constant in world pixels today; it is constant only relative to the HUD scale. The short side of the view spans 320 to 450 world px across screens. With Math.round(view.S*view.zoom), the 720p, 1080p and 1440p screens all become 640x360, the 768p and 900p screens grow about 20%, and 2560x1600 keeps 640x400. The range narrows to 360-450. The fix needs no Math.random and no hero-art change. It does change per-screen gameplay geometry, though, which makes it the owner's call: spawn ring hypot(cam.W,cam.H) in spawner.js:418/443, on-screen targeting at game.js:2255, the HORDE_CAP cull at game.js:3164, and weapons-light. The seeded tests run at view.S=1, where cam.S stays 1, so they are unchanged. The fix as written is incomplete. hero-showcase.js:27-29 heroMenuScale() repeats the camera's formula as Math.max(1/view.S, view.zoom). The test in hero-selection.test.js:115-133 ("the showcase draws the selected hero at gameplay size") checks |menuW*uiScale - fieldW| <= uiScale at uiScale 1, 2 and 4. If only syncCam changes, the menu figure stays at 1.6 or 3.2 while the field figure goes to 2 or 3. That is a gap of roughly 0.2-0.4 × sprite width, well over 2-4 px, so CI fails on deploy. hero-selection.js is in another session's uncommitted work but only imports heroMenuScale, so it needs no edit. The critic is right that hud.js z = cam.S/view.S adapts on its own. Better fix: Put the scale in one place so the camera and the menu showcase cannot drift apart. In view.js add: export function worldScale() { return Math.max(1, Math.round(view.S * view.zoom)); } // whole screen px per art px In game.js syncCam: c.S = worldScale(); In hero-showcase.js: export function heroMenuScale() { return worldScale() / view.S; } Leave view.zoom (0.8) as it is. Do not touch hud.js (z = cam.S/view.S adapts) or hero-selection.js (another session's work; it only imports heroMenuScale). Before the owner decides, tell them the per-screen field of view in world px. Before → after: 1280x720 800x450 → 640x360; 1366x768 569x320 → 683x384; 1600x900 667x375 → 800x450; 1920x1080 600x338 → 640x360; 2560x1440 640x360 unchanged. Spawn distance and on-screen targeting follow these numbers. If 900p showing 800x450 looks too wide, the alternative is Math.max(1, Math.round(Math.min(view.pxW, view.pxH) / 360)). That gives 900p 533x300 instead, and gives 720p, 1080p and 1440p the same 640x360. The Cultist (scale 1.5) and the reforged bosses (non-integer hires) stay fractional either way, by the owner's earlier calls.

### monsters-12 [confirmed] The Abyss horde is one red mass: Fiendling and Imp share the same colours and silhouette
- defect / art - public/js/art/monsters-maps.js:797 (fiend), :833 (demonbrute); public/js/art/monsters.js:400 (imp); data/maps.js:110 (hell roster) plus the unthemed imp pocket (weight 4.5). Art lens.
- Evidence: Dominant colours (art-extra/hues.mjs):

| Kind | #a22633 | #e43b44 |
|---|---|---|
| imp | 49% | 33% |
| fiend | 41% | 14% |
| demonbrute | 18% | |
| cultist | 33% | 42% |

Imp and fiend are both small horned, winged red demons; the fiend only adds a pitchfork (C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/art-extra/zoom-imps-witches.png). field-hell-horde.png and field-hell-horde-crop-centre-x2.png show a field of near-identical red horned shapes on red-brown plates; the kinds differ only in size.
- Hell's roster spans a narrow hue band (h 354-24): imp, fiend, horned brute, cultist, tormented soul, magma slime and hellhound. Because the map still sends unthemed Darkwood imps, the new Fiendling can't be told from them. Of all the maps, Abyss has the weakest threat readability.
- Fix: Palette only, no redraw:
- give the fiend a soot or abyss skin, for example R→#3e2731, r→#733e39 or #4b3d44, m→#2a1f2d, keeping ember-orange horns and eyes emissive;
- push the horned brute toward black-iron armour, with red hide only at the joints.
Then red means imp and cultist, and black-ember means hell-native.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The finding is real. I checked the code and my own renders. **What the code shows** - The two sprites use the same palette letters. `fiend` (public/js/art/monsters-maps.js:797) and `imp` (public/js/art/monsters.js:400) both have a body of R #a22633, highlights in r #e43b44, shadow in m #3e2731, and glowing yellow Y eyes. - They are also the same shape and size. Both are small horned red demons with r 4. The art is 12x12 for the imp and 15x13 for the fiend, and the fiend's extra width is its grey pitchfork. The pitchfork is the only real difference. - Imps do reach the Abyss. survivalSchedule (data/maps.js) swaps out only the kinds that have an Abyss variant, and no Abyss kind stands for `imp`. So the Darkwood imp comes in at weight 4 from minute 10 (the critic's "4.5" is slightly off). Its weight-5 slot goes to the fiend, which stands for the goblin and comes in threes. - The timed events make it worse: an imp swarm at phase 14.7 (26 imps) is followed by a fiend pack at phase 15.5 (18 fiends). Belzakor also summons fiends. - The two play very differently. The imp has 30 hp, speed 60 and flies. The fiend has 12 hp, speed 45 and walks. **What I saw in my renders** In verify/imp-fiend-x4.png and imp-fiend-x2.png, blocks of 9 imps and 9 fiends in the Abyss by night look nearly the same. In the mixed group you can only tell them apart by picking out the fork. **Where the critic overstates it** - "One red mass" is too strong. The same horde has blue Death Knights, white skeletons, purple spiders, orange slimes and tan Damned; see field-hell-horde.png and my verify/field-hell-ab.png. - "Differ only in size" is wrong: they are the same size and differ by the pitchfork. - The confusion is really between imp and fiend only, so I rate it polish rather than defect. **Why the proposed fix is not sound as written** Recolouring only the fiend's palette is the right minimal approach; def.palette already exists for this. The chosen colours are the problem: - R→#3e2731, r→#733e39, m→#2a1f2d makes the fiend almost black. #3e2731 is the palette's own shadow colour and sits close to the #181425 outline, so the inner shading merges into the outline. In verify/imp-fiend-alt-x4.png (B) and field-hell-ab.png (B) it reads as a dull mauve smudge on the dark plates. - That goes against the owner's settled call for bright, high-contrast monsters. - It says to keep "ember-orange horns", but the fiend's horns are grey ash (A/a/h), so the fix misdescribes the sprite. - The second bullet, putting the Horned Brute in black iron, isn't needed. The brute is 20x19 with grey bat wings and is not part of the mix-up, and it would also break the bright-monster rule. Better fix: Change only the fiend's palette, keeping it as bright as the imp but in a different hue, so the imp stays the red one. This is one line in the art data (public/js/art/monsters-maps.js); data/enemies.js and the hell roster stay as they are. Recommended, shown as variant D in verify/imp-fiend-alt-x4.png and verify/field-hell-ab.png: `MAP_MONSTER_ART.fiend.palette = { R: '#68386c', r: '#b55088', m: '#3b2346' };` This gives a violet fiend that stays bright and is clearly apart from the red imp. The grey fork, grey horns and glowing yellow eyes stay as they are, and the ash death burst picks up the new colours on its own. Belzakor's summoned fiends follow automatically. If violet looks too close to the purple Widow Spider (a very different low, wide shape), use the ash-grey option instead, variant E: `{ R: '#4b3d44', r: '#7d6a6a', m: '#2a1f2d' }`. The owner picks looks by eye, so show both A/B renders and let them choose. Leave the Horned Brute alone. My scripts and images are all in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/: imp-fiend.mjs, imp-fiend-alt.mjs, field-ab.mjs and their PNGs. Nothing in the repo was touched.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): Part of the finding holds: on the Abyss the Fiendling and the Imp can't be told apart. The wider claim that the Abyss horde is "one red mass" does not hold. Captures of the same field show blue Death Knights, white skeletons, purple spiders, orange slimes and hounds, the grey Magma Golem and the tan Damned. The Horned Brute is 22x21 px, so its size already sets it apart. The fiend/imp clash is real and nothing makes it deliberate: - **Same look.** Both are small horned, winged red demons with yellow eyes, mostly #a22633 / #e43b44 (public/js/art/monsters-maps.js:797 and public/js/art/monsters.js:400). The fiend's only difference is a dark pitchfork, which is lost in a crowd. - **Different threats.** The fiend stands for the goblin: 12 hp, quick pace, a walker that comes in threes. The imp has 30 hp, swift pace and flies (data/enemies.js:27, :39, :161). - **They meet.** Both are in the minute 10-12 pockets: goblin→fiend weight 5, imp weight 4 (waves.js:18-19). The imp swarm at phase 14.7 (10:30) is followed straight away by the 18-strong "A fiend raid!" at phase 15.5 (about 11:04). Both come back in the last stand. The Abyss replaces no imp, unlike the Frostfell's iceimp. - **No earlier decision.** The fiend art has not changed since 116f331 (git log -S / -G). No review in reviews/ mentions it, and no owner call covers it. Production Game.draw A/B in the Abyss by night: - Shipped: the imps and fiends near the hero, and the alternating imp/fiend rows out in the dark, are all red horned shapes. Only the fork tells them apart. - Any soot body (the critic's palette, or a variant with ember edges) makes every fiend readable at a glance, and the night lift plus ring keeps them visible. The change is palette only. It does not touch gameplay or Math.random, does not touch hero looks, and stays out of the other session's uncommitted files (monsters-maps.js is clean). Images: - C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/abyss-red/fiend-variants-x2.png - C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/abyss-red/fiend-variants-crop-x4.png - C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/imp-fiend-alt-x4.png Severity is polish rather than defect: the problem is confined to two fodder kinds and the fork does tell them apart up close. Better fix: Recolour only the fiend and leave the Horned Brute alone. A black-iron brute would add nothing to telling the fiend from the imp. It would also look like a second Magma Golem: another bulky charcoal-and-ember shape on the same map. The critic's fiend palette turns its lit side (r) into dull #733e39. That makes the fiend the dimmest horde monster on the map, which goes against the owner's calls for brighter, higher-contrast monsters (enemies.js NIGHT_EDGE notes). Keep an ember lit edge instead by adding this to MAP_MONSTER_ART.fiend in public/js/art/monsters-maps.js: `palette: { R: '#3e2731', r: '#be4a2f', m: '#2a1f2d' }` The result is a soot body with ember-lit edges. The ember-orange eyes (Y) stay emissive, and the ash horns and grey pitchfork are untouched. All three colours are already in PAL, so the death burst takes no new particle slots. The ink 'k' is unchanged, so the champion gold outline still finds the outline. The owner usually picks colours by eye, so the same palette can go in as a sprite-tuner bake (art/tuned.js TUNED.fiend) instead. Show three swatches: - the critic's soot - soot with ember edges (above) - ash with ember edges: `R '#4b3d44', r '#d77643', m '#2a1f2d'`

### monsters-15 [confirmed] Three map sprites fall short of the set's finish: Anubite (flat, blocky), Frost Witch (a blue box) and Thorn Witch (robe ramp flattened)
- polish / art - public/js/art/monsters-maps.js:193 (anubite), :310 (frostwitch), :960 (thornwitch palette). Art lens.
- Evidence: - Anubite: art-extra/zoom-knights.png and lineup-x2.png desert #9. The body is flat greys Q/W/V with no hue-shifted ramp. The jackal head has the same value as the torso and sits in profile on a frontal body, and the khopesh hook floats off a bar arm.
- Frost Witch: rows 9-14 of the source are the same rectangle ('.uuuKuuunK.'), and 40% of the body is one colour (#124e89, L 30). Scaled 1.5x it reads as a blue tombstone or phone box: field-frozen-horde.png at (240,150), (230,300), (190,550) and (570,180); field-frozen-horde-crop-centre-x2.png (550-610,150-230).
- Thorn Witch: the recolour maps robe light r→#2e6e50 (L 87) and shadow R→#68386c (L 76). The original step was ΔL 35; now it is ΔL 11 with a 140° hue jump, so the robe reads as green-and-purple camouflage blotches: field-meadow-horde-crop-edge-x2.png (60-130,300-390) and (440-510,190-290).
- The 2026-10-27 pass brought the Darkwood set to a shared finish: ENDESGA ramps, a readable face and connected limbs. These three map kinds fall short of it and stand out in a crowd as unfinished.
- Fix: - Anubite: redraw with a warm-black ramp (#2a1f2d, #4b3d44, #7d6a6a, plus a cool #5a6988 rim on the lit side). Give the head a lighter muzzle and gold nemes stripes, and join the khopesh to a forearm.
- Frost Witch: taper the robe into an A-line, wider at the hem. Light the lit side with #4fd8e8 and #0099db over the #124e89 base, and add a white fur hem.
- Thorn Witch: set R→#1f4a3a (dark green-teal) so the robe has a real shadow step, and keep violet only for the lining and trim (p→#3b2346).
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): Two of the three claims hold up in the pixels and in measurements. The third is mostly taste. FROST WITCH (confirmed). In field-frozen-horde.png, at (240,150), (230,300), (190,550) and (570,180), the witches read as blue tombstones. They look the same in the hit-flash silhouette and inside the champion's outline (looks-frozen-1.png). In the source (monsters-maps.js:310), rows 6-14 run the full width from col 1 to col 10 with square shoulders. Rows 9-13 are the same width. The critic wrote rows 9-14, but row 14 is the hem. Measured on the production buffer, #124e89 covers 50 of the 126 body pixels (40%), at L* 33. The lit U edge only reaches rows 7-9. THORN WITCH (confirmed). This is the cultist recolour at :960. Crops verify/meadow-thorn-a.png and verify/meadow-thorn-b.png show a green left half and a magenta right half, with magenta fold streaks, so the robe looks patchwork. By day, r #2e6e50 has luma 87 and R #68386c has luma 76, matching the critic's figures. The visible problem is the jump from green to purple, not the value step. Under nightLift (Bloomvale is a night map) the colours become #1ec479 (L* 70) and #b63fc0 (L* 49). The cultist's own r and R collapse to L* 56 and 54 at night, so a flattened night ramp is how nightLift treats every robe, not something special to this sprite. ANUBITE (partly real, mostly taste). The head and torso do use the same Q/W/V greys, and the top body value is only L* 46, against L* 81 for the deathknight. But the khopesh does not float. Its hilt at row 9, col 13 touches the gold bracer at row 10, col 13 (visible in verify/anubite-vs.png). A profile jackal head on a frontal body is the classic Anubis pose. The 2026-11-14 graphics review also judged the Anubite readable, and at game scale (verify/desert-anubite-a.png) it reads clearly as a jackal guard. WHY THE FIX IS NOT SOUND: 1. The Frost Witch already has a white fur hem: row 14 is 'XXXGXGGggg' in #c8f4ec, #c0cbdc and #8b9bb4. #4fd8e8 is not a palette key, so that colour would need a def.palette remap. 2. Thorn Witch: p is already #3b2346, so that part changes nothing. R→#1f4a3a turns into #18b27a at night, against r's #1ec479. That is luma 126 vs 138 (Δ12, L* Δ5), the same flatness the critic objects to now (Δ11). It removes the hue jump, but it does not give the 'real shadow step' it promises in the light the sprite is actually seen in (verify/proto-witches.png). 3. The Anubite redraw is not minimal. The warm ash ramp looks muddier on the warm sand than the current cool charcoal (verify/proto-anubite.png). Better fix: Do the Frost Witch first. It is a reshape only, inside the same 12x16 frame, both frames: - Narrow the shoulders to cols 2-8 on rows 6-8. - Widen rows 11-13 out to cols 0-11 into a bell / A-line. - Run the existing U (#0099db) lit edge down the left side. - Widen the existing X/G/g fur hem to match. Don't add a new one. A prototype frame is in verify/proto.mjs (fwA) and verify/proto-witches.png. It reads as a robe, not a box. Thorn Witch: one palette value. Set R to a darker, cooler green, such as palette v #193c3e: - By day that is L* 23 against r's 42. - At night it becomes teal #12a7ae against green #1ec479. This keeps one material with a hue-shifted shadow. Violet stays only on p (already #3b2346) and the pink trim. Don't promise a strong night step, since nightLift flattens every robe ramp, the cultist's included. Anubite: lowest priority. Keep the black-jackal greys, which give good hue contrast on sand. Make two targeted pixel tweaks: - Put one lighter value (Z #a8b5b2 or g #8b9bb4) on the snout top (row 4 'QQQ') and the ear tips, so the head separates from the torso. - Optionally move the khopesh hilt down so it sits in the hand (row 11, cols 13-14) rather than on the bracer. No full redraw and no warm ramp.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): Two of the three points hold up. The third, the Anubite, is taste, and an earlier review already passed it. Nothing marks the three as deliberate. All of them landed in the first draft of the Survival maps (116f331) and have not been touched since. Git log -S and -L on monsters-maps.js show no later commit. art/tuned.js has no tune for them. The reviews/ folder mentions only the Anubite: hero-polish-graphics-2026-11-14.md says Anubite's "attached limbs, weapons and gear... remain readable". I rendered current against fixed, by day and by night (nightLift + ring), at 6x and in-scale 2x: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/witches/ab-all.png (script ab.mjs in the same folder). - **Thorn Witch: real, and it is the clearest of the three.** The recolour maps the cultist's shadow key R to #68386c. That is the cultist's own p, so the fold shading turns into violet blotches with almost the same luma: 87 against 76, where the original step was 110 against 76. At night the lift and saturation make it worse; it reads as magenta camouflage on green. With R→#1f4a3a the robe shades like the cultist reference, and violet stays only in the p lining. That is a one-line palette change and a clear gain. - **Frost Witch: real.** Rows 7-13 are a straight 10-px slab and the cultist it stands for is drawn at 1.5x. Beside the cultist's bell shape it reads as a blue box, and night makes that plainer. A quick A-line taper of rows 7-14 in my mock already reads more like a robed caster.   - Part of the critic's fix is off. The white fur hem already exists: row 14 is 'XXXGXGGggg', and row 6 is a fur collar.   - #4fd8e8 is not a PAL key. Drawn frames use palette keys, so the light side should use U (#0099db) with c (#2ce8f5) as the highlight. - **Anubite: not a clear gain.**   - The 2026-11-14 review already judged its limbs and weapon attached and readable. In the zoom the khopesh joins the arm (row 8 'QWtd').   - A right-facing profile head is the set's own convention: every monster faces right.   - A near-black Anubis in V/W/Q with gold ears and gold and blue bands reads as itself.   - The critic's warm ramp (h/a/A) turns it purple-brown at night in my render. That is a different taste, not better finish. Gameplay is untouched. The hit radius comes from data (cultist r 7.5). The 12x16 frame size stays, so the shadow width (enemies.js:919) stays too. Palette changes only change the colours of the death burst. No Math.random is involved, and no hero looks are touched. test/fae-thread.test.js spawns a thornwitch, but only for its behaviour. The fix as written is not sound as a whole for three reasons: - it redraws the Anubite, which brings no gain - it adds a fur hem that is already there - it names an off-palette hex for drawn art Better fix: Limit the fix to the two witches and leave the Anubite as it is. 1. **Thorn Witch:** one line at public/js/art/monsters-maps.js:960. Change the palette's R from '#68386c' to '#1f4a3a' and keep p '#3b2346'. '#193c3e', the v key, is fine too: it gives a deeper fold, and since it is already a palette colour it uses no new debris-palette entry. 2. **Frost Witch:** reshape only rows 6-15 of both frames at :310 into an A-line, keeping the frames 12x16.    - Narrow the shoulders to about cols 2-8 and widen one px each side every two rows, down to cols 0-11 at the hem.    - Keep the existing XXXG fur hem row, the collar, the staff and the hand.    - Run U down the lit left edge of the skirt, with one or two c highlight pixels, over the u base.
