### monsters-18 [confirmed] Slimes 'hop' without leaving the ground, their art frame is out of sync with the code stretch, and the fractional squash makes the pixel grid wobble
- polish / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:146-156 (renderScale: sy×1.12, sx×0.92 when (life·1.25+wob)%1<0.42; landing squash up to 1.25x0.8), :527-534 (hopper phase), :894 (frame from this.anim at 3-4 fps). Both lenses (art + tech).
- Evidence: From the code: the hop moves at 2.3× pace for 42% of each 0.8 s cycle with no vertical offset. The art frame index comes from this.anim (def.anim 3 fps, quartered while slow), a clock separate from the hop phase. Frame 1 of every slime is the squashed pose (box 0..13 wide and one row lower; sheet-forest-small.png 'Bog Slime' #1), so it can show while the code stretches the body tall.

The hopping kinds are slimes and slimelets with their sand, frost, petal and magma variants. At world 3.2 a hopping slime is 2.94 x 3.58 px per art px, so columns drop to 2-3 px while normal foes are 3-4. The flappers (bat, vulture, owl, bee) alternate only wings-up and wings-down at 9 fps, with 102-128 px changing between frames, more than their whole body.
- The most common small kinds slide while stretching. The drawn squash frame fights the code squash, and the fractional stretch makes rows appear and disappear and changes the pixel pattern on every hop. The flappers strobe in a swarm.
- Fix: Drive the slime from the hop phase alone:
- frame 0 (tall) while airborne, frame 1 (flat) for the landing and rest;
- lift the sprite by round(sin(ph/0.42·π)·3) whole art px during the hop, with the shadow staying on the ground and shrinking;
- quantise the stretch to whole art pixels (sy = (h+1)/h, sx = (w−1)/w), or replace it with authored squash and stretch frames.
This touches draw only, with no new Math.random. For the flappers, as an art add-on, draw one mid-wing frame each and play [0,2,1,2].
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The slime part of the finding is real; I confirmed it both in the code and in rendered pixels. **Code** - In `public/js/game/enemies.js`, the hopper (lines 527-534) moves at 2.3x pace while ph < 0.42 and 0.08x the rest of the time. - `renderScale` (line 152) stretches the sprite sx 0.92 / sy 1.12 during that window. The landing sets `squash = 1`, which scales up to 1.25 x 0.8 and fades out over 0.2 s. - `draw()` (lines 966-975) adds a vertical offset only for `def.fly`, so a slime never leaves the ground. - `frameIndex()` (line 894) picks the art frame from `this.anim`, which advances at def.anim (3 fps for slime, 4 for slimelet; line 612). That clock has nothing to do with the hop phase. - Frame 1 in `art/monsters.js` (lines 64-73) is the flat, wide pose: 12 px wide against 10, with the top row empty. **Pixels** I drew a Bog Slime through two hop cycles at world scale 3.2 using the production `Enemy.draw` (`verify/slimehop/hop.mjs`, `hop-slime-strip.png`, `hop-slime-ab.png`). In the sample, the stretch phase shows the flat frame stretched tall (ph 0.08-0.42), and the landing then shows the tall frame squashed. That is the opposite of the intended squash-and-stretch. The two clocks repeat every 4 s, and over that loop about 21% of all time shows the flat frame during the stretch. The ground line confirms the feet never lift. **Where the finding overstates** - "Quartered while slow" does not apply during the rest phase. The pace is still above 1 px/s there, so `anim` keeps running at the full 3 fps. - "Rows appear and disappear" is too strong. At world 3.2 no art row is lost. Only the screen widths of rows and columns shuffle between 2, 3 and 4 px. During the stretch, 41 px holds 14 columns, so 13 are 3 px wide and one is 2 px. At the landing peak, 26 px holds 10 rows, so 6 are 3 px tall and 4 are 2 px. During the 0.2 s fade the pattern changes every frame. This is real but mild mixel shimmer. - The flapper half (bat, vulture, owl and bee with two wing frames) was already settled. `reviews/hero-polish-graphics-2026-11-14.md` line 60 says: "bat/owl/vulture/bee wing positions ... short cycles are intentional arcade animation." It should not be raised again. **Why the proposed fix is not sound as written** 1. The lift does not "touch draw only". The comment at enemies.js line 143 says drawing and collision share the same squash and stretch. `body()` (line 901, used by `hitbox.js`) and init's `reach` (`HOP_BOUNDS` plus hover, lines 220-228) would also need the lift. Otherwise shots would hit the empty ground under the lifted body and miss its top 3 art px, which is about 30% of its height. 2. Rounding the stretch to sx = (w-1)/w and sy = (h+1)/h changes almost nothing. For a 14-px sprite that is 0.929 against the current 0.92, and it is still a fractional nearest-neighbour blit, so the shimmer stays. I also mocked resampling at art resolution (row 3 of `hop-slime-ab.png`). It fixes the grid but squeezes the 2-row eyes into a 1-px dash at landing. Only frames drawn by hand keep the art clean. **Files another session is editing** None of this touches the files with uncommitted work in progress. Images and scripts are in `C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/slimehop/`. Better fix: **Minimal fix (draw-side, no new `Math.random`, hitbox follows automatically):** take a hopper's frame from its hop phase instead of `this.anim`. `body()` already calls `frameIndex()`, so the hitbox stays in step. In `frameIndex()`, just before the final `return` (line 894), add: `if (this.def.ai === 'hopper') { const ph = (this.life * 1.25 + this.wob) % 1; return ph < 0.42 ? 0 : this.squash > 0 ? 1 : Math.floor(this.anim) % 2; }` This gives: - the tall frame while it lunges stretched; - the flat frame for the 0.2 s landing squash; - the existing idle wobble only while resting. `frameIndex()` runs ahead of the `spr.poses` and idle branches for other kinds, so the check must sit after them, or be guarded by `!s.poses`, just as the current fallback is. **Optional follow-ups:** 1. Pixel-clean squash and stretch: draw two more frames per slime kind (a tall 10x11 stretch and a 13x7 pancake; the map variants inherit them through palette spreads in `monsters-maps.js`). Show them by phase and drop the hopper's code scaling (line 152 and the squash term for hoppers), keeping `HOP_BOUNDS` and the frame boxes consistent. Do not use a rounded scale factor or art-resolution resampling: one keeps the shimmer and the other loses the eyes. 2. If a real lift is wanted: write one helper, for example `hopLift(e) = ph < 0.42 ? Math.round(Math.sin(ph / 0.42 * Math.PI) * 3) * e.drawScale : 0`. Subtract it in `draw` (`by`), in `body()` (`top`) and in init's `reach` (as `hover` is for flyers). Shrink the shadow's alpha or width in `drawShadow` by the same amount. Leave the flappers alone; the 2026-11-14 review already accepted them.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The slime part holds up. The flapper part was already settled, and the fix does more than draw. Checked in public/js/game/enemies.js: - The art frame comes from `this.anim` (line 894). It advances at def.anim, 3 fps for the slime and 4 for the slimelet (line 612). It only drops to a quarter when sp <= 1, and a resting hopper still moves at about 0.08 x pace, which is over 1 px/s. - The hop runs on its own 1.25 Hz phase (lines 529-533 and 152). The two clocks drift against each other with a 4 s beat. - I replicated renderScale and frameIndex at world 3.2 over one hop: verify/slime-hop/slime-hop-strip.png under the monsters review folder. With the anim clock offset by one frame, the flat art pose f1 plays for the whole stretch-tall HOP, and the tall pose f0 plays at rest. The squash and stretch read upside down on about half of all hops. - The slime never leaves the ground (no lift in draw). The 2.94 x 3.58 px per art px figure is correct. - The stretch has been in the code since the first commit (84963a5, moved into renderScale in 6a7d8bb). Nothing in the code, comments or git log marks the desync as intended. - The 2026-11-14 reviews only judged the source frames: "slimes and oozes use coherent squash", "short cycles are intentional arcade animation". They never looked at how the frame clock lines up with the hop. So the slime desync is new. - Those same reviews explicitly settled the flappers: "bat/owl/vulture/bee wing positions ... intentional arcade animation" and "two-frame creatures ... clear wing silhouettes". Drop the mid-wing frame add-on. Problems with the proposed fix: 1. "Touches draw only" is wrong. renderScale() and frameIndex() also feed Enemy.body(), which is the hitbox. The owner's rule (26 October 2026, hitbox.js header) is hitbox = as drawn, and the 2026-11-14 playthrough fixed exactly this kind of draw/hitbox mismatch. A lift drawn without a matching lift in body() and in HOP_BOUNDS/reach would break that rule. Adding them changes collision, and the test that checks all 76 kinds against broad-phase reach would need updating. 2. Quantising sy to (h+1)/h does not stop the wobble. At zoom 3.2 every art row still maps to 3.6 screen px, so rows stay uneven. Most of the per-frame shimmer comes from the landing squash shrinking a little every frame over 0.2 s. 3. The flapper add-on contradicts the settled call above. No Math.random is involved in any of this. Even so, moving the hitbox by about 1 px could shift the seeded-run tests through hit/miss timing, so CI should be watched. Better fix: The smallest fix: in Enemy.frameIndex(), when def.ai === 'hopper', take the frame from the hop phase instead of this.anim: ```js const ph = (this.life * 1.25 + this.wob) % 1; return ph < 0.42 ? 0 : this.squash > 0.3 ? 1 : 0; ``` Optionally, also show f1 for the last ~0.1 of the cycle as a crouch before the next hop. - body() calls frameIndex(), so the hitbox still matches what is drawn. The box changes by at most 1 px. - No Math.random calls are added or moved. - The art's flat frame now does the landing squash. So either drop the code's continuously decaying landing squash in renderScale, or step it (for example, apply a fixed 1.12 x 0.9 only while squash > 0.5). That removes the 12-frame shimmer on each landing. The in-air stretch can stay, since it now always stretches the tall pose. If the owner wants a real hop, add a whole-art-pixel lift, round(sin(ph/0.42·π)·2-3), as a separate change. - Mirror it in body() the way HOVER is for flyers, and add it to the hopper reach bounds. - Keep the shadow on the ground. Skip the flapper mid-wing frames; the 2026-11-14 reviews settled them. Expect seeded-run tests to possibly shift through ±1 px hit timing, and watch CI.

### monsters-19 [confirmed] Smite brand overlaps the curse and confusion marks, and the marks are drawn at fractional sizes
- polish / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:1023-1027 (same anchor markY for both), :1040-1047 (brand at Y-9S..Y-2S, 7x7), :1052-1073 (hourglass at Y-11S..Y-6S, sparks at Y-5S±1.5S via fillRect(cx + dx*S, …, wd*S, S)). Tech lens.
- Evidence: looks-forest-1.png, looks-forest-2.png and looks-pressure-thief.png, 'marks' column: the violet hourglass sits on top of the yellow Smite sigil and the sparks cross it. From the code, the rows overlap by 3-4 art px. With S = 2.4 or 3.2, the fillRect edges land on fractions such as cx - 6.4, so Chrome blends them into soft seams between the hourglass rows and around the 6.4-px spark squares.
- A foe that is both Smite-branded and cursed or confused shows a muddle of two icons. These marks are the only monster overlays built from fillRect at fractional sizes; everything else is a nearest-neighbour drawImage at a rounded position, so these are the only blurry pixels over the horde.
- Fix: - When brandT > 0, raise the hourglass and the spark orbit by (7 + 2) * S so they stack above the brand.
- Draw on whole pixels: x0 = Math.round(cx + dx * S), x1 = Math.round(cx + (dx + wd) * S), then fillRect(x0, y0, x1 - x0, y1 - y0).
- For the sparks, use k = Math.max(1, Math.round(S)) and squares of 2k.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): I confirmed both halves of the finding in the code, in headless renders and in real Chrome. **1. The marks overlap the brand.** Both are placed from the same markY (public/js/game/enemies.js:1025-1027). - The brand covers Y-9S to Y-2S. It bobs by up to 1S, so its top can reach Y-10S. - The hourglass covers top-8S to top-3S, where top = Y-3S. That is Y-11S to Y-6S, so 3-4 art px of it sit on the brand. - The sparks orbit at Y-5S ±1.5S in 2S squares, about Y-6.5S to Y-1.5S, and x ±5S. They pass straight over the 7-px-wide sigil. This happens in real play. Smite, Judgement and Beacon brand foes. Hex Hourglass curses them and Puffcap Spores confuses them, and both are shared weapons. Smite and Hex also both aim at the mightiest foes (weapons.js:773, `mightiest()`). So a Paladin carrying Hex will often show this muddle on champions, lords and bosses. The earlier reviews don't settle it. Image: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/marks/marks-S.png. In the brand+curse, brand+confuse and all-three cells, the hourglass sits on the sigil and the sparks cross it. It happens at S=3 too, so it doesn't depend on the scale. **2. The marks are filled at fractional sizes.** cam.S = view.S × 0.8 (game.js:2787), and view.S is always a whole number. So cam.S is 3.2 at 1080p, 2.4 at 900p and 1.6 at 720p. `fillRect(cx + dx*S, ..., wd*S, S)` and the sparks' `2*S` squares therefore start and end on fractions. I counted colours in the top band of the curse-only cell: - At S=3 there are exactly 3 hourglass colours plus the background. - At S=3.2 there are 26 colours and at S=2.4 there are 27, so the extras are blends. A whole pixel row at each row boundary is blended (dump from measure.mjs). - The spark cells show the same: 6 colours, including blends such as #888357. A tiny Playwright page in real Chrome drew the same rows at S=3.2 and gave 22 distinct colours, so Chrome blends these edges as well. The capture tool's own drawing is not the cause. One correction to the finding: the brand's drawImage size is not rounded either (`img.width*S`). Even so, it showed only 2-3 blended pixels, so the claim that the marks are the main blurry overlay holds. **Severity.** Polish: two marks that collide and a few pixels of soft edges, on a status combination that will come up fairly often. **The proposed fix.** It is sound. I applied it as a scratch-only monkeypatch to check (fix-ab.png in the same folder): - The stack reads brand, then sparks, then hourglass, with nothing overlapping. - The sparks clear the brand by 0.5S even when the brand bobs up. - Rounding both edges (x0/x1, y0/y1) makes neighbouring rows meet exactly with no blending. enemies.js is not among the files the other session has uncommitted. Better fix: The same idea, with three small refinements: - **Lift.** Take the lift from the brand sprite instead of a fixed 9: `(img.height + 2) * S`, where img is `SPR[WEAPON_SPRITES.brand]`'s frame. - **Gap when only cursed.** Lift the hourglass alone by about 5S, so its bottom sits about 1px above the bobbing brand. A lift of 9S leaves a 6-px empty band where the sparks would have been. - **Spark size.** Use `Math.round(2 * S)` instead of `2 * Math.max(1, Math.round(S))`. At S=2.4 that gives 5 px instead of shrinking the sparks to 4. In the hourglass helper, round both edges with the same formula so rows always meet: `x0 = Math.round(cx + dx*S)`, `x1 = Math.round(cx + (dx+wd)*S)`, `y0 = Math.round(top + (dy-8)*S)`, `y1 = Math.round(top + (dy-7)*S)`, then `fillRect(x0, y0, x1-x0, y1-y0)`. Optional: also round the brand's drawImage width and height, as Enemy.draw already does for the body.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): Both halves of the finding hold up in the code and in renders. 1. Overlap. drawEmissive (public/js/game/enemies.js:1023-1027) passes the same markY to drawBrand and drawMarks. The brand is the 7x7 sigil from hero-paladin-items.js:47. It is drawn from Y-9S to Y-2S and bobs by up to 1S either way. The hourglass covers Y-11S to Y-6S, so three of its five rows sit on the sigil. The sparks circle at Y-5S +/- 1.5S, 5S either side of centre, so they cut across it.    This was never a design choice. Commit 8cd0005 added the shared anchor only to allow for padded frames, and no review in reviews/ mentions the marks.    It also comes up often. Smite (the Paladin's signature) strikes "the mightiest foe near", and the shared Hex Hourglass curses "the toughest foes near you". A Paladin carrying Hex therefore stacks both marks on the same foe, and an evolved Lantern can brand as well. 2. Blur. cam.S = view.S * 0.8 (game.js:2787), which gives 1.6, 2.4 or 3.2 on common screens. drawMarks uses fillRect at fractional x positions and sizes (cx + dx*S, wd*S, 2*S), and Skia antialiases those edges. I rendered the production code with @napi-rs/canvas, which uses Skia like Chrome. At S=2.4 the hourglass has 92 off-palette blended pixels. With whole-pixel edges it has 0. At S=3.2 the soft seams around the rows and spark squares are plain to see.    Everything else on a monster is a drawImage, and Chrome does not antialias an axis-aligned drawImage. So these marks really are the soft pixels over the horde. Images: - C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/marks-skeptic/marks-now-vs-fix.png: production vs fix at S 2.4 and 3.2, for all three marks, brand+curse, brand+confuse and curse+confuse. - C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/marks-skeptic/seams-x10.png: the seams at 10x. Fix check: - It touches only the draw path in enemies.js. That file is not in the other session's uncommitted set. - It makes no Math.random call and changes no gameplay or hero look. - The 9S lift is the smallest that works. The spark orbit reaches down to Y-1.5S - lift, and the bobbing brand rises to Y-10S, so the lift must be at least 8.5S. Severity is polish. The muddle only shows when a foe carries two marks at once, and the blur is only at the edges, but it is clearly visible in the Paladin+Hex build. Better fix: Keep the critic's fix, with two small changes. The whole change goes in drawMarks: ```js const S = cam.S, cx = Math.round(X + w / 2); const top = Math.round(Y - 3 * S - (this.brandT > 0 ? 9 * S : 0)); ``` For the hourglass, write px as: ```js const x0 = Math.round(cx + dx * S), x1 = Math.round(cx + (dx + wd) * S); const y0 = Math.round(top + (dy - 8) * S), y1 = Math.round(top + (dy - 7) * S); ctx.fillRect(x0, y0, x1 - x0, y1 - y0); ``` For the sparks, keep the rounded positions they already have and use `const k = Math.max(2, Math.round(2 * S))` as the square size. That is closer to the drawn 2-art-px size than the critic's 2*round(S): at S=2.4 it gives 5 px for the intended 4.8, where 2*round(S) gives 4. Optionally, for consistency, round the brand's drawImage size too: Math.round(img.width * S) and Math.round(img.height * S). Chrome does not blur that one, though.

- bug / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:1124-1130 (eliteOutlineBuf: every ink pixel becomes GOLD), used at :959 and NIGHT_LOOKS[3] :1293; the same pattern in rimBuf :1306-1312 (lord and boss rim). Both lenses (art + tech).
- Evidence: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/art-extra/champion-interior-ink.png, 'plain' against 'as shipped':
- the cultist's black hood void becomes a gold face;
- the ogre's eye ring, the yeti's mouth and the skeleton's sockets and nose turn gold;
- the death-knight visor slit turns gold;
- the third column shows the edge-only fix.

In-game: looks-forest-2.png (champion column) and closeup-champions-thief-forest.png (Vampiric cultist at about 1480,560; Bonehide's eye at about 1200,215). lineup-x2.png champion row, item 1: gold ribs and eye sockets. closeup-crypt-lord-skeletonarcher.png: the lord's face and ribs glow red as a wireframe (x 740-840, y 240-360).

Exterior flood-fill audit (C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/tech-extra/ink-audit.mjs), interior ink of all ink:

| Kind | Interior/total |
|---|---|
| skeleton | 17/84 |
| ogre | 25/120 |
| orc | 21/85 |
| sand golem | 32/112 |
| yeti | 38/100 |
| frost troll | 39/118 |
| treant | 45/134 |
| fiend | 29/78 |
| magma golem | 31-38/110 |
| skeleton archer lord | 106/212 |

All 69 kinds use ink inside the body (art-extra/interiorink.mjs).
- The swap recolours every pixel equal to the ink colour, not just the silhouette edge. The art uses that same ink for eyes, mouths, visor slits, rib gaps, hood voids and the lines between arm and torso or between the legs. Every champion, the most watched monster on screen, gets a gold-filled face, merged limbs and a gold-scribble silhouette, and loses the detail that tells it apart. Crypt lords get the same from rimBuf, as a pulsing red wireframe inside the face.
- Fix: In eliteOutlineBuf and rimBuf, recolour an ink pixel only if it is 4-adjacent to an exterior transparent pixel or to the frame edge. Find the exterior by flood-filling the transparent region from the buffer border once per frame, so enclosed transparent holes don't count. Cache it with the variant. Interior ink stays dark, at one extra pass per frame.

### monsters-4 [unchecked] Frozen, burning or winding-up champions drop the gold outline; wind-up strobes it at 10 Hz; a lulled champion would crash
- bug / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:940-960 (status branches come before `else if (this.elite)`), :962 (night look = look + 3), :1293-1295 (night-*-e looks have no gold), :1163 (crash site). Tech lens.
- Evidence: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/tech-extra/elite-status.png:
- the frozen champion orc (2nd) has no gold outline and reads as a plain frozen orc;
- the winding-up champion (3rd) has gold in the top frame and an ungilded burn tint 0.05 s later.

Latent crash: a champion with lullT > 0 gets look 6+3 = 9, NIGHT_LOOKS[9] is undefined, and Game.draw throws a TypeError at enemies.js:1163 (reproduced in memory). It can't happen today only because fae.js:276 lulls only e.movable foes, which excludes champions.
- Only the plain branch applies eliteOutlineBuf. These all swap the champion's gold for a tinted plain frame:
- freeze (Frozen Heart and similar);
- the sunrise burn;
- the charger or caster wind-up: wolf 0.45 s, orc 0.6 s, hellhound 0.4 s, cultist 0.55 s, plus their map variants.
The champion's identifying outline vanishes exactly when the player is targeting it, or flickers 10 times a second during a wind-up. The matching night looks 4 and 5 are also ungilded and unringed.
- Fix: - Build tinted champion variants on top of the gold outline, for example fr.variant('frozen-e', b => eliteOutlineBuf(TINTS.frozen(b))), and the same for burn and charmed, with the tint skipping the ink (see the tint finding). Use them whenever this.elite is set.
- Build NIGHT_LOOKS 4-5 as nightBuf(eliteOutlineBuf(TINTS.x(nightLift(b))), 0).
- Give look 6 a champion twin (append it and map 6 to its index), so the +3 arithmetic can never index past the table.

### monsters-9 [unchecked] Frozen, burn and charmed tints wash out the outline
- defect / code - public/js/engine/sprites.js:342-349 (TINTS) via public/js/engine/pixels.js:81-92 (tint blends every opaque pixel, ink included); night looks at public/js/game/enemies.js:1291-1292, 1294-1295, 1299; sealed-guardian overlay enemies.js:999. Tech lens.
- Evidence: The outline #181425 (L 23) becomes:
- #62889d (L 127) when frozen;
- #805e76 (L 107) when charmed;
- #69322d when burning.
C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/tech-extra/status-x2.png (from closeup-status-forest.png): the frozen skeleton has no dark edge, just three light bands (tinted outline, grey-blue ring, halo), and looks out of focus next to the crisp goblin. In the looks-*.png 'frozen' and 'fae sleep' columns, every kind's edge goes soft.
- tint() pulls the ink toward the tint colour as hard as the body (0.55 for frozen). A frozen foe loses its outline, the one thing that separates it from the ground and its neighbours, and by night the ring then sits on a light edge. On Frostfell's snow a frozen frost-blue foe nearly disappears. Related to the Frozen-readability finding, which is about the body.
- Fix: Add a skip set to px.tint and have TINTS pass b.ink ?? OUTLINE and b.rim, so the outline stays as drawn. Or tint the ink toward a dark shade of the tint (for example #1b3346 for ice), which follows the owner's 'edges are dark shades of their own colour'. This also fixes the sealed-guardian overlay at enemies.js:999.

### monsters-11 [unchecked] The Cultist family (x1.5) and champions (x1.35) are scaled, not drawn, so their art pixels are fatter and uneven
- defect / art - public/js/data/enemies.js:32 (cultist scale 1.5, inherited by frostwitch and thornwitch); public/js/art/monsters.js:312 (cultist 14x18), public/js/art/monsters-maps.js:310 (frostwitch), :960 (thornwitch); public/js/game/enemies.js:214 (elite x1.35). Both lenses (art + tech).
- Evidence: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/art-extra/scale-unevenness-x3.png: next to the ghost and wolf, the cultist's outline steps alternate 1 and 2 px and its pixels are visibly fatter. The champion skeleton and goblin have doubled columns. The same shows in the lineup-x2.png Darkwood #12 and the champion row.

At world 3.2, screen px per art px:
- normal foes: 3.2;
- cultist: 4.8 (3.6 at 2.4);
- champion: 4.32.
true1080p-forest-crop-1to1.png shows the cultists at the right edge.
- These are the only horde sprites with a different pixel density. Thick-and-thin outlines and chunky pixels mark them as upscaled next to the 1 px-ink Darkwood set, and even an integer world scale wouldn't fix it, because the multipliers resample the art by non-integer factors. The 1.5x Cultist size is the owner's call; the problem is that it comes from scaling, not from drawing.
- Fix: Cultists: keep the 1.5x size but redraw it natively. Author cultist, frostwitch and thornwitch at about 21x27 art px (same palette and frames, 1 px ink) and set scale 1.

Champions are an owner call:
- (a) keep x1.35 and accept the uneven pixels;
- (b) draw them at x1, relying on the gold outline, aura, name and bar;
- (c) snap to x1.5 and hand-clean the doubled rows once per kind;
- (d) once the world scale is an integer k, draw them at a whole number of screen px per art px, ppx = Math.max(k + 1, Math.round(k * 1.35)), which is 3/2 at k=2 and 4/3 at k=3. The same Math.round(1.5k)/k works for any 1.5x caster that isn't redrawn.

### monsters-13 [unchecked] Wind-up tint strobes at 10 Hz even with FLASHES: SOFT
- defect / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:951 (`this.state === 'wind' && Math.floor(this.life * 20) % 2`); compare :943, where the sunrise burn honours softFlash. Tech lens.
- Evidence: From the code, the burn and plain frames swap every 0.05 s, a 10 Hz on/off cycle, for the whole wind-up: wolf 0.45 s, orc 0.6 s, hellhound 0.4 s, cultist 0.55 s, crypt archer 0.35 s (data/enemies.js:25, 33, 37, 42, 59), plus the map variants that stand in for them. The night look swaps between look 0 and 2 with it, so the ring flickers too. The looks-*.png 'wind-up' column shows the tinted frame.
- FLASHES: SOFT (and the Low effects preset, screens.js:1153) exists to remove strobing, yet every charger still flickers at 10 Hz before it charges. That is inside the photosensitive 3-30 Hz band, and in a wolf pack many flicker at once.
- Fix: Hold the tint steady under SOFT: `else if (this.state === 'wind' && (this.g.settings.softFlash === true || Math.floor(this.life * 20) % 2))`, the same pattern as line 943. Optionally slow FULL to about 6 Hz (life * 12).

### monsters-14 [unchecked] Champion names collide, cover the hero, and the label ignores flyer hover
- defect / code - public/js/game/hud.js:940-947 (overHead: e.y - e.h - 6, no hover), :968-979 (drawNames: no overlap check between labels or with the hero). Tech lens.
- Evidence: - field-desert-combat-crop-centre-x2.png, top centre: 'Grimfang' and 'Bloodmaw' print on top of each other ('Grir Bloodmaw').
- chrome-forest-knight-1920x1080-crop-1to1.png: 'Nightthorn' and its bar sit across the Knight's helmet (x 380-560, y 165-220).
- C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/tech-extra/elite-flyers.png: three champion flyers; the names overlap and the imp's health bar crosses its horns.
Flyers are drawn HOVER.up 6 + bob 2 px above e.y - e.h.
- Up to three champions are named, but each label goes straight above its owner with no layout, so a champion pack (the usual case) stacks its names. The labels are HUD, drawn after everything, so a champion near the hero pastes its name over him, which breaks the spirit of 'hero always on top'. For flying champions (bat, imp, vulture, owl, bee and the rest) the name and bar sit 6-8 px too low.
- Fix: - In overHead, add the hover: AT.y = (e.y - e.h - 6 - (e.def.fly ? HOVER.up + HOVER.bob : 0) - cam.y) * z (export HOVER from enemies.js).
- In drawNames, keep the rectangles already placed (name plus bar). If a new label overlaps one of them, or the hero's sprite box (about x ±9, y -34..0 in world px), move it up by GLYPH_H + 2, at most two steps. Failing that, drop the name and keep the bar.

### monsters-16 [rejected] The Frozen status barely shows on Frostfell's own blue monsters
- polish / code - public/js/engine/sprites.js:342-344 (TINTS.frozen = tint #9ee7ff at 0.55); public/js/game/enemies.js:948-950. Art lens.
- Evidence: Mean CIE ΔE from plain to frozen (art-extra/frozendelta.mjs):

| Group | Kinds | ΔE |
|---|---|---|
| Frostfell (blue) | frostslime | 16 |
| | iceimp, frosthound | 21 |
| | rimeknight, yeti | 23 |
| | snowowl | 25 |
| | frostwisp, icetroll | 26 |
| | icewidow | 28 |
| | frostwitch | 30 |
| Bone white | skeleton | 20 |
| Warm | orc | 39 |
| | zombie | 40 |
| | goblin | 41 |
| | cultist, imp | 57 |

looks-frozen-1.png, 'frozen' column: the frost slime, ice widow and frost wisp are almost the same as in the 'plain' column.
- Tinting toward ice blue changes little on monsters that are already ice blue. Frozen is a gameplay state (they stop moving and hitting) that the player can't read on the map built around ice, and it also reads weakly on the bone-white skeleton. The tint-washes-outline finding is a separate outline problem.
- Fix: Give the frozen look a shape cue as well as the tint:
- recolour the silhouette-edge pixels (the same exterior-edge test as the interior-ink finding) to #c8f4ec or white;
- add 2-3 fixed white glint pixels;
- for already-cold kinds (mean hue 180-240°), use a stronger desaturate-and-lighten instead.
Cache it as a variant, as now.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The finding holds, though it is a bit overstated. In code, TINTS.frozen (public/js/engine/sprites.js:343) runs px.tint toward #9ee7ff at 0.55 on every pixel, ink included. enemies.js:948-950 uses it as the only steady frozen cue; the other cues are a 4-particle burst at onset and the walk animation dropping to quarter speed (line 612). Re-running art-extra/frozendelta.mjs gives exactly the critic's numbers: frostslime 16, iceimp/frosthound 21, skeleton 20, against 39-57 for warm kinds. On the Frostfell ground I rendered a crowd at the real 1080p world scale (S=4 x zoom 0.8, production Game.draw, 13 of 44 foes frozen). Image: verify/frozen-skeptic/crowd-now-1to1.png and the 2x crop crowd-compare-x2.png, top band. On blue kinds, frozen is almost only a lightness change. A frozen frost slime reads as a slightly paler frost slime. Frozen hounds, the yeti and the rime knight read as faded or ghostly, not as iced, and look like the frost wisp, which is pale by design. I can pick them out only by the washed outline, by looking hard, and that would be harder in motion with snow weather. Overstated: in looks-frozen-1.png the ice widow and frost wisp are visibly paler when frozen, not 'almost the same'. The frost slime (and the hound and imp) is the true weak case. This is polish, not a bug: the state works, it is only hard to read on its own map. The change touches sprites.js and enemies.js, neither of which is in the other session's uncommitted work. The proposed fix is unsound in its main part. Recolouring the silhouette edge to #c8f4ec or white is wrong for exactly the map that motivates it, because Frostfell's ground is near-white snow. I prototyped it in memory (crowd-a-1to1.png, middle band of crowd-compare-x2.png): the frozen hound and slime almost vanish into the snow, and the frozen witch turns into a faint pale box. Frozen foes become harder to see, which is the opposite of the goal. The glints and the stronger desaturate-and-lighten for cold kinds are fine. Better fix: Keep the silhouette dark and change the body, still cached as the 'frozen' variant. 1. Leave the ink/rim pixels (b.ink ?? OUTLINE, and b.rim) untinted, or map them to a deep ice blue such as #1b3346. This is the same change the separate tint-washes-outline finding needs, so do both in one edit. 2. For cold kinds (mean hue 180-240, computed once per buffer since the variant is cached), desaturate each body pixel to about 25% of its chroma, then lift it about 0.55 toward a pale ice such as #d2f4ff. Warm kinds keep the current #9ee7ff @0.55 tint, which already reads well. 3. As the shape cue, set to white every opaque pixel whose upper neighbour is transparent (a frost cap along the top edge), and add 2 white glints inside the body. Prototype (in memory only): verify/frozen-skeptic/crowd.mjs mode b; result in crowd-b-1to1.png and the bottom band of crowd-compare-x2.png. Frozen foes read as pale ice statues with dark edges, clearly apart from every live blue kind and still clear of the snow. Also: at night the frozen look passes through the night lift (NIGHT_LOOKS[1]), so frozen warm foes come out pink/lilac rather than ice. Check a forest-night frame after any change (crowdnight-compare-x2.png).
- Intent sceptic (not real, taste): The finding says frozen barely shows on blue monsters, but the real game draw doesn't bear that out. I drew each kind twice, once plain and once frozen, through the production Game.draw on the Frozen map by day and on the Forest at night (verify/frozen-cur.png, verify/forest-cur.png). Every frozen Frostfell kind turns pale and ghostly against its plain twin, and the dark navy edge washes to light blue. That holds for frost slime, ice widow, frost wisp, ice imp, hound, rime knight, witch, owl, yeti and frostbitten, and for the skeleton too. Frost slime is the weakest case, but the change is still clear. The critic's own ΔE numbers (16 to 30) are well above what the eye notices, so "almost the same as plain" is overstated. At night the frozen look also keeps the night ring and halo (NIGHT_LOOKS 'night-frozen'), and it reads plainly. The proposed fix makes things worse. I prototyped it in verify/frozen-critic.png: edge pixels recoloured to #c8f4ec, three white glints, and a stronger desaturate-and-lighten for kinds with a mean hue of 180 to 240°. On the Frozen map's white snow, frozen Frostfell monsters then almost vanish into the ground. The monster stops being readable at all, which is worse than the state being hard to read. I also tried the opposite: keep a dark ice-blue edge and tint only the inside (verify/frozen-alt.png). That keeps the silhouette but makes frozen look more like plain on the cold kinds. The washed-out edge is in fact the main frozen cue now. So there is no clear quality gain on offer, and the current tint already does its job on every map. None of the earlier reviews or the git history settle this, and it doesn't clash with the owner's calls. It is a matter of taste, not a defect. Better fix: No change is needed. If the owner still wants a stronger frozen cue for the Frostfell kinds, add only 2 or 3 fixed white glint pixels inside the body to the cached 'frozen' variant (TINTS.frozen in sprites.js). Leave the silhouette edge alone and don't lighten further: the prototype shows a white or mint edge and a stronger lighten make frozen foes disappear into the Frozen map's snow by day. The change stays draw-only with no Math.random. Check it on the Frozen map by day before shipping. Prototypes: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/monsters/verify/frozen-ab.mjs (modes cur, critic, alt), with renders frozen-cur.png, frozen-critic.png, frozen-alt.png and forest-cur.png in the same folder.

### monsters-17 [rejected] The night lift clips light colours to pure white: half a skeleton is #ffffff, the same as a hit flash
- polish / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:1263-1271 (nightColour: lift 90, then sat 40 and contrast 25). Art lens.
- Evidence: Share of body pixels at ≥250/250/250 after nightLift (art-extra/nightramp.mjs):

| Kind | Pure white |
|---|---|
| rabbit | 50% |
| skeleton and minion | 49% |
| mantis | 32% |
| flameskull | 29% |
| skeletonarcher | 26% |
| mummy | 18% |
| shieldbearer | 16% |

The skeleton's bone ramp goes from z #f4f4e4 / Z #a8b5b2 (ΔL 65) to #ffffff / #c6dcd6 (ΔL 44). chrome-forest-knight-1600x900.png and chrome-hell-necromancer-1600x900.png show skeletons as flat white cut-outs with red eyes, the brightest objects on screen.
- Contrast-brightening is the owner's call and stays. But on colours that are already light, the pop step only flattens the shading and pushes them into hit-flash white. Skeletons are the most common horde kind on every map, and at night they lose their bone modelling.
- Fix: In nightColour, leave colours already above about L 200 alone, or scale lift and contrast by (1−L/255)², so bone stays #f4f4e4 with its grey step. Dark and mid tones keep the same lift as now. Rebuild the colour cache; there is no other cost.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The core defect is real; two parts of the description are overstated. **What reproduces.** I copied nightColour (enemies.js:1268-1271, with NIGHT_TUNE then NIGHT_POP through art/tune.js shade). My copy matches the production nightLift on the skeleton with 0 pixels different. - Bone white #f4f4e4 (L242) becomes #ffffff. It is clipped by contrast 25 (×1.375), because any channel at about 214 or above, after gamma, lands past 255. - Bone grey #a8b5b2 (L177) becomes #c6dcd6 (L213), so the lightness gap shrinks from 65 to 42. - Pure-white share of body pixels: skeleton and minion 49%, rabbit 50% (15% was already white), mantis 32%, flameskull 29%, skeletonarcher 26%. These match the critic's numbers. - Across the roster, the clamp makes 7 pairs of distinct light colours identical (for example the rabbit's #f4f4e4 and #ffffff) and turns the warm cream bone into neutral white. - In the real Chrome capture (chrome-forest-knight-1600x900.png, crops in verify/nightwhite/crop-forest-skel-*.png), skeletons away from the hero show bone at about 245,245,247 (neutral, cream gone) and shadow at about 190,210,207. **What is overstated.** 1. They are not "flat cut-outs". The pale mint grey step and the blue rib shading are still clearly visible at about ΔL 42. 2. "The same as a hit flash" holds only inside the buffer. On screen at night, a flashing foe has look -1, so it is drawn lit at ambient and cut out of the night layer (drawNightEdge / nightCut). Beyond the hero's light, the flash is therefore darker than the lifted skeleton (art-extra/ab-night-hitflash.png, row A). **Why the proposed fix is unsound.** Measured with verify/nightwhite/variants.mjs; the sheet is variants-x6-left.png. - Option 1, leaving colours above L 200 alone, keeps the bone unclipped but still lifts the grey to 213. That makes the ramp flatter than now: skeleton gap 42 → 29, minion 52 → 15. - Option 2, scaling lift and contrast by (1−L/255)², keeps the bone ramp (gap 56). But the average lift of dark colours drops 39.6 → 26.4 and of mid tones 28.3 → 15.8. That contradicts the fix's own claim that darks and mids keep the same lift, and it erodes the owner's "brighter monsters" call. **The trade-off.** The ramp compression is the cost of lifting upper-mid greys, which the owner asked for. Clipping itself only costs the cream hue and merges distinct light colours. Any fade weighted by lightness that keeps darks and mids lifted still leaves the skeleton's gap at about 35. Better fix: Fix only what clipping breaks, and leave the lift as it is. In nightColour, do the pop (sat 40, then contrast ×1.375) on unclamped channels. If the brightest channel goes past 1, divide all three channels by it instead of clamping each one separately. Then keep the existing "never darker than before" fallback. Measured result: - Bone #f4f4e4 becomes #ffffe7, so the cream is kept. - #ffffff and #f4f4e4 stay distinct; merged colour pairs go from 7 to 0. - Pure white across the horde drops from 6.4% to 1.0%, which is only art that was white to begin with. - The skeleton's lightness gap stays at about 40, close to the 42 now. - Dark and mid lift stay as they are: 40.8 and 27.9, against 39.6 and 28.3 now. The change is a few lines in one function, and the colour cache rebuilds by itself. Restoring the full day-time ramp (gap 65) would need less lift on upper-mid greys such as the bone grey, the ring grey and the ghost. That is the owner's call, not a fix.
- Intent sceptic (not real, taste): The arithmetic is right. With contrast +25 (factor 1.375 around 0.5), any channel at or above about 220 after the lift clips to 255. Bone z #f4f4e4 comes out #ffffff, and Z #a8b5b2 comes out #c6dcd6. That shrinks the ramp step from ΔL 65 to 42. Everything else in the finding is overstated or wrong: (1) Skeletons are not flat cut-outs. I rendered forest night in-memory at x4 with every monster beyond the 60 px near fade (verify/nightclip-skeptic/ab-forest.png, zoom-forest-skel.png). The pale blue-grey step still models ribs, pelvis and joints. Next to day bone, the top tone only moves #f4f4e4 to #ffffff (ΔL 13), which is barely visible. (2) The "same as a hit flash" claim is false at night. A flashing foe gets _look -1. drawNightEdge cuts it out of the night layer, so the white silhouette is lit at the ambient level and comes out grey-blue, darker than the lifted skeleton (art-extra/ab-night-hitflash.png row A). The flash also turns the ink outline and red eyes white; nightLift keeps both. The two cannot be confused. (3) Intent. Commit 309b8e4 added the sat +40 / contrast +25 pop at the owner's 22 Oct request: "contrast brighter ... shiny and easy to see and stand out". On 27 Oct the owner reviewed night visibility again and asked for more of it (ringA up, halo, vignette moved under the horde), not less. Skeletons being the brightest thing on a dark map is what that call produces. (4) Both proposed fixes are unsound. - Scaling by (1−L/255)² does not leave mid and dark tones alone, as claimed. At L 128 the factor is already 0.25. Results: #3e8948 147→120, #262b44 86→71, #5a6988 136→113, #9e2835 96→86. The A/B shows the archer's cloak and bow, the goblin, the cultist and the mantis visibly dimmer, which reverses the owner's "brighter" call. It also has 21 grey-ramp inversions against 6 now. - "Leave colours above L 200 alone" is a hard step that inverts ramps that cross it: #c8c8c8 (L 200) goes to #eeeeee (238), while #cacaca (L 202) stays 202, and #c4c4b4 (194) goes to 232 while #d0d0c0 (206) stays 206. (5) No fix gets the gain for free. A monotonic soft knee on the contrast avoids pure white (bone about #f6f6ef) but squeezes the bone/grey step further (to about 34). The only way to bring back the 65 step is to lift Z less, which makes the skeleton dimmer, against the owner's call. The result is a trade of brightness against modelling, which is taste, and the owner has already chosen brightness. Gameplay, Math.random and hero looks are untouched by any variant. Only enemies.js nightColour and its cache would change, and that file is not one with the other session's uncommitted work. Better fix: Leave it as shipped. If the owner ever asks for warmer, less clipped bone at night, the smallest safe change is a soft shoulder on the contrast step in nightColour: compress per channel only above about 0.8, for example x = k + (1−k)(1−e^(−(x−k)/(1−k))) with k = 0.8. Mid and dark tones stay exactly as now, order stays monotonic, and bone comes out about #f6f6ef instead of #ffffff. That buys only a warmer top tone, not more shading (the bone/grey step shrinks slightly), so it should go to the owner as a look question, not land as a defect fix. Do not use the (1−L/255)² scaling or the L>200 threshold.

### monsters-20 [rejected] Monsters use one uniform ink outline (#181425) while the Knight's edges are dark shades of his own colours
- taste / owner - public/js/art/palette.js:68 (OUTLINE = PAL.k); public/js/engine/sprites.js:241-247 (spriteBuffers outlines every frame in that ink). Art lens.
- Evidence: art-extra/analyze.mjs: all 69 captured kinds use ink #181425 and no other outline colour. In lineup-x2.png, every row's Knight (self-coloured edges, no outline) stands next to monsters with a uniform near-black rim, which also makes the monsters look crisper-edged than the hero.
- This is a deliberate system, not a slip. The night lift, champion gold, lord rim and death debris all find the outline by its ink colour, and the uniform ink helps readability in a crowd. Still, judged by 'every sprite follows the plumed Knight', the horde is in a slightly different, more cartoon-outlined style than the heroes. Only the owner can decide whether that split is wanted.
- Fix: Owner decision. If wanted, add a build-time 'sel-out' pass in spriteBuffers:
- each ink pixel next to a body colour becomes that colour darkened about 55%, with a hue shift toward violet;
- the ink stays only where it borders transparency on the shadow side;
- keep a per-frame edge mask (b.edge), so the champion, lord-rim and night code stop keying on the ink colour.
Try it on the Darkwood 16 first and compare at 2.4x.
- Reproduce sceptic (not real, taste): One measured fact in the finding holds. spriteBuffers draws a 1-px #181425 ink outline on every monster, and verify/selout/edges.mjs shows it covers 100% of the silhouette edge on 68 of the 69 kinds (the pixie 95%). The comparison the finding builds on is wrong, though. The Knight has `outline: false` only because his sheet already has its own dark rim drawn in. It is not because his edges are self-coloured. Across all 347 Knight frames (verify/selout/knightall.mjs): - 71.6% of his silhouette-edge pixels are near-black (luminance under 30). - Their mean colour is rgb(21,4,23), a near-black violet that is darker than the monster ink rgb(24,20,37). - Only 16.8% are lit edges in his own colours: the sword, the shield's right rim, the top of the helmet. At 12x (verify/selout/side-x12.png) and 6x (verify/selout/side-x6.png) the Knight plainly reads as dark-outlined, with the same weight of line as the Death Knight and the others. So 'Knight (self-coloured edges, no outline)' and 'monsters look crisper-edged than the hero' are misreadings. The monster ink is itself a dark violet, not pure black, in the same hue family as the Knight's line. The only real difference is that monsters have no lit-side breaks in their outline. That is a small matter of taste, not a split into a 'cartoon-outlined' style. The 'Knight style' rule (plumed-knight-style memory) was also written for heroes; it was aimed at the old Ranger's pure-black outline. The owner already has a per-sprite ink override in the sprite tuner (art/tune.js t.ink → tuned.js). Better fix: No change is needed. The horde's outline already matches the Knight's dark violet rim in hue and is no darker; the Knight's is in fact a little darker. The proposed fix would move the horde away from the Knight, not toward him: - Darkening each ink pixel to about 55% of the body colour next to it would make most monster edges much lighter than his. A bone-white skeleton's edge would land near luminance 110, against the Knight's 4-20. - That costs readability in a crowd. - It also means reworking the champion, lord-rim and night-ring code (enemies.js:1125-1308, sprites.js:106/173) to use a new edge mask. If the owner wants closer parity anyway, the minimal version is a narrow selective-outline pass in spriteBuffers: - Recolour only the outline pixels on the top or left edge whose inside neighbour is light (luminance above about 120), at most about 15-20% of the edge, to roughly match the Knight's 17%. - Give them the darkest shade already in that body colour's ramp, not a computed 55% shade. - Keep #181425 everywhere else, so the code keyed on the ink still finds nearly all of the outline. - Alternatively, try a per-sprite ink in the existing sprite tuner first.
- Intent sceptic (not real, taste): This is deliberate, it is taste, and the premise does not hold up when measured. (1) The settled call covers the heroes only ("every hero follows the plumed Knight"). On 2026-10-27 the owner asked for a monster pixel pass (ddd10e9). It redrew 18 monsters on top of the engine's ink outline and left the outline alone. (2) The owner already has a lever for this and has not used it. The Sprite Tuner (public/js/art/tune.js) can swap the ink per sprite or for all monsters at once (`FS tune <name> ink=rrggbb`; combine() merges a group ink). The baked file public/js/art/tuned.js is still `TUNED = {}`. (3) Several systems depend on the uniform ink (enemies.js): - eliteOutlineBuf turns ink into gold for champions. - nightLift skips the ink, so a dark line stays between the body and the settled grey-blue night ring and halo. - rimBuf draws a boss's rim from the ink. - Death debris finds the outline the same way. boss-art-2026-11-15.md even builds on this, using "no literal OUTLINE colour" for the empty-rim fix. (4) The premise is weak when measured. I ran verify/selout/knight.mjs and heroedges.mjs. The Knight's own silhouette edge is near-black violet: #0b000f to #17031b, 67-88% of edge pixels below luma 30, mean luma 19-39. The ink #181425 is luma 23. So the Knight is rimmed about as darkly as the monsters, just in varied near-black violets. In the x6 zoom the difference cannot be seen. The "crisper" look comes from the monsters' lower detail, not from the ink colour. (5) I built a rough sel-out prototype (verify/selout-skeptic/zoom-x6.png). It makes the monsters look softer and less readable. The green goblin and zombie blend into the forest ground. The pale skeleton loses its edge on sand. In a crowd that is a loss, not a gain. It would also change the settled night look (body, then dark ink, then ring). Better fix: No change is needed. If the owner ever wants warmer monster edges, the smallest route needs no code: in the existing Sprite Tuner, set an ink colour for the group (all monsters) or for one sprite, e.g. `FS tune skeleton ink=2a1f3a`, then bake it with tools/bake-tune.mjs. b.ink already flows through eliteOutlineBuf, nightLift, rimBuf and the debris code, so champions, the night ring and lord rims keep working. The proposed build-time sel-out pass would mean adding a new edge mask and rewriting every ink-keyed path. It would also blur crowd separation and change the owner's settled night look. That is too much risk for a taste-only difference.

## bosses

The bosses are not pixel perfect yet, but they are close at the sprite level. Both critics, one looking at the art and one at the rendering, agree that most of the faults come from how the engine presents the art, not from the art itself. The technical audit passed all 759 boss and lord frames: Bone King floating, legged and encounter (3x29), Azgaroth 14, the six rigs 532, Ea-nasir 117 and crypt lords 9. It found no orphan pixels, no detached islands and no frames cut off at the canvas edge. The only partial alpha is the Bone King's hem wisps and 7 leftover pixels on the legged version. Feet anchors hold steady. The 57 px boss scale is consistent. The Butcher, Frost Giant, Thorn Queen and Pit Lord rigs have clear silhouettes, separate materials and hue-shifted shading. The arrival sequence is polished: stir, letterbox name card, bar slam, catch-up drain, heartbeat and bar break. The ring and disc telegraphs and the Bone King's rasterised crescent sweep warning are real pixel art. The headless captures match real Chrome. Two layering bugs matter most. Horde monsters behind a boss stamp their glowing eyes through his body (game.js:3355). Each boss's additive aura is painted over his own sprite (game.js:3376), which turns the Lich khaki-lime, the Bone King's navy khaki, the Pharaoh pale gold and the Frost Giant mint-cyan on the snow, and so undoes much of the 2026-11-15 colour pass. Both have small, local fixes. Next come HUD collisions: banners and hints land on the overhead bar, the bar vanishes whenever the boss speaks, and 'ENRAGED' spawns hidden under the bar. After those: hazard strips drawn as vector shapes off the pixel grid (line telegraphs, hedges, faults); volleys spawned stacked into one clump; themed telegraph colours forced to red; non-fire blasts that misread (dark and blood look alike, frost is invisible on snow); and one generic fire death, hidden under the horde. On the art side, Azgaroth falls clearly below the rest of the roster (a halved third-party sprite whose wings flip shape every idle frame and whose face can't be read). The Bone King's known walk and nova pops are still open, and the boss shots use near-black ink outlines. The owner needs to decide on three things: the six rigs' half-world-pixel density, which is exact only at 2560x1440 and loses detail at S=2; the missing night rim on six bosses; and whether red always means danger in telegraphs. Most fixes are small code changes. Azgaroth, the Bone King frames and the density question need art. Neither critic edited the repo. Their new evidence is under the scratchpad folders gfx-review/bosses/art-extra/ and gfx-review/bosses/tech-extra/. No finding depends on the other session's uncommitted hero or UI files.

### bosses-1 [confirmed] Horde monsters' glowing eyes are drawn on top of bosses and crypt lords
- bug / code - public/js/game/game.js:3355-3361 (the unlit pass runs `for (const o of list) o.drawEmissive` after the bosses (`tops`) are drawn at 3318-3323); Enemy.drawEmissive public/js/game/enemies.js:1012
- Evidence: [tech lens] C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/tech-extra/horde-behind-boss-x2.png: six foes hidden behind each boss. Red eye pairs and the cultist's yellow eyes float on the Butcher's chest, the Lich's robe and Azgaroth's body. tech-extra/horde-behind-butcher-passes.png and butcher-day-behind-x4.png: the same by day. The eyes go away only when the horde emissive pass is switched off. The base sprites are layered correctly (logged order: skeleton, goblin, butcher). In real play, tech-extra/forest5-boss-x5.png (crop of real-forest-5-low-hp.png, about x690-850, y390-530) shows red skeleton eye pairs across the Bone King's shield.
- Bosses are meant to stand over the whole horde (comment at game.js:3315). The horde's unlit layer runs later: glowing eyes, embers, and the lamp and crypt-door emissives from the same loop. So every monster standing behind a boss stamps its eyes through his body, and his sprite gets speckled with red pixel pairs that move with the crowd. The crypt lords and Ea-nasir, who are also in `tops`, get the same.
- Fix: Protect the bosses across that one loop. Make a second screen-size mask that holds only the tops (maskBosses on a cleared mask). Take a copy of their lit pixels with keepHorde, run the list emissive loop, put the copy back with putHorde, then draw the tops' own emissives. A cheaper alternative uses the same approach as drawNightEdge: draw the list emissives into the night canvas, run nightCut(n, e, p) for each top, then composite.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, bug): The finding is real. I confirmed it in the code and again in my own render. Code (C:/Users/I/Desktop/GIT/forest-survivor/public/js/game/game.js 3318-3361): the bosses and crypt lords (`tops`) are drawn at 3318-3323, after the whole horde. The unlit loop at 3355-3360 then calls o.drawEmissive(ctx, c) for every foe, minion, lamp and crypt in `list`, straight onto the screen with nothing masked. Enemy.drawEmissive (enemies.js 1012-1037) blits fr.em (the eye pixels) at full alpha wherever the foe stands, so anything under a boss prints its eyes through his body. The code base already intends otherwise: drawNightEdge (enemies.js 1148-1206) cuts every top's shape out of the horde's night layer (`for (const e of g.topList) cut = nightCut(...)`), and horde-over.js puts the bosses in front of the weapons' glows. Only the emissive pass is missing that cut. Pixels: my own script, C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/eyes-verify.mjs, renders the production Game.draw headless at 1600x900 density with six foes overlapping each boss, three behind and three in front by y. Output: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/eyes-verify-x2.png. - Red eye pairs float on the Butcher's chest and apron, on the Lich's robe, and on the Bone King's chest and shield by day in the desert. - Counting saturated red pixels inside the boss's own drawn shape: Butcher 131 in production against 49 (his own blood) with the horde emissive pass off; Lich 29 against 0; Bone King by day 48 against 0. - Eyes of foes standing outside the boss shape are unchanged. The critic's capture also uses production Game.draw (lib.mjs render), so it is not a capture artefact. There is a second leak in the same pass that the critic missed. Pickup emissives are drawn at game.js 3362, after the tops. Gems lying under a boss print crisp cyan and yellow gem shapes on his body: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/gems-under-boss-x2.png. This is common in play, because bosses walk over the horde's gem carpet. I rate it a bug: a visible draw-order error on the most prominent sprite of a boss fight, present whenever the crowd presses against the boss, which is most of the fight. Fix: both of the critic's options work. - Option 1 (second mask plus keep/put) is heavier. keepHorde reads the module-level maskC, so it needs a mask parameter and a second screen-size canvas, plus a full-screen copy each frame. It also has to avoid clobbering the `horde` mask that is reused at 3364-3368. - Option 2 (an off-screen layer with the tops' shapes cut out) is the minimal, sound one. I prototyped it and it removes every leak while leaving everything outside the bosses pixel-identical. The prop and crypt emissives are plain source-over drawImage, so routing them off-screen and compositing gives the same result. Neither option covers the gem emissive. Note that this touches only game.js and enemies.js, not the other session's uncommitted files. Better fix: Use option 2. Guard it so it costs nothing without a boss, and fold the pickups in. 1. In enemies.js, export a small helper next to nightCut. Something like `export function emissiveLayer(cam)`, which returns the existing night context (nightContext(cam)) cleared, with composite set to source-over. Pair it with `export function cutTops(n, tops, p)`, which runs nightCut for each top and resets the composite to source-over. The night canvas is free to reuse here, because drawNightEdge has already composited it by this point. 2. In game.js, around 3355-3362, add `const em = tops.length ? emissiveLayer(c) : ctx;` and send the list loop's drawEmissive, drawCryptEmissive and drawPropEmissive calls to `em`. Also move `for (const pk of this.pickups) pk.drawEmissive(em)` up before the tops, so gems lying under a boss are cut too. 3. Then add `if (em !== ctx) { cutTops(em, tops, p); ctx.drawImage(em.canvas, 0, 0); }`. Only after that run `for (const e of tops) e.drawEmissive(ctx, c)`, so the boss rims still go on top. With no boss on screen nothing changes, and there is no extra canvas work. With a boss on screen it costs one clear, one cut per top and one full-screen draw, and it reuses the same _img/_rect cut that the night edge already uses.
- Intent sceptic (real, bug): Confirmed in the code and in a render. game.js 3318-3323 draws the bosses, crypt lords and Ea-nasir (`tops`) after the y-sorted list. The comment at 3315 and commit 4d27286 say they stand over the horde. The unlit pass at 3355-3360 then runs `o.drawEmissive` for every list object without any occlusion. So eyes, embers, brand/curse marks, and lamp or crypt-door emissives of anything standing behind a boss are stamped on his body. This is an oversight, not a choice. The two other overlays in the same frame already respect the bosses: drawNightEdge cuts `g.topList` out of the night layer (enemies.js 1196), and maskBosses adds them to the weapons-under-horde mask. Only the emissive pass was missed. None of the owner's settled calls or earlier reviews (the reviews/ folder has nothing on emissives showing through) covers it. It shows in real play: red skeleton eye pairs on the Bone King's shield in forest5-boss-x5.png. My monkeypatch prototype (verify/eyes-fix.mjs, image C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/eyes-fix-x2.png) puts the horde emissives in an offscreen layer and cuts out each top's drawn shape. With it, every red and yellow pair is gone from the Butcher's chest and the Bone King's shield. The eyes of foes standing in front of or beside the boss still show. The fix touches draw code only. It has no Math.random and no gameplay change. game.js, enemies.js and horde-over.js are not among the other session's uncommitted files. Related but out of scope: the same pass also lets a foe's eyes show through a tree or a bigger foe standing in front of it. Better fix: Use the critic's cheaper alternative and gate it on tops.length, so frames with no boss on screen cost nothing. In horde-over.js, add a screen-size layer with a helper. It reuses screen() and the existing drawShape: `export function emissiveLayer(cam){ const l=(layerC=screen(layerC,cam)).getContext('2d'); l.globalCompositeOperation='source-over'; l.globalAlpha=1; l.imageSmoothingEnabled=false; l.clearRect(0,0,cam.pxW,cam.pxH); return l; }` and `export function underTops(ctx,l,cam,tops){ l.globalCompositeOperation='destination-out'; for(const e of tops) drawShape(l,e,cam); l.globalCompositeOperation='source-over'; l.globalAlpha=1; ctx.drawImage(l.canvas,0,0); }`. In game.js, write `const el = tops.length ? emissiveLayer(c) : ctx;`, make the list loop at 3355-3360 draw into `el` instead of `ctx`, then `if (el !== ctx) underTops(ctx, el, c, tops);`, then the existing `for (const e of tops) e.drawEmissive(ctx, c)`. All those emissive draws are source-over with globalAlpha, so drawing into a layer and compositing gives the same result as before everywhere off the bosses. drawShape cuts at the boss's own alpha, uses its exact _img/_rect (shake included) and skips a boss still rising. imageSmoothingEnabled=false matters: the em canvases are scaled by S, and smoothing would blur the eyes. This adds no second mask, no full-screen keep/put copy, and does not interfere with maskC/keepC, which the weapon-glow keepHorde at 3364 still needs.

### bosses-3 [confirmed] The 'ENRAGED' text appears exactly behind the lord's bar and speech bubble, so it is hidden
- bug / code - public/js/game/eanasir-boss.js:122 and public/js/game/enemies.js:809: `g.numbers.text(e.x, e.y - e.h - 8, 'ENRAGED', 'fire')`, the same height as the overhead anchor AT.y = e.y - e.h - 6 (hud.js:946)
- Evidence: [tech lens] C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/tech-extra/ea-enraged.png (field-eanasir-crypt-x2.png, 'enraged' panel x150-480, y1150-1270): only the bottom pixel row of the orange letters shows under the 'POOR QUALITY COPPER!' bubble. tech-extra/ea-walk.png, 1.15 s later: the word is still half under the boss bar. The text lives 1.6 s (numbers.js:67) and rises slowly.
- The world-space text is drawn under the HUD layer at the very spot where the bar or the bubble sits. The player misses the one cue that the lord has started firing three shots at once.
- Fix: Spawn it at mid-body (e.y - e.h * 0.5) or above the name (e.y - e.h - 28). Alternatively, flash the bar name to 'ENRAGED' in the HUD for about 1 s.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed this in the code and at the in-game display scale. Both call sites (eanasir-boss.js:122 and enemies.js:809) spawn the word with numbers.text at e.y - e.h - 8. That is a world-space number, drawn in game.draw (game.js:3387) and therefore under the HUD. The lord's bar is drawn opaque at HUD AT.y-6..AT.y, where AT.y = (e.y - e.h - 6) world (hud.js:780, 946). At the in-game zoom 0.8 the bar covers world head-6 to head-13.5, and the name label covers about head-17 to head-27. While a speech bubble is up, the bar gives way to the bubble, which covers about head-7 to head-23.5. The word starts at head-8 and rises only 22 world px/s. The cited capture used 'world' mode, with the HUD at world scale rather than the in-game 1.25x. Its bubble was also left over from the PQC step 1.6 s earlier. So I re-rendered at S=4, zoom 0.8 (verify/enraged-eanasir-bar-real1080.png, enraged-eanasir-speech-real1080.png, enraged-cryptlord-bar-real1080.png). The defect holds even without any speech. Over Ea-nasir's bar the word is fully behind the bar at 0.03 s, shows only a sliver at 0.2 s, is behind the bar or name at 0.4 s and is half under 'EA-NASIR' at 0.6 s. It first clears the name at about 0.75-0.8 s, and it starts fading at 1.04 s. With speech up, it is under the bubble until about 0.45 s. For the skeleton crypt lord (enemies.js path) it is behind the bar at spawn and shows below the bar from about 0.4 s. So the whole attention-grabbing part (the 1.75x pop, the white flash and the shine sweep over the first 0.37 s) plays behind the bar or bubble. The word then shows for about 0.8 s, mostly while fading. 'Hidden' overstates it a little, because the word does appear late above the name. The cue for the triple-shot phase is weakened rather than lost. That makes this a defect rather than a bug. Neither file is in the other session's uncommitted set (last commit 79c35c4). Better fix: Take the critic's second option, spawning the word above the name, but give it a little more room. Spawn at e.y - e.h - 34 in both eanasir-boss.js:122 and enemies.js:809, ideally as one shared constant next to CRYPT_LORD. I rendered both offsets (verify/enraged-offsets-real1080.png). At -28 the word's 1.75x pop overlaps the top of the 'EA-NASIR' label for about 0.1 s. At -34 it barely touches at the pop peak and is clear by 0.12 s. Both clear the speech bubble. Reject the mid-body option (e.y - e.h*0.5). The word rises 35 px over its life, so it climbs back into the bar and the name. For a 27-px-tall skeleton lord that happens at about 0.9 s, so the problem is only postponed. The same thing happens to the reaction words in reactions.js:72 (e.y - e.h - 6). When the target is g.boss or a lord with its bar up, they land behind the bar too, so the same raised offset could apply there.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed this in code and in a real-view render (S=3, zoom 0.8, Skeleton Archer lord, bar up, no speech): C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/enraged-shipped.png. Why it happens: - numbers.text spawns the word centred at e.y - e.h - 8. It rises 22 world px/s, lives 1.6 s and starts fading at about 1.04 s (numbers.js). - The overhead bar (hud.js drawBoss) is anchored at AT.y = e.y - e.h - 6 and the name label sits 11 HUD px above the bar. HUD px are 1.25x world px, so in world px the bar covers about -13.5..-6 and the name about -27..-16 above the head. - The HUD is drawn over the world, so the word sits behind the bar from +0.05 to about +0.3 s. Until about +0.9 s it is then mixed up with the name's letters, which are a similar orange. It comes out above the name only around +1.0 s, when it is already fading. - For Ea-nasir, the speech bubble (world about -23.5..-7) covers the same spot. Why it is not deliberate or settled: - The line arrived with the enrage mechanic (01812a7 for the lords, 7b84daa for Ea-nasir). There is no comment on where it is placed. - No review in reviews/ mentions it, and it touches none of the owner's settled calls. - It is a real gameplay cue (cooldown x0.75, 3-shot spread), not a matter of taste. The files involved (enemies.js, eanasir-boss.js, hud.js) are clean in git status, so this is not the other session's work in progress. I rate it a defect rather than a bug because the word does surface faintly in its fade-out tail. The proposed fixes are not quite right: - Mid-body (e.y - e.h*0.5): readable for about 0.6 s, then it rises back into the bar at about +0.75 s. It also covers the lord's bow-draw telegraph. Render: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/enraged-midbody.png - -28: still overlaps the top of the name label (about -27) while the word pops in at 1.75x. - HUD name flash: a bigger change, and still lost while Ea-nasir's speech hides the bar. Better fix: Change only the y argument at both call sites: g.numbers.text(e.x, e.y - e.h - 34, 'ENRAGED', 'fire') in public/js/game/eanasir-boss.js:122, and the same with this.x/this.y/this.h in public/js/game/enemies.js:809. - Add a one-line comment saying the word clears the overhead bar and its name (Hud.drawBoss) and Ea-nasir's speech bubble. - -34 world px puts the word just above the name label at z=0.8, including the 1.75x pop, and it rises clear from there. Render: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/enraged-above34.png - Safe for the seeded tests: Numbers.spawn makes the same rand/Math.random calls whatever the y, and text() does no onBody or nearHero checks, so the seeded runs are unchanged and gameplay does not change. - Optionally, share the offset as a small constant so the two call sites stay in step.

### bosses-4 [confirmed] Banners and hints collide with the overhead bar: slain, dawn, lord-wake and hint texts sit on the bar and name
- defect / code - public/js/game/hud.js:1301 (banner stack y = max(round(H*0.22), topLines+4), unaware of the bar); hud.js:777-811, 779 and 941-947 (overhead bar follows the boss's head); barFall label hud.js:911/935; public/js/game/game.js:1338 (lord's name banner, slamAt 0 so the bar arrives at the same moment), :1752-1753 (slain banner), :2683 ('Dawn breaks!' while burnBoss keeps the boss and bar alive); hintsHeld game.js:2294
- Evidence: [both lenses] chrome/bk-death-0.35s.png (real Chrome): the first-run 'A crypt' hint, its subline, the fading 'OSSARIAN' bar name and 'Ossarian is slain!' are stacked. real-forest-bar-states-x2.png last two panels and real-forest-7-death-explosion.png: the slain banner (y about 250) runs across the bar's name. field-dawn-burn-x2.png: 'Dawn breaks!' covers the GRIMGUT bar in all three frames. field-eanasir-crypt-x2.png t=0.60 s and field-crypt-lords-x2.png t=0.70 s: the lord's name banner covers his bar label. [tech] Measured at 1600x900 (H=300): banners take HUD y 66-95. The bar's y is (dy+124.5)*0.8-6 for a 57-px-tall boss, so they overlap whenever the boss's feet are between 43 world px above and 16 px below the hero, which is ordinary melee range.
- Banners are drawn at a fixed screen line. The overhead bar and the falling bar, which stays about 1.5 s after the kill, follow the boss's head near screen centre. Nothing keeps them apart, so the kill banner, a lord's wake banner, the dawn banner and first-run hints routinely print over the bar and its name. Hints are not held back during the boss-death moment.
- Fix: In drawBanners, check whether this.barPoint or this.fall.point falls inside the banner stack's height. If it does, start the stack just below the bar (barY + 10) or above the name. Skip barFall's label, or fade the bar out over 0.2 s, while a '<name> is slain!' banner is showing, since the banner already names him. Hide the bar at once when burnBoss starts. Add `|| this.momentRunning` to hintsHeld (game.js:2294), or hold hints for about 3 s after a boss or lord falls.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): The collision is real. I confirmed it in the code, in a real-Chrome capture and in my own renders at real 1600x900 geometry. Code: hud.js drawBanners (line 1301) starts the stack at max(round(H*0.22), topLines()+4), which is HUD y 66 at H=300. Nothing there looks at the boss bar. drawBoss puts the bar at the boss's head (AT.y-6), and the label sits 11 px above it. barFall freezes the bar at the point where the boss died, for 0.8+0.15+0.7 = 1.65 s. bossIntro gives a lord slamAt=0, so his name banner and his bar arrive together. hintsHeld (game.js:2293) has no momentRunning check, so a first-run hint keeps running through a boss's fall. checkLocks, by contrast, does wait for the moment. Pixels: - chrome/bk-death-0.35s.png, cropped and upscaled to verify/bk-death-035-crop-x2.png: the 'A crypt' subline cuts through 'OSSARIAN', and 'Ossarian is slain!' sits on top of the bar's frame. - verify/slain-vs-bar-real-alone.png: I set the stall clock out of the way so the slain banner is the only banner. With the Bone King 70 px to the side, the banner overlaps the falling bar's label or the bar itself whenever his feet are about 10 to 40 world px north of the hero. At dy 0 and +10 there is a small gap. - verify/slain-vs-bar-real.png: any second banner (here the merchant's 2-line banner) pushes 'is slain!' down to y 100. It then covers the bar at dy 0 and even at +20 south. Some of the cited evidence is weaker than claimed: - real-forest-7 and the last panels of real-forest-bar-states show the slain banner just above the name, not across it. - field-crypt-lords t=0.70 shows no overlap. - field-eanasir, field-crypt-lords and field-dawn are world-scale HUD captures (S=1, zoom 1), not the game's real geometry. The mechanism still holds in real geometry. The dawn banner has a sub-line and runs 66 to about 95, so a burning boss within roughly 43 px north to 16 px south of the hero overlaps it. hud.js and game.js are not among the other session's uncommitted files. The proposed fix is only partly right: - Adding '|| this.momentRunning' to hintsHeld is sound and minimal. hints.js:90 already uses hintsHeld to hold back new hints too. - Hiding the bar once burnBoss starts is sound. - Moving the banner stack to 'barY + 10' is not. With one or two banners it puts them over the hero's head (the hero's head is at about HUD y 131). The fixed banner line would also jump as the live bar moves across the threshold every frame. - Fading the bar, or cutting barFall short, undoes the deliberate BAR_FALL hold/flash/22-piece break. Better fix: Make the bar yield and keep the banners fixed: the bar already moves every frame, so moving it a little more adds no new jitter. 1) Add a small Hud helper that works out the drawn banner stack as {top, bottom}. Use the same y0 and the same per-banner steps as drawBanners (24 for a title alone, 34 with a sub-line), skip held hints, and return null when banners are hidden: under the levelup, chest, item or paused states, under g.card, or when no banner is showing. 2) In drawBoss, after y is computed: if the band exists and the bar's box (x..x+w, y-11..y+7) intersects the band horizontally, i.e. the bar overlaps the banners' left-right span, which is centred on W/2 at the width of the widest banner, and also vertically, set y = band.top - 9. The label then sits at band.top - 20, about HUD y 46, still below topLines()+16. In other words, the bar lifts above the banners instead of the banners moving. 3) Do the same clamp each frame in barFall on F.point.y. The 'is slain!' banner is added in update in the same frame the fall starts, from the last unclamped barPoint, so the clamp has to happen in barFall itself. 4) Add `|| this.momentRunning` to Game.hintsHeld (game.js:2293). 5) In drawBoss, skip a boss with burnT > 0 so the dawn bar does not sit under 'Dawn breaks!'. No barFall needed: set this.barE = null when you skip it. No change to banner placement, BAR_FALL or barFall's timing.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): Confirmed, and it is not deliberate. drawBanners (hud.js:1300) still starts the stack at max(H*0.22, topLines()+4) under the comment "a boss's own banner came down on its name". That comment describes the old layout, where the bar sat fixed at the top (bossBarY 43/48) and topLines() reserved room for it. Commit 0b4d66e (2026-11-13) moved the bar over the boss's head (drawBoss: y = AT.y-6, label at y-11), made topLines() a constant lineBottom(8), and left the banner rule as it was. Nothing keeps the two apart since then, so this is a side effect of that change, not an owner call. Seen at production geometry (1600x900, HUD x3, zoom 0.8): - In chrome/bk-death-0.35s.png, the 'A crypt' hint, its subline, the OSSARIAN label and 'Ossarian is slain!' are stacked on one another. - The earlier verify render verify/slain-vs-bar-real-alone.png shows the slain banner printed straight across the falling bar and its label whenever the boss falls 10-40 world px above the hero, and nearly touching at 0 to +10. That is ordinary melee range, at the climax of every boss kill. barFall holds about 1.65 s inside the 3.2 s slain banner. - On a survival win the slain banner and 'Dawn breaks!' stack on the same spot. - The crypt-lord crops (HUD at world scale) do not show the lord banner covering the label. That case follows from the geometry but this capture does not prove it. No earlier review settles this. Nothing here touches a settled call or the other session's files (hud.js, game.js and hints.js are clean). Hints have no Math.random calls, so nothing seeded changes. The proposed fix is not sound as written: 1. It reads this.barPoint, which is never cleared, so it stays stale after the bar stops drawing (boss off screen, speaking, gone). It must be gated on this.barE (set only on a frame that drew the bar) or on this.fall. 2. Its default, 'just below the bar (barY+10)', lays the banners over the boss's head and the hero. 3. Fading the bar out, or skipping it, while the slain banner shows removes the designed BAR_FALL beat (empty, then white, then 22 pieces). So does hiding the bar when burnBoss starts. Neither is needed once the stack avoids the bar. 4. Re-placing the stack every frame makes it jump when a walking lord's bar crosses the threshold. 5. Adding momentRunning to hintsHeld also blanks a hint that is already on screen and pauses it mid-beat. The narrower change is to gate only new hints, as checkLocks already does (game.js:2658 waits on momentRunning).
