Better fix: 1. hud.js drawBanners: before the loop, add up the visible stack height, hgt (sum of b.sub ? 34 : 24 over banners not skipped as held hints). Take the obstacle as P = this.fall ? this.fall.point : this.barE ? this.barPoint : null, with rows [P.y - 12 - GLYPH_H/2 .. P.y + 8] (label at y-11, bar to y+7). Optionally skip the move when the bar is horizontally clear of the widest banner. If the default stack [y0, y0+hgt] meets those rows, set y = P.y - 14 - hgt when that is >= topLines()+4 (above the name), otherwise y = P.y + 10 (below; the boss is dead in the slain case). Ease the stack's y with this.bannerY += (y - this.bannerY) * (1 - 0.8 ** (this.lastDt*60)), snapped when no banner is up, so a moving lord does not jerk it. 2. hints.js:90: add `|| g.momentRunning` to the update guard, so a first-run hint waits out the boss, lord or revive beat (as checkLocks does) without hiding one already on screen. 3. Keep barFall, its label and the bar during the dawn burn unchanged. Pure drawing and hint timing: no gameplay, no Math.random, no hero art.

### bosses-5 [confirmed] The boss bar disappears whenever the boss speaks, including during the Butcher's charge
- defect / code - public/js/game/hud.js:773 (`if (!b || b.speech || ...) return;`); drawSpeech hud.js:1102-1115 puts the bubble at the bar's spot (AT.y); speech lengths bosses.js:40 (2.2 s default) and bosses.js:272 (35% of Butcher/Frost Giant charges, 1.6 s)
- Evidence: [tech lens, also noted by art lens] vfx-telegraphs-A-x2.png row 3: no GRIMGUT bar during the whole 0.8 s charge wind-up while 'FRESH MEAT!' shows. real-hell-4-fight.png: no Azgaroth bar during 'THE SKY FALLS!'. field-azgaroth-attacks-x2.png ('I WILL BURN THIS WORLD!', 'YOU DARE?!') and field-butcher-attacks-x2.png ('NOW I AM ANGRY!'): the bar blinks off for 1.6-2.2 s on every line.
- The speech bubble takes the bar's place instead of sitting above it. So the most important HUD element vanishes and comes back several times per fight, often exactly as a dangerous attack is winding up.
- Fix: Always draw the bar. In drawSpeech, when e === g.boss and a bar is drawn, put the bubble's bottom above the bar name (y = this.barPoint.y - 13, about y-24 from the head) instead of at AT.y. The existing clamp to topLines()+14 still applies.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real. The code does what it says: hud.js:773 `if (!b || b.speech || !this.overHead(b, W, H) || !seenBy(g, b)) return;` skips the whole overhead bar while `b.speech` is set. drawSpeech (hud.js:1102-1115) puts the bubble at AT.y, the same spot over the head that the bar uses (bar y = AT.y-6). The check came in with 0b4d66e (2026-11-13, "simplify HUD"), the commit that moved the bar from the top edge to over the boss's head. No review or memory note records it as the owner's call. The only test touching it (test/hud.test.js:295-329) checks that the bubble stays below the clock, not that the bar hides. How often it fires: speech runs for 1.6-2.2 s of real time (game.js:2983). Triggers are 35% of Butcher/Frost Giant charges (bosses.js:272, set as the 0.55-0.8 s line telegraph starts, so the bar is gone for the whole wind-up), 50% of Azgaroth's meteor casts (:370), 60%/50% of summons (:198, :324), every enrage or phase line (:222, :345), the Thorn Queen's 'MY THORNS...!' and Ea-nasir's 'POOR QUALITY COPPER!'. What I saw in the images: - vfx-telegraphs-A-x2.png: Bone King rows keep their OSSARIAN bar. The Butcher charge row shows 'FRESH MEAT!' and no bar in all 4 frames. The Azgaroth meteor row shows 'BURN!' and no bar. - real-hell-4-fight.png and real-hell-5-low-hp.png (real Chrome, 1600x900): no Azgaroth bar during 'THE SKY FALLS!' or 'I WILL BURN THIS WORLD!'. The low-HP shot, meant to show the white heartbeat pulse, has no bar at all. - real-hell-bar-states-x2.png: every panel with the boss in view has a bubble and no bar. My own render (verify/speech-bar-x1.png, shipped hud.js): Butcher at 60% hp saying 'NOW I AM ANGRY!' has no bar or name. One more effect the critic missed: while the boss speaks, drawBoss returns after `this.barE = null`. So if the boss dies mid-line (likely right after Azgaroth's 25% 'I WILL BURN THIS WORLD!'), `was` is null on the next frame and the bar's empty-hold, white flash and break-apart (barFall) never play. The 'fallen' panels of real-hell-bar-states-x2.png show no bar, which fits. hud.js is not among the other session's uncommitted files. The fix is sound and minimal. I monkeypatched it in verify/speech-bar.mjs and rendered the result to verify/speech-bar-x1.png: - Normal case: the bubble sits cleanly above the name, with the bar below. - Head near the top of the screen (bar held at topLines()+16): the existing top+14 limit puts the bubble over the boss's name for the length of the line. The bar stays visible and the tail touches the bar frame by about 1 px. That is acceptable and matches how elites' names give way to speech. Two implementation details: test 'a bar was drawn this frame' with `this.barE === e`, not `e === g.boss`, because barPoint goes stale when the bar is not drawn (off screen, not seen, during the fall). And drawSpeech already runs after drawBoss in the same frame, so barPoint is current. Better fix: Same approach, made concrete. In drawBoss (hud.js:773), drop `b.speech ||` so the bar, its slam-in, its low-HP pulse and its fall bookkeeping (barE/barId) all keep running while the boss talks. In drawSpeech, inside the loop, after overHead(e) sets AT, use `const base = e === this.barE ? this.barPoint.y - 13 : AT.y;` and then `y = Math.round(Math.max(base, top + 14))`. This keys on barE, which is set only when the bar was really drawn this frame, rather than on g.boss/barPoint, which can be stale. Keep x from AT.x so the tail still points at the head. Known trade-off, no further code needed: when the bar is held at the screen top, the bubble covers the boss's name (not the bar) for the 1.6-2.2 s line. Optionally update the drawSpeech comment ('below its own name' becomes 'above its own bar and name').
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): Confirmed in code and in the images. hud.js:773 returns before drawing the bar whenever b.speech is set. say() (bosses.js:40) holds a line for 2.2 s, or 1.6 s for the Butcher/Frost Giant charge line (35% of charges, bosses.js:272) and Azgaroth's meteor line (50%, bosses.js:370). Enrage and phase lines (bosses.js:222, 345) always speak, so the bar is gone right as the fight escalates. vfx-telegraphs-A-x2.png row 3 has no GRIMGUT bar through the whole charge wind-up, and real-hell-4-fight.png has no Azgaroth bar under 'THE SKY FALLS!'. Nothing marks this as an owner call. The skip came in 0b4d66e (2026-11-13, "simplify HUD"), when the bar moved from the top of the screen to over the boss's head. It was a quick way to avoid a collision, because the bubble is drawn at AT.y, exactly where the bar now sits. drawSpeech's own comment still states the intended contract: "below its own name at the top, not on it", meaning speech must not cover the boss's name or bar. That comment went stale with the move. No test asserts the bar hides (hud.test.js:295 checks only the bubble's order and that it stays below the clock), and no earlier review mentions speech or bubbles. There is a second effect. drawBoss sets this.barE = null on speaking frames, so a boss that dies within 1.6-2.2 s of a line (for example after a phase or enrage line at low hp) skips the bar's break-apart animation (barFall). The bar just vanishes. hud.js and bosses.js are not in the other session's uncommitted set. The fix is HUD-draw only, so it has no gameplay or Math.random impact. Why the critic's fix is not quite sound: with GLYPH_H = 9, topLines() = 18 and the bar's y is clamped to at least 34. The bubble clamp is max(y, top + 14) = 33. When barPoint.y is between 34 and 45 (boss head within about 50 HUD px of the top edge, common when a boss walks in from above), barPoint.y - 13 gets clamped back down. The opaque bubble then covers the top of the gold name and the name's bottom rows stick out under it. That is messier than today. The idea (always draw the bar, move the bubble off it) is right, but the placement needs to handle that band. Better fix: Always draw the bar. While the boss speaks, the bubble takes the name's place (the name was already shown on the arrival card and the bubble's tail points at the bar), so the stack stays as short as today and never overlaps anything near the top edge. hud.js:773, drop `b.speech ||`:   if (!b || !this.overHead(b, W, H) || !seenBy(g, b)) return; hud.js:810-811, skip the label only while speaking:   if (!b.speech) { const hw = ...; drawText(ctx, label, ...); } drawSpeech, hud.js:1107:   const y0 = e === this.barE ? this.barPoint.y - 2 : AT.y;   ... y = Math.round(Math.max(y0, top + 14)); The bubble's rows are barY-16..barY-3, just clear of the bar frame's top row (barY-2). Because barY >= topLines()+16, the clamp can only move it by 1 px (tail touching the frame), never onto the name. Also update drawSpeech's stale comment. If the owner wants the name kept, a hybrid works: use barPoint.y - 13 (above the name) when that is >= top + 14, and otherwise fall back to the name-replacing placement above. barE and barPoint are current because drawBoss runs before drawSpeech in Hud.draw, and barE is null when the bar isn't drawn (before the slam, unseen), so AT.y stays the fallback.

### bosses-6 [confirmed] Boss volleys spawn every shot on the same point, so they start as a black-outlined clump
- defect / code - public/js/game/bosses.js:74-87 (shoot/ring/fan, where `out` defaults to 0); calls without `out`: 137 (Bone King sweep, 5 bones), 210 (volley fan), 302-303 (Lich/Thorn Queen 12-16-bolt ring and homing fan), 359 (Azgaroth fan), 383 (Azgaroth nova ring), and likewise the Pharaoh/Pit Lord fans. Only the Bone King nova ring at 190 passes `{ out: 19/25 }`
- Evidence: [both lenses] field-lich-attacks-x2.png t=0.25 s: 12 skulls form one black-and-white knot at the staff. At t=1.85 s the 16-ring and fan make a black wreath over the Lich. field-thornqueen-attacks-x2.png t=0.25 s: a black spiky clump inside a pink flash. closeup-qhd-telegraphs-fx.png 'sweep release': the 5-bone fan is one white blob at his chest. field-azgaroth-attacks-x2.png t=0.25 s: the 5 firebolts read as a single fireball. At 58-115 px/s with 0.2-0.5 rad spacing, the shots need about 0.1-0.3 s to separate.
- Overlapping copies of the same sprite merge into noise, often over the boss's face. The player cannot read how many shots there are or where they are going at the moment that matters most. The Bone King's nova, which already passes out, reads far better.
- Fix: Pass `out` in these calls, as line 190 already does: about 10-14 px times e.scale for fans (about 20 along the aim for the sweep) and 14-25 for rings. For the sweep, you could instead spawn the bones along the sword arc using SWEEP points 0-9 from boneking-sweep.js. Alternative: keep the origins and give EnemyShot.draw a 0.08 s scale-in from 0.5 with a fade. `out` shifts the first-hit timing by a few px and changes no Math.random call.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The finding is real, and the capture shows what the game does. In public/js/game/bosses.js:74-87, shoot() puts every shot at (e.x, e.y-10*e.scale) unless opts.out is set. Only the Bone King's nova ring passes out (line 190). That out came from commit db3987d, whose comment says it is there "so the ring never stacks on one point". These calls pass no out: line 137 (sweep fan), line 210 (the phase-2 fan used by the Pharaoh and Pit Lord), lines 302-303 (the Lich and Thorn Queen ring and homing fan), line 359 (Azgaroth's fan) and line 383 (Azgaroth's nova ring). The capture script triggers the attacks through production code and grabs the frame about 0.05 s after the spawn, so nothing about the clump comes from the capture. I re-rendered frame by frame at a real 1600x900 window (verify/verify-lich-frames.png and verify/verify-bk-az-frames.png). For the Lich's 12-ring, frames +1 to +4 show a black knot inside a white glow at his chest. By +8 to +12 frames it is a dense black wreath, and the skulls only read as separate at about 0.3 s. The phase-2 16-ring takes about 0.4 s. The Thorn Queen shows the same black spiky clump inside a pink flash (verify/thornqueen-row1.png). Azgaroth's 5-fan looks like one fireball until about 0.2 s. With out=20/25 simulated, the skulls form a readable ring from the first frame. The geometry agrees: skull sprites are about 11 world px wide, so a ring of 12 needs a radius of about 21 px and a ring of 16 about 28 px before neighbours stop overlapping. At 58 px/s that is 0.36-0.48 s. One part is overstated: the clump is not there "at the moment that matters most". The rings are long separated by the time they reach a hero 85 px or more away. The overlap is a short blemish of 0.2-0.4 s at the boss, repeated on the Lich's main attack every 2-2.7 s. That makes it polish, not a readability defect. The Bone King's sweep bones are also mostly hidden by the slash flash, and a hero 45 px away is hit within about 0.2 s anyway. Better fix: Keep the idea for the rings and fix the numbers. Space the shots about 10 px apart, as line 190 does, which means out of about 1.75 px per shot. Line 302: ring(..., e.data.off, { out: e.phase ? 28 : 21 }) for the Lich and Thorn Queen. Line 383: { out: 28 } for Azgaroth's 16-ring. The suggested 14 px is too small: for 12-16 skulls it leaves neighbours 5-7 px apart, which still overlaps. Line 303, the homing fan, can take { homing: 0.9, life: 5, out: 14 }. Note that this brings the Lich's ring to the hero 0.36-0.48 s sooner at 58 px/s, which is a small balance change and not just "a few px". The fans gain little from 10-14 px. The Bone King's and Azgaroth's e.scale is 1, so neighbours 10-14 px out are only 2-3 px apart at 0.2 rad. The fan stays a clump and only moves off the face. If you want that, use a flat out of about 20 at lines 210 and 359. Do not multiply it by e.scale, which is about 2 for the Pharaoh and Pit Lord and would give about 40 px. Leave the Bone King's sweep fan (line 137) as it is. In my simulation, an out of about 20-28 along the aim put the bones straight into a hero standing in sweep range: all 5 had hit within 4 frames. Spawning them on the blade has the same problem. Drop the 0.08 s scale-in alternative: the overlap lasts 0.2-0.4 s, so it would only shrink the knot for 5 frames. No Math.random calls change, since out is applied after the existing rand(TAU).
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The finding is real. In public/js/game/bosses.js:74-87, shoot() starts every shot at the chest unless `opts.out` is passed. Only the Bone King's nova ring at line 190 passes it. That came from the 2026-10-16 review, which logged bones "all spawning on one point" as a confirmed bug (db3987d). The same stacking is still there in the Lich and Thorn Queen 12/16 ring and homing fan (302-303), the kit volley fan used by the Pharaoh and Pit Lord (210), Azgaroth's fan (359) and nova ring (383), and the Bone King's sweep fan (137). The sweep fan arrived later in 8cd0005 without `out`. No owner call or earlier review settles this, and I found no comment or test that says the clump is wanted. test/bosses.test.js:206-212 pins the skulls to the chest, but only to check that the origin height scales with the Lich's size. Frame-by-frame checks at a real 1600x900 window show the problem clearly (verify/verify-lich-frames.png, verify-bk-az-frames.png): - The Lich's 12-ring is a black-outlined knot over his body from +1f to +12f, and still a tangled wreath at 0.40 s. He fires it every 2-2.7 s. - Azgaroth's 5-fan reads as one fireball, then as a single bar, until about 0.4 s. I rate it polish, not defect. The knot lasts 0.2-0.4 s and blooms outward in motion, like a normal bullet-hell ring, and where the volley is going is never in doubt. The critic's claim that the shot count "can't be read at the moment that matters" is overstated: the shots have separated long before they reach a hero 85-160 px away. The main fix is not gameplay-neutral. `out` moves the spawn point, not only the drawing: 1. The Lich ring flies at 58 px/s, so `out` of 19-25 px brings it to the hero 0.33-0.43 s sooner. That is about 15-25% less dodge time on his main attack. The fans at 105-115 px/s with `out` of 20-29 px (×e.scale) arrive 0.2-0.27 s sooner. The critic calls this "a few px". 2. With the hero in sword range, a sweep fan with out=28 lands the bones on him at once: all five are gone by +4f instead of travelling (verify-bk-az-frames.png row 2). 3. A hero closer than `out` to the Lich's chest is skipped by his ring. Unlike the Bone King's nova, no blast covers that zone. 4. It breaks the exact chest-position assertion in test/bosses.test.js:211. 5. In seeded replays it changes when shots hit and die, which moves the dice in particles.burst during update. The critic's alternative, a 0.08 s scale-in, is too short: by then the ring has spread only about 5 px. I tested a longer draw-only bloom: shots drawn at size 0.4→1 and alpha 0.3→1 over 0.3 s (verify/volley-bloom.mjs → verify/verify-bloom-frames.png). It softens only the first ~0.15 s, removing the white disc of stacked glows and the harsh knot. The 0.2-0.4 s wreath stays the same, because the shots really do overlap. Neither file is in the other session's work in progress: bosses.js and projectiles.js are unmodified in git status. Better fix: Keep `out`, but pair it with a matching hold, so arrival times and range stay what they are now. In shoot() (bosses.js:76), when `out` is set, pass `hold: out / speed` in the EnemyShot opts. In EnemyShot (projectiles.js:448 onward), set `this.hold = opts.hold || 0`. At the top of update(), when hold > 0, do this and nothing else: subtract dt from hold and from life, still check touches(p), and skip the move and homing steps. The volley then appears as a ring or arc `out` px round the chest. It hangs there for out/speed s (0.2-0.4 s, which also works as a readable telegraph) and then flies. It reaches every point beyond `out` at exactly the same moment as today, over the same maximum range. The change adds no Math.random call. Values to pass: - out 19/25 (the Bone King's own values) on the Lich/Thorn Queen rings at 302. - About 12×e.scale on the fans at 210, 303 and 359. - Azgaroth's nova ring at 383 can take plain `out: 25` with no hold, exactly like the Bone King's nova precedent, since his r=84 blast already covers that zone. For the Bone King's sweep fan (137), don't use `out`. Spawn the five bones along the drawn sword arc (SWEEP points from boneking-sweep.js), so they leave the blade instead of his chest. Update test/bosses.test.js:211 to expect each skull 'out along its own line from his chest', as test/bone-shot.test.js already does for the Bone King's nova. If the owner wants no change at all in the update path, a draw-only bloom of about 0.3 s in EnemyShot.draw/drawGlow is the only safe option. It only softens the first ~0.15 s.

### bosses-9 [confirmed] Map-boss telegraph colours are thrown away: fills and outlines are always #ff0044, and the frost one turns muddy pink
- defect / code - public/js/game/effects.js:1222-1224 (the circle's growing fill is hard-coded '#ff0044'; only the faint base uses o.color); 1231-1236 (the line shape ignores o.color); 1248 and 1253 (drawThreat outline '#ff0044'); colours are passed at public/js/game/boss-mechanics.js:58 ('#d77643' sands) and :69 ('#2ce8f5' rime)
- Evidence: [both lenses] vfx-telegraphs-B-x2.png rows 1-2: the Pharaoh's sands at 1.00 s are three solid red discs, with orange only in the faint 0.18-0.38-alpha base. The Frost Giant's rime at 0.65-1.10 s: red over the cyan base mixes into a muddy pink-magenta disc. By 0.6 s both look the same as a meteor or nova warning. vfx-telegraphs-A-x2.png row 3: the Thorn Queen's briars and the Pit Lord's fault are the same red bars as the Butcher's charge line.
- The four map mechanics were written with their own colours, so the player could learn 'orange = quicksand, cyan = ice sheet' and tell terrain changes from damage. The renderer only half-applies them, so every hazard reads the same generic red. The tech lens files this for an owner decision on whether red always means danger.
- Fix: Grow the fill in o.color (or a darker shade of it). Give the briars '#3e8948' and the fault '#f77622', and honour o.color in the line branch. Pass o.color to ringSprite at line 1224. Keep the flashing #ff0044 outline in drawThreat as the shared danger cue, perhaps only where the stamp also deals damage (rime), and leave the sands, which only slow the hero, fully orange. Owner to confirm the red-means-danger rule.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed it in both the code and the pixels. In public/js/game/effects.js, Telegraph.drawGround uses o.color only for the faint base disc (line 1222, alpha 0.18 to 0.38). The growing fill at line 1224 is hard-coded to '#ff0044'. The line branch (1231-1236) ignores o.color completely, and drawThreat (1248, 1253) is always '#ff0044'. The colours are passed on purpose in boss-mechanics.js: line 58 gives '#d77643' for the sands and line 69 gives '#2ce8f5' for the rime. These came in the recent G3 commit 6a674bf, but the renderer they feed (84963a5) never used them for the fill. The briars and fault calls pass no colour at all. The capture script (vfx.mjs / runner.mjs) draws through the production Game.draw and the real boss AI, so the result is not a capture artefact. I sampled vfx-telegraphs-B-x2.png. The sands fill at 1.00 s is #f93450, plain red. The rime fill over the cyan base is #d0668d at 0.65 s and #de4477 / #e54f81 at 1.10 s, a muddy pink-magenta as described. Rows 3 and 4 show the briars and the fault as the same red bars as the Butcher's charge line. I also patched the drawing in memory only (the repo is untouched) and rendered two versions: verify/tele-variant-same-x2.png and verify/tele-variant-dark-x2.png. With the colour honoured, orange sands and cyan or deep-blue rime read clearly and differ from the kit's red warnings. A green fill for the briars is faint on the meadow's tan and blue-green ground. The red outline that is kept still carries that warning. The game has no colour-blind mode that depends on the red. This is a real defect: colours the author chose on purpose are thrown away, and every map hazard reads as a generic red blast. Better fix: The direction is right, with two refinements, both checked in verify/tele-variant-dark-x2.png. 1) Take the growing fill from the colour of the ground that will land there, a shade darker than the light hue. On light ground the plain hue loses contrast; the darker shade reads better and shows in advance what is coming. Add an optional o.fill: - line 1224: ringSprite(..., o.fill || o.color || '#ff0044', 99) - line branch: fillStyle = o.color || '#e43b44' for the base and o.fill || o.color || '#ff0044' for the growing bar In boss-mechanics.js pass: - sands: color '#d77643', fill '#733e39' (the quicksand patch's own brown) - rime: color '#2ce8f5', fill '#0099db' (the ice patch's own deep blue) - briars: color '#3e8948', fill '#265c42' (the hedge's own green) - fault: color '#f77622', fill '#be4a2f' (the fault's own ember) The kit telegraphs in bosses.js pass no colour, so they stay exactly red as now. 2) Keep the flashing '#ff0044' outline in drawThreat on all four, the sands included. Quicksand that slows the hero to a third during a boss fight is still a danger, and on the meadow the briars' green fill is faint, so the red edge is what makes that warning readable. Drop it on the sands only if the owner decides red means damage only.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): This is a real defect. Commit 6a674bf (21 Nov, G3) gave the sands telegraph colour '#d77643' (boss-mechanics.js:58) and the rime one '#2ce8f5' (:69). They are the only callers that set o.color. The Telegraph renderer, which has not changed since the first commit 84963a5, applies o.color only to the faint base disc at 0.18-0.38 alpha. The growing fill at effects.js:1224 is hard-coded '#ff0044' at up to 0.75 alpha, so the colour the author chose barely shows. Nothing in the code, the git log or the reviews makes the all-red fill deliberate. The 2026-10-27 review (D1d) went the other way: it asked for warnings in their own orange so different markers can be told apart. The 2026-11-07 design review settled only the layering (outline above glows, steady under soft flashes, fill kept on the ground), not the colour. No owner call says red is the only warning colour. I rendered it again from the production Game, patching drawGround only inside the scratch script: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/tele-color-skeptic.png (script: verify/tele-color-skeptic.mjs in the same folder). Shipped, the rime turns a muddy pink-magenta and the sands at 1.0 s are solid red discs, the same as a nova or meteor. With the fill in o.color the rime reads as a clean cyan ice warning on the snow. The sands, though, almost vanish on the desert sand at 0.6 s (orange on tan). A darker rust '#be4a2f' reads clearly and is still distinct from the red. Parts of the proposed fix go too far: - Giving the briars green '#3e8948' would put a green warning on the green meadow quilt, which is low contrast. The briars and the fault both deal damage, so red already fits them. - Dropping the red outline for the sands is unnecessary, and the shared flashing red outline is a good danger cue. Keep drawThreat as it is. The change is draw-only: no gameplay change, no Math.random, and no hero art touched. Neither file is in the other session's uncommitted set. Better fix: Smallest version: 1. effects.js:1224, circle branch: `ringSprite(Math.max(1, o.r * u), o.color || '#ff0044', 99)`. Telegraphs without o.color (every night boss's nova, slam, meteor and charge) stay exactly as they are. 2. boss-mechanics.js:58: give the sands '#be4a2f' (a rust from the palette) in place of '#d77643', so the fill reads on desert sand. Alternatively, add an optional o.fill used only for the growing fill and pass fill: '#be4a2f' for the sands. Keep the rime's '#2ce8f5', which reads well on snow. 3. Optionally, for consistency, the line branch at 1231-1236 could use `o.color || '#e43b44'` for the base and `o.color || '#ff0044'` for the growing bar. No line caller passes a colour today, so nothing visible changes. Leave the briars and the fault red, since both deal damage, and do not give the briars green, which would be poor contrast on the meadow. Keep the flashing '#ff0044' outline in drawThreat (lines 1248 and 1253) unchanged for every telegraph, including the sands, as the shared danger cue.

### bosses-10 [confirmed] Every boss and crypt lord dies in the same fire explosion and meteors, and the horde is drawn over it
- defect / code - public/js/game/game.js:1723-1726 (bossDefeated: explode(..., 60, 0, null, 'fire') plus 6 Meteor whatever the boss); lordDefeated also uses 'fire'; all effects draw at game.js:3311-3313 between keepHorde/putHorde (horde-over.js), so under the horde and under maskBosses
- Evidence: [both lenses] deaths-A-x2.png and deaths-B-x2.png, columns +0.35 s and +0.9 s: the same orange fireball, meteors and scorch for the Bone King (bones), Lich (ecto), Frost Giant on snow (ice) and Thorn Queen in the meadow (sap). Each boss's own death kind shows only in the pixel-shatter particles at +0.03 s. [art lens] real-forest-7-death-explosion.png: at +0.5 s only a gold ring and a small flash show through the skeletons around (800,450). The six meteors are hidden under the horde.
- Each boss already has a theme in def.death and def.blast (bones/blood/ecto/ash/sand/ice/sap), but the death ignores it, so a frozen giant or a fae queen explodes into hellfire. Because all effects are masked under the monsters, the live horde also covers the biggest moment of the night. The owner's 'monsters above weapons' rule is about the hero's weapons, not a boss's own death. The tech lens rated this as taste/owner; the art lens as a code defect.
- Fix: Choose the blast by theme: use `e.def.blast ?? 'fire'`, or a map such as {bones:'dark', blood:'blood', ecto:'soul', sand:'holy', ice:'frost', sap:'soul', ash:'fire'}. Keep fire and meteors only for Azgaroth and the Pit Lord. The others shed themed debris in their death colours (bone or ice shards, petals, sand puffs). This depends on the baked non-fire bursts in the blast finding. Keep the same number of rand() calls (6x2) so the seeded tests stay valid. Give boss-death effects (and boss blasts) an 'over' flag and draw them after putHorde, above the horde and under the hero.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): I confirmed both halves of the finding in the code and in the pixels, but two details are overstated. 1. Every boss and lord dies in the same fire. game.js:1723-1726 calls explode(..., 60, 0, null, 'fire') and adds 6 Meteors whichever boss dies. lordDefeated at game.js:1792 also calls explode(..., 40, ..., 'fire'). In deaths-A-x2.png and deaths-B-x2.png, the +0.35 s and +0.9 s columns show the same orange fireball, firebomb domes and scorch for the Bone King, Butcher, Lich, Frost Giant on snow, Thorn Queen in the meadow, Pharaoh, Ea-nasir and the Skeleton Archer lord. Only deathFx's pixel shatter and its death-kind burst are themed. git blame dates this code to the first commit (2026-10-15), when there were only four bosses, all forest or hell. The map bosses (116f331, 10-28) inherited it, and no review settled it as a choice. One correction: def.blast exists only on pharaoh ('holy'), frostgiant ('frost') and pitlord ('fire'). The other bosses have no blast field. 2. The horde is drawn over the death blast. Explosion.draw, the FxBurst entries it pushes and Meteor.draw all draw inside keepHorde/putHorde (game.js ~3307-3313), and their drawGlow does the same at ~3364-3368. Only particles, the white flash and the banner go over the horde. I re-rendered the scene myself: verify/death-horde.mjs, image verify/death-horde-x3.png, Bone King killed inside a loose ring of 26 skeletons and ghouls, world 1:1, effects counted with and without the horde. Monsters hide 36% of the fx pixels at +0.3 s, 25% at +0.9 s and 30% at +1.3 s. The skeletons sit clearly on top of the fireball and domes. What is overstated: in real-forest-7 (+0.5 s real, about 0.175 s of game time at timeScale 0.35), only the first meteor (delay 0.1) has landed. "The six meteors are hidden under the horde" is a timing misread of that frame, though the layering it describes is real. The real-forest scene is also a fresh run jumped to 4:30 with an unusually dense catch-up horde. Severity: polish, not defect. Nothing breaks, and the death still reads as a big moment (flash, slow motion, shatter, particles above the horde). Whether a boss's death should draw over the horde is a reading of the owner's "monsters above weapons" rule (horde-over.js: "no monster is under a weapon"), so that half is an owner call. Better fix: Theme the death but keep its exact call structure, so the seeded tests stay valid. Why the proposed fix falls short: (a) `e.def.blast ?? 'fire'` leaves the Bone King, Butcher, Lich, Thorn Queen and Azgaroth on fire, and gives the Pharaoh 'holy', which has no baked art: only a ring and sparks at R=60. (b) In the proposed map, bones→'dark' turns the gold Bone King pink and purple. (c) Every non-fire EXPLO style except frost (vfx_shatter) has no baked art, so the biggest moment would shrink. (d) "Keep 6x2 rand() calls" is not enough. With particles off in headless runs, the death also consumes Math.random through: - explode's sfx pitch rand; - the fire branch's addDecal('scorch'), which calls rand(6); a 'bones' decal consumes no roll; - each Meteor's falling-frame emit arguments (6 rand + pick, evaluated even when particles are off); - each Meteor's landing explode('fire'), which adds a sfx rand and a scorch rand. Better fix: - Add one helper, deathBlast(e, R, impacts), called from bossDefeated (6 impacts) and lordDefeated (0 impacts). - It keeps one explode and N Meteor-type impacts with the same rand(-30,30)/rand(-24,24) draws, the same falling-frame emit, the same landing explode, and one addDecal per blast. - Only the look changes, through a theme: ring and particle colours, the art, and the decal kind. Use a splat kind that consumes rand(6) (ice/sand/sap/blood/dark/ash), never 'bones'. - Read the theme from a per-boss field in data/enemies.js (e.g. `fall`), not from def.death. Suggested: boneking bone-white/gold, butcher blood, lich soul-green, thornqueen petal-magenta (her colour is #b55088), pharaoh sand/holy, frostgiant frost. Azgaroth and the Pit Lord keep fire and meteors. - Until themed baked bursts exist, use palette-swapped bakes of fx_explosion and fx_firebomb, not the art-less EXPLO styles. For the horde layering, ask the owner first. If he agrees, a lone `over` flag on the Explosion is not enough: - explode/Explosion must pass it on to the FxBurst they push and to each impact's landing explode; - Game.draw must draw flagged effects after putHorde in both passes (draw and drawGlow), still under drawHero.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The finding is accurate, and nothing marks it as deliberate. bossDefeated (game.js:1723-1726) and lordDefeated (1792) call explode(...,'fire') whatever the boss is, and bossDefeated also adds six firebomb Meteors. git log -S puts both lines in the initial commit 84963a5, back when every boss was a forest-night one. The map bosses added on 28 October (Neferkhet with blast 'holy', Hrimgar with 'frost', Sylvaine, Belzakor) got death and blast themes, but the death beat was never updated, so they inherited the hellfire. deaths-B-x2.png shows the Frost Giant on snow and the Thorn Queen on a fae ring going up in the same orange dome, meteors and black scorch as Azgaroth. No review in reviews/ covers it. The only nearby note is the 28 Oct Meteor comment ("the boss's dying shower ... only the dome"), which is about vfx art, not theme. The horde-over part is also accurate: every effect draws between keepHorde and putHorde (3310-3313), and real-forest-7 shows the dome hidden under the skeletons. I rate it polish rather than a defect. Nothing is broken, and the moment still lands through the white flash, slow motion, banner, shake, sound and the boss's own themed pixel shatter, which draws over the horde. The problem is a thematic mismatch on four of the eight bosses. The proposed fix is not sound as written, for three reasons. (1) The dice count is wrong. I measured it with verify/death-dice/dice.mjs. With particles on, as in a plain test Game, explode('fire', R 60) makes 450 Math.random calls, 'frost' makes 351, and holy/dark/blood/soul make 281 each. In headless mode the counts are 2 and 1, because only fire adds the scorch decal. The six meteors take 12 rolls in their constructors plus 1594 more in update and crash (82 headless). Swapping the style or dropping the meteors while keeping only "6x2" rolls would shift every later roll after a boss kill in the seeded tests. (2) Apart from frost (vfx_shatter), the non-fire EXPLO styles have no baked art. Switching to them would shrink the biggest kill of the night to a thin ring. (3) Drawing the boss death "over" the horde would cover the monsters around the hero for about 1.3 s of real time in slow motion. That works against the owner's 27 Oct monsters-over-effects readability rule (horde-over.js). The rule was stated about weapons, but its purpose is to keep the monsters visible. Better fix: Keep the update path identical: the same explode(..., 'fire') call and the same six Meteor constructions with the same arguments and delays, so every Math.random call stays where it is. Add a draw-only theme through opts: const look = DEATH_LOOK[e.def.death], with ash or undefined mapping to null so Azgaroth and Belzakor stay as they are. Pass { look } to explode and to Meteor (as opts.look, which Meteor already forwards when it calls explode on its crash). Inside Explosion, a look changes only what is drawn. Keep the same three bursts with the same counts, using the theme's palettes. Each particle takes one roll for its colour whatever the palette length. Take the ring, glow and light from an existing EXPLO entry: frost for ice, holy for sand, soul for ecto and sap, dark for bones, blood for blood. Use a palette-recoloured copy of fx_explosion and fx_firebomb, built once at cache time. Swap the 'scorch' decal for the theme's splat (ice, sand, sap, blood, dark or ash). Each of these rolls rand(6) exactly once, as scorch does. Never use 'bones', which takes no roll. The Meteor keeps its same emit call (7 rolls a frame) with the theme's colour, and its rock and glow are tinted. Apply the same to lordDefeated. Leave the layering under the horde as it is. The white flash and the themed shatter particles already draw over the horde. Lifting only the death's light or glow above the horde is a question for the owner, not part of this fix.

### bosses-11 [confirmed] Azgaroth is below the roster's standard: a halved third-party sprite that pops every idle frame and has no readable face
- defect / art - public/assets/art/bosses-polished/demonlord.js (96x80, 20 colours; 'the Gothicvania demon, halved' per public/js/art/bosses.js:220-222); idle/walk [0,1,2,3,4,5] at motionRate 3
- Evidence: [art lens] sheet-azgaroth-all-x3.png, idle/walk 0-5: the silhouette changes every frame. Frame 0 has the wings raised in a V, 1 flat, 2 long drooping blades, 3 the near wing folded in front of the body (a depth flip), 4-5 spread wide. art-extra/azg-qhd-x3.png (QHD crop): the body is a dark lumpy sack with grey smears, the wing a flat red flag, and the skull and face can't be read. art-extra/azg-real-x4.png (1600x900): the same, with the face lost under the crown. He has the fewest colours of the eight bosses (20, against 29-45).
- The final Abyss boss is the least finished sprite of the eight. Halving the source destroyed the wing membranes and the facial detail, and the frames don't form a smooth flap, so he jitters while idling. Next to the Pit Lord rig on the same map he reads as a smear.
- Fix: Art pass (owner to approve): redraw him natively at 1:1 world px in the Knight style. Give him a horned skull with emissive ember eyes, crimson and iron chest planes with hue-shifted shading, and a 6-frame flap keyed up, mid, down, mid, with finger bones in the membranes and the wings always behind the body. Aim for 30-40 colours, like the Bone King. Interim: take frame 3 out of the idle/walk list (e.g. [0,1,2,1,0,5]) to stop the depth flip.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The core claim holds. Some of the details are wrong. CONFIRMED: (1) He is a halved third-party sprite. tools/art/bosses.mjs:9-12 says so: ansimuz's Gothicvania demon, "shrunk to half". It was then painted over twice: in the 2026-11-15 boss pass and again by tools/boss-chunky/demonlord.mjs. (2) You cannot read a face. In verify/azg-head-x10.png the skull sits inside the ember crown in the same orange and yellow. The eyes are 1-px dark slits, and it reads as a burning crown, not a skull. The original (verify/source-demon-idle-x2.png) shows a clear cyan horned skull. (3) In game he reads as a dark sack. Of the eight bosses he has the lowest mean luma: 52, against 72-105. 63% of his opaque pixels have L<45 (verify/luma.mjs). On the Abyss at night (verify/azg-aura-frames-x3.png) he is a black blob under a flaming crown, next to the crisp Knight. Every other boss in closeup-qhd-bosses.png has a readable face. REFUTED OR WRONG: (a) "Pops every idle frame / the frames don't form a smooth flap / depth flip": this is a misread. Idle/walk 0-5 is ansimuz's own demon-idle sequence, frame for frame (verify/source-demon-idle-x2.png). Read in order, it is a coherent flap: - up V - mid - down - downstroke wrap (frame 3, the near wing sweeping in front of the body, exactly as in the source) - rise - mid It plays at 7.5 fps while he moves (anim 2.5 × motionRate 3), 8 fps while he sleeps or spawns (IDLE_FPS), and 1.9 fps while he stands still. That is a normal wingbeat, not a jitter. (b) The colour count is off. Azgaroth has 20 including the outline ink, and he is the fewest, but the others span 23-44, not 29-45: Pharaoh 23, Lich 29, Bone King 29, Frost Giant 40, floating Bone King 44. (c) Much of the in-game "grey smears / dark lumpy sack" look comes from somewhere else: the boss aura. Enemy.drawGlow (enemies.js:1100-1121) paints his orange #f77622 glow over the sprite at 0.18-0.38 alpha. That turns his navy body muddy brown-grey. With the aura off, the plates are navy again (azg-aura-frames-x3.png). Not settled before: the 2026-11-15 boss pass accepted a "crowned burning face" but deliberately kept the silhouettes and frames. The 10-27 exclusion covered only that pixel pass. FIX: not sound as given. - The interim [0,1,2,1,0,5] is worse than what ships. It repeats frame 1, adds a 0→5→0 flutter at the top of the stroke, and cuts the source's downstroke. - The full redraw's "wings always behind the body" overrides the source artist's flap design. The redraw is also the heaviest option, and it leaves the aura muddying in place. None of this touches the other session's in-progress files. Better fix: Leave idle/walk [0,1,2,3,4,5] alone; it is the source's authored flap. If the owner dislikes the wrap frame, use [0,1,2,4,5]. Never use [0,1,2,1,0,5]. Make targeted edits in tools/boss-chunky/demonlord.mjs instead, then rebuild with `node tools/boss-chunky/build.mjs --apply demonlord`: (1) Separate the skull from the crown. Paint the face plate in bone/ash, using the palette's own #c8d9f3/#9cafd2 or the source's cyan, under the ember horns. Give it two bright emissive eye pixels (#fee761, already in his glow list) in all 14 frames, so a face reads at 2.4-4 screen px per world px. (2) Lift the body's darkest navy/indigo ramp (#20182c, #281d36, #261526) one value step, so the steel plates and the cloth panel separate. Aim for a mean luma near the roster's 72+. (3) For the aura, raise it with the owner, because it touches every boss. In Enemy.drawGlow, draw the aura under the sprite, or as a ring, rather than additively over it. Or lower its alpha for dark-bodied bosses. Today it browns Azgaroth's body. A native 1:1 redraw in the Knight style stays an optional, owner-approved art pass after these. If it happens, it can keep ansimuz's wrap-forward downstroke.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The main point holds and nothing settled contradicts it. Azgaroth is the only one of the eight bosses still built on a third-party base: ansimuz's Gothicvania demon, mirrored and halved (tools/art/bosses.mjs:9-10, 73-96). He has 14 frames and 19 colours. The six reforged rigs have 84-94 frames and 24-41 colours, and the Bone King variants have 30-45. In the QHD capture (closeup-qhd-bosses.png) he reads as a dark sack with a red flag beside the Pit Lord on the same map. My head crop (verify/azg-head-f0-f3-f9-f13-x8.png) shows a face only about 4-5 px of ember under the horn crown. Why this is not already settled: neither earlier pass judged a redraw and turned it down. The 2026-11-15 boss-art pass (c2d2c99) and the 2026-11-17 world-grid repaint (05acef7) both limited their own scope to keeping every alpha pixel, silhouette and frame allocation ("throws if any opaque/transparent pixel changes"). Separately, the memory note says the owner protected only the Lich, Butcher and Bone King in the 10-27 pass; leaving Azgaroth out of it was the agent's own choice. The finding overstates the animation problem. Frames 0-5 form a coherent, symmetric flap from the source: - 0 is wings up; - 1 and 5 are mid; - 2 and 4 are down; - 3 is the bottom of the stroke, with the near wing swept forward, which is normal for a 3/4 view. My onion-skin check (verify/azg-idle-onion-x3.png) counted silhouette pixels that change between frames. The steps 2->3 and 3->4 are the smoothest in the cycle, at 581 and 596 px; every other step is 951-976 px. So frame 3 is not a "depth flip pop". Severity: this is a consistency and quality gap on the final boss, not a broken sprite. An independent review accepted it a week ago, so I rate it polish, not defect. The redraw needs the owner's approval. Why the fix as written is not sound: - **The interim [0,1,2,1,0,5] makes things worse.** It removes the bottom of the stroke and adds a 0->5->0 half-flap hitch. It also changes which frame's box is live at a given time, and body() and reach use those boxes for hits. - **"Redraw natively at 1:1 world px" is moot.** Per the tools/boss-chunky README he already renders at one world pixel per paint cell. - **A free redraw would change gameplay.** Enemy.body() (enemies.js ~899) builds the hit box from each frame's opaque bounding box, and reach comes from the frame extents. Changing the wing shapes, or keeping the wings always behind the body, would move where weapons strike him. Better fix: Drop the interim frame-list change and keep idle/walk [0..5]. If the owner approves an art pass, redraw Azgaroth (horned skull with ember eyes, iron and crimson planes in the Knight style, finger bones in the wing membranes, 30-40 colours). The redraw must keep the 14-pose order and pose map (volley/meteor/nova/summon), the body box [26,40], the anchor and the clocks. Also keep the hit geometry, in one of two ways: - keep each frame's opaque bounding box, or - follow the Bone King pattern: draw the new art as a separate sprite (e.g. demonlord_drawn), returned from Enemy.drawSprite, while SPR.demonlord stays the encounter geometry. This is a one-line extension of the existing boneking branch in enemies.js drawSprite. The source's flap cycle with frame 3 as the bottom of the stroke can stay as the timing reference. No Math.random is involved.

### bosses-12 [confirmed] Bone King animation pops (listed as known on 2026-11-15, still open)
- defect / art - public/assets/art/bosses-polished/boneking_floating.js (walk [7..14], nova [22..28], summon [15..18]); boneking_legs.js nova 26-28
- Evidence: [art lens; the tech lens deliberately did not re-raise these known items] sheet-boneking-floating-idle-walk-x3.png: walk 7-11 hold the sword diagonally and 12-14 horizontally, so the sword snaps 45 degrees twice per cycle (11->12 and 14->7). sheet-boneking-floating-poses-x3.png: #26 flashes the body and shield but not the sword, which then reads as a separate prop. In #27-28 the shield vanishes and the sword covers the face and crown, and the shield pops back on the return to idle. #27's cream loop is an unshaded one-colour blob. #17-18 raise an empty open hand. sheet-boneking-legs-poses-x3.png repeats the #26-28 problems.
- He is the default night boss of the Darkwood and the first boss most players meet, and the pops show in every walk cycle and every nova. The 2026-11-15 review recorded them as 'known work retained', not as settled.
- Fix: Redraw walk 12-14 with the diagonal sword (or move 7-11 onto the horizontal one), and add one in-between frame where the cycle loops. Include the sword in the #26 flash. Keep a lowered shield in #27-28 and move the sword off the face. Replace #27's loop with a 2-3-colour shaded smear arc, or drop it. Give the summon hand a necrotic glow, such as a sickly-green or bone spark.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed each claim in the production sprites (rendered from SPR, same as the game). Close-ups are in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/bk-pops/ (walk-seam-x5.png, nova-tail-x4.png, nova-27-28-x7.png, summon-hand-x5.png, orig-walk-x3.png, legs-nova-tail-x4.png). (1) Walk, the main defect. In boneking_floating, frames 7-11 hold the sword diagonally and 12-14 hold it horizontally. Walk is [7..14] with motionRate 3, and anim goes up by dt*3 while he moves (enemies.js:612, :894), so the cycle runs at about 9 frames per second real time (GAME_SPEED scales movement, not the clock). The roughly 30 px blade therefore snaps 45 degrees twice in every ~0.9 s cycle, the whole time he walks. The field capture shows it in game too (f12 horizontal at t=1.55, f7 diagonal at t=4.15). The snap comes from Foozle's source (the original boneking.js has the same 12-14). The LEGGED look does not have it: tools/boss-chunky/boneking-legs.mjs:112-121 copies frame 7's diagonal sword and forearm onto every travel frame. The floating builder, boneking-shield.mjs, never got that step, and its addShield even has a sideSword exception for frames 12-14. (2) #26 nova flash: the body and shield turn cream and the sword stays steel. Confirmed in both looks. polishTorso flashes only palette 2/17, and the shield uses shieldPaint(...,26). This frame lasts one 50 ms tick, and the dark blast drawn that same moment covers most of the body, so it barely shows. (3) #27-28: the sword blade covers the skull's centre and crown. The shield is not literally deleted. The grips table moves it from [18,37,-24] in #25 to [34,38,16] and [41,38,14], which tucks it behind the crossguard and arms. Only a sliver shows and it reads as a chest tabard, then the full kite shield jumps back on the left in walk/idle. That is a real pop, but it lasts only about 0.3 s after each nova (cast 1.15 s, and the pose runs to 1.35 s). (4) #27 loop: cream #f3e8cd (220 px) with an ice rim #e3f2fc (84 px) and the outline. That is two near-identical light tones with no value ramp, so it is effectively flat, as claimed. (5) Summon #17-18: an open bony hand is raised and nothing marks it as magic. The sword also disappears between #16 and #17, which the finding didn't mention. Frame 18 is held about 0.65 s. The legged sheet repeats (2)-(4). The 2026-11-15 review lists these under "Known work retained" (inherited, left for an animation review), not as settled, so raising them is legitimate. Severity: defect, mainly because of the walk snap, which is constant on the first boss most players meet. Items (2)-(5) are polish. The files involved (public/assets/art/bosses-polished/*, tools/boss-chunky/*) are not among the other session's uncommitted files. Why the proposed fix is not minimal: the extra in-between frame at the loop is unnecessary and costly. test/boss-animation-detail.test.js asserts that the floating and legged walk/poses arrays deepEqual the original boneking's, and the game takes the frame index from the original sprite's geometry (this.spr). Adding a frame would mean changing the original asset, its hitbox frames and the tests. Once 12-14 carry the diagonal sword, the 14->7 loop is seamless, as the legged look already shows. The "sickly green" hand glow brings in a hue that is not in his palette. Also, the assets are generated, so any fix belongs in the tools/boss-chunky builders, not in hand-edited base64 data. Better fix: Make every change in tools/boss-chunky and rebuild with tools/boss-chunky/build.mjs. Keep all frame counts, arrays and timings. (a) Walk: port the legged look's "carry" step (boneking-legs.mjs:112-121: copy frame 7's sword and forearm columns x>=45 onto travel frames 7-14, shifted by each frame's torso bob) into the floating path in boneking-shield.mjs, and drop the sideSword (12-14, px>=46) exception in addShield. The legged look shows this fits the hitbox and clearance tests. No in-between frame is needed. (b) #26: also map the sword's steel indices in frame 26 to the flash cream. Low priority, since the blast covers this 50 ms frame. (c) #27-28: change the two grips entries [34,38,16] and [41,38,14] so the shield stays on his left side, easing from #25's [18,37,-24] toward the walk grip [26,49,-5]. It then stays visible and doesn't jump back. Moving the sword off the face is optional: it is the source's sword-salute pose and would need source pixels redrawn. (d) #27 smear: repaint it with the steel ramp of the #20 sweep trail (2-3 values, dark trailing edge), or drop it. (e) Summon #17-18: put a 3-5 px spark in the raised palm in his own emissive glow colours (#ffd670 core, #cb6b38 edge, already in data.glow, so it glows at night like the nova orb), rather than a new green. Do (a) first; it is the only fix players will notice in every encounter.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): Partly real. The part worth fixing is the walk; the nova and summon parts are mostly hidden in play or were meant that way. What holds up: - The 2026-11-15 boss review lists these under "Known work retained". It calls them inherited limitations that need "an animation and opaque-geometry review", so nothing settled them. The only later commit to these assets, 8cd0005 (11-17), did not touch the floating walk. - The default floating look still snaps the sword 45 degrees twice per walk cycle: frames 7-11 hold it diagonally and 12-14 horizontally. The walk runs at anim 3 x motionRate 3 = 9 fps, so the cycle is about 0.9 s, and the King walks for most of the fight. - The snap comes from Foozle's CC0 walk (verify/bk-pops/orig-walk-x3.png). - The LEGGED look already fixes it. tools/boss-chunky/boneking-legs.mjs ("Carry the diagonal sword and its entire gripping forearm together") pastes frame 7's sword side into every travel frame, so legged walk 12-14 keep the diagonal sword (sheet-boneking-legs-idle-walk-x3.png). The default look is simply missing that step, and the two looks disagree. What is overstated, checked in game at 60 Hz (verify/bk-skeptic/ingame-nova-summon-{floating,legs}-x2.png): - #26 shows for one tick, completely under the Explosion, so the unflashed sword cannot be seen. - #27's cream loop shows for two ticks, mostly under the outgoing bone ring and blast glow. - In #28 the shield does not vanish. It shrinks to the chest under the crossguard (grips 27-28, widthScale 0.86 in boneking-shield.mjs). The sword over the face is visible for only about 0.2 s, among flying bones. - The summon's empty raised hand is intended. tools/art/bosses.mjs says "Raising the dead: a fist held up." With "THE DEAD OBEY ME!" and skeletons rising, it reads as a command gesture. The sword vanishing in 17-18 is a side effect, and a glow there is taste. Severity: polish, not defect. It is inherited source animation, nothing is broken, and the 11-15 reviewers accepted the art with these noted. None of the touched files are among the other session's uncommitted files. Better fix: The proposed fix is broader than it needs to be, and part of it is unsafe: - An in-between walk frame changes the walk array and its clock. A test pins those to the encounter sprite (test/boss-animation-detail.test.js, idle/walk/poses/poseFps/motionRate/body deepEqual), and the encounter sprite's per-frame boxes drive body(), the boss's hitbox. - Flashing the sword in #26 and reshading #27's loop change frames the player hardly sees. Smallest fix: 1. Reuse the legged generator's existing carry step in the floating build. In paintFloatingBoneKingShield in tools/boss-chunky/boneking-shield.mjs, for frames 7-14, copy rows 18-64 for x >= 45 from data.frames[7], shifted by that frame's torsoOffset/eyeOffset dy, exactly as boneking-legs.mjs does. 2. Pass the carried buffer to addShield as `original`, so the sword still draws over the shield's right edge. The sideSword exception for 12-14 then refers to the carried diagonal blade. 3. Rebuild with `node tools/boss-chunky/build.mjs --apply boneking_floating`. This touches no clocks, frame counts, encounter boxes or randomness. The diagonal drawing stays inside the 12-14 encounter boxes, as the legged look already shows by passing the fits-the-encounter-hitbox test. The walk then matches the idle (also diagonal) and the legged look. Optional, lower value: in floating and legged 27-28, keep the shield at frame 26's left grip instead of the chest grip, so it doesn't pop on the return to walk. Leave #26 and the #27 loop alone, since the blast covers them. If the owner wants the summon to read as magic, a 2-3 px bone-white or sickly-green spark baked into the raised fist on 17-18 is fine (static pixels, no Math.random), but that is taste.

### bosses-16 [confirmed] Legged Bone King idle frames 2-6 contain 7 stray semi-transparent pixels between the legs
- polish / art - public/assets/art/bosses-polished/boneking_legs.js, idle frames 2-6, built-frame region x46-57, y68-75 (colour #262b44 at alpha 174/179)
- Evidence: [tech lens] C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/tech-extra/bk-hem-alpha.png: the magenta-marked cells on 'boneking_legs #3' sit in the gap between the legs. alpha.mjs: frames 2-6 have 7 such pixels each, and frames 0-1 and 7-28 have none. The floating version's hem tails use the same colours and alpha on purpose (22 px per frame, the ghost wisps), so these look like leftovers from it.
- They show as a faint dark smudge between the legs that blinks on and off as the idle cycle runs. Because the alpha is partial, they also only partly cut the night layer and the horde mask beneath them.
- Fix: Clear those pixels in frames 2-6 of boneking_legs.js, or make them opaque leg shading.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The data matches the finding exactly, but the finding misdescribes how it looks. What I confirmed: idle is frames 0-6. With my own copy of the alpha census (verify/bk-alpha.mjs), the built SPR.boneking_legs has exactly 7 partly transparent pixels in each of frames 2-6. They are #262b44 at alpha 179, with one at alpha 174. They sit at x46-57, y68-75. Frames 0-1 and 7-28 have none. The cause is in the generator, tools/boss-chunky/boneking-legs.mjs. Its cloak-erase loop only clears pose pixels that equal frame 0's cloak colour, so hem-wisp pixels that animate in frames 2-6 survive. The legs are then laid in only where pixels[i] is empty (`if (!pixels[i] && legs[i])`), so these leftovers win over the leg paint. nightCut and the horde mask cut with the foe's own image using destination-out, so the partial alpha also cuts the night layer only partly. That claim holds too. Where the finding is wrong: the pixels are not in the empty gap between the legs. They replace pixels of the legs themselves. In frames 0-1 the same spots are opaque leg pixels: ink #20182c/#181425/#262b44, steel #5a6988, gilt #9e8c60. Rows 71-77 of f0 and f2 are identical apart from these pixels (verify/bk-cols.mjs, verify/bk-dump.mjs). So they do not show as a dark smudge. They show as a ~30% see-through hole in a dark leg pixel. Compare frame 2 as shipped with frame 2 forced opaque: verify/bk-legs-hem-x16.png and verify/bk-legs-idle-hem-x8.png. On Frozen's bright snow (the Bone King spawns there by day) the spots are faint pale grey-blue specks. On forest/meadow night ground they can't be told apart. The 'blink' against frames 0-1 is lost among the much larger fauld/torso bob change between those frames. It is also only seen with the non-default 'BONE KING: LEGGED' setting (boneKingLegs defaults to false). The floating sprite's wisps are intentional, as the finding says. Real, but minor polish. Better fix: Don't clear them. They stand in for leg pixels, so clearing would punch fully transparent holes in the leg outline, which is worse than the 30% holes now. Hand-patching boneking_legs.js is also not durable: the file is generated ('Rebuild: node tools/boss-chunky/build.mjs --apply boneking_legs') and a rebuild would bring the pixels back. Fix it in tools/boss-chunky/boneking-legs.mjs instead. Let the leg paint replace any partly transparent hem leftover when the legs are composited: `const a = palette[pixels[i]] >>> 24; if ((!pixels[i] || a < 255) && legs[i]) pixels[i] = legs[i];`, or clear pixels with alpha < 255 inside the erase window (x33-45, y53-64 before padding). Then rebuild boneking_legs only. Optionally look at the opaque #181425 leftovers at built (50-51,74) in frames 2-6, which close the leg gap in those frames and are probably more hem leftovers. A rebuilt frame should match frames 0-1 at these spots: opaque ink/steel/gilt.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The finding is correct, and the pixels are a build leftover, not a choice. The builder (tools/boss-chunky/boneking-legs.mjs) rebuilds the shipped file byte for byte, so I could trace where they come from. Its loop at line ~130 is meant to "Erase only the original trailing cloak", but it only clears a cell when the pose's source pixel equals frame 0's cloak pixel (`pose === base`). In idle frames 2-6 the old hem wisps sway (source indices 18/19, alpha 179/174), so 7 wisp cells per frame survive. They also block the leg paint, which only fills empty cells. The engine's outline pass counts the half-transparent cells as solid, so the built frames get 3 more opaque ink pixels around the ones in the open gap: 10 changed pixels per frame, not 7. Frames 0-1 and 7-28 are clean. No settled call or earlier review covers this: the legged look arrived in 8cd0005 on 11-17, after the 11-15 boss reviews, and reviews/ never mentions it. The fix only changes what is drawn. Hit geometry comes from SPR.boneking, there is no Math.random, and no hero is touched. The file is committed and is not one the other session is editing. Visibility is low: the Legged look is opt-in (the boneKingLegs setting defaults to false), and the pixels are dark, partly transparent and sit between dark legs. So it is polish, not a bug. The proposed fix is not sound as written. (1) boneking_legs.js is generated ("Rebuild: node tools/boss-chunky/build.mjs --apply boneking_legs"), so a hand edit is lost on the next rebuild. (2) Clearing the cells by hand leaves 5 see-through holes per frame. In a scratch build, 5-6 of the 7 cells became leg paint once the wisp was gone, so they lie inside the painted leg. Images: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/bk-legs-idle-fixes-x6.png and C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/bk-legs-idle-before-after-x6.png. Better fix: Fix the builder, not the baked file. In tools/boss-chunky/boneking-legs.mjs, in the cloak-erase loop, replace `if (base && (flash ? pose >= 20 : pose === base)) pixels[i] = 0;` with `const ghost = data.palette[pixels[i]] >>> 24; if ((base && (flash ? pose >= 20 : pose === base)) || (ghost && ghost < 255)) pixels[i] = 0;` then run `node tools/boss-chunky/build.mjs --apply boneking_legs`. I tested this in scratch (C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/legs-fixA.mjs). Compared with the shipped asset, only frames 2-6 change, 7 cells each: 5-6 become opaque leg paint and 1-2 become clear. Palette, metadata, per-frame opaque bounds and frames 0-1 and 7-28 stay identical, so the hit-box test and the build's bounds checks still hold. An optional wider variant, `pose === base || (index < 7 && pose)`, also clears 6-7 opaque leftover cells of the old black hem ink per idle frame, along the fauld's lower edge (x49-54, y66-67). It looked no better at 6x, so the ghost-only change is the smallest fix.

### bosses-17 [confirmed] Boss name card and banners scale their pixel text by fractions during the pop-in
- polish / code - public/js/game/hud.js:881 (card s2 = 3*pop, pop 1.6->1 over 0.2 s); hud.js:1307-1311 (banner s = 2*pop, pop 1.6->1 over the first 12% of its life, 0.38 s for a 3.2 s slain banner)
- Evidence: [tech lens] From the code: the name canvas is drawn at Math.round(width*s) with s passing through values like 4.8, 4.1, 3.4 and 3.2, then the UI canvas is enlarged again by S. During the pop each letter stroke comes out 1 or 2 HUD pixels wide, unevenly. The settled state is clean (s=3 and s=2, as in real-forest-2-namecard.png).
- A brief but visible mixel on the loudest text in a boss fight: OSSARIAN, AZGAROTH, '... is slain!'.
- Fix: Step the pop through whole-number scales, for example 5->4->3 for the card and 3->2 for banners, holding each for about 0.06 s. Or render the pop at the target scale and animate only its alpha and vertical position.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): I confirmed this in the code and in a render. Both draws go onto view.uctx (gamescreen.js:182-184 calls hud.draw on the W×H UI canvas). That canvas has smoothing off (view.js:67), and presentUI then enlarges it by a whole-number S. hud.js is not one of the files the other session has changed. Card (hud.js:881): s2 = big*pop. With the letterbox it runs 4.80, 4.39, 4.04, 3.76, 3.53, 3.36, 3.22, 3.13, 3.07, 3.03, then 3.00 at about t=0.167 s. Banners (hud.js:1310): s = 2*pop runs 3.20, 2.91, 2.68, 2.49, 2.33 ... 2.01, then 2.00 at about 0.33 s of a 3.2 s banner. I drew the production textCanvas with the production formulas and counted how wide each source column lands. In the card, columns come out 4 or 5 HUD px wide, then 3 or 4. In the banner they come out 3 or 4, then 2 or 3. At scale 3 and scale 2 every column is the same width. In the magnified crops (verify/pop/pop-crops-x6.png) the O's left stroke is wider than its right at s=3.36, and the 's' strokes are uneven at s=2.68. Once the pop ends the text is clean. Two small corrections to the critic's write-up: - Strokes are not "1 or 2 HUD px wide". Each stroke is one HUD pixel wider or narrower than its neighbours. - The pop runs on every banner (map name, floor names, shrines, hints, copper), not only boss ones. It is hard to see in play. Nearly all of the uneven frames fall inside the alpha fade-in. Card alpha reaches 1 at 0.12 s, so only about 3 frames at s 3.13 to 3.01 show at full opacity. Banner alpha reaches 1 exactly when its pop ends. So this is polish: brief shimmer on the boss name and the slain banner. The finding's fix of whole-number steps is sound and keeps the pop. Its other option, animating only alpha and position, removes the pop, which is a change of feel. The width cap Math.min(..., (W-20)/name.width) could also produce a fractional scale. The widest boss name, Neferkhet, is 56 px, so it only applies when W is below about 188, which does not happen in practice. Scripts and images: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/pop/ (pop.mjs, crop.mjs, card-pop-frames-screen.png, banner-pop-frames-screen.png, pop-crops-x6.png). Better fix: The smallest version keeps the existing ease and rounds the scale, so no new timings are needed. - hud.js:881: s2 = Math.max(1, Math.min(Math.round(big * pop), Math.floor((W - 20) / name.width))). The card steps 5 (one frame), 4 (until about 0.07 s), 3 with the letterbox, and 3 then 2 without it. Flooring the cap keeps any later long name whole as well. - hud.js:1310: const s = Math.round(2 * pop). Every banner shows at 3 for about the first 0.1 s, then 2. The y term c.height*(s-2)/2 is already passed through Math.round. If the one-frame 5 looks like a flicker, use Math.min(big + 1, Math.round(big * pop)) so the card steps 4 then 3.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The finding is correct. In hud.js:881 the card's scale s2 = big*pop runs from 4.8 down to 3. In hud.js:1310 the banner's scale s = 2*pop runs from 3.2 down to 2. Both are drawn with drawImage onto the UI canvas, which has image smoothing turned off (view.js:67), so the 1-bit pixel-font canvas from textCanvas gets nearest-neighbour resampling at non-integer factors. I rendered the card's 60 fps frames for OSSARIAN (bold, 47x11) at s=4.80/4.04/3.53/3.22/3.07/3.01/3.00 and saved them to C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/card-pop-frames.png. Between 3.5 and 3.07 the letter strokes and outline come out uneven (some pixel columns are doubled). Integer scales 5/4/3 stay clean. It isn't deliberate. The pop came in with 8e0220b ("Bosses arrive as events") and in the initial import, and no comment asks for fractional scaling. Elsewhere in the HUD, pixel text is drawn at whole scales: the death name at hud.js:1286 uses s = 2 or 1, and the banner sizing at :707 uses x2. No earlier review in reviews/ mentions it, and it doesn't touch any of the owner's settled calls. hud.js is not in the other session's uncommitted files. The effect is short. For the card it lasts about 0.2 s, and alpha is still ramping up for the first 0.12 s of that. For the 3.2 s slain banner it lasts about 0.38 s. It still shows on the biggest text in a boss fight, and the owner's question is literally whether it's pixel perfect, so this is a real polish item, not taste. The fix only touches drawing. It doesn't change gameplay, adds no Math.random call and doesn't affect any hero sprite. Better fix: The simplest form of the critic's stepping idea is to round the existing ease curve rather than adding new timing. In hud.js:881 use s2 = Math.min(Math.round(big * pop), (W - 20) / name.width), which steps 5, 4, 3 for a letterboxed card and 3, 2 otherwise. In hud.js:1310 use const s = Math.round(2 * pop), which steps 3, 2. The y-offset term (s - 2) on :1311 keeps working with whole s, so nothing else changes. Leave the (W-20)/name.width clamp alone: it only takes effect when a long name doesn't fit a narrow screen, and that is a separate, rarer case. If it should be fixed too, use Math.max(1, Math.floor((W - 20) / name.width)).

### bosses-2 [unchecked] Each boss's aura is drawn additively on top of his own sprite and washes out his palette
- bug / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:1100-1121 (Enemy.drawGlow: glow(def.color) at 0.28-0.38 alpha, h*2.6*S wide, centred on the body); called in the 'lighter' pass at public/js/game/game.js:3376, after putHorde at 3368 and after the bosses are drawn at 3319
- Evidence: [art lens] art-extra/boss-aura-on-off-x4.png (script art-extra/glow-test.mjs), same scene with the aura on and off. Mean RGB of a 28x36 box over each body, off -> on: Lich 83,62,78 -> 132,133,109 (violet robe goes khaki-olive). Bone King 76,77,83 -> 148,131,100 (navy shield goes khaki-grey). Frost Giant 121,107,105 -> 134,169,173 (brown boots and leather go mint-cyan and he blends into the snow). [tech lens] tech-extra/boss-aura-over-sprite-x2.png at 1600x900 density, luminance on vs off: Lich 85.0 vs 63.2 (+35%) with saturation 0.359 vs 0.406, Pharaoh 171.0 vs 145.5, Frost Giant 210.0 vs 192.5, Thorn Queen 163.2 vs 145.8. The Pharaoh's lapis stripes flatten to pale gold even by day on his own map (lineup-day-x2.png row 2). Also visible in field-lich-attacks-x2.png and in the Lich and Frost Giant tiles of closeup-qhd-bosses.png. Both captures are in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/.
- The aura was meant as a halo around the boss. It is drawn after the finished sprite with additive blending, so its brightest middle sits on his chest and face. That adds roughly 30-50 to every channel, flattens the contrast inside the sprite and shifts his colours toward the aura colour, by day as well as at night. It undoes the per-boss colour work of the 2026-11-15 pass, and on the Frostfell it removes the Frost Giant's warm accents so he melts into the white ground. This is a separate effect from the settled NIGHT_EDGE halo and ring.
- Fix: Simplest (tech): move the elite/boss `e.drawGlow` loop from 3376 to just before `if (horde) putHorde(ctx)` at 3368. maskBosses already adds the bosses to the kept mask, so their own pixels then go back over the aura, which stays as a halo around the silhouette. Also compute `horde` whenever a top is on screen (`armed || tops.length`), so the restore always runs during a boss fight. Alternative (art): mask the glow with the frame's alpha via 'destination-out' in an offscreen, or use a hollow glow sprite whose inner radius is about half the body width. Either way, lower the peak alpha to about 0.2. Quick check: e.drawGlow = () => {} brings back the authored colours.

### bosses-7 [unchecked] Line telegraphs, Thorn Queen hedges and Pit Lord faults are anti-aliased rotated rectangles off the pixel grid; quicksand and ice patches are squashed and not rounded
- defect / code - public/js/game/effects.js:1225-1237 and 1249-1256 (Telegraph 'line': ctx.rotate + fillRect); public/js/game/boss-mechanics.js:198-212 (Hedge.drawGround), 241-257 (Fault.drawGround), 139-145 (Patch: drawImage at unrounded sx - w/2 with height *S*0.85)
- Evidence: [both lenses] art-extra/fault-edge-x6.png and tech-extra/fault-edge-x6.png: the fault band's diagonal edges step one screen pixel at a time with blended colours, while the Pit Lord next to it steps 4 px, so it reads as a vector stripe on a pixel-art floor. tech-extra/hedge-edge-x6.png: the hedge is a smooth green bar whose 2x2 thorns and 3x3 roses are rotated into soft blobs. field-thornqueen-attacks-x2.png t=2.85/4.35 s: the hedge is a flat, see-through green ribbon with pink squares. field-pitlord-attacks-x2.png t=4.65 s: the 'burning crack' is a flat brown band with an orange centre line, like a road marking. vfx-telegraphs-B-x2.png rows 3-4: soft edges on the briar and fault telegraphs. tech-extra/sands-patch-x4.png: the quicksand's dither rows come out uneven from the 0.85 squash.
- canvas rotate plus fillRect is always anti-aliased, whatever imageSmoothingEnabled says, so these strips are the only off-grid art in the boss fights. Every other warning is real pixel art: ringSprite and discSprite, and BoneKingSweepWarning even rasterises its crescent onto the world grid (boneking-sweep-fx.js:7-66). The strips also have no silhouette: no bramble edge, no thorns, no cracked lips, no flicker.
- Fix: Reuse rasterize()/paintRows() from boneking-sweep-fx.js. Build each rotated rectangle as a 4-point polygon in world pixels and rasterise it into an offscreen at 1 px per world px. Hedge and Fault are static, so do it once at construction. Blit at S with nearest neighbour, as the miasma mist does (effects.js ~292). Hedge: an irregular thorny edge with stem lines 'v'/'E', thorn ticks 'T' and the odd rose 'P', all on whole grid cells. Fault: jagged charred lips 'm'/'h', a flickering ember core 'o'/'Y' and a few rising embers. Patch: bake an ellipse sprite (rx=r, ry=round(r*0.85)) like groundRing and draw it with Math.round at img*S.

### bosses-8 [unchecked] Boss blasts misread: dark and blood are the same pink-white flash, non-fire styles have no pixel art, and frost vanishes on snow
- defect / code - public/js/game/bosses.js:57-70 (blast() calls `new Explosion` directly; BLAST_RING has no dark/blood entries, so both fall back to pink '#f6757a'); boss-mechanics.js:71-72 the same; this skips effects.js explode() (203-225), so EXPLO_VFX (193-197, frost: vfx_shatter) never plays; effects.js:156-165 (EXPLO: only 'fire' has art; dark ring '#f6757a' and glow '#b55088'; frost ring '#ffffff', Ring '#c8f4ec'); effects.js:280-284 (white core flash on every blast)
- Evidence: [both lenses] vfx-boss-blasts-A-x2.png: the Bone King's 'dark' r64 and the Butcher's 'blood' r54 look the same at 0.05 and 0.15 s (white core, pink ring, pink sparks). Frost Giant 'frost' r54 on the Frostfell: a white bloom and white ring on white snow, gone by 0.15-0.3 s. vfx-boss-blasts-B-x2.png row 4: the rime stamp does the same. Compare the crisp fx_explosion in the fire rows. tech-extra/frost-blast-vs-explode-x2.png: sending the blast through explode() adds the shatter, but vfx_shatter is only about 20 px, too small for an r54-64 blast.
- Four of the five boss blast styles are just a magnified 64-px gradient glow plus a 2-px ring. The skeleton king's dark nova and the Butcher's blood slam read as the same pastel pink flash, and neither looks dark or bloody. By day on the Frostfell, the Frost Giant's slam and rime stamp land with almost no visible impact. The ice sheet left behind is the only clue.
- Fix: Route blast() through explode(g, x, y, r, 0, null, style) and add a large baked burst per style, sized to r (frost shards, dark bone shards, blood splash). Colours: dark ring '#68386c' with a '#3b2346' edge and a violet glow, with no white core (skip 280-284 for this style). Blood ring '#a22633', glow '#e43b44', dark red droplets, no white core. Frost ring '#0099db' with a '#124e89' 2-px edge, a cyan glow and darker shard particles, so it reads on snow. Add matching BLAST_RING entries for dark and blood.

### bosses-13 [rejected] Six detailed rigs are drawn at half-world-pixel density next to the 1:1 sprites; only 2560x1440 lands them on whole screen pixels
- polish / owner - public/assets/art/bosses-reforged/* (224x192 sources, hires 4.07-4.56, drawScale = scale/hires = 0.5); Enemy.init public/js/game/enemies.js:216-217; Game.syncCam public/js/game/game.js:2787 (cam.S = S*0.8); public/js/engine/view.js:24 (world zoom 0.8) and :58 (S = round(min(pxW,pxH)/300))
- Evidence: [both lenses] 10-14% of each rig's 2x2 cells are half-world-pixel detail. At 1600x900 (S=3, 2.4 screen px per world px) one rig source pixel is 1.2 screen px, so pixels render 1 or 2 px wide in a 4:1 pattern. At 1080p they are 1.6 px. Only at 2560x1440 (S=5) is each a clean 2 px (closeup-qhd-bosses.png). The Knight, Bone King and Azgaroth sit at 2-3 px. [tech] At S=2 (a 1280x720 window, or a 1366x768 laptop whose viewport is about 650 px tall), 0.8 px per source px makes nearest-neighbour drop 1 in 5 rows and columns: tech-extra/decim-lich-0.png and decim-butcher-0.png show the Lich's face and staff skull reshuffled and the cleaver and hood outlines broken. tech-extra/density-butcher.png shows the Butcher beside the Knight. [art] art-extra/pitlord-head-x4.png: no readable eyes or face under the horn arc. In the closeup-qhd-bosses.png Lich tile, the skull face is only a few pixels beside the crown.
- Mixed pixel density is the textbook 'not pixel perfect' fault. The Butcher, Lich, Pharaoh, Frost Giant, Thorn Queen and Pit Lord carry detail finer than the world pixel that every hero, monster, the Bone King and Azgaroth follow. They render with uneven pixel widths at common window sizes, lose detail at S=2, and lose their faces at real scale. The rigs were accepted on 15 November, but the density was never addressed.
- Fix: Owner decision. (a) Art, the pixel-perfect answer: re-quantise each rig frame to 1 source px = 1 world px with a 2x2 majority, palette-aware downsample, then hand-clean the 10-14% of mixed cells, faces first (eyes at least 1 world px, emissive). That puts every boss on the Knight's grid and cuts the about 87 MiB of decoded frame buffers to about 22 MiB. (b) Code, smaller: when cam.S*drawScale < 1, draw from a pre-downsampled half-size copy (majority filter) instead of letting nearest-neighbour drop rows. You could also snap the world zoom so S*zoom is an integer at common sizes.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The defect is real, but it is much narrower than the finding says, and part of its story is wrong. Confirmed in code: drawScale is exactly 0.5 for all six rigs (57/26 ÷ 4.3846, 57/25 ÷ 4.56, 57/28 ÷ 4.0714). Enemy.draw draws them with nearest-neighbour drawImage at round(w·cam.S·0.5), and cam.S = S·0.8, so a source pixel is 0.8, 1.2, 1.6 or 2.0 screen px at S=2, 3, 4, 5. What is wrong: the finding says the rigs carry paint detail finer than the world pixel and that density was never addressed. Commit 05acef7 (17 Nov, "Repaint boss sprites on the world pixel grid", tools/boss-chunky/world-grid.mjs) already paints one colour per 2x2 cell. test/boss-animation-detail.test.js enforces it. My count (verify/cells.mjs) over all 532 frames finds 0.0% of cells with mixed colour at the (0,0) phase in every rig. The critic's "10-14%" is exactly the cells with partial alpha: Butcher 12.6, Lich 9.7, Pharaoh 12.2, Frost Giant 12.4, Thorn Queen 14.1, Pit Lord 14.6.
