That partial alpha is deliberate: the README says "The original alpha within each cell carries the existing hit silhouette". So the off-grid detail sits only on the silhouette: - half-world-pixel ink outline segments - half-step stair edges - lone quarter-cell specks, such as the orange dots on the Lich's crown tips (verify/edge-lich-0-70-52.png, verify/edge-butcher-0-96-52.png) Inside the silhouette, every colour block is 2 or 3 screen px wide at S=3 and 3 or 4 at S=4, the same as the Knight's pixels. The Knight is just as uneven at every S except S=5. S=2 claim: confirmed for outlines only. The Butcher's cleaver loses its left outline for almost its whole height, while a cell-snapped copy keeps it (verify/s2-butcher-0-cleaver.png, verify/s2-butcher-0.png). Interior colour cells cannot vanish: nearest-neighbour at 0.8 drops one source row in five, never two next to each other. The Lich's face, crown and staff gem come out the same in the shipped and snapped versions (verify/s2-lich-0.png). So "face reshuffled" is the same 1-or-2 px irregularity the Knight has at cam.S 1.6. The Pit Lord's dark, unreadable face is an art-readability question; it is already on the grid, and re-quantising would not change it. The proposed fix is not minimal: - (a) redoes the colour quantisation that already ships, and "hand-clean faces, eyes ≥1 world px" is already true by construction. - (b) adds a runtime downsampled copy, which is more code than fixing the bake. - Snapping the zoom changes the field of view for everything. Better fix: This is the owner's call, because the README chose exact alpha on purpose. Fix it in the bake, not at runtime: 1. In tools/boss-chunky/world-grid.mjs, change the final loop of paintWorldGrid. Instead of `alpha = d[position] & 0xff000000`, write 0xff000000|rgb to all four source pixels of every cell with coverage ≥ 2, and drop cells with coverage 1. Across the rigs about 3-4% of cells have coverage 1, 4-7% have 2 and 3-5% have 3. Frame boxes move by at most 1 source px (0.5 world px). 2. Rebuild the six rigs with `node tools/boss-chunky/build.mjs --apply <id>`. 3. Extend the existing "world pixel grid" test so it also requires uniform alpha in each 2x2 cell. 4. Re-check the hitbox-containment and silhouette-height tests. 5. Update the README sentence about exact alpha. That removes every half-pixel outline, step and speck, and S=2 can no longer drop an outline. Optional second step: since every cell would then be uniform, pack the frames at 112x96 with hires halved (drawScale 1.0). The rigs then draw exactly like the Knight at every S, and decoded buffers drop from about 87 MiB to about 22 MiB. That memory risk is still listed as open in reviews/review-followups-2026-11-15.md. The Pit Lord's face readability is a separate art note and should not be bundled into this fix.
- Intent sceptic (not real, taste): The finding's core claim is out of date and mostly wrong. The owner already fixed the boss density in commit 05acef7 on 2026-11-17, "Repaint boss sprites on the world pixel grid", two days after the 15-November acceptance the critic cites. That commit ran tools/boss-chunky/world-grid.mjs paintWorldGrid, which is exactly the critic's fix (a): it gives every 2x2 source cell one colour by majority vote and adds an ink outline plus bevels. test/boss-animation-detail.test.js now fails on any 2x2 cell with mixed colour. I decoded all 532 frames on disk with verify/measure.mjs. Across the six rigs (butcher, lich, pharaoh, frostgiant, thornqueen, pitlord) there are 0 mixed-colour cells. The critic's "10-14%" is the cells where only 1-3 of the 4 source pixels are opaque. Every one of those sits on the outer silhouette: 55-68% are the ink outline and the rest are edge cells such as spikes and shafts (verify/partial-ink.mjs). Their alpha was kept on purpose. tools/boss-chunky/README.md says "Each detailed rig uses one world pixel per paint cell. The original alpha within each cell carries the existing hit silhouette." test/boss-polish-contract.test.js also asserts the alpha mask stays unchanged ("retains its complete alpha mask and hit bounds"). The claim that S=2 loses detail does not hold either. At 0.8 screen px per source px, nearest-neighbour drops at most 1 row or column in 5 and never two in a row, so no single-colour 2x2 cell can vanish. The critic's own decim-lich-0.png and decim-butcher-0.png show identical inside colour blocks at all four scales, including the eyes and the skull; only one-source-pixel outline steps change. A world pixel being 1 or 2 screen px wide at S=3 (2.4 px per world px) happens to every sprite, the Knight included, because of the 0.8 zoom, so it is not a boss problem. The Pit Lord's dark face under its horns is an art-readability question about the world-grid repaint the owner accepted on 11-17, not a density defect. What is really left is outline steps half a world pixel fine on the silhouette edges. That is a small polish matter at most, and it is held in place deliberately by the hit-mask contract. Better fix: Nothing is needed; the colour half of fix (a) shipped in 05acef7. Fix (b), a pre-downsampled copy, gains nothing, because nearest-neighbour at 0.8 already keeps every single-colour 2x2 cell. Snapping the world zoom to integers would change how much of the world the player sees, which affects gameplay. The memory cut also goes against the 11-15 follow-up's direction of "no resolution reduction". If the owner ever wants the silhouette edge on the grid too, the smallest change is one rule in tools/boss-chunky/world-grid.mjs paintWorldGrid: fill a cell completely when 2 or more of its pixels are opaque, otherwise clear it. Then rebuild with --apply and re-baseline tools/boss-polish/geometry-baseline.json. That is an owner call, because it changes the alpha and hit-mask contract that is currently locked by test.

### bosses-14 [rejected] Night rim lights only the ink-outlined bosses: the six rigs and Ea-nasir get none
- polish / owner - public/js/game/enemies.js:1029-1035 (boss/lord rim), 1089-1097 (drawRim skips when !fr.hasInk) and 1307-1312 (rimBuf recolours only `b.ink ?? OUTLINE` pixels); the rigs in public/assets/art/bosses-reforged/ and Ea-nasir contain no #181425
- Evidence: [both lenses] lineup-night-x2.png: the Bone King, Azgaroth and both crypt lords pulse a coloured rim. The Butcher, Lich, Pharaoh, Frost Giant, Thorn Queen, Pit Lord and Ea-nasir have none. [tech] edge.mjs and audit.mjs confirm that the six rigs' 532 frames contain no ink colour, while the Bone King and Azgaroth frames do.
- The rim exists so 'the night can't swallow' a boss (comment at enemies.js:1009-1010). Whether a boss gets it now depends on how his art was authored, not on a design choice, so two visual rules for 'a boss at night' sit side by side in the same roster. The memory fix in review-followups-2026-11-15 skipped the empty rim to save canvases, but never decided whether the rigs should have one.
- Fix: Owner decision. If rims are wanted, build the rim for frames without ink from the alpha silhouette: opaque pixels with a transparent 4-neighbour, 2 source px thick on the hires rigs (1 world px). Build it at world resolution (112x96, half-size) to keep the memory cost down, and cache it per frame as hasInk is. Otherwise, state that only the 1:1 bosses get rims.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): I confirmed this in the pixels, in the code and in a frame count. Pixels: a x4 crop of lineup-night-x2.png (verify/rim-skeptic/row1-bk-butcher-x4.png) shows the Bone King edged all round in a pulsing gold rim. The Butcher beside him has only his own dark edges. In row 3, Ea-nasir has no rim, while both skeleton crypt lords do. Code: drawEmissive (enemies.js:1029-1035) calls drawRim for every boss or lord. drawRim (1089-1097) draws only when fr.hasInk is true. rimBuf (1307-1312) recolours only pixels equal to b.ink ?? OUTLINE. Count (verify/rim-skeptic/ink.mjs, run against the real buildSprites): - No frame contains ink: butcher 0/88, lich 0/84, pharaoh 0/94, frostgiant 0/88, thornqueen 0/84, pitlord 0/94, eanasir 0/117. - Every frame contains ink: boneking 29/29, boneking_legs 29/29, demonlord 14/14, skeletonmage 6/6, skeletonarcher 3/3. The rigs are built with outline:false (bosses-reforged.js reforgedBossDef), and Ea-nasir's data has outline:false. The capture is faithful. lineup.mjs calls the real Game.draw, and it spawns Ea-nasir with lord:true as spawner.spawnLord does in a crypt. drawEmissive runs by day as well, so the split shows by day too, not only at night. The history makes this more than a neutral owner question. Before 6a7d8bb (2026-11-15), all bosses came from the baked bosses.js, which said "A baked boss: outlined (so his rim can burn in his colour at night)", with outline:true. The 2026-11-15 rigs lost the rim as a side effect. review-followups-2026-11-15 and boss-art-2026-11-15 then saw the missing ink and kept it out only to save memory. No one decided the look. Ea-nasir has been outline:false since his source art was added, so he never had a rim. Severity is polish, not defect. Every boss still gets its own white light (game.js ~3321), so the rigs stay readable at night. The cost is an inconsistent rule within one roster. Whether the detailed rigs should wear a coloured rim is the owner's aesthetic call, but the default should lean towards restoring it, since it was dropped by accident. The proposed fix's numbers hold. For all six rigs, drawScale = def.scale / hires = 0.5000 exactly, so 2 source px = 1 world px, and world resolution is 112x96. One caveat about the half-size build. The rig silhouettes do not lie on a fixed 2x2 grid: at the best offset, 9.7-14.5% of their opaque 2x2 blocks are partly transparent (verify/rim-skeptic/grid.mjs). A 112x96 rim would therefore spill up to 1 source px onto the ground in some places and leave gaps in others, giving a bumpy edge. Same cause, outside this finding: eliteOutlineBuf also swaps only ink. Ea-nasir, an elite lord, therefore gets no gold champion outline either, unlike the other crypt lords, and his 'eliteo' variant is a full copy of the frame with nothing changed. Better fix: Add one lazily cached helper on Frame, such as an `edge` mask. It holds the opaque pixels within k source px of a transparent 4-neighbour, with k = round(1 / drawScale): k = 2 for the six rigs and k = 1 for Ea-nasir. Use this mask whenever !hasInk, in both rimBuf and eliteOutlineBuf, so that one change brings back the boss rim and also gives Ea-nasir his champion gold outline. Compute the edge at source resolution so it follows the silhouette exactly. For storage, choose one of two options: (a) Downsample to 112x96 only with a conservative rule: a world px counts as rim only if all four of its source px are opaque and at least one is an edge pixel. This never spills onto the ground, and the memory cost is that of the critic's fix. (b) Cache nothing. Each draw, composite the rim into one shared scratch canvas: tint the frame's c/cf with source-in, then destination-out its erosion (the frame AND its copies shifted by k px in ±x and ±y). One boss is on screen at a time, so this costs about six drawImages per frame and no memory per frame. Keep the ink path unchanged for the 1:1 bosses. If the owner decides against rims on the rigs, record in the drawEmissive comment that only outlined (ink) bosses get rims, and give Ea-nasir's lord look an explicit choice either way.
- Intent sceptic (not real, taste): The observation is accurate, but the asymmetry is known and earlier passes kept it on purpose. The captures also show no quality loss, so this is a consistency preference. 1) What the code does. The six rigs are built with outline:false in public/js/art/bosses-reforged.js. Ea-nasir's frames are also outline:false. My ink.mjs audit in verify/rim-skeptic found ink in 0 of 88/84/94/88/84/94 rig frames and 0 of 117 Ea-nasir frames, against 29/29/14/6/3 frames with ink for the Bone King (both versions), Azgaroth and the two crypt lords. 2) Earlier passes recorded and kept this: - reviews/boss-art-2026-11-15.md says: "The six detailed bosses still contain no literal engine OUTLINE color, preserving the empty-rim allocation fix." - The later repaint (05acef7, 2026-11-17) explains it in tools/boss-chunky/README.md: "The eight main bosses use dark contours, hard color blocks ... The Bone King and Demon Lord already render at that resolution and retain their outer rim effects." So rims on only those two were a stated outcome of the last boss-art pass. - test/boss-rim-memory.test.js asserts that none of the 532 rig frames draws or allocates a rim, in either colour or facing. 3) No visible gain. In lineup-night-x2.png and field-pitlord-attacks-x2.png, the Butcher, Lich, Thorn Queen and Pit Lord all read clearly at night. Their painted dark contours, their own light and drawGlow keep the night from swallowing them. In field-eanasir-crypt-x2.png, the pale statue Ea-nasir stands out more than anything else in the crypt. A pulsing coloured rim would also cover the dark contours the 11-17 pass painted on purpose. What is actually wrong is two stale comments that still say every boss has a rim. One is in the night-edge block of enemies.js: "Bosses and crypt lords have a light and rim of their own and get none". The other is in drawEmissive: "a boss's outline burning in its colour, so the night can't swallow it". That is a documentation nit, not a graphics defect. On the fix: its numbers are right. All six rigs have drawScale = scale/hires = 0.5, so 2 source px = 1 world px and 112x96 is world resolution. It adds no Math.random calls and changes no gameplay. But it misses three things: - It must rewrite test/boss-rim-memory.test.js. - It adds per-frame canvases while the boss-memory risk is still open. - It needs a separate path for Ea-nasir, whose 80x64 hires-1 frames are shared with the playable merchant hero. Better fix: No art or render change. Correct the two stale comments in public/js/game/enemies.js. In the night-edge block ("Bosses and crypt lords have a light and rim of their own") and in drawEmissive ("a boss's outline burning in its colour"), say that only ink-outlined bosses get the coloured rim: the Bone King, Azgaroth and the crypt lords. The detailed rigs and Ea-nasir rely on painted dark contours, their own light and drawGlow (see tools/boss-chunky/README.md). If the owner ever asks for rims on them, add a branch to Enemy.drawRim for frames without ink. It would build an inner silhouette edge from alpha at world resolution (112x96, which is 2 source px for drawScale 0.5) and cache it lazily per frame and colour. Ea-nasir would get a 1-px edge at hires 1, drawn only through the enemy path. Update test/boss-rim-memory.test.js in the same change.

### bosses-15 [rejected] Boss shot sprites use the engine's near-black ink outline and black eyes
- polish / art - public/js/art/items.js:198-209 (bone and skullbolt lack outline:false, so the builder adds #181425 ink, and the skull's eyes and mouth use 'k')
- Evidence: [art lens] sheet-boss-shots-x6.png: bone and skullbolt are ringed in #181425, while firebolt, iceshard and darkbolt have self-coloured edges. field-lich-attacks-x2.png t=1.85 s: the 16 skulls' black rims and eyes make a black dotted wreath over the Lich. closeup-qhd-telegraphs-fx.png 'Bone King nova blast + bone ring': 11 black-rimmed bones read as a black-and-white scribble.
- The Knight standard edges each object in dark shades of its own colour, not pure ink. In rings of 11-16 shots the ink outlines turn into noise and fight the boss sprite underneath.
- Fix: Set outline:false and draw self-coloured edges. Skullbolt: rim 'e' (#3e8948), eye and mouth holes 'E' (#265c42), lit top 'l', keeping its '#9ee562' glow (projectiles.js:415). Bone: rim and underside 'a'/'A' (ash) under 'Z' grey and 'z' white, keeping the ember knobs.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The finding holds up in both the code and the pixels. **Code** - In public/js/art/items.js:198-209, `bone` and `skullbolt` have no `outline:false`. So `spriteBuffers()` (engine/sprites.js:250) rings them with the engine ink, OUTLINE = PAL.k #181425. - skullbolt's eye and mouth holes are drawn in 'k', the same ink. - No tune in tuned.js changes either sprite. - The other boss shots already have `outline:false` with self-coloured edges: firebolt, darkbolt, iceshard, and fireball (the crypt lords' shot). **Pixels (my own deterministic re-render)** I rendered the same seeded scenes three ways at a QHD view, where one art pixel is 4 screen pixels. The only thing that changed was SPR.bone / SPR.skullbolt, swapped in memory. - **Lich, current sprites:** at 0.25 s after the ring is thrown, and at 0.35 s for the phase-2 sixteen-skull ring, the skulls overlapping over the Lich form a black-and-white checkered wreath. Skulls flying at diagonals also shred into jagged black scribbles, because each skull is turned nearest-neighbour toward where it flies (`f.rotated(ang, 32)`). - **Lich, self-coloured edges:** the same moments read as green-rimmed skull orbs. They stay readable against the night ground because the green glow behind each skull does the popping. - **Bone King:** the nova bone ring and the five-bone sweep fan, where they stack at the moment they're thrown, are a black-and-white scribble with the ink. An ash rim softens this, though less dramatically than on the skull. **What the finding overstates** - The premise that "the Knight standard edges each object" goes too far. That settled call is about heroes. The horde (monsters.js has no `outline:false`) and most hero weapon shots (dagger, axe, blade, hammer, glaive) also carry the engine ink. - The defect is narrower: among the boss shots, bone and skullbolt are the ink-outlined outliers, and in rings of 11-16 the ink turns into visible noise over the boss. - The worst noise only lasts about the first 0.3 s of each volley, so this is polish, not a bug. - The capture is not an artefact. It uses the production Game.draw, and the shots are drawn unlit over everything, exactly as in the game. None of the cited files are among the other session's uncommitted work. Evidence: - C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/shot-edges-skeptic-b/lich-qhd.png - C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/shot-edges-skeptic-b/boneking-qhd.png - Script: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/shot-edges-skeptic-b/compare-b.mjs Note: another agent overwrote verify/shots-ink/compare.mjs, which is why my files are in shot-edges-skeptic-b. Better fix: There is a smaller fix that looks the same in game: recolour the ink per sprite instead of redrawing the rows. ``` skullbolt: { emissive: 'lz', palette: { k: '#265c42' }, frames: [...] } bone: { emissive: 'zZyY', palette: { k: '#4b3d44' }, frames: [...] } ``` - `swap(map, OUTLINE)` sends the engine's outline through the same palette map. So one property recolours both the outline and the 'k' eye and mouth holes. - The frame sizes stay 9x8 and 17x8, so the bone's 'reach' hit bar and the draw size don't change. - `addColour` skips colours it already has, and both are base palette colours, so the particle colour list is unaffected. - My render shows this palette version is practically indistinguishable from the finding's hand-drawn e/E version. Two follow-ups worth adding: 1. **thornbolt** (items.js:216) is also ink-outlined. The Thorn Queen throws it as `queenthorn` in the same Lich-style rings, and her ring shows the same dark clump where it is thrown. Recolour it the same way, e.g. `palette: { k: '#68386c' }`. That keeps every boss shot consistent. 2. The skull's remaining scribble mostly comes from turning a 7x6 face toward where it flies, at 32 nearest-neighbour angles. Drawing skullbolt upright would clear most of what is left. That means a SHOT_LOOK flag such as `upright: true`, with EnemyShot.draw using `f.canvas` and no rotation. A skull is a face, not a point that needs aiming. On the bone, ash (#4b3d44) gains only a little over ink, because the scribble there is mainly bones overlapping and rotating where they are thrown. If more softening is wanted, the lighter 'A' (#7d6a6a) could be used, but check it on the bright nova blast.
- Intent sceptic (not real, taste): This is taste, and the bone half has already been dealt with. The finding rests on a "Knight standard" for every object, but that settled call covers heroes only. Monsters keep the engine's ink outline by design: the 10-16 night ring is baked "outside its ink outline" (db3987d). So do the physical shots, such as thornbolt, arrow, axe, dagger and hammer. Only the glowing energy orbs (firebolt, darkbolt, iceshard, fireball) are drawn without it. The bone and the skull follow that split on purpose. The bone ring's "black-and-white checkerboard" was the 2026-10-16 visual-review item. It was fixed by size and spread, and test/bone-shot.test.js now pins the sprite as "15 x 6 (17 x 8 outlined)". The critic's redraw would break that test, because it is 17 px wide and drawn without the outline. I rendered both rings with the production glow (additive 25 px #9ee562 / #feae34), the 1.25 shot growth and the 32/16-step rotation. The comparison is at C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/bosses/verify/shots-ink/compare.png. The black wreath only shows in the first ~0.2 s, while all 16 skulls still overlap at the Lich's centre. At 0.4 s the inked skulls are the crispest, most readable danger shots of the three versions. The critic's own fix (rim 'e' #3e8948) makes each skull melt into its own green glow, so the ring is less readable. A darker own-colour ink (skull #265c42, bone ash #4b3d44) gives a slightly softer ring. It reads about as well, so it is a trade-off and not a clear gain. No gameplay or Math.random concern either way, since this is sprite data only. Better fix: If the owner wants softer rims anyway, recolour the engine ink rather than redraw. Add palette: { k: '#265c42' } to skullbolt, which turns both the ring and the eye/mouth holes deep green, and palette: { k: '#4b3d44' } to bone. Both stay 9x8 and 17x8, test/bone-shot.test.js still passes, and the art strings are unchanged. Do not use the mid green 'e' (#3e8948) for the rim, because it disappears into the skull's #9ee562 glow.

## world

The world and lighting are not pixel perfect yet, though both critics agree much of the environment art is very good. The best of it is the Sunscorch dry-stone ranges and cave arches. Also strong: the Frostfell ice sheets with their cracks; the Abyss plate floor with its lit strata, bridges and inlaid plaza; the Darkwood's roads, ruts, crossroads and landmark clearings; the Bloomvale quilt layout; the five crypt looks; and the night progression (ambient curve, fog, vignette with no banding, glowing mushrooms). At x4 no seam shows at any 256-px chunk border on any map. Ground-colour steps are smooth noise gradients. Y-sort and the canopy fade work, and the light map upscales cleanly. The biggest problems are systemic, not in single sprites. (1) The world is drawn at a fractional scale (3.2 at 1080p, 2.4 at 900p and 768p, 1.6 at 720p), so art pixels render at uneven device widths and the 1-px dither beats into moiré. Fixing it needs an integer scale, which changes the field of view, so it is the owner's call. (2) The emissive pass ignores occlusion: monster eyes float on canopies, and ember trees and crypt-door glow paint over monsters in front. (3) The day maps still get the night's torch and prop lights, which bleach a disc round the hero, with a per-channel clamp fringe: pink on snow, a strong orange ring in the duststorm. (4) The Abyss lava is a patchwork of opaque baked 32-px tiles on random phases (frame means 126-154 L), with repeating ring and octagon bubbles, and it clashes with the painted, dithered shore lava. (5) Crypt and Dungeon floors re-phase as the camera moves, so actors slip against the floor, and the spike plates blur. After those come: Bloomvale crop stripes with 3-5x the local contrast of any other ground, burying the horde; a full-strength Bayer zone dither that stamps a 4-px dot lattice on snow and grass; imported CC0 pieces below the house style (Dungeon Crawl shrines with pure-black ink, noisy gargoyle, sword in stone, rock pile); landmarks halved by nearest neighbour, with broken twigs and orphan pixels; the 14-px HUD-icon merchant stall; the Frostfell's noisy side-cliff strip; and antialiased vector tells, Fae Thread and lightning, with rain and grains at half an art pixel. Smaller polish: blocky fog off the pixel grid, an invisible Ranked blood moon, palette-swapped tomb and ice-cave crypts with a flat red carpet, flat dead trees, pop-in of the two tallest landmarks at the bottom edge, and, as a matter of taste for the owner, the uniform navy outline on all props. None of these findings touch the other session's uncommitted files (hero assets, hero-sheets.js, hero-selection, gamescreen, screens, ladder). Every fix is in the world, lighting, weather, topology, dungeon or bake code, or in landmark and prop art. The critics' evidence images and scripts are in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/world/ (art-day-lights.png at that level, plus the art-extra/ and tech-extra/ subfolders). Bare screenshot names in the findings are the shared capture set in that review folder.

### world-1 [unchecked] The unlit (emissive) pass ignores occlusion: monster eyes float on canopies, and ember trees and crypt-door glow paint over monsters in front
- bug / code - public/js/game/game.js:3353-3362 (unlit pass runs after the y-sorted draw, in list order, with no cuts); public/js/game/enemies.js:1012-1022 (drawEmissive, unconditional); public/js/game/forest.js:388-392 (drawPropEmissive), 368-376 (canopy fade alpha is not passed on); game.js:3358 / 3602 (drawCryptEmissive); the problem is acknowledged in public/js/art/flora-hell.js:220-221 and :314
- Evidence: Both critics found this. ysort-trees-detail.png: skeletons and zombies staged 6 px behind four trunks are correctly hidden by the canopy, but their red and yellow eyes float on the leaves of every tree. ysort-trees-zoom.png: two yellow eyes on the right oak's canopy at about (862,397). The night lift (enemies.js:1157-1194) is cut correctly by later props, because drawNightEdge cuts each later object's silhouette (enemies.js:1213 nightCut). The emissive loop draws every fr.em unclipped after everything. flora-hell.js:220 says the glow is 'drawn over what passes in front of it', and ember cracks were kept off the lowest rows as a workaround, but a skeleton's torso in front of an ember tree still gets the cracks painted on it. forest.js:374 fades a canopy to 0.45 while drawPropEmissive draws fr.em at alpha 1, so Abyss ember-tree cracks stay full-bright over a ghosted tree.
- This is a z-order error in the glow layer. Anything with an emissive mask (monster eyes, ember trees, glowing mushrooms, crypt doors, moonblooms) is drawn over whatever stands in front of it. In the Darkwood at night the canopy is often thick, so floating eye pairs show constantly, at the very time the emissive layer matters most. It reads as a rendering error.
- Fix: Draw the unlit layers with the same ordered-cut method as drawNightEdge. Walk g.drawList in order into an offscreen canvas the size of the screen: draw each object's fr.em, then destination-out every later opaque object's frame at its _rect (reuse nightCut). Composite that canvas once, in place of the current loop. Also store the canopy alpha (p._a = alpha in drawProp) and set ctx.globalAlpha = p._a ?? 1 in drawPropEmissive. Do not reuse _rect[5], which nightCut reads as the flip. Cheaper fallback: skip a foe's emissive when any prop drawn after it has a _rect that covers the foe's head rect.

### world-2 [unchecked] Day maps keep the night's point lights: a blown-out disc round the hero with a clamp-coloured rim (pink on snow, orange in the duststorm) and orange blotches round props
- bug / code - public/js/game/game.js:3332-3334 (the hero's warm 125-px and white 46-px lights are always added); public/js/game/forest.js:404-409 (prop lights), 421-429 (sun ambient); public/js/game/pickups.js:365 (stall and chest light); public/js/game/lighting.js:116-127 ('lighter' onto the ambient fill, each channel clamps at 255); biomes/desert.js:160, biomes/frozen.js:133
- Evidence: Both critics found this. gfx-review/world/art-day-lights.png, measured 60 px right of the hero, as played vs point lights off: Frostfell blizzard (220,229,241) vs (181,196,219); Frostfell clear (220,229,240) vs (196,209,230); Sunscorch duststorm (188,153,91) vs (177,128,75), plus a large yellow-orange blotch SW of the hero. gfx-review/world/tech-extra/daylight-desert-duststorm-lights-vs-none.png: a cream disc about 250 device px in radius round the hero, with a saturated orange ring at its rim (x about 100-480, y about 90-460). The right half, with lights off, is even sand. Duststorm ambient is (238,205,159). Warm light (255,186,120)·k saturates R at k>0.07, G at k>0.27 and B at k>0.8, so the rim, where only R has saturated, goes orange. tech-extra/daylight-frozen-lights-vs-none.png: clear Frostfell snow inside the pool is (231,238,247) against (204,216,236). frozen-19-blizzard-gust.png: a pinkish-violet arc runs from about (700,150) to (1000,470); close-ups in art-extra/crop-frozen-fringe.png and tech-extra/frozen-fringe.png. frozen-03-igloo.png: white halos round snow crystals at about (230,60), (350,150), (880,420). desert-18 and frozen-14: orange glare round the campfire and the stall.
- Lights meant to cut through the dark are added onto a near-white sun ambient. R clamps first, then G, then B, so each pool is warm or pink at its rim and bleached white inside. By daylight that gives a spotlight with a coloured fringe. It washes out the snow and sand round the hero, exactly where the horde must read.
- Fix: In Game.draw, when world.daylight, skip the hero's two lights. Scale every other light by how dark the ambient is: k = clamp((255 - max(ambient)) / 90, 0, 1), or k = clamp((215 - L) / 90, 0, 1) with L = 0.299r + 0.587g + 0.114b. This gives 0 to about 0.3 by day: nothing under a clear sun, a little in a storm. If storm lights stay, draw them with key 'white' at about 0.25, or composite with 'lighten' instead of 'lighter' so channels cannot overshoot unevenly. With no lights left, skip lighting.render and multiply the flat sun colour directly. Keep the chest and stall glow sprites, which are separate.

### world-3 [unchecked] Crypt and Dungeon floors slip against props and actors while the camera moves at fractional scale; spike plates are drawn with sub-pixel fillRects
- bug / code - public/js/game/dungeon.js:1610-1616 (drawGround blits a sub-rect starting at floor(cam.x), floor(cam.y)); dungeon.js:1736-1742 (inferno emissive, same blit); dungeon.js:1620-1637 (drawTraps: fillRect at x + (3+i*4)*S, (T-2)*S, 2*S ...); dungeon.js:1677 (props anchored to the world as in Forest)
- Evidence: Lens: technical critic. gfx-review/world/tech-extra/crawl.mjs reproduces both blits at cam.S 3.2 with the camera on whole device px. With the dungeon blit, world column 42 is 4 px wide for camX·S 32-35, then column 43 takes the 4 px at 36-38, so the floor's 3/4-px pattern re-phases every time floor(cam.x) steps. With Forest.drawGround's fixed-block blit, column 42 stays 4 px throughout. The offset term round(sx·S) - sx·S changes by up to ±0.5 device px, so wall torches, props and monsters shift by 1 device px against the bricks and flagstones as the hero walks. drawTraps puts rect edges at multiples of 3.2, which Chrome antialiases, so the plates' holes and spikes are soft at 1080p. All crypt captures are at integer S 2 and cannot show either effect.
- Sub-pixel jitter: in crypts and the Dungeon Run, the floor and wall art swims against everything standing on it during movement at 1080p and 900p, and the spike plates blur. The Survival ground does not have this, because it blits in fixed world blocks.
- Fix: Blit the crypt canvas in fixed world blocks as forest.js:343-345 does. For each 128-px block bx,by in view: x0 = round((bx - cam.x)*S), x1 = round((bx + 128 - cam.x)*S), same for y, then drawImage(canvas, bx, by, 128, 128, x0, y0, x1 - x0, y1 - y0). Do the same for this.emissive. In drawTraps, compute every edge with a world-anchored helper px = (w) => Math.round((w - cam.x) * S), for example fillRect(px(t.x+3+i*4), py(t.y+3+j*4), px(t.x+5+i*4) - px(t.x+3+i*4), ...). This becomes moot only if the integer world scale lands.

### world-4 [unchecked] Tall landmark props can pop in at the bottom screen edge: the prop cull margin (60 px) is shorter than the skull tree and the gibbet
- bug / code - public/js/game/forest.js:351 (collectProps: forEachVisible(cam, 60)); sprites lm_skulltree 88x84, lm_gibbet 52x70
- Evidence: Lens: technical critic. Derived from code, not caught in a still. drawProp puts a prop's bottom at p.y + 1 (forest.js:367), so its top is on screen while p.y < bottom + h - 1. A chunk is collected only if it starts within 60 px below the view. A skull tree whose base lies 61-83 px below the screen, in a chunk starting more than 60 px below, is not collected, so its top 1-22 px appear suddenly when the chunk enters the margin. The 70-px gibbet has the same problem over 61-69 px. A scan of SPR found these as the only world props taller than 60 px.
- The two tallest landmarks pop in at the bottom edge, in the graveyards and the gallows clearing, while the hero walks north.
- Fix: Use a margin at least as tall as the tallest prop: this.forEachVisible(cam, 96, ...) in collectProps, or compute it once from the tallest sprite among the look's props.

### world-5 [unchecked] World is drawn at a fractional scale on most screens, so art pixels come out at uneven device widths and the terrain's dither shows moiré
- defect / owner - public/js/game/game.js:2787 (syncCam: c.S = view.S * view.zoom); public/js/engine/view.js:24 (zoom 0.8), :57 (view.S = round(min(pxW,pxH)/300)); camera rounding at game.js:3233
- Evidence: Both critics found this. cam.S is 3.2 at 1920x1080, 2.4 at 1600x900 and 1366x768, and 1.6 at 1280x720. It is a whole number only at 1440p and 4K. Art lens: art-extra/crop-browser-hero-x6.png (browser-forest-start.png 740,380 160x110 x6): at 2.4 the hero's columns, his shadow and the ground's dither cells alternate between 2 and 3 device px. art-extra/crop-hell-moire.png (browser-hell-41s.png 880,170 x5): the Abyss's 1-px checker dither becomes irregular 2- and 3-px cells. Technical lens: device run-length histograms {1:16, 2:25, 3:8, 4:5, 5:4} on browser-meadow-start.png row 420 (x 120-250) and {2:15, 3:9} on browser-forest-start.png row 440. tech-extra/crawl.mjs, using Game.draw's own arithmetic at 3.2, gives art columns of 3,3,4,3,3,3,3,4 px. The painters' 1-px Bayer dither (paint.js BAYER: rock, lava, ice, quicksand, crop rows) turns into an uneven dot grid with a doubled column about every 5 art px (tech-extra/browser-hell-dither.png). The camera snaps to device px, so the pattern holds still while panning, but each moving sprite carries its own phase of it, which shows up as shimmer. Headless frames at cam.S 2 hide all of this.
- This is the largest single gap to pixel-perfect, and it affects every map. Terrain, props, monsters and the hero are all resampled nearest-neighbour by a non-integer factor, with 33% size differences between neighbouring pixels at 3.2. Dither patterns (Abyss ash, Frostfell snow, Darkwood grass) beat into moiré, and 1-px outlines vary between thin and thick. Art fixes elsewhere cannot fully land while this stays.
- Fix: Use an integer world scale and derive W/H from it (c.W = pxW / c.S, c.H = pxH / c.S). Option A, per screen height: c.S = Math.max(1, Math.round(view.pxH / 338)), which gives 1080p 3 (640x360 field), 1440p 4 (640x360), 4K 6, 900p 3 (533x300). Option B: c.S = Math.max(1, Math.round(view.S * view.zoom)), which gives 1080p 3 (640x360, 6.7% more each way than now), 900p/768p 2 (800x450 / 683x384), 720p 2 (640x360). Either way the field of view and the screen-edge spawn ring change, and seeded test runs may shift, so the owner must approve it. If a fixed field matters more, use S 3 at 1080p and accept 640 px, or S 4 and accept a smaller field.

### world-6 [unchecked] Abyss lava is a patchwork: opaque 32-px baked tiles on random phases, with repeating ring and octagon bubbles, over a differently paletted painted lava
- defect / code - public/js/game/topology/draw.js:17-29 (drawLavaTiles, opaque; line 25 gives each tile its own phase via hash2); public/js/game/topology/plates.js:1169-1188 and islands.js:413-431 (only wholly-lava 32-px tiles animate); public/js/game/forest.js:748; public/js/game/topology/paint.js:162-169 and abyss.js:583-600 (painted lava: ramp with BAYER dither); public/js/game/biomes/hell.js:194 (ramp #be4a2f/#f77622/#feae34/#fee761); public/assets/art/crypts.js 'lava' (Foozle 'Lucifer', CREDITS.md line 13; palette 138,42,34 / 168,58,38 / 190,74,47 / 247,118,34 / 254,174,52)
- Evidence: Both critics found this. hell-11-lava-far-zoom.png: square patches of lighter and darker orange with hard straight edges at the top left (140-520, 20-330), and outlined rings and octagons at about (300-500, 230-290). hell-09-landmarks.png (ruins and gallows tiles): the same octagon-and-ring chain repeats every 32 px down the lava river (x about 580-700), with lighter and darker squares. hell-05-lava-lake.png and hell-03 (right-hand lake, about 930-1200, 440-690) show the motifs on a rigid 32-px grid. browser-hell-41s.png: left lava (0-300, 180-320) shows rings, octagons and square seams at real scale. tech-extra/lavaframes.mjs: the 10 baked frames have mean luminance 153.9, 138.1, 134.7, 125.9, 132.0, 142.2, 143.9, 146.5, 141.4, 141.4, so neighbours on different phases differ by up to 28 L (about 20%), and the bubbles are cut at every tile edge. The painted lava along every shore (12 px) and in every partial tile uses the ramp with yellow #fee761 and a 1-px Bayer checker. The tiles have neither yellow nor dither but have two dark reds the ramp lacks, and the two styles meet on straight 32-px edges.
- A tileable animated texture is tiled with random per-tile phases, which breaks its continuity. It is also mixed with a painted lava in a different style and palette, which outlines the tile grid. The lava is the Abyss's biggest and brightest surface, so this is where the problem shows most.
- Fix: Best: stop replacing the painted lava and animate it by palette cycling. Offset the ramp index t in paint.js by floor(time·fps) per frame over a cached lavaG field, or cycle the colours of a chunk-sized lava canvas. That gives one style, no grid and no seams. Lighter option: draw the baked frames additively ('lighter', alpha about 0.2-0.3) over the painted flow, as dungeon.js:1729-1735 already does in crypts. If the tiles stay as they are: use one global phase (LAVA[Math.floor(time*fps) % n], no hash), draw through a per-chunk lava mask (o < 0) with 'source-atop' so shores and partial tiles animate too, remap the baked palette to the ramp (darks to #be4a2f, add #fee761 for the top highlight), repaint the frames without outlined rings or octagons, and feather the border with a 4-px dithered edge.

### world-7 [unchecked] Bloomvale crop stripes are the loudest thing on screen, louder than the horde
- defect / code - public/js/game/topology/paint-quilt.js (8-px rows: 3 furrow, 5 flowers; tulip beds in other crops' colours); crop palettes in public/js/game/biomes/meadow.js (terrain.crops); night ambient meadow.js:171-178
- Evidence: Lens: art critic. Mean luminance difference between adjacent art px: tulips 16.7, cornflower 20.3, against Darkwood 3.8, Sunscorch 6.4, Frostfell 6.1, Abyss 5.4. Luminance sd is 33-37 on the crops vs 7-11 elsewhere (art-extra/contrast.mjs on browser shots). art-extra/crop-meadow42.png (browser-meadow-42s.png 500,200): purple, salmon, blue, pink and white tulip stripes behind white rabbits, cyan gems and red blood; the blood decals and the hero's red bar sink into the salmon rows. layers-meadow-midnight.png: 'as played' is barely darker than 'ambient white'.
- The value order (hero > threats > pickups > ground) is upside down here. The ground has the highest local contrast and saturation in the game, and the nearly unlit night does not damp it. Stripe direction flips per plot, which adds more edges. It looks bold in stills but buries the fight.
- Fix: Halve the furrow-to-flower value step by raising the furrow tone toward each crop's darkest flower tone. Desaturate crop tones by 15-20% and pull them about 10% darker. Limit tulip beds to two neighbouring hues per field. Re-measure, aiming for a local adjacent-px difference of 8 or less, so contrast-brightened monsters and gems sit clearly above the field.

### world-8 [unchecked] Zone and tone dither uses Bayer at full strength, so snow and grass carry a regular 4-px dot screen
- defect / code - public/js/game/darkwood.js:815 (zone threshold zt = 0.5 + b*0.9 + n*0.3), darkwood.js:817/824 (tone t ... + b*0.22)
- Evidence: Lens: art critic. Frostfell snow (frozen-01-start.png 800,60 300x160): 36% of the darkest 6% of art px fall on a single cell of the 4x4 Bayer grid (6% expected). Darkwood (forest-01-start.png): 27% fall on two cells (12.5% expected). Sunscorch shows no concentration, because its ripples break the pattern. Visible as an even lattice of blue dots in art-extra/crop-snow-fog.png and frozen-06-ice-zoom.png. The technical critic's moiré finding (browser-hell-dither.png) shows how fractional scale makes this worse.
- Where the pine-shade or cursed (ice) zone is weak but not zero, the darker zone colour appears only on the low-threshold Bayer cells. That gives a perfectly regular grid of dots over large areas. On flat Frostfell snow it reads as a screen-door texture, and the fractional world scale turns it into moiré.
- Fix: Make zone selection mostly hash noise: zt = 0.5 + b*0.25 + n*0.55, or use a precomputed 64x64 blue-noise threshold. Cut the Bayer share of the tone ramp to b*0.12 + n*0.2. Keep ordered dither only for shading inside sprites.

### world-9 [unchecked] Imported CC0 shrines and gothic landmarks don't match the house style: pure-black outlines and painterly, high-colour noise
- defect / art - public/js/art/props.js:278-283 (shrines baked from Dungeon Crawl, CREDITS.md line 21); public/js/art/landmarks.js:5-17 (gargoyle, sword in stone, rock pile from ETTiNGRiNDER, CREDITS.md line 18)
- Evidence: Both critics found this. art-extra/inkcount.mjs on the built sprites: shrine_fury 103, shrine_wisdom 84 and shrine_protection 108 edge pixels of pure #000000. lm_gargoyle has 51-52 colours and 71% isolated pixels, lm_swordstone 41-42 colours and 58%, lm_rockpile 34-35 colours and 56%, with neutral-grey #181818 edges. In-house pieces: oak1 8 colours and 26%, snow_oak1 12 and 12%, crypt 11 and 3%. art-extra/crop-shrines.png, art-extra/crop-lm.png, sheet-04-landmarks.png. transition-frozen-1.png: the gargoyle at about (740-820, 285-365) reads as an olive noise blob among clean snow props. forest-03-shrine-crossroads.png: the haste shrine reads as a glowing cyan screen. forest-05's glade tile: gargoyles.
- These pieces use a different pixel density, noisy shading with no clusters, neutral greys and black ink. They sit in the most visited places: shrines at every crossroads, gargoyles and swords at landmarks. Next to the clean, hue-shifted in-house props they read as clip-art.
- Fix: Re-bake with a cleanup pass: quantize each material to a 4-5 tone hue-shifted ramp, merge orphan pixels into clusters of 2 or more, relight from the top left, and recolour the outline to each material's darkest shade (no #000000). Or redraw the five altars (28-30 px) and the gargoyle (39x38) in-house in the crypt door's style, one art subagent per sprite.

### world-10 [unchecked] Landmarks halved by nearest neighbour lose pixels: broken twigs, dotted outlines and stray speckle
- defect / art - tools/bake-art.mjs:142-149 (scale 0.5 keeps 1 pixel in 4); tools/art/landmarks.mjs:44-50 (lm_skulltree, lm_skulltree2, lm_driedtree, lm_monument at scale 0.5); tools/art/crypts.mjs:53, 59 (lavaFlag, door at 0.5); baked into public/assets/art/landmarks.js
- Evidence: Lens: technical critic. gfx-review/world/tech-extra/halved-landmarks.png (x5): lm_driedtree's twigs break into disconnected single pixels and lose their outline; lm_skulltree2's branch tips are dotted; lm_monument's rim has orphan pixels and speckle. In game: the skull tree in forest-05's graveyard tile (top centre).
- Dropping every other row and column of pixel art deletes 1-px lines wherever they fall on a dropped row or column, which leaves orphan pixels and holes. These landmarks are the Darkwood's set pieces, so the damage sits where players look.
- Fix: Re-bake from source with a reduction that keeps lines: for each 2x2 block, make it opaque if at least 2 of its pixels are opaque, and take the darkest pixel when any is an outline or dark colour, otherwise the most frequent colour. Then hand-clean twig tips and add a dark edge in the same hue. Or have one art subagent per sprite redraw these four at native size in the game palette. Give crypts.mjs lavaFlag and door the same treatment.

### world-11 [unchecked] The merchant's stall is a 14x14 HUD icon dropped into the world, under trees
- defect / art - public/js/art/icons/boons.js:226-245 (i_stall); public/js/game/pickups.js:39; drawn in the flat pickup pass at game.js:3257; placed by game.js:1145-1150 (checks only world.free = topology, forest.js:668-670)
- Evidence: Lens: art critic. art-extra/crop-forest-stall.png (forest-09-midnight-woods.png, stall at about 880-940, 330-380): a dead tree's trunk and branches are drawn straight through the cart. The same icon is at about (900,355) in frozen-19 and desert-18. It is 14 px wide, smaller than the hero (about 20x28) and half the crypt door (26x31).
- A world structure the player is told to walk to ('A merchant sets up shop') is drawn as a pickup. It never takes part in the y-sort, can stand inside a trunk, and is scaled like a coin. It reads as loot, not a stall.
- Fix: Author a world-scale stall prop (about 30x28: awning, wheels, a hooded merchant) in the house style, and keep i_stall for the cards. Add it to the y-sorted list as shrines are (game.js:3279). Place it with world.seek(x, y, 8, 80, 8, need, true) plus clearance from trunks and props.

### world-12 [unchecked] Frostfell's side cliffs have a soft, noisy 12-px face strip next to the crisp range, plus dithered crevice smears across the terraces
- defect / code - public/js/game/topology/paint.js:223-235 (side faces fh = FACE_H*0.42 ≈ 12 px; strata t = hash2(along >> 1, 0, 7)*3 + (h/fh)*3 with along = wy, so a random shade every 2 px); paint.js:236 (crevice when ridge < 0.05 + bay*0.03)
- Evidence: Both critics found this (art critic: defect; technical critic: polish). frozen-09-edge-lo-zoom.png and the bottom-right column of seams-3.png (x about 1130-1200): between the sharp, outlined snowy range and the band runs a column of low-contrast grey blocks in random 2-px horizontal bands, like an upscaled texture. frozen-19-blizzard-gust.png left edge (x about 240-265) reads as a ladder or rail. gfx-review/world/tech-extra/browser-frozen-cliff.png (4x of browser-frozen-42s at 440-700, 620-760): about 11 art px of soft 2-px grey blocks, with no dark edge against the snow and no courses, while the terraces beside it have crisp 1-px dark risers. In the same crop (y about 250-330) a horizontal dithered crevice band crosses three terraces at one height and reads as a stain; also tech-extra/frozen-range-streak.png.
- Each 2 rows of a side face pick a random tone, with nothing tying them to ledges or light. A cliff running north-south shows no face in the game's 3/4 view, so this strip has no structure to show and only adds noise at the corridor's edge, the one place where a hard, clean border helps the player. It is on screen whenever the hero is near either edge, and it is unlike the Sunscorch's drystone faces.
- Fix: Option A, drop the face: for side faces (f < side), paint a 1-px P.foot line and a 2-px lit P.lip on the band side of the right cliff (it faces the top-left light), cast a 4-6 px soft shade onto the band from the left cliff, and run the terraces' riser and lip art right up to the edge. Option B, give it structure: paint stone courses as the Sunscorch does (stone.js sideRun, stone.js:563) with a snow cap, or at least quantise strata to 6-8 px courses (hash2(along >> 3, ...), keyed on h), add a 1-px P.face[0] edge on the land side and a P.lip top. In both cases restrict the crevice rule to riser rows (fs >= 0.3), or draw it as a 1-px line instead of a dithered band.

### world-13 [unchecked] Vector strokes and half-art-pixel weather in a pixel-art world: tell outlines, Fae Thread, lightning and gust rings are antialiased paths; rain, splashes and dust grains are half an art pixel wide
- defect / code - public/js/game/topology/draw.js:43-54 (quicksand and ice tells: stroke through unrounded points, lineWidth S); public/js/game/weather.js:354-365 (duststorm grains: diagonal half-pixel strokes), 413-425 (rain: lineWidth round(S/2), unrounded x), 426-436 (splashes: round(S/2), unrounded fillRect), 439-457 (bolt: lineWidth S*3 and S*0.75, round joins); public/js/game/fae.js:494-555 (rim and thread); public/js/game/hazards/gusts.js:160-170
- Evidence: Both critics found this (art critic: polish; technical critic: defect). forest-12-rain-lightning.png: the bolt (1010-1050, 22-440) is a smooth antialiased polyline with round joins and a soft glow; hell-21-emberstorm.png shows the same in red. meadow-11-fae-thread-walking.png: the thread (530-910, 380-690) is a smooth sub-pixel curve, also at the top left of art-extra/crop-meadow42.png; meadow-12 shows the rim. desert-04-quicksand.png: the pool's tell is a soft half-alpha polyline. gfx-review/world/tech-extra/ice-tell-crop.png (8x of frozen-04 at 500-580, 262-352): the white tell is a smooth antialiased curve about half an art pixel wide inside the stepped ice edge. ysort-trees-zoom.png: rain streaks are half the width of the grass tufts' pixels and greyed by antialiasing.
- These are the only vector-looking marks in an otherwise strict pixel world. Their diagonals blend across widths below one art pixel (mixels), and at fractional scale their thickness changes along the line. The tells and the thread are on screen constantly on the Survival maps, and rain covers whole runs.
- Fix: Rasterize them on the art grid. General route: draw into an offscreen canvas at art resolution (cam.W x cam.H, smoothing off, 1-px Bresenham via fillRect) and blit at S, or step each segment in art px and fillRect(S, S). Tells: trace each patch's 48 rim points once with Bresenham at 1 art px (or take the rim pixels from the patch mask) and draw it like a sprite at round((x - cam.x)*S), size w*S, nearest neighbour. Rain: fillRect columns one art px wide (Math.max(1, Math.round(S))) at x snapped to the art grid; splashes and grains become art-px squares and steps at snapped positions. Bolt and Fae Thread: Bresenham segments with a 1-art-px core and 1-art-px glow neighbours in two tones (about 3 art px total) instead of a blurred 3S stroke.

### world-14 [unchecked] Fog is drawn as hard 3x3-art-px squares off the pixel grid, crawling by sub-pixel steps as it drifts
- polish / code - public/js/game/forest.js:450-467 (96x96 fog tile drawn at scale S*3 with imageSmoothingEnabled false; ox/oy unrounded, moved by drift and 0.85/1.15 parallax); forest.js:776-797 (makeFogTexture)
- Evidence: Lens: technical critic. gfx-review/world/tech-extra/fog-layer-alone.png shows Forest.drawOverlay alone at cam.S 3.2 with alpha stretched: hard square texels of 9.6 device px, runs on row 180 of '10 9 11 8 10 10 1 10 19 9 9 1 8', and 1-2 px slivers where the two layers' grids cross, aligned to neither the art grid nor each other. Darkwood fog peaks at alpha 30/255, so it is subtle there. Frostfell fog (0.12 x (1 + 1.5*haze)) over white snow is stronger.
- A soft fog is upscaled nearest-neighbour at arbitrary sub-pixel offsets, which produces moving square blocks of a size unrelated to the art pixels.
- Fix: Set ctx.imageSmoothingEnabled = true for the fog loop and restore it after, because the fog is meant to be soft, like the light map and the haze hole. If blocky fog is wanted, snap ox/oy to whole art px (Math.round(v / S) * S) so its texels sit on the art grid.

### world-15 [unchecked] Ranked's blood moon cannot be seen
- polish / code - public/js/game/forest.js:437-441 (lerp at most 0.45 toward 170,70,90); vignette public/js/game/biomes.js:93
- Evidence: Lens: art critic. forest-16-ranked-bloodmoon.png (43:20, phase 46.5): corner mean RGB (21,24,29) vs an ordinary rainy night in forest-11-rain.png (22,27,34). No red appears anywhere in the frame.
- The sky is meant to announce the lethal phase of the endless night. After the rain's dimming and the navy vignette, the 45% lerp toward a muted mauve is lost.
- Fix: Raise the lerp to 0.8 toward (190,56,64) over phases 30-45. While it lasts, tint the night vignette to '48,4,10' at 0.75 and pull the fog toward (200,120,130). Optionally add a faint red wash from the top edge, as a moon would cast.

### world-16 [unchecked] Tomb and ice-cave crypts are palette swaps, and the lord's carpet is the same flat red in every look
- polish / art - public/js/game/dungeon.js:123-144 (tomb and icecave change only colours); dungeon.js:826-827 (carpet, Foozle 'Lucifer', CREDITS.md line 14)
- Evidence: Lens: art critic. crypt-15-frozen-icecave-montage.png: blue brick, flagstones, cobwebs, clay urns and the red carpet, with no ice, icicles or frozen floor. crypt-14-desert-tomb-montage.png: the same rooms in brown. In crypt-04, 13, 14 and 15 the carpet is a flat, unshaded, saturated red rectangle, brighter than the hero standing on it.
- Every biome's crypt has the same room dressing in a new colour, so the Frostfell's 'Ice Cave' and the Sunscorch's 'Tomb' don't read as places of their own. The carpet's flat saturated block is the brightest value in every lord's room and pulls the eye from the lord and the hero.
- Fix: Ice cave: ice-rimmed wall tops with icicles, a few floor tiles painted with paint.js icePixel, frost-capped urns. Tomb: sandstone sarcophagi and a painted frieze band. Carpet: tint per look (deep blue in the ice cave, ochre in the tomb), drop its value about 25%, and add worn edges and fold shading.

### world-17 [unchecked] Dead trees are flat 1-2 tone stick drawings
- polish / art - public/js/art/procgen.js:124-148 (genDeadTree), used heavily in the cursed wood (public/js/game/darkwood.js:628-629)
- Evidence: Lens: art critic. art-extra/crop-forest-stall.png: two dead trees as flat lavender stick figures with limbs of even width. sheet-01-darkwood-flora.png: deadtree1 and deadtree2 next to the shaded oaks and pines. transition-forest-cursed-edge-midnight.png: dead tree at about (600,100).
- Each branch is one tone with a 1-px light edge, with no taper, knots or root flare. They are the main tree of the cursed wood and the weakest flora sprite next to the shaded oaks and pines.
- Fix: Give branches a 3-tone hue-shifted ramp (lit top-left edge, mid tone, violet shadow underside). Taper the trunk from 5 px to 1 px with trunk()'s root flare, add 2-3 broken stubs and a knot, and bias the colour to ash-violet so they still read in the cursed fog.

### world-18 [unchecked] Every world prop wears the same navy ink outline, which is the hardest mark on the day maps
- taste / owner - public/js/engine/sprites.js:243-250 (px.outline with OUTLINE); public/js/art/palette.js:7,68 (#181425); precedent public/js/art/terrain.js:27-29
- Evidence: Lens: art critic. Built-sprite edge colour (art-extra/inkcount.mjs) is #181425 for oak1 (132 px), snow_oak1 (132), sand_palm1 (185), bloom_tree1 (153), hell_tree1 (232) and crypt (96). browser-frozen-start.png and frozen-17-far.png: snow oaks and rocks are edged harder than the hero on white snow.
- The plumed Knight's standard uses edges that are dark shades of the sprite's own colour. The world instead uses one navy-black ink, which on sand and snow by day becomes the highest-contrast element and competes with the horde. terrain.js already moved the desert stones to their own ink after the owner said the navy 'clashes'.
- Fix: Add an opt-in self-coloured outline in buildFrames (def.selout): each outline pixel takes the adjacent interior colour, darkened about 55% and hue-shifted 15-20 degrees toward blue-violet, keeping #181425 only on the ground-contact row. Try it on the day-map flora first and let the owner judge.

## vfx

Both critics agree it is not pixel-perfect yet, though the baked layer is close. The baked VFX family is strong and on-style: the Sprite Fusion slashes, holy arcs, zaps, pillars, the ult flash and the silver burst. All 78 vfx_* sprites have clean frame edges. Projectiles are rotated at art resolution through Frame.rotations, and ringSprite, discSprite, slashFrame and crescentSprite are all drawn on the art grid. Excalibur, Meteor, Judgement, Thunderstorm, Smite and the Miasma/Pestilence mist look right, and so do pickups by day and night (tiered gem glows, gear pillars, chest glint), the casino digits, death bursts, decals and the brand and curse marks. One problem dominates the screen: the per-foe hit flash. It is a flat white silhouette drawn in the lit pass on every blow with no cooldown. In late fights it covers 27-59% of the foes on screen (40-47% of those round the hero), and it raises near-white pixels round the hero to 28-50%. At night away from light it turns a struck foe dark slate, and the FLASHES: SOFT option never touches it. After that the runtime layer breaks the pixel grid in four ways. Sprites are drawn at fractional scales: gems and the boss chest at 1.5, blades, axes, glaives, copper discs, meteors, enemy shots and the flash stretch. Damage numbers scale continuously and rotate. Chain and ray bolts, Scythe, Runestone, Starfall, telegraphs, Trailblazer and Liquidation are anti-aliased vector strokes. The half-res glow is blown up 2x into a checker. On top of that the fx_flame frames are cut by their 20 px source cells, and the world scale itself is fractional (3.2 at 1080p, which is the owner's call). There is one mechanical bug: Hellfire's blows are typed 'frost', so Scald and Combust never fire. Several things don't read on screen. The recruit and Ea-nasir signatures are about 16x smaller than the original heroes'. Recruit melee cuts are flat cardboard fans. Gale Horn and Tempest show no gust, the Overgrowth ultimate has no light, and Raise Dead allies look like enemy skeletons. Reaction words are buried under their own splash numbers. Slow, root, stun, fear, poison, bleed and Exposed have no marks, and the Hex hourglass collides with the brand. On the UI side, the fury comet is a placeholder square, the ult cut-in resamples the portrait and squashes long names, and the HUD ult label is struck through by the band's rule. Damage numbers on breakable boxes are the owner's call. None of this touches the owner's settled calls, and none depends on the hero files another session is editing. Everything was judged from stills, so timing and flicker are unjudged. Nothing in the repo was touched. The critics' evidence and scripts are under C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/vfx/, in art-extra/ (flash-measure.mjs, hellfire-scald.mjs, fallback-measure.mjs, crops) and tech-extra/ (flashfix.mjs, flashcount.mjs, numscale.mjs, edges.mjs, logow.mjs, crops). Below, art-extra/ and tech-extra/ mean those folders.

### vfx-1 [unchecked] Hit flash: a flat white silhouette on every blow, in the lit pass. It drowns the horde and the hero, turns struck foes dark slate at night, and ignores FLASHES: SOFT
- bug / code - public/js/game/enemies.js:940-942 (img = fr.white in draw), :962 (_look = -1: the flashed foe loses its night lift and NIGHT_EDGE ring), :943 (softFlash gates only the burn), :1018 (eyes off), :422 (DoT ticks call hitEnemy); public/js/game/game.js:1436-1437 (e.flash = 0.09 on every hitEnemy, ticks and splashes included, no cooldown), :3297-3298 (foes drawn before :3338 lighting.render and :3352 drawNightEdge); public/js/engine/sprites.js:153-155 (px.silhouette); public/js/engine/storage.js:650 and ui/screens.js:1185 (FLASHES: SOFT, cited only as a reference)
- Evidence: Both lenses measured this. Coverage, from art-extra/flash-measure.mjs and tech-extra/flashcount.mjs on the real spawner:
- knight_forest: 1,401 blows/s; 39.9% of the ~66 foes round the hero are white in any frame, and 27% of the 284 on screen (77). Near-white pixels in the 240x136 centre: 28.5%, against 10.2% with the flash zeroed.
- mage_hell: 3,070 blows/s; 47.1% of the 236 foes near the hero; 41% of the 450 on screen (184), and 59% in the final frame (258 of 440). Near-white: 37.4% against 3.5% in the centre, and 50.6% in a 440x250 crop. Removing all weapon glows gives 50.5%, so the flashes are the cause.

Night colour, from status-marks.png: in the forest row the hit-flash foe samples rgb(82,87,127) (#535780) at (192,110). The plain skeleton's bone samples rgb(152,177,216) at (60,112), so a hit makes the foe darker. Zoom: tech-extra/st-flash.png. On snow by day (busy-necro_frozen) the flash is white on white.

Images:
- art-extra/flash-share.png: white clouds against the dense horde underneath.
- busy-1080p-knight_forest.png: grey holes at about (1380-1500, 230-340); zoom tech-extra/b1080-grey.png.
- busy-1080p-mage_hell.png: a cream field round the Sorcerer swallows the '6486' number.
- Grey ghosts in weapons-07.png (Whip, 2.25 s) and weapons-09.png (Bow, 1.00 s).
- busy-numbers-on-off.png: the white blob stays with numbers hidden.

Fix prototype (monkeypatch only, repo untouched): tech-extra/flashfix-knight_forest.png and tech-extra/flashfix-mage_hell.png. Near-white in the mage_hell crop falls from 50.6% to 33.5%.
- This is the single most visible defect. Both critics found it: one through the night/lighting lens, one through coverage and accessibility.

The flash replaces the foe's frame with a full-body white silhouette inside Enemy.draw, which runs before the light map multiplies the scene. The night-edge pass also cuts flashing foes out. So the same hit looks different depending on the light: pure white near any light, a dull slate cut-out (a 'hole') outside the hero's 125 px light. In both cases the foe loses its outline and ring.

Every blow restarts the 0.09 s flash. That includes Sanctuary and Consecration ticks, meteor pools, reaction splashes and DoT ticks. At late-game rates (1.4-3k blows/s) foes near the hero spend almost half their frames white, flickering at roughly 2-10 Hz. Neighbouring silhouettes merge into one mass that hides threats, the hero and the numbers. The FLASHES: SOFT option, meant for photosensitive players, never reaches this flash.
- Fix: 1. In Enemy.draw, keep drawing the normal lit frame while flash > 0, and keep _look so the night lift and ring stay.
2. In drawEmissive (unlit), draw a 'hit' overlay. Either use fr.white(flip) at this._rect, or better, a variant with opaque non-ink pixels lerped about 70% toward #ffffff and the frame's ink/outline pixels kept dark, so silhouettes stay separated. Use alpha min(1, flash/0.05) x about 0.6-0.85 x this.alpha. With settings.softFlash, use FLASH_SOFT or about 0.3.
3. Add a re-flash guard in hitEnemy: if (!o.tick && this.clock >= (e.flashNext||0)) { e.flash = 0.09; e.flashNext = this.clock + 0.22-0.25; }.
4. Give o.tick DoTs, o.raw splashes and aura/pool ticks no white flash. Use a brief tint instead (TINTS.burn, or a poison or bleed tint) or nothing.
5. Re-run art-extra/flash-measure.mjs. Target: under 15% of foes flashing and under 12% near-white in the centre.

All of this is draw or cosmetic state, with no Math.random calls added (seeded tests).

### vfx-2 [unchecked] Hellfire's blows are typed 'frost', so it never Scalds or Combusts, though its card and Codex say Fire
- bug / code - public/js/game/effects.js:496 (POOL_LOOK.hell num:'frost', glow '#2ce8f5', light 'cyan'), effects.js:573 (Pool hits use type: this.look.num); public/js/data/reactions.js:40 (FIRE = withEvos(['fireball','flask','lantern']) includes hellfire), :67-72 reactionLine; public/js/game/reactions.js:24-26 (scalds and combusts need o.type === 'fire')
- Evidence: art-extra/hellfire-scald.mjs:
- The Codex line for hellfire is "Fire: Scalds the frozen, Combusts the poisoned." With Frost Nova held, the card says "Scald: Frost Nova".
- Over 6 s on 12 staged frozen/poisoned skeletons, Firebomb Flask L8 dealt 677 'fire' blows: 5 SCALD and 5 COMBUST words, rate-limited.
- Hellfire dealt 204 'frost' blows: 0 SCALD, 0 COMBUST.
- Its pools use the exact frost palette (#2ce8f5 glow, 'cyan' light, frost-blue numbers). In busy-1080p-knight_forest.png it fuses with Frozen Heart into one cyan field.
- The style key that colours the numbers is reused as the damage type. The blue flames are the owner's design, but tying them to the frost number style makes Hellfire mechanically and visually a frost weapon. The G1 reactions then silently break for the flask's evolution, the card lies to the player, and frozen foes can't be told from foes standing in hellfire.
- Fix: 1. Separate the hit type from the look. Give POOL_LOOK a 'type' field ('fire' for 'hell'), and use { type: this.look.type || this.look.num } at effects.js:573.
2. Add a 'hellfire' number style (for example ['#ffffff','#8fa0ff','#5b6ee1'], edge '#262b44'): blue, but not frost.
3. Optionally shift the hell pool's glow and light toward a violet-blue soulfire (#5b6ee1 / '#8a6cff').
4. Re-run hellfire-scald.mjs and expect SCALD > 0.

- Original heroes' evolved signatures: excalibur 31305, meteor 85421, judgement 34462, purifier 21093, worldroot 14726, bloodstorm 14100, pestilence 12878, legion 11696, shadowstrike 3765, stormstring 2432. Median about 14,400.

- weapons-04: Seraph is dotted 2-px squares.

- Damage was raised into the signature band, but the pictures were not. Each recruit's class-defining weapon is hard to see at real scale, and evolving gives almost no visual payoff. Next to Excalibur, Meteor or Judgement they read as unfinished.
- Fix: Give each recruit signature one readable shape, sized like the originals (aim for at least 5k px evolved):

- **Templar:** crescentSprite.
- **Marksman and Boltguard:** bow/crossbow glints and the silver-burst VFX on impact.
- **Ea-nasir:** copper discs with a dark #733e39 outline and an unlit 'em' rim highlight.

Bake through tools/vfx/bake.mjs where possible.

### vfx-4 [unchecked] Fractional sprite scales put mixed pixel sizes on screen: gems and boss chest at 1.5, blades, axes, glaives, copper discs, meteors, enemy shots, flash stretch
- defect / code - public/js/game/pickups.js:20 GEM_SIZE 1.5 (used :304, :318-320); public/js/game/chests.js:27 boss chest scale 1.5; public/js/game/effects.js:734 Guardian Blades/Aegis S*min(1.3, area), :1060 meteor S*1.2/1.6; public/js/game/weapons.js:532,545,896 scale: w.s.area -> projectiles.js:374; public/js/game/weapons-copper.js:158-164 copper discs min(1.5, area); public/js/game/projectiles.js:404 SHOT_GROW 1.25 (look.scale 1.25-1.5, :408-429) -> :527; public/js/game/enemies.js:150 flash stretch 1.12x0.9
- Evidence: Both lenses; the first critic flagged gems and the boss chest, the second the whole family.

Gems and boss chest (art-extra/gems-crop.png, tech-extra/gal-gems-day.png): at camera 3, gem pixels are 4-5 screen px against the ground's even 3, and the T4 gem's stair steps are uneven. At camera 2 they are 3 px against 2. In pickups-gallery the boss chest is chunkier than the champion and lord chests.

Weapons at camera 2 (world pixels an even 2 px):
- tech-extra/w08-blade-zoom.png: Guardian Blade cells alternate 2 and 3 px.
- tech-extra/w08-axe.png, tech-extra/w09-glaive.png: the same 2/3 mix.
- tech-extra/w05-disc.png: copper disc cells are 3 px.

Others: tech-extra/bk-bone.png shows the Bone King's bone shot at 3.75 px; tech-extra/st-flash.png shows the uneven flash stretch.
- Each of these multiplies an art-resolution canvas by a fractional factor at draw time. With nearest sampling, some art pixels become 2 screen px and others 3, next to world sprites on a regular S grid. At 1080p gems end up at 4.8 px per pixel against the world's 3.2. Blades and axes shimmer as they grow with Area.
- Fix: Add Frame.scaled(k) next to Frame.rotations (engine/sprites.js:184-199): a nearest-neighbour resample at art resolution to round(w*k) x round(h*k), with k quantised to 1/8 and cached. Then draw at plain S, and use it in drawCentered/blitCentered, Orbit.draw, the copper discs and enemy shots.

Art: redraw gem1-4 natively at the 1.5x size (about 10x10, 12x12, 13x13, 14x15) with self-coloured dark edges, and draw them at k = 1, keeping GEM_SIZE only for shadow, glow and twinkle radii. Redraw the boss chest at 24x18.

Quantise the flash stretch to whole art pixels (+2 px wide, -1 px tall), or drop it.

### vfx-5 [unchecked] Vector, anti-aliased strokes and canvas rotations in a pixel world: Chain Lightning, Storm Lash, Arcane/Prismatic Ray, Scythe, Runestone, Starfall, telegraphs, Trailblazer, Liquidation
- defect / code - public/js/game/effects.js:965-1003 (Bolt.drawGlow: ctx.stroke lineWidth S*1.2/2.4/4/5, round joins and caps; also copper chain weapons-copper.js:196); weapons-light.js:289-352 (Scythe/Harvest Moon arcs, quadraticCurveTo, rotate); weapons-light.js:176-192 (Runestone rotate + fillRect); weapons-light.js:469-484 (Starfall gradient streak); effects.js:1226-1236, 1250-1255 (Telegraph line rotate + fillRect); weapons-copper.js:52-75 (Liquidation/CopperRecall ring stroke); expansion/weapons/trailblazer-behavior.js:66-81
- Evidence: Both lenses. Crops:
- art-extra/arcane.png and tech-extra/w11-arcane.png: soft anti-aliased beam edges, no pixel steps.
- art-extra/chain.png and tech-extra/w11-stormlash.png: a round-capped polyline about 1.2 art px wide with a soft halo.
- tech-extra/w16-harvest.png and weapons-16 rows 1-2: the Scythe haft is a hairline thinner than one art pixel.
- weapons-16 rows 3-4: Starfall is a gradient line.
- tech-extra/w15-rune.png: the rotated Runestone has 1-screen-px stair steps.
- tech-extra/bmage-tele.png: anti-aliased telegraph edges.

By contrast, the Hook rope (crowdfx.js), LeechChain, Kraken and the Wraith thread are drawn per art pixel and match the art.
- These are Canvas2D paths at device resolution, with sub-art-pixel widths, round caps and smoothing that no sprite has. That makes them the clearest 'not pixel perfect' tell in the combat layer. The Scythe is the worst: about 1 px thick at camera 2, it disappears in busy frames.
- Fix: 1. Add a shared art-grid line helper: Bresenham on world coordinates, fillRect(round(x)*S, round(y)*S, t*S, t*S), with a 1-2 art px white core and a dimmer coloured edge or halo. Reuse the Hook/Wraith loop.
2. Sample arcs the same way.
3. For the Starfall streak, step 3 alpha bands of art-px squares instead of the gradient.
4. For the Scythe, author a blade sprite with Frame.rotations(32) and a ringSprite wake.
5. For the Runestone, author a 7x7 rune sprite with rotated(spin, 16).
6. Rasterise the telegraph lines and the Liquidation ring with the same helper.

### vfx-6 [unchecked] Damage numbers use a continuous fractional scale and canvas rotation: uneven font pixels, rows lost below camera scale 1.7
- defect / code - public/js/game/numbers.js:265 (scale = st.scale*n.boost*k*S), :276-280 (ctx.rotate for crit spin), :283 drawImage with that scale, :289 shine at fractional x0*scale
- Evidence: Crops in tech-extra/:
- numscale.png: at camera 1.6 a '7' and the fading '48' are squashed and lose rows; at 3.2 the strokes alternate 2 and 3 px.
- num-normal.png: '48' at camera 3 has 1- and 2-px strokes side by side.
- num-crit-pop.png: a crit is rotated on screen pixels and breaks the grid.
- busy-word.png: frost numbers in the S=1 busy frame lost their top rows.
- The 9 px pixel font is drawn at 0.6 x boost x pop x S, which is almost never a whole number of screen pixels per font pixel, and the pop and fade scale it continuously. Below a factor of 1 (small values at 720p, and every number in its last 0.3 s) digits garble. Above 1 the strokes are uneven. These numbers are the most frequent thing on screen.
- Fix: Quantise the scale: px = Math.max(1, Math.round(st.scale*n.boost*k*S)), and draw at c.width*px by c.height*px. Run the pop as integer steps (1->3->2). Replace the rotation with an integer ±1 font-px jitter, or with 2-3 glyphs pre-rotated at font resolution. Align the shine band to the same integer grid.

### vfx-7 [unchecked] Global world scale is fractional (3.2 at 1080p, 2.4 at 768/900p, 1.6 at 720p), so every art pixel alternates widths
- defect / owner - public/js/engine/view.js:24 (zoom: 0.8); public/js/game/game.js:2787 (c.S = Math.max(1, view.S * view.zoom))
- Evidence: tech-extra/b1080-skel.png: a skeleton at true 1080p (native crop of busy-1080p-knight_forest.png) whose outline and eye columns alternate 3 and 4 px. Every n*S fillRect in the VFX code lands on fractional coordinates (praetorian-behavior.js:106, templar-behavior.js:112, enemies.js:1057 hourglass), so those edges anti-alias.
- view.S is an integer, but the world is drawn at S*0.8, so the most common screens use an uneven pixel grid. This is the largest single source of mixels, and it compounds the fractional-scale findings. The 0.8 zoom (25% more view) looks deliberate.
- Fix: The owner chooses:
- A: c.S = Math.max(1, Math.round(view.S*view.zoom)). 1080p becomes 3, 900p becomes 2, 720p stays 2.
- B: Math.floor, for a slightly wider view.
- C: keep 0.8.

With A or B, check the spawn and despawn radii that depend on cam.W/H.
