It is also not redundant work: the chest overlay already paints its own lit build row at the top (gamescreen.js:1165, g.hud.drawSlots), so the dimmed belt under it repeats what is on screen. The trophy overlay runs with g.state='chest' (gamescreen.js:170), so it is covered too. The cost is that the dimmed build no longer shows behind the level-up cards. The cards already show LV, NEW WEAPON and pips, and the pause screen lists the build. It changes no gameplay and adds no Math.random. The positions of the orbs, labels and indicators stay the same, because they read the belt's layout, not whether it was drawn. Better fix: In public/js/game/hud.js Hud.draw, replace `drawLoadout(ctx, belt, t);` with:   `if (g.state !== 'levelup' && g.state !== 'chest' && g.state !== 'item') drawLoadout(ctx, belt, t); // under the cards, a chest (which shows its own build row) and a boss's trophy, as drawBanners does` Keep `belt` computed and passed to drawOrbs, drawCryptStrike, drawIndicators and drawMapHint as now, so nothing else moves. Optionally add `&& !g.showMap` so the open crypt/dungeon map box (drawn at up to H-24) stops clipping the plate rims; the other way is to cap the map's height in dungeon.js drawMinimap. Leave the overlays' own layout code (gamescreen.js level-up foot, COLLECT at H-22, chestLayout, trophy.js TAKE NONE) unchanged. That avoids the card shrinkage and text collision at 480x270, the collapse at 240x210, and edits inside the gamescreen.js file that has uncommitted work. Optional and separate: lift the level-up tip's default colour from '#5a6988' to '#8b9bb4' (gamescreen.js, `let tip, color = '#5a6988'`) for contrast on daytime snow and sand. That is a one-line change in the WIP file.

### hud-9 [confirmed] Orb glass: the sheen is a grey disc on empty glass, the locked orb is a see-through ghost, a low orb reads paler than a full one, and an empty orb shows a stray liquid sliver
- defect / code - public/js/game/hud.js:45 (GLASS=1 flat disc, radius 0.28R), 66-77 (sheen +35% toward white, also on EMPTY_RGB), 99-114 (level at 0% = cy+R with WAVE ±1.9; tone bands from depth/(2R)), 664-670 (locked orb at globalAlpha 0.45) [both critics: art + tech]
- Evidence: Sheen over EMPTY (13,11,20) → (98,96,101), a ~9 px mid-grey disc: c1280-01-play (fury 64%, x~1205, y~665), c1280-18-after-ult-b (fury 1%), tech-extra/hp-low-640.png (HP 26), tech-extra/fury0-640.png, h-crowded-640x360 (HP 21%), h-ultreceipt-240x210, h-bosscard-240x210. art-extra/lockedorb-240.png: grass shows through the locked orb's glass and rim, and the disc touches the padlock shackle. h-crowded-240x210: HP at 21% shows only light FOAM/TOP tones; MID and DEEP never appear. tech-extra/fury0-640.png / h-bossbar-640x360 (1212-1230, 722): a 4x1 pink FOAM sliver at the bottom of the 0% orb.
- The highlight is a uniformly lightened circle, which reads as a sticker over liquid and as a grey moon over empty glass. The 45%-alpha locked orb (including its INK ring and rims) breaks the opaque, self-coloured-edge HUD language. Tones are measured from the surface against the whole orb, so the liquid gets lighter as HP drops. At 0% the surface wave still lets liquid pixels through, and they wander from frame to frame.
- Fix: Make GLASS=1 a crescent: the R*0.28 circle minus the same circle offset 2 px toward the centre, plus a 2x2 core at +60%. Lighten liquid tones only, and use 6-8% (or a 1-px arc) over EMPTY. Draw the locked orb opaque: desaturated rims (#5a6988 lit, #3a4466 dark), no sheen, flat #1d1928 fill, then the padlock. Compute depth as (y-level)/(cy+R-level), so the bottom of any fill is DEEP. Scale the wave amplitude by min(1, shown*8), or skip liquid when shown < 0.02. Optionally pulse the HP orb rim below LOW_HP on ARRIVAL.beat, off under FLASHES: SOFT.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): All four claims hold, both in the code and in pixels. hud.js is committed and is not one of the other session's uncommitted files. I rendered the Orb class straight from the hud.js source on disk, with a 4x nearest-neighbour upscale: verify/orb/orb-sheet.png and verify/orb/proto.png. The measurements come from verify/orb/count.mjs. (1) The sheen (hud.js:45) is a flat 12x12 px disc (113 px; the critic's "~9 px" undercounts). Over EMPTY (13,11,20) its tone is (98,96,102), so a mid-grey moon sits on every empty or low orb. It shows in tech-extra/fury0-640.png and hp-low-640.png. At 64-75% fill the disc sits across the liquid's surface, so it comes out half grey and half pink. (2) The locked orb is drawn at globalAlpha 0.45 (hud.js:665), so grass shows through its glass, ink ring and rims (art-extra/lockedorb-240.png). The sheen disc runs into the top-left of the padlock's shackle. (3) The tone bands are depth/(2R) measured from the surface. HP 26% shows only FOAM and TOP (246,117,122 and 228,59,68), and HP 15% shows only FOAM. MID and DEEP never appear, so a low orb is paler than a full one. (4) At shown=0 the bottom liquid row (y=44, centre 44.5) passes the test 44.5 > 45 + WAVE whenever WAVE < -0.5. Over 600 frames, a 0% fury orb showed a pink FOAM sliver in 209 of them, up to 4 px, and the sliver moves around. A locked orb shows it too, because its fury is 0. On the fix: the crescent sheen, the 6-8% sheen over EMPTY, the opaque locked orb and the wave scaled by min(1, shown*8) are all sound. I prototyped them and they remove the grey moon and the sliver cleanly. The depth change is the problem. depth=(y-level)/(cy+R-level) packs DEEP and MID into the few pixels of a low fill. At 26% HP the liquid turns dark wine, (92,15,28) and (162,38,51), against near-black glass, and at 10% it is a thin dark line. That cuts the low-HP orb's contrast exactly when the player most needs to read it (LOW_HP is 0.1). The pale shallow fill is a reasonable reading of shallow liquid, so I would call that part taste, not a defect. The rim-pulse extra is out of scope. Better fix: Keep four of the critic's changes and drop the depth formula. (a) In the Orb constructor, light the sheen tone over EMPTY by about 0.08 instead of 0.35: `this.tones[i+3] = c + (255-c) * (k === EMPTY ? 0.08 : 0.35)`. Optionally make GLASS=1 a crescent: the R*0.28 circle minus the same circle moved 2 px toward the orb's centre, plus a small core. The prototype in verify/orb/proto.png reads as glass, not a sticker. (b) In render, multiply WAVE[x] by Math.min(1, this.shown * 8) so a 0% orb has no liquid. (c) Leave `depth = (y - level) / (2 * R)` as it is. If the owner wants richer low fills, use `/ Math.max(R, cy + R - level)` so that only orbs under half full compress their bands, and never let a 2-4 px fill go mostly DEEP. (d) Build the locked orb once as its own static opaque canvas, with no per-frame render and no globalAlpha. From SHAPE: INK ring, desaturated rims (#5a6988 lit, #3a4466 dark), a flat #1d1928 fill with no sheen or liquid, then the padlock drawn at full alpha. This needs either a rims parameter on Orb or a small separate builder, because RIMS is module-level.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): All four claims hold, both in the code on disk and in a side-by-side render (verify/orb-skeptic/compare.png). (1) The tones table applies the 35% sheen to EMPTY_RGB too, so every empty or low orb shows a flat grey disc about 12 px across, (98,96,101) on (13,11,20). It is the brightest thing in a 0% fury orb, which you see at every run start and after every ult, and in a low HP orb (fury0-640, hp-low-640). (2) At level = cy+R, the bottom liquid row y=43 passes y+0.5 > level+WAVE whenever WAVE < -1.5. WAVE swings about ±1.9, so a FOAM sliver blinks on and off at the bottom of an empty orb. The render shows it in 2 of 7 frames. (3) depth = (y-level)/(2R), so a fill under 30% only ever reaches FOAM/TOP: the HP orb at 21-26% is all light pink and never shows MID/DEEP. (4) The locked orb, used for early unlocks and for whole runs under the Oath of Silence (game.js:407), is drawn at globalAlpha 0.45. On the day maps (desert sand, frozen snow) it washes out to a tan or grey ghost and its rims nearly vanish. The HUD's other locked element, lockedSlot (hud.js:577), is an opaque dim frame plus padlock, so the see-through orb is the odd one out. None of this was a considered decision. The sheen and depth bands date from the initial commit 84963a5 and were never revisited. 58ead70 added the 0.45 alpha only to make the orb look "dim", and an opaque dark orb is dim too. No review in reviews/ covers the sheen, the locked orb or the bands (casino-review only confirms the fury % is numerically right). Nothing conflicts with a settled owner call. All changes are draw-only in Orb.render and drawOrbs, with no Math.random and no gameplay effect. hud.js is not among the other session's uncommitted files. The critic's fix works in the render: the crescent reads as glass, the 8% sheen leaves only a faint arc on empty glass, depth over the fill gives low orbs a dark bottom without striping, and the damped wave removes the sliver. Two parts are extra and should be cut. The 2x2 core at +60% needs a fourth GLASS state and a wider tones stride for little gain. The HP-rim pulse under LOW_HP is new scope that touches flashing rules. Better fix: Keep the fix to these few lines in hud.js. (a) Line 45: GLASS=1 only where Math.hypot(hx,hy) < R*0.28 && Math.hypot(hx-1.3, hy-1.5) >= R*0.28, with hx=dx+R*0.38 and hy=dy+R*0.42. That is the same circle minus itself shifted about 2 px toward the centre, giving a crescent. Drop the 2x2 +60% core. (b) Line 74: use 0.35 for the four liquid tones and 0.08 for the last entry (EMPTY_RGB). (c) Line 107: depth = (y - level) / Math.max(1, cy + R - level). (d) Line 103: multiply WAVE[x] by Math.min(1, this.shown * 8). (e) Lines 665-669: draw fImg opaque (remove globalAlpha 0.45) and keep the padlock. That matches lockedSlot's opaque dim frame plus padlock. If you also want the rims to read as locked, bake one cached canvas from SHAPE once: slate rims #5a6988/#3a4466, INK as is, LIQUID #1d1928. Skip the LOW_HP rim pulse; it is scope creep and touches the FLASHES rules. All of this is draw-only, with no RNG and no gameplay change.

### hud-10 [confirmed] Crypt/Dungeon map: a translucent, unframed box sized to the whole floor, drawn over the banners and across the loadout, with the explored room stuck in a corner
- defect / code - public/js/game/dungeon.js:1789-1836 (drawMinimap: 0.85-alpha #0d0b14 rect, mw=this.W*scale from H-60, centred at H/2+6, title via drawText); public/js/game/hud.js:400-403 (banners, then the map); hud.js:1067 (map hint at belt.y-15, 13 tall) [both critics: art + tech]
- Evidence: out-chrome/c1280-20-crypt-map.png: 'Vault of the Nameless', 'Your Excalibur alone', 'Press or hold X or Shift', the hero and the strike prompt ghost through the panel (440-860, 180-305). The explored room is a 150x150 block in the top-left corner of a panel spanning x290-990, y110-676, and the bottom edge cuts the plate headers at y≈672-680. cphone-20: same ghosting. h-dungeonmap-640x360: the fireball glows through. h-cryptmap-580x216: the panel covers the WEAPONS plate. c1280-19-crypt.png: the 'PRESS M FOR MAP' pill touches the plate rim (2 px gap, 548-735, 640-664).
- The box uses the floor's full grid and the screen height, not the tiles seen or the belt. Early in a crypt it is a big dark rectangle with a small map in one corner. It has no frame, unlike panel() and the plates, and the layers beneath read through its 85% opacity.
- Fix: Crop the map to the seen tiles' bounding box plus 2 tiles and centre it. Use an integer scale ≤ min((W-40)/bw, (belt.y-oy-8)/bh), fitted into [topLines()+14, belt.y-4]. Draw it inside panel(ctx,…,{title:name}) at full opacity. Skip drawBanners while g.showMap. Move the hint pill to belt.y-18 and update its keep box.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed this in the code and in the images. In dungeon.js:1789-1796 (HEAD, with no local edits to dungeon.js, hud.js or game.js) the box size is this.W*scale by this.H*scale, with scale = floor(min((W-40)/W_grid, (H-60)/H_grid)). It is centred on H/2+6 and filled with #0d0b14 at 0.85 alpha. There is no frame, and nothing checks the belt. At 640x360 a 70x56 crypt gets scale 5, so the box covers art y 44..330. That matches c1280-20, where the box spans 286-994 x 110-678 in the labelled image (354x284 art px). The belt starts at about y 325. In art-extra/cryptmap-bottom.png the box's lower edge cuts through the WEAPONS/POWERS plate rims and the top half of their lettering. On cphone-20, h-cryptmap-580x216 and h-cryptmap-240x210 the box swallows the whole WEAPONS plate. hud.js:400-403 draws the banners first and the map after them, at 85% opacity. That is why 'Vault of the Nameless', its subline, 'Your Excalibur alone' and 'Press or hold X or Shift' ghost through at about 15% in c1280-20 and cphone-20. The fireball glow in h-dungeonmap-640x360 shows through the same way. The explored room is a block of about 15x15 tiles in the panel's top-left corner, and the rest of the panel is empty. This is not a capture artefact. The script sets g.showMap 1.8 s after the crypt is entered. That is exactly when the game's own 'PRESS M FOR MAP' pill invites the player to press M, so real players will see the same thing. Two claims are overstated. First, the pill does not touch the plate: my 6x crop shows a 2 art px gap. Also mapHint() returns null while the map is open, so the pill and the map are never on screen together. That part is minor polish, not part of this defect. Second, the opacity is partly a design choice. The game keeps running under the map (gamescreen.js:153 only toggles it), so the world showing faintly through is not wrong in itself. The bright banner text is what makes it look broken. The proposed fix has four flaws: (1) 'Skip drawBanners while g.showMap' brings back the bug fixed on 27 October. Game.fadeReal keeps running b.t down because hintsHeld does not include showMap, so a first-run hint or a Codex-lock banner opened under the map would expire unseen and still count as told. (2) The integer scale fitted to the seen bounding box has no upper limit. A first crypt room of about 15+4 tiles would be drawn at about 15 px per tile at 640x360, three times the floor's scale of 5. It would then shrink and shift each time a tile is revealed, and that happens while the map is open, because the game runs under it. (3) Several markers can lie outside the seen tiles: the Dungeon Run guardian is drawn through walls while it hunts, the lit way's path and the broken-wall exit hole are found by pathing and holeSpot over unexplored floor, and the stairs are always drawn. With a crop to seen tiles only, these dots would land outside the panel, on the open game. (4) panel() fills with a stone texture unless o.fill is passed, and the map colours were tuned for the dark fill. Better fix: The fixes are in hud.js, dungeon.js and game.js, all at HEAD with no work in progress from other sessions. (a) Draw the big map from hud.js inside a rectangle that hud.js passes in. Its top is topLines()+14, which leaves room for the panel's title plate. Its bottom is belt.y-4. drawMinimap should use that rectangle instead of H-60 and H/2. (b) Keep one scale per floor: S0 = max(2, floor(min(rectW/this.W, rectH/this.H))). Do not zoom to the explored part. (c) Crop to the bounding box of the seen tiles plus every marker the map draws: stairs, entrance, exit hole, the lit way's path points, and the guardian or lords when they are shown. Pad it by 2 tiles and clamp it to the grid. Centre that box in the rectangle at scale S0. The panel then starts small and grows as the hero explores, but the tile size never changes. (d) Frame it with panel(ctx, x-3, y-3, w+6, h+6, { fill: '#0d0b14', title: name }), which replaces the drawText title at oy-14. Draw it fully opaque, or at 0.92 or more if the owner wants the game to show through faintly. (e) Stop the banner ghosting without losing hints. Either add `|| this.showMap` to Game.hintsHeld and to the `hidden` test in fadeReal, then skip drawBanners while showMap, or move the map call ahead of drawBanners. These are getters and drawing only, so no Math.random calls change. Leave the map-hint pill alone, or treat it as a separate polish item: it has a 2 px gap and never shows while the map is open.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real, and the code backs it up. git log -L shows drawMinimap has not changed since the first commit (84963a5, 2026-10-15). No comment says the 0.85-alpha, full-floor box was chosen on purpose, and no earlier review covers it (the 2026-11-07 review only lists the crypt map as a scene it captured; 2026-10-27 is about the guardian dot). None of the owner's settled calls cover the map. I ran drawMinimap's own formula against the real loadoutLayout for a 70x56 crypt floor (verify/minimap/overlap.mjs): - 640x360 (1280x720 and 1920x1080 screens): the box ends at y 328 and the belt starts at 323, so it covers 5 px of the plate headers. - 633x293 phone, touch: it covers 32 px, which is the whole WEAPONS/POWERS belt. cphone-20 shows this. - 580x216: it covers 15 px. - A Dungeon Run floor (76x64) at 640x360 just misses the belt. The arrival banners are big and bright, and they show during the same 12 seconds that the 'PRESS M FOR MAP' pill invites you to open the map. At 85% opacity they ghost through and repeat the crypt's name right under the map title (c1280-20). The explored room sitting in a corner of a mostly empty box is partly a matter of taste. The belt collision and the ghosting are clear defects that don't depend on taste. The game keeps running while the map is open (gamescreen.js frame calls g.update every frame), so a little see-through view may be wanted. The ghosting comes mainly from the banners, not from the alpha. The proposed fix doesn't change gameplay or randomness, but it isn't sound as written. It fits the cropped seen-area with the largest integer scale that fits. With one room seen, that is about 15 art px per tile at 640x360, and the map would then shrink and jump at each new room. The scale also depends on oy, which itself depends on the scale. panel() would put its stone texture behind the tiles unless it is given a plain fill. Hiding the banners while the map is open also hides Dungeon Run warnings, which is acceptable because pause already hides them. The fix bundles five changes where two of them solve the defect. None of these touch the other session's uncommitted files: hud.js and dungeon.js are not on its list. Better fix: The smallest fix has two parts: 1. **Fit the map above the belt.** At hud.js:403, pass the belt and the top line into the map: `g.world.drawMinimap(ctx, W, H, true, belt, this.topLines())`. In drawMinimap(big):    - Set top = topLines + 16 (this leaves room for the title) and bottom = belt.y - 4.    - Set scale = Math.max(1, Math.floor(Math.min((W - 40) / this.W, (bottom - top) / this.H))). This is computed from the full grid, so the scale stays the same for the whole floor and never zooms as you explore.    - Centre the box in [top, bottom], so it can never cross the belt. 2. **Stop the ghosting.** In hud.js drawBanners, add `|| g.showMap` to the early return. This follows the existing pattern: banners still run out under pause and cards. Optional polish, on top of those two: - Crop the drawn box to the seen tiles' bounding box plus 2 tiles (clamped to the grid), at that same fixed scale, and centre it. - Frame it with panel(ctx, x, y, w, h, {fill: '#0d0b14', title}) at alpha 1, in place of the bare 0.85 rect and the drawText title. - Move the map-hint pill from belt.y - 15 to belt.y - 18, in both drawMapHint (hud.js:1067) and its keep box (hud.js:1188).

### hud-11 [confirmed] Boss arrival letterbox cuts the orbs and plates in half and covers the clock and XP bar
- defect / code - public/js/game/hud.js:872-878 (letterbox bars in drawArrival, called at 401 after drawOrbs/drawLoadout) [both critics: art + tech]
- Evidence: out-chrome/c1280-14-boss-card.png: the top bar (to y~108) hides the XP bar, clock and LV. The bottom bar (from y~655) leaves only the orbs' top arcs (20-90 and 1190-1260, 640-655) and hides the plates. out-headless/h-bosscard-240x210.png: the 25-px bars cut the HP orb through '85' and the fury orb through '0%', and slice the POWERS plate (570-860, 1060-1130).
- The bars are painted over the finished HUD at a height unrelated to it. HUD elements are cut along a straight line, which reads as a clipping error rather than a deliberate frame.
- Fix: Choose one: (a) draw the bars before the HUD chrome, so the HUD sits whole on top; or (b) fade drawXp/drawTop/drawOrbs/drawLoadout/indicators with globalAlpha 1-k, where k is the bar progress at hud.js:873, and restore them as the bars retract. (b) is more cinematic.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real. Hud.draw (hud.js:381-401) draws the XP bar, loadout, top line and orbs first and calls drawArrival last. In drawArrival, lines 872-877 fill two solid black bars of round(H*0.12*ease(k)) over everything. For a night boss (game.js:1344, letterbox = mode !== 'ranked') they stay up about 1.2 s: 0.15 s in, 0.8 s held, 0.25 s out (arrival.js). Neither capture faked anything: both spawn the boss through the real spawnBoss -> bossIntro path, and the Chrome shot is the live game. Measured against the pixels: - c1280-14: H=360 art px, so the bar is 43 art px = 86 screen px. The image has a 22-px label strip, so the top bar ends at y~108 and the bottom bar starts at ~656. That matches the critic exactly. - At 1280 the clock, XP bar and LV are hidden completely by the top bar (a clean hide). At the bottom, only the orbs' top arcs show above the bar: the red arc at x~20-90 and the orange arc at x~1190-1260, y~640-655. - h-bosscard-240x210 (x6): the bar cuts the HP orb through "85" and the fury orb through "0%", and cuts the POWERS plate just under its title. - h-bosscard-480x270: the bar cuts the WEAPONS and POWERS plate titles through the middle of their letters. A straight cut through text and orbs reads as a clipping error, and it happens at every night boss's entrance. So this is a defect, not taste. Why the proposed fix is not sound as written: the preferred option (b) is to fade the chrome with globalAlpha = 1-k. That cannot work as a plain outer alpha. hud.js has 23 lines that set `ctx.globalAlpha = 1`, including inside drawXp (about lines 452-466) and drawOrbs (669, 677, 686, 731, 739). The locked orb also sets an absolute 0.45. Each of these would snap the chrome back to full opacity partway through drawing. Option (a) does work, but drawArrival would have to be split. Its stir vignette and bars would draw before the chrome, and its name card after the chrome, so the card still sits on top. Better fix: Split the bars out of drawArrival into a small drawLetterbox(ctx, W, H) that returns its progress k. For the minimal fix, call it in Hud.draw before drawXp/drawLoadout/drawTop/drawOrbs, and leave the name card in drawArrival where it is now (line 401). The chrome then sits whole on top of the bars. For the cinematic version, retract the chrome instead of fading it, using transforms. globalAlpha cannot be used because the HUD's draws keep resetting it to 1, but no HUD draw calls setTransform, so a translate survives: - Wrap drawXp + drawTop in ctx.save(); ctx.translate(0, -topH*k) ... restore, where topH = lineBottom(8) + 2. - Wrap drawLoadout + drawOrbs in ctx.translate(0, (H - min(belt.y, orbTop))*k), so the bottom chrome slides fully off-screen under the bar and slides back as the bars retract. If a fade is really wanted, draw the chrome into a cached offscreen canvas only while k > 0 and composite it once with globalAlpha 1-k.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The finding is confirmed. In hud.js draw(), drawArrival (line 401) runs after drawXp, drawLoadout, drawTop, drawOrbs and drawIndicators. Its letterbox bars at 872-878 are black fillRects 0.12*H tall, so they land on top of the finished HUD. I re-rendered it headless from the production Hud. At 640x360 (a 1280x720 window), the bottom bar leaves only the top arcs of the orbs and hides the WEAPONS/POWERS plates. The top bar hides the XP bar, the clock and LV. At 240x210 the bars cut through the HP '85', the fury '0%' and the POWERS plate. Both read as a clipping error. Nothing marks this as intended. Commit 8e0220b (the letterbox was ported from 8002) only says "its name card shows big in its colour with letterbox bars", and no comment asks for the HUD to be covered. None of the files in reviews/ mention the letterbox, and the owner's settled calls don't cover it. I rate it polish, not defect. It lasts about 1 s per night boss (in for 0.15 s, held to 0.8 s, out by 1.05 s), and it sits inside a deliberate cinematic. Still, it is a clear quality gain at no cost. A side gain: the HP orb stays readable while the boss arrives. Fix (a) checked: I monkey-patched Hud in a scratch copy of the headless script and rendered at 640x360, 480x270, 320x240 and 240x210. Output: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/hud/verify/verify/letterbox/h-bosscard-*-fixa.png. The orbs, plates, XP bar, clock and LV now sit whole on the black bands and look framed on purpose. The name card stays on top. Bars drawn this way cover only the world. Fix (b) as written would not work. drawTop and drawOrbs set absolute globalAlpha values (0.45, 0.85, 1, the 0.45+0.4*sin blink), and loadout.js sets .4 and then 1. A wrapping globalAlpha of 1-k would be overwritten partway through, and parts of the HUD would pop back to full opacity. Making it work needs an offscreen HUD layer. It would also hide the HP orb during the arrival. Neither option changes gameplay or calls Math.random. hud.js is not among the other session's uncommitted files. Better fix: Take option (a) and skip (b). Move the letterbox block (hud.js:872-878) into a small method such as drawLetterbox(ctx, W, H). It should use the same k and bh math, including `gone`. Call it first in Hud.draw(), before drawRingWarnings, drawUltLaunch and drawXp, and delete the bars from drawArrival. drawArrival keeps the stir vignette and the name card, and still uses c.letterbox for the size-3 name, so the card stays drawn last, on top. It is about 10 moved lines, with no gameplay change and no RNG. Don't do (b): the HUD functions set absolute globalAlpha values internally, so fading them needs an offscreen layer, and it would hide HP during the boss's arrival.

### hud-12 [confirmed] The chest screen shows the build twice in two skins: legacy blue-steel slots with pips at the top left, and the dimmed bronze plates at the bottom
- defect / code - public/js/ui/gamescreen.js:1159-1167 (g.hud.drawSlots over the dim); public/js/game/hud.js:475-604 (drawSlots/slot: #181425/#3a4466 frames, yellow pips) vs public/js/game/loadout.js:77-124 (plate/cell: bronze rim, rank numbers) [both critics: art lens rated polish, tech lens folded it into the overlay finding as a defect]
- Evidence: out-chrome/c1280-07-chest-landed.png and c1280-09-chest-evolution-end.png: a top-left strip (x5-220, y40-120) of blue-grey slots with yellow pips and padlocks, while the real plates (x430-830, y668-735) sit at 30% brightness. c240-sheet1 'ELITE CHEST REELS': same.
- The flash that marks a raised slot plays on a strip the player never sees during play, in a different skin (pips instead of rank numbers, blue steel instead of bronze). The chest's payoff therefore does not land on the HUD the player knows.
- Fix: Over the dim, draw drawLoadout(g.hud.loadout(W,H)) instead of drawSlots. Then apply litMark to the cells the reveal raised (cell x/y = left+(i%cols)*19, y+11+row*20), or reskin the strip with loadout.js cell()/plate(). Keep drawGold. This removes the last legacy slot() use outside the evolution path.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real, and the capture script did not cause it. In code, Hud.draw (hud.js:381-404) only draws the bottom belt via drawLoadout. Commit cb5a89b (21 Nov, the loadout redesign) took out the HUD's own drawSlots call. ChestOverlay.draw still calls g.hud.drawSlots + drawGold over the 0.7 dim (gamescreen.js:1159-1167, from 7efa75b, 28 Oct). drawSlots is now the only caller of slot(), lockedSlot(), evoMark() and litMark(), so that skin appears nowhere except on the chest screen. In pixels, c1280-01-play has no top-left strip, but c1280-07 and c1280-09 do. I made crops in verify/chest-slots/. topleft-x3.png shows bright blue-steel slots with yellow pips and padlocked relic slots. belt-x3.png shows the bronze WEAPONS/POWERS plates with rank numbers at about 30% brightness. The SLOT_LIT flash plays only on the stale strip, so the chest's payoff never lands on the HUD the player knows. The two skins also count differently: the pips show the learned level (h.level), while the belt shows level+rankBonus and effectivePassiveRank. So the same weapon can read differently in the two places. The 240x210 sheet shows the same thing. The capture used openChest directly, but that runs the same overlay code a real chest does. About the other session: gamescreen.js is in their uncommitted list, but their diff only adds 3 lines (an import and two constructor lines, around lines 33 and 42). The hunk at 1159-1167 is committed code and they have not touched it. Why the fix as written is not sound: drawing the whole belt in full light over the dim ignores the chest screen's layout. chestLayout reserves only CHEST_FOOT = 52 px (the gold line and COLLECT), not the belt. At 640x360 art px the belt runs y 323-357, with weapon cells at x 228-341 and power cells from 355. COLLECT sits at x 280-360, y 338-352. So the button and its arrow already cover weapon cells 4-6 and the first power cell; belt-x3.png shows this, and a lit belt would make it worse. At 240x210 the belt stacks into two plates about 70 px tall (y 137-207). It sits right under "Plate Armor LV 3", "+36 GOLD" and COLLECT (c240-sheet1, ELITE CHEST LANDED), so a full-light belt would collide with that text. litMark is also drawn for the old 17x17 slot with a pip row: its frame runs x-1..x+17 and y-1..y+19. On an 18x18 belt cell it would overlap the cell's right column and hang below it. The claim "the last legacy slot() use outside the evolution path" is also wrong: drawSlots is the only caller of slot(), and there is no separate evolution path. Better fix: 1. Stop drawing the old strip over the dim. In ChestOverlay.draw, replace g.hud.drawSlots(...) with a redraw of only the belt cells the reveal has just raised. Keep drawGold, because it is where the chest's coins fly. To do this:    - Have loadout.js export a cell-position helper, e.g. loadoutCell(layout, groupName, i) returning {x, y}, with x = left + (i % cols) * 19 and y = group.y + 11 + floor(i / cols) * 20.    - Export cell() too, or a drawLoadoutCell().    - Map row 'w' to the WEAPONS group's index i, and row 'r' to POWERS index i. This works because held.relics lead the powers list in loadoutItems.    - For each lit entry, draw that cell (with its plate area) over the dim, plus an 18-px light mark: frame at x-1..x+18 and y-1..y+18 (the frame lines land in the 1-px gaps between cells), and a wash over x+1..x+16, alpha k. Respect softFlash as litMark does now.    This puts the flash on the belt the player knows. Because only a raised cell is relit, and only for SLOT_LIT, the chest text and COLLECT never compete with a permanently bright belt on small screens. 2. If the owner would rather keep the whole build lit over the dim (the 28 Oct intent), do two things first. Make chestLayout reserve the belt: replace the fixed CHEST_FOOT with H - belt.y + 22 + 4 for the gold line, falling back to item scale k = 1 as now. Then draw COLLECT above the belt (y = belt.y - 18) rather than at H-22. 3. COLLECT already covers weapon cells 4-6 and the first power cell at 640x360, so it should move above belt.y either way. 4. Afterwards, delete the dead code: drawSlots, slot, lockedSlot, evoMark, litK, slotsLeftOf, and the evoLogo cols argument. The belt sits at the foot, so EVOLUTION! can no longer cover it. 5. Separate note for the owner, not part of this fix: the top-right gold counter (drawGold) is also shown only on the chest screen. drawTop stopped drawing gold in play, so the coins fly to a counter the player never sees otherwise. The surrounding code is committed, but gamescreen.js has the other session's uncommitted import and constructor lines, so stage by hunk.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real. It is a leftover from the loadout redesign, not a deliberate choice. Commit 7efa75b (2026-10-28) added g.hud.drawSlots over the chest's dim so that "the build's slots ... in full light" would match the HUD under the dim, which showed them at a third of their light. That still holds as the comment at gamescreen.js:1151-1157. Then cb5a89b (2026-11-21, "redesign the combat loadout HUD") moved the in-play build to the bronze WEAPONS/POWERS plates at the bottom (hud.js:383-388, drawLoadout). It left gamescreen.js:1165 calling the old drawSlots. A grep shows that call is now the only caller of drawSlots, slot, lockedSlot, litMark and slotsLeftOf. So the chest screen draws a second copy of the build at the top left, in the retired skin (blue-steel #3a4466 frames, yellow pips, padlocks), and the SLOT_LIT flash plays on that copy. Meanwhile the real HUD plates sit at 30% brightness under the dim. The screenshots c1280-07-chest-landed.png and c1280-09-chest-evolution-end.png confirm this exactly. The earlier reviews do not settle it the other way: 2026-10-27-review.md line 298 is the proposal that created this feature, and its intent ("lands on the HUD the player knows") is the thing the leftover now breaks. It goes against none of the owner's settled calls. It is not a gameplay bug, but it is a visible inconsistency on a payoff screen, so I rate it defect, not polish. The proposed fix is sound in substance. It only changes drawing, uses no Math.random and doesn't touch gameplay or hero looks. The index mapping holds: revealOn returns {row:'w',i} as an index into held.weapons and {row:'r',i} into held.relics, and loadoutItems(g, held) lists held.weapons first (then null fillers, then gear weapons) and held.relics first in powers. So 'w' i maps to cell i of the WEAPONS group and 'r' i to cell i of the POWERS group. g.hud.loadout(W,H) already reads hud.held, so the copy over the dim matches the build under it exactly. Two details need care. (1) litMark is sized for the old 17px slot with pips: its frame runs from y-1 to y+19. A loadout cell is 18x18, so the mark needs a 20x20 frame round the cell (x-1..x+18) and a 16x16 wash, or the flash is a pixel or two off. (2) The cell position has to use each group's own x, y and cols, because the groups stack and wrap on narrow screens (width < 228 puts the belt above the orbs). Caveat, not a blocker: COLLECT is drawn at H-22, over the middle of the belt on desktop (visible already in the screenshots, over the dimmed plates). The flashes fire before COLLECT appears, so the payoff still lands. The final frame will show the button over full-light plates, though, and moving it would mean growing CHEST_FOOT. That is a separate layout follow-up. Note: gamescreen.js has another session's uncommitted hunks (demoChoice import, and app.choice in enter()). They don't overlap lines 1151-1167, but stage only your own hunk. Better fix: Keep the drawing and the soft-flash logic in hud.js by adding one Hud method, for example drawBelt(ctx, W, H, t, lit). It does: const belt = this.loadout(W, H); drawLoadout(ctx, belt, t); Then, for each lit entry, it finds the group (row 'w' → name 'WEAPONS', 'r' → 'POWERS') and the cell: left = group.x + Math.floor((group.w - group.cols*19)/2) cx = left + (i % group.cols)*19 cy = group.y + 11 + Math.floor(i / group.cols)*20 It then draws a cell-sized mark: a 20x20 frame at cx-1, cy-1 in white (#fee761 under softFlash) at alpha k, and a 16x16 wash at cx+1, cy+1 at 0.45*k (FLASH_SOFT*k when soft). Simplest is to make litMark take a size and pass 18. In gamescreen.js:1165, replace `g.hud.drawSlots(ctx, g.player, lit, evoLogo ? slotsLeftOf(evoLogo.x) : undefined)` with `g.hud.drawBelt(ctx, W, H, t, lit)`. Keep the `if (!ceremony)` guard and drawGold. Drop the slotsLeftOf argument: EVOLUTION! sits at y 10, and the belt is at the screen's foot. Optionally delete the now-dead drawSlots/slot/lockedSlot/evoMark/litK/slotsLeftOf, and the slotsLeftOf import in gamescreen.js. As a separate follow-up, consider lifting COLLECT above belt.y the way drawFinish does at gamescreen.js:207, which means adding the belt height to CHEST_FOOT. Stage only this hunk in gamescreen.js: the file carries another session's uncommitted demo-mode edits.

### hud-13 [confirmed] Rotated LUCKY! and level-chain stamps resample pixel text into jagged mixels
- defect / code - public/js/ui/gamescreen.js:686-690 (LUCKY! ctx.rotate(-0.12)), 700-704 (chain stamp ctx.rotate(0.12)), with non-integer pop k=2.2→1 [tech critic]
- Evidence: tech-extra/lucky2-1920.png (crop of raw/c1920-04-levelup-ready.png x1360-1660, y60-190): the 2x text strokes break into stair steps of uneven width. The U and C have notched outlines, the ! is split, and 1-UI-px steps sit beside 2-UI-px pixels.
- Rotating an x2 pixel-text canvas by ~7° with smoothing off gives irregular pixel sizes on a stamp meant to look celebratory.
- Fix: Draw the stamps unrotated, or pre-bake the tilt as an integer shear at 1x (shift each column down 1 px every ~8 columns), then draw at x2 so every pixel stays 2x2. Replace the 2.2→1 pop with whole steps (x3 → x2).
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): Confirmed in the real Chrome capture and in a headless re-render. The level-up screen draws into the low-res W×H UI canvas, which has smoothing off (view.js: "HUD/menus are drawn into a W×H canvas and scaled up by S"; uctx.imageSmoothingEnabled = false). In public/js/ui/gamescreen.js:682-705 both stamps are drawn at x2 (cw = c.width*2*k) and then rotated by ∓0.12 rad, so nearest-neighbour resampling happens at UI resolution and only then is the canvas blown up ×S. I sampled raw/c1920-04-levelup-ready.png on its UI grid (S=4, verify/lucky/lucky-ui-grid.png). The LUCKY! strokes, which should be 2 UI px everywhere, show 1-UI-px stair steps, single 1-px slivers (a lone green px under the U, 1-px pieces in the C and K), a 5-wide run where 6 is due, and a broken "!". The chain stamp in raw/c1920-05-levelup-chain.png ("2 of 3", rotated +0.12; verify/lucky/chain-ui-grid.png) has the same fault: the 3's top bar drops by 1 px, the f's crossbar is in 1-px pieces and the o's base is stepped. My headless re-render with the game's own textCanvas (verify/lucky/variants.png, z-now.png) reproduces it exactly. So it is not a capture artefact: the capture is a plain page.screenshot of the production page. This is not one of the settled calls, and no earlier review mentions it. The pop part of the claim is real but only briefly visible. k = 2.2 - 1.2*outBack(t/0.25) is fractional for 0.25 s and dips to about 0.88 at the overshoot. At rest k=1, so the lasting defect is the rotation alone. Note: gamescreen.js is on the other session's uncommitted list. Its hunk there only adds the demoChoice import and guard (lines 33 and 42-43) and does not touch the stamp code, so a fix there must be staged hunk-by-hunk. Better fix: The core of the proposal works. I rendered it (verify/lucky/z-shear.png): shift the 1x textCanvas column by column, 1 px every 8 columns (slope 0.125, about the current 0.12 rad), up to the right for LUCKY! and down to the right for the chain stamp. Then drawImage it at exactly x2 with no ctx.rotate. Every pixel stays 2x2, the tilt still reads, and the outline stays closed because whole columns move together. Keep the change small and cache the result: - Add a helper `tilted(c, dir)` that builds a canvas c.height + floor((c.width-1)/8) tall and copies column x to dy = floor(x/8) (or n - that). Cache it by the textCanvas key or by canvas in a WeakMap, so nothing is rebuilt each frame. - Use the sheared canvas's height when centring (cy and ch) and its width in the `fits` test. It is n px taller than c. Per-glyph baseline steps also keep the grid, but they need per-character advance and padding work and break the "2 of 3" spacing. The column shear is simpler. Drawing the stamps unrotated is the zero-risk fallback. Treat the pop change as optional and separate. It only lasts 0.25 s, and the LEVEL UP! logo beside the stamps already pops with a fractional factor (pop 1.6→1, and s is fractional on narrow screens). Changing only the stamps to x3→x2 steps would be inconsistent. Either leave the pop alone, or snap both the logo and the stamps to whole factors in the same pass.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding holds. In public/js/ui/gamescreen.js:683-705 the LUCKY! and chain stamps draw a textCanvas at x2 onto the UI canvas, which is at art-pixel resolution and later upscaled by S with nearest-neighbour. The code rotates that x2 canvas by plus or minus 0.12 rad with smoothing off. The rotation stays on screen for as long as the player is choosing; it is not only part of the pop. I sampled the 1920 Chrome capture at UI-pixel resolution (verify/lucky/lucky-ui-grid.png). Strokes are 1 UI px wide in some places and 2 UI px in others, the outlines are notched and the ! is split. "2 of 3" in raw/c1920-05-levelup-chain.png looks wobbly at 1:1 right beside the crisp LEVEL UP! title (verify/stamp-skeptic/chain-zoom.png). The UI elsewhere keeps pixels whole on purpose: view.js draws overlays in "whole multiples", and textCanvas only ever draws at integer scales. Why it is not ruled out: - None of the owner's settled calls covers it. - No earlier review in reviews/ mentions LUCKY, the stamps or rotated text. - git log shows the tilt was added in 1c84500 (LUCKY!) and ffafb3a (chain stamp). The comments call it "stamped beside the title", so the tilt is deliberate, but the mixels are only a side effect of how it is drawn. A fix should keep the tilt, which counts against simply drawing the stamps unrotated. I ranked it a defect, not a bug: it is cosmetic only, persists while the player chooses, and appears on screens meant to celebrate. Problems with the proposed fix: 1. The integer column shear keeps every pixel 2x2, but it puts a jog inside the glyphs. In my comparison (verify/stamp-skeptic/compare.png and zoom-fixes.png) the base of the 2, the 3 and the Y's arms get broken horizontal strokes. 2. Drawing the stamps unrotated drops the stamp look the code comments describe. 3. Replacing the 2.2-to-1 pop with whole steps is unnecessary scope that changes how the animation feels. It is a 0.25 s transient, and the title right next to it does the same thing (a 1.6-to-1 pop, plus a fractional s on narrow screens). What is safe either way: nothing touches gameplay or Math.random, and no hero looks are involved. Note for staging: gamescreen.js is one of the files with another session's uncommitted work. Its current diff is +3 lines elsewhere in the file, not in these lines, so stage this change by hunk. Better fix: Keep the tilt, but bake it at 1x as a stepped baseline per glyph, so every glyph stays whole and the x2 draw keeps every pixel at 2x2. The cleanest of the four variants I tried is the per-glyph step column in verify/stamp-skeptic/compare.png and zoom-fixes.png. This changes only the stamp draw code in gamescreen.js. Add a small cached helper (makeCanvas and ctx2d from ../engine/util.js): const tilted = new Map(); function tiltCanvas(str, colors, dir) { // dir -1: right end up (LUCKY!), +1: left end up (chain)   const key = dir + '|' + colors + '|' + str; let o = tilted.get(key); if (o) return o;   const full = textCanvas(str, colors, '#181425'), chars = [...str], t = Math.tan(0.12);   const drift = Math.round((full.width - 1) * t);   o = makeCanvas(full.width, full.height + drift); const g = ctx2d(o);   let x = 0;   for (let i = 0; i < chars.length; i++) {     const gc = textCanvas(chars[i], colors, '#181425'), dy = Math.round((x + gc.width / 2) * t);     g.drawImage(gc, x, dir < 0 ? drift - dy : dy);     x = textWidth(chars.slice(0, i + 1).join('')) + 1; // next pen; each glyph canvas carries its 1-px pad   }   tilted.set(key, o); return o; } At lines 685 and 696, use tiltCanvas('LUCKY!', ..., -1) and tiltCanvas(stamp, ..., 1) in place of textCanvas. Delete the two ctx.rotate(+/-0.12) lines. Leave the 2.2-to-1 pop as it is, since it is transient and matches the title's own pop. The width and fits maths are unchanged, and the canvas is only `drift` px taller, centred on the same cy.

### hud-14 [confirmed] At 240x210 the stacked loadout fills 43% of the screen and covers the hero
- defect / code - public/js/game/loadout.js:52-75 (aboveOrbs only when width < 228; between the orbs room = width-112, so 6 columns); camera centred at H/2 in public/js/game/game.js:432-433 [tech critic; the art critic noted it under the layer-order finding]
- Evidence: out-headless/h-crowded-240x210.png: 6 weapons + 10 powers give WEAPONS h34 + POWERS h54, stacked y117-207 (90 px). The hero's HP bar is at the WEAPONS rim (660-780, 700-730), and his legs are under the banner sub-line. The hero is completely hidden by the plates and banner. h-early-240x210 (3 weapons, no powers) is fine.
- With a full build on a short screen, the belt grows up past the screen centre where the camera keeps the hero.
- Fix: When belt.y < H/2+24, centre the hero in the free band: cam.y += (H/2 - (topLines()+belt.y)/2)/z (i.e. offset by (H-belt.y)/2), in Game.syncCam or the follow. Alternatively, cap POWERS to one row at short heights with a '+N' cell, or pick the aboveOrbs layout by height as well as width.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The defect is real, but it only shows up at very small screens, and the critic overstated part of it. Code: at 240x210, loadout.js computes room 128, so WEAPONS (6) and POWERS (10) stack: belt.y = 207 - 90 = 117. The camera aims at p.y - 8 (game.js:3033, zoom 0.8), which puts the hero's feet at about y 111. The world HP bar (hud.js:1003, drawn after drawLoadout) lands on y 114-118, on the WEAPONS rim. Renders without banners are in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/hud/verify/belt-hero/ (240x210-keyboard.png, 240x210-keyboard-south.png, 240x210-touch.png): - Keyboard, standing: the body is visible but stands on the plate, and the HP bar overlaps the rim. Everything south of the hero is hidden. - Keyboard, walking south: the camera lags about 10 px, so the feet and HP bar go behind the WEAPONS header. - Touch: bottom is H-25, so belt.y is 95, above screen centre. Only the helmet shows and the HP bar sits on the weapon icons. - With 13 or more powers (passives + blessings + skull + boons + timed buffs), the belt covers the body even on keyboard. Overstated: in the cited h-crowded-240x210.png the hero is "completely hidden" mostly by two stacked transient banners, not by the plates. And the belt is 43% of the height, about 22% of the area. Exposure (grid.mjs, same folder, using view.js S = round(min/300)): every phone (W633xH293 landscape, W293xH633 portrait) and every common desktop window is clear by 40-200 px. Only S=1 windows under about 437 device px wide and 215 tall (embeds, odd zoom) hit it, plus touch at 640x480 with 13+ powers (by 1 px). The repo does treat 240x210 as supported: hud-review.mjs, and the hud.test.js full-belt test even uses touch at 240x210. That test checks only that the belt fits, not that the hero stays clear. So this is real but rare: polish. The proposed fix is only partly sound: 1. "In Game.syncCam" is wrong. syncCam only sizes the camera and runs in both update() and draw(), so adding to cam.y there would pile up each call. Only the follow target ty at game.js:3033 is a valid place. 2. topLines() does not exist. The "i.e. offset by (H-belt.y)/2" wording is a different amount from the formula before it (the formula's sign is right). 3. The camera then has to read the HUD belt every frame. The view would also shift whenever a timed shrine buff adds or removes a POWERS row. 4. The "pick aboveOrbs by height" option makes it worse. aboveOrbs sets bottom = H-69, so at 240x210 the belt would start near y 71. Better fix: Keep the fix inside loadoutLayout so the camera, the seeded tests and the other HUD anchors stay untouched (ultLabelY, markerBand, drawMapHint and drawCryptStrike already read belt.y): - Add a hero clearance line, about clear = Math.ceil(H/2) + 24. This covers the feet at H/2+6.4, the HP bar, and the camera lag when walking south. - When the groups are stacked and bottom - h < clear, first drop the 'WEAPONS'/'POWERS' header captions (HEADER 11 to about 2). - If it still doesn't fit, limit POWERS to the rows that fit above `clear` (at least one row). Make its last cell a '+N' count; the pause menu's pause_powers list already shows every power. - At 240x210 this gives belt.y of 129 or more on keyboard (WEAPONS + one POWERS row of 5 + '+5') and about 137 on touch with no captions. - Extend the hud.test.js full-belt test to assert belt.y >= H/2 + 24 at 240x210, for both keyboard and touch. If the owner would rather keep every power visible, centre the hero in the free band instead. Do it only in the follow target: ty += (H/2 - (topHud + belt.y)/2) / zoom when belt.y < H/2 + 24, never in syncCam. That costs a camera shift whenever the belt changes rows.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed this with my own renders in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/hud/verify/loadout/ (hero-belt.mjs, belt-*.png, sweep.mjs). I used the crowded build (6 weapons, 10 powers) with the banners cleared, at 240x210 S1: - Keyboard: the belt starts at y117 and is 90 px tall (43% of H). The hero's feet are at y113 and his HP bar at y116, so he stands on the WEAPONS rim and nothing south of him is visible across the middle 128 px. - Touch: the belt starts at y95. The plates cover his legs and his HP bar is drawn over the WEAPONS cells. - 300x250 S2 touch (a 600x500 window) does the same: the hero stands on the plate. The critic's evidence overstates it a little. In h-crowded-240x210 the hero is mostly hidden by the two banners that time out. Without them, the plates cover him fully only on touch. On keyboard they sit right under his feet. Nothing shows this is deliberate: - The belt was redesigned only on 2026-11-21 (cb5a89b). No commit message or review settles short screens. - The owner's own test 'the full bottom loadout fits both orbs, side insets and the touch-button strip' runs exactly [240,210,touch] with 6+12 slots. It checks the orbs and the touch strip but never the hero, and in that exact case the belt top is at y95, above the hero. - tools/hud-review.mjs also renders 240x210, so the owner treats that size as supported. It is not a rejected edge case. It is still narrow. A sweep of window sizes through view.js's S rule shows it only at art H up to about 295 and art W up to about 310. That means small desktop windows under 450 px tall, or near-square ones of about 450-600 px. The aboveOrbs case (W<228) reaches it too. Phones in either orientation, full-screen desktop and 480x270/640x360 are unaffected. So it is a defect, but a rare one. On the proposed fix: - The camera idea is the right one. It needs no change to the HUD layout or the hero looks, and it adds no Math.random call. - Game.syncCam is the wrong place: it only sets cam.S, cam.W and cam.H. The follow target is in Game.update (game.js:3033, ty = p.y - 8 - c.H/2) and in snapCam (2101-2105). game.js:432-433 is only the run-start snap. - The critic's gate ('when belt.y < H/2+24, centre in the free band') jumps the camera about 30 px at the threshold. - cam.y also feeds the spawner (Forest.edgePoint and Spawner.inView), so spawns follow the shifted view. They stay just off-screen, which is correct, and the change only happens at the affected sizes. At the 480x270 the tests use the lift is 0, so seeded replays are unaffected. - The alternatives are worse. Choosing aboveOrbs by height lifts the belt about 66 px higher and breaks the test's between-orbs assertion. A '+N' cell hides powers. - None of the files involved are in the other session's uncommitted work. Better fix: Put the shift in the camera follow, not in syncCam, and make it continuous. Add a helper to Game: beltLift() { const b = this.hud.loadout(view.W, view.H); return Math.max(0, view.H / 2 + 40 - b.y) * view.S / this.cam.S; }. It keeps about 40 UI px of ground visible below the hero. Use it in Game.update's follow (game.js ~3033): ty = p.y - 8 - c.H / 2 + this.beltLift(). Use it in snapCam as well (c.y = p.y - 8 - c.H / 2 + this.beltLift()). The lift is 0 at every normal size (480x270, 640x360, phones), so seeded tests and their spawns are unchanged. It grows smoothly through the existing damp as powers are added, with no threshold jump. The HUD's HP bar, name tags and markers read the same cam, so they stay aligned. There is no new Math.random call, and loadout.js and its tests are unchanged. Centring the hero in the free band, (topLines()+belt.y)/2, without the critic's gate is a fine variant of the same formula, as long as it uses max(0, ...) and the same continuous form.

### hud-15 [confirmed] Anti-aliased vector shapes and flat pills in an otherwise crisp pixel HUD (edge pointers, touch stick, aim chevron, ring warning, map/strike pills); the clock sits 1 px above LV
- polish / code - public/js/game/hud.js:1194-1222 (pointer: rotated path triangles), 1318-1345 (touch stick ctx.arc at 0.35/0.7 alpha; pause/MAP flat rects), 1042-1055 (aim chevron, 1.5 px stroke), 1152-1166 (ring-warning ellipse, lineWidth 2), 1027-1035 and 1064-1071 (strike and PRESS M FOR MAP pills: plain #181425 rects); clock hud.js:613 (y8) vs LV hud.js:468 (y9) [both critics: art + tech]
- Evidence: art-extra/pointer-240.png, tech-extra/arrow-240.png, tech-extra/arrow-1280.png (raw/c1280-01-play.png 200-300, 640-720): half-tone blended edges with a ragged dark outline, next to crisp 8x8 marker icons. art-extra/stick-640.png / h-touch-640x360 (75-165, 560-645): a soft grey disc with an anti-aliased rim and no outline. c1280-19-crypt.png: 'PRESS M FOR MAP' (548-732, 638-664) and the 'X / SHIFT · READY' pill are flat boxes with no rim, above the bronze plates. tech-extra/toprow.mjs on h-early-240x210: the clock top is at row 8 and LV at row 9, and the clock's outline row 7 touches the XP bar frame (rows 0-6).
- The HUD's standard is opaque, hard-edged pixels with self-coloured rims. Canvas path primitives bring in soft sub-pixel edges and semi-transparent fills that look like debug overlays. Separately, the top-row labels are off by one.
- Fix: Bake the pointer in 16 directions (or 8 plus mirrors) with a #181425 rim: draw into an offscreen 13x13 canvas, threshold alpha at 128, add a 1-px outline, and pick by angle, as marks() does. Do the same for the chevron (8 directions). Build the stick from ringSprite(22) plus an opaque pixel-disc knob, and the ring warning from ringSprite or a baked pixel ellipse. Draw the pause/MAP buttons and pills with loadout.js plate() (stepped caps, bronze rim). Draw the clock at y=9.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): I confirmed it in the real Chrome captures, the headless captures and the code. - **Edge pointer (hud.js:1194-1222).** In raw/c1280-01-play.png at (228-256, 648-666), each HUD pixel is 2x2. The red arrow has #7e2734, an exact 50% blend of #e43b44 and #181425, plus #b1313c and #4b1e2d at its edges. Its dark rim is only partly covered (#171b25, #181c26). art-extra/pointer-240.png shows the same blocky half-tones at a larger scale. A rotated path fill always anti-aliases on the low-res HUD canvas. - **Aim chevron (hud.js:1042-1055).** In raw/c1280-19-crypt.png at (598-616, 344-364), the yellow #fee761 stroke is fringed with the blends #c4b252, #8b7d43, #514934 and #626155. It looks mushy next to the crisp hero and the crisp pills. - **Touch stick (hud.js:1318-1333).** out-headless/h-touch-640x360.png shows a soft grey disc with an anti-aliased rim. The code draws ctx.arc at alpha 0.35 and 0.7, centred on raw touch coordinates. - **Ring warning (hud.js:1152-1166).** Confirmed in code only: ctx.ellipse is stroked at lineWidth 2 around an unrounded centre, so it anti-aliases. It is also low alpha (0.18-0.4), so it shows less than the others. - **Pills.** The strike pill and the PRESS M FOR MAP pill (hud.js:1027-1035, 1064-1071) are crisp and opaque. They are plain #181425 rectangles with no rim, sitting right above the framed bronze plates. That is a style mismatch, not anti-aliasing. The finding's crop box for the map pill is a little off: it is at y=617-641 in c1280-19, not 638-664. - **Clock vs LV.** In c1280-19 the clock's glyph rows are 8-14 with its outline on rows 7 and 15. LV's glyph rows are 9-15 with its outline on rows 8 and 16. The clock's outline on row 7 merges with the XP bar frame (fillRect(0,0,W,7) covers rows 0-6, also #181425). LV keeps a 1-px gap. The code matches: the clock is drawn at y=8 (line 613) and LV at y=9 (line 468). The comment at line 756 says both "sit just below the experience bar", so the offset is unintended. - **Extra, not in the finding.** drawTouch puts the pause and MAP boxes at W/2-9 and W/2+19. view.W = ceil(pxW/S) can be odd (for example 1366/2 = 683). When it is, those boxes land on half pixels. A canvas test at W=683 gave blended edge columns (#1c2a23). This is polish, not a bug. The edge pointer is the most visible piece because it is on screen most of the run, it rotates and it bobs. The chevron shows only in crypts, the stick only on touch, and the ring only for a second or so. The fix points the right way, but several details are wrong or incomplete: - marks() does not pick by angle; it only builds icons from pixel rows. - plate() is private to loadout.js. It also draws a header name at y+2 and 2x2 rivets at y+4, which would collide with the text in a 13-px pill. - The ring warning is an ellipse (r by 0.8r). ringSprite only draws circles; the existing groundRing() is the right helper. - An opaque knob would drop the stick's deliberate translucency, so it would hide whatever is under the thumb. - Moving the clock to y=9 also needs topLines() changed (line 758, lineBottom(8)). BAND.top, the speech bubbles, the banners and the boss bar all key off it, and LV at y=9 already pokes one row past it. - The odd-W half-pixel boxes are not covered. Better fix: 1. **Pointer.** In fxsprites style (pixelCanvas), bake a cached arrow per colour and quantised angle (32 steps, or 16). Fill a pixel when its centre falls inside the inner triangle (4,0)(-3,-3)(-3,3) rotated by the angle; give it #181425 when it is inside the outer triangle (6,0)(-4,-5)(-4,5) but not the inner one. Use a 13x13 canvas and drawImage it at the rounded SPOT position. This needs no canvas anti-aliasing and no alpha threshold, and the icons stay as they are. 2. **Chevron.** Do the same by testing each pixel's distance to the two segments: core 1 px, plus a 1-px #181425 rim. Or hand-draw 8 directions. Swords only ever use 0 or pi. 3. **Touch stick.** Base: ringSprite(22, '#c0cbdc', 1, ['#c0cbdc', a]); ringSprite's fill already takes a colour and an alpha. Knob: ringSprite(9, '#c0cbdc', 9). Draw both at Math.round'ed positions and keep a globalAlpha, so they are crisp but still translucent. 4. **Ring warning.** Use groundRing(Math.round(r), Math.round(r * 0.8), '#e43b44') at a rounded centre with the same pulsing globalAlpha. Draw it twice, 1 px apart, if 2 px of width is wanted. 5. **Pause and MAP buttons.** Use Math.floor(W / 2) so an odd W does not put them on half pixels. 6. **Pills (optional, a taste call).** Add a small rim-only plate helper: stepped corners, a 1-px bronze #8c765c top rule and a #625044 bottom rule, with no rivets or header. Use it for the strike pill, PRESS M FOR MAP, and the pause and MAP buttons. Do not reuse plate() as it is. 7. **Clock.** Draw it at y=9 and change topLines() to lineBottom(9) in the same edit.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): Most of this finding holds, but the pills part is taste. The HUD is drawn into a low-res W×H canvas and scaled up by an integer S with nearest-neighbour (view.js resize/presentUI). Any anti-aliased path drawn there therefore turns into visible blocks of blended colour. I rebuilt the current shapes at native scale (verify/sk-hudvec/proto.mjs, proto.png): - **Edge pointer (hud.js:1194-1222):** 38-58 of its 60-68 pixels are off-palette blends. A pixel-sampled version has 0. - **Crypt aim chevron (1042-1055):** about 50 of its ~55 pixels are blends. It looks visibly mushy at diagonal angles. - **Touch stick (1318-1330):** soft-edged discs. - **Ring warning (1152-1166):** an anti-aliased ellipse. The pointer is on screen nearly all run, for the boss, shrines, crypts, chests and the thief. It sits right beside the crisp 8×8 marker icons, so the mismatch shows. The codebase already bakes crisp shapes for this job: fxsprites.js ringSprite ("Crisp ring") and groundRing ("A crisp ring lying on the ground"). The HUD paths are older code (84963a5, 0b4d66e). No comment or git log entry says they are anti-aliased on purpose, no earlier review settled them, and none of the owner's calls touches them. hud.js is not in the other session's uncommitted files. **Clock vs LV:** confirmed. The clock is at y=8 and LV at y=9 (hud.js:613 vs 468). The XP bar's ink frame fills rows 0-6, and drawText's outline sits at y-1. So the clock's outline lands on row 7 and merges with the bar, while LV keeps a one-row gap (verify/sk-hudvec/top-320.png). The comment "The clock and level sit just below the experience bar" shows the two are meant to match. This is a real but tiny 1-px nit. **Not real (taste):** the PRESS M FOR MAP and strike pills, and the pause/MAP buttons. They are opaque and hard-edged, not anti-aliased. A flat #181425 box is the HUD's shared text-box style: there are 18 such fills, including speech bubbles and the crypt door card. **Why the proposed fix is not sound as written:** - loadout.js plate() is not exported, and it draws a titled bronze plate (stepped caps, name on top) made for the WEAPONS/POWERS belt. Reusing it for pills restyles every hint box for no clear gain. - Moving only the clock to y=9 leaves topLines() at lineBottom(8). The marker band, banners and speech bubbles are laid out from topLines(), so they would sit 1 px tighter under the clock. The rest is safe: draw-only, no gameplay change, no Math.random. Severity is polish. Better fix: These are draw-only changes in hud.js (plus one helper in fxsprites.js), with no gameplay effect and no Math.random. 1. **Pointer.** Add a cached helper `arrowSprite(col, step)` built with pixelCanvas, using 16 steps of TAU/16:    - Colour each pixel whose centre lies in the inner triangle (4,0) (-3,-3) (-3,3), rotated by the step angle.    - Then mark #181425 on every empty pixel that has a coloured 4-neighbour, the same way groundRing tests its edge.    - Cache by `${col}|${step}`.    - In pointer(), replace the two path fills with `drawImage(arrowSprite(col, Math.round(a / TAU * 16) & 15), Math.round(x + cos(a)*bob) - half, Math.round(y + sin(a)*bob) - half)`. Leave the icon code untouched. 2. **Chevron.** Do the same with a 16-step chevron sprite: colour where the distance to either segment (-2,-4)→(3,0)→(-2,4) is under about 0.8, then add the same 1-px ink outline. Keep the existing translate position, rounded. 3. **Touch stick.** Blit ringSprite(22, '#c0cbdc', 99) at globalAlpha 0.35 and ringSprite(9, '#c0cbdc', 99) at 0.7, centred on rounded coordinates (sprite size is 2r+3). That keeps the intended translucency without anti-aliased rims. 4. **Ring warning.** Draw groundRing(Math.round(r), Math.round(r*0.8), '#e43b44') and the same at r-1, which keeps the 2-px weight. Leave its globalAlpha pulse unchanged. 5. **Clock.** Draw it at y=9 in drawTop, and change topLines() to `return lineBottom(9);` so the layout that hangs below the clock moves with it. Leave the map-hint and strike pills and the pause/MAP buttons as they are. The flat ink box is the HUD's common text-box style.

### hud-16 [confirmed] The same icon is upscaled three different ways across overlays: Scale2x on level-up cards, nearest x2 on merchant cards, nearest x3/x4 in the evolution ceremony
- polish / owner - public/js/ui/gamescreen.js:839 and 862 (smoothScaled = px.scale2x); public/js/ui/trophy.js:69 and 73 (nearest x2); public/js/ui/gamescreen.js:1307-1310 and 1352-1353 (ceremony nearest 3x/4x) [art critic]
- Evidence: c1280-04-levelup-ready.png: Guardian Blades (x360-410, y170-225) has Scale2x rounded diagonals with 1-art-px steps. c1280-10-merchant.png: Healing Draught and Whetstone are blocky nearest x2. c1280-08 and c1280-09: the sword at nearest x3 and x4. Scale2x adds 1-art-px detail on a 2x sprite, so density is mixed within one icon.
- Pixel density is inconsistent between overlays that share one card frame, so the same icon family visibly changes style from one screen to the next.
- Fix: Owner's call. Pick one 2x treatment for UI icons. Either nearest everywhere (replace smoothScaled(icon,1) with a drawImage at x2), or Scale2x for every 2x icon (including trophy.js) and Scale2x twice for the 4x ceremony.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The finding is real; I confirmed it in the code and in the pixels. The two level-up card icon draws, public/js/ui/gamescreen.js:839 and :862, are the only UI icon draws that go through smoothScaled (sprites.js:370, which calls px.scale2x). Every other enlarged item icon uses nearest-neighbour: - trophy.js:69 and :73 (merchant and trophy cards, drawImage at x2) - reward-reels.js:50 (chest reels) - gamescreen.js:1307-1310 (ceremony crack phase at x3) - gamescreen.js:1352-1353 (ceremony new icon at 4*pop) Pixel check (crops in verify/icon-scale/): - **Level-up:** lu-guardian.png and lu-axe.png, from c1280-04, show 1-art-px stair-steps on icons that are drawn at 2x. The Throwing Axe handle diagonal and the Guardian Blades edges are rounded at half-icon-pixel resolution. - **Merchant:** mer-whet.png and mer-heal.png, from c1280-10, show clean 2x2 blocks. - **Ceremony:** evo-crack.png, from c1280-08, shows the sword as clean 3x3 blocks over the dimmed chest. So inside the same panel() card family, pixel density changes from one screen to the next. One small correction to the evidence: c1280-09 is the result screen after the ceremony. Excalibur there sits in its chest reel at nearest x2 (evo-reel.png), not at x4. The x4 draw is confirmed only in the code (line 1353), not in that image. This is not a settled call. No review mentions Scale2x or nearest for icons, and the smoothScaled call has been there since the first commit. The uncommitted diff in gamescreen.js from the other session is 3 added lines and doesn't touch lines 839, 862 or the ceremony, so the fix wouldn't collide with that work. Severity is polish: the two styles never appear on screen together, and the difference is subtle at 1280x720. Option A of the fix is sound and minimal. Option B is weaker: - The ceremony's crack phase is x3, and no number of Scale2x passes gives x3. - Its new icon pops at a fractional 4-6x. - It would touch trophy.js, reward-reels.js and the ceremony instead of two lines. Better fix: Go with nearest-neighbour everywhere (option A). Only the level-up cards change, matching the merchant, trophy, chest reels, evolution ceremony and HUD. In public/js/ui/gamescreen.js, line 839 becomes: `ctx.drawImage(icon.c, x + 6, y + lift + 5, icon.c.width * 2, icon.c.height * 2);` Line 862 becomes: `const ic = icon.c; ctx.drawImage(ic, Math.round(x + w / 2 - ic.width), y + lift + 8, ic.width * 2, ic.height * 2);` The layout doesn't move, because both paths give 2x the icon's size. Then remove the now-unused smoothScaled from the import on line 7, keeping SPR. Leave sprites.js smoothScaled in place, since other modules may still use it. Don't take option B (Scale2x everywhere): x3 and the fractional 4x pop can't be made with Scale2x, and it would touch several files.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The finding holds up, but the code tells a narrower story than the critic did. The level-up cards are the only in-run overlay that still uses Scale2x. The two smoothScaled(icon, 1) calls at public/js/ui/gamescreen.js:839 and :862 have been there unchanged since the first commit (84963a5). Every other overlay that shows the same icons now draws them nearest-neighbour: the chest reveal reels (reward-reels.js:50, 2x), the merchant, trophy and altar cards (trophy.js:69/73, 2x), the evolution ceremony (3x and 4x) and the gear reels. The codebase has also been moving away from Scale2x on purpose. f416b0e removed smoothScaled from the hero portraits, and ee1cc16 replaced the chest reveal's Scale2x icon with nearest reels. The level-up card was simply never updated. No review in reviews/ mentions Scale2x or icon upscaling. No comment or commit message says the level-up Scale2x is intentional, and it does not touch any of the owner's settled calls. I checked it visually. I cropped the same Guardian Blades icon at the same 2x size from c1280-04 (level-up card) and c1280-07 (chest reel, shown seconds later). The crops are in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/hud/verify/icon-scale/lu-gb.png and C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/hud/verify/icon-scale/chest-gb.png. On the level-up card, Scale2x adds 1-pixel stair-steps to the 2x blade and its dark edge, and it breaks up the orange crossguard into notched bevels. So the icon shows two pixel sizes at once, and it visibly changes style between the level-up card and the chest reveal that follows it. This is a real consistency defect, not taste, but it is cosmetic, so it rates polish. The critic's first option (nearest everywhere) is sound. It is visual only, with no gameplay effect and no Math.random calls, and it does not touch any hero look. The second option (Scale2x on trophy.js, and Scale2x twice for the 4x ceremony) would be larger and would go against the direction the code is already taking. Note: gamescreen.js has 3 uncommitted lines from another session. They are not at these call sites, so the change would need to be staged by hunk. Better fix: Change only the level-up card (public/js/ui/gamescreen.js) and leave trophy.js and the evolution ceremony as they are, since both already use nearest-neighbour like the rest. - Line 839: ctx.drawImage(icon.c, x + 6, y + lift + 5, icon.c.width * 2, icon.c.height * 2); - Line 862: const big = icon.c; ctx.drawImage(big, Math.round(x + w / 2 - big.width), y + lift + 8, big.width * 2, big.height * 2); - Remove smoothScaled from the import on line 7, since no other code in that file uses it. - Keep the export in engine/sprites.js. The file holds another session's uncommitted edits, so stage only this hunk (git diff | git apply --cached). The ceremony's brief 0.3 s pop at a non-integer scale (s = 4 * pop) is transient and not worth changing.

### hud-6 [unchecked] Touch pause and MAP buttons blur at odd art widths (half-pixel fillRect), e.g. every 390x844 phone
- bug / code - public/js/game/hud.js:1336-1342 (fillRect at W/2-9, W/2-4, W/2+1, W/2+19); W = ceil(pxW/S) in public/js/engine/view.js:57 [tech critic]
- Evidence: 844x390 at DPR 3 gives pxW 2532, S=4, W=633, so W/2 = 316.5 and every rect sits at x.5. tech-extra/phone-pause.png (crop of raw/cphone-01-play.png x1200-1440, y1080-1170): a half-transparent 1-art-px column on both sides of the plate, and grey half-tone columns on the pause bars. The MAP plate does the same in cphone-19/20.
- Canvas anti-aliases fills at half-pixel coordinates, so the only touch-only HUD controls get soft edges on the most common phone width.
- Fix: const cx = Math.floor(W/2); use it for all four rects and the MAP label.

### hud-17 [unchecked] On day maps, banner text and LV rely on the 1-px outline alone against snow and sand
- polish / code - public/js/game/hud.js:1309-1312 (banner title ['#ffffff', color, color], sub '#c0cbdc'), 468 (LV yellow), 613 (clock) [art critic]
- Evidence: art-extra/phone-banner-snow.png (from cphone-01): the white top half of 'Lucky Clover' vanishes into the snow, and the #c0cbdc sub-line 'Relic · yours when this run ends' is close to the snow's value. cphone-18: the 'BEST' sub-line on snow. c1920-01: 'LV 9' yellow on tan sand.
- Desert and frozen maps are played by day (a settled call). Text designed for the night forest loses its fill-to-ground contrast there, which breaks the hero/threats-then-UI readability order.
- Fix: When BIOMES[look].sun is set, draw a soft #181425 band at 0.45 behind the banner block (2-px stepped ends, block height), and one at 0.35 behind the clock/LV row. Alternatively, switch the sub colour to #8b9bb4 with the dark outline. Night maps stay as they are.

## menus

Both critics agree the menus are close to pixel perfect but not there yet, and that the faults are concentrated rather than everywhere. A lot is right. Every menu path draws on a W×H UI canvas with smoothing off and blits it at a whole-number S, so there is no blur anywhere. The smoothing and fractional paths (view.js overlay()/picture(), drawTextScaled) have no callers. Text is cached, drawn at rounded positions and outlined consistently. The 5x7 body font is clean. The Jacquard 24 logo and Jacquard 12 headings are crisp. The trial scenes use whole-number scales, and the title campfire draws the real hero sheets at 1:1. The Lucifer 9-slice and Kenney skin is recoloured into one palette and used on every screen. The faults that matter are these. (1) A rendering bug that both critics found: hero select draws every hero at 0.65-0.8 on the nearest-neighbour UI canvas. That drops 20-35% of the pixel rows and columns, so the showcase looks cruder than the game itself. (2) An off-by-one in the panel 9-slice makes the corner-bracket arms grow with panel width, from 13 to 33 px. (3) Type: the Pixtura12 plate capitals misread ('Save Vnreadable', 'Throwing Are'). The Scale2x'd 5x7 banners turn D into O ('RUN ENOEO'), get punched with holes and specks, and add a second display voice that clashes with the blackletter. (4) The 14-px focus arrow paints over its neighbour in every tight row. (5) Layout collisions, worst at 480x270 (1920x1080), at 456x256 (1366x768) and on phones. NEXT runs over the campfire heroes. Dialog plates and the music dropdown cover the screen headers. The pause and embedded Options overlays sit on the HUD. Text and icons sit on frame lines, including the results panel's last line. Buttons under 20 px collapse into a '+' cross. Trial badges sit over their thumbnails. Scroll windows cut glyphs mid-height. Polish items: a dark outline slab under every button label, flat-rectangle clouds that look like glitch lines, moon nubs, a 2x campfire among 1x heroes, ad-hoc panel trim colours, the one-off flat prologue style, empty Codex/Armory detail panels and ragged oath ribbons. Wrapped body text also uses a 9-px pitch for a 9-row glyph box. All of these are code fixes except two. The menu campfire wants art at hero density. The view-scale rounding is an owner call, because it also changes how much of the world the player sees. The other session has uncommitted DEMO work in screens.js, hero-selection.js, gamescreen.js and ladder.js. None of the cited lines are inside its hunks, so stage the fixes by hunk. The red-haired Paladin on disk is that session's work and was not judged. Evidence is in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/art-extra/ and .../menus/tech-extra/, together with the measuring scripts and a mock of the proposed type (art-extra/type-mock-x3.png).

### menus-1 [confirmed] Hero select draws every hero at a fractional 0.65-0.8 on the low-res UI canvas, dropping 20-35% of pixel rows and columns
- bug / code - public/js/ui/hero-showcase.js:27-29 (heroMenuScale = max(1/S, view.zoom) = 0.8), :84-92 (stage scale, camera S = 0.8), :103 g.drawPlayer on the UI ctx; public/js/ui/hero-selection.js:249-252 (roster rows, min(0.8/hires, ...)) and :140 (stage drawn on view.uctx). Compare background.js:662 (title campfire at 1:1). Found by both critics (art and technical lenses).
- Evidence: Base dir C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/. art-extra/knight-0.8-vs-1.png: the Knight idle frame at 1:1 next to nearest 0.8; the helm's left edge, the shoulder and the sword-arm outlines are missing. art-extra/stage-knight.png (1920x1080, S=4). Measured in Chrome (tech-extra/showcase-scale2.mjs, roster-scale.mjs): all 12 stage heroes draw at 0.8, so the Knight's 80x64 frame lands as 64x51.2. Roster portraits draw at 0.65-0.8 (knight 24x33→19x26, mage 27x40→20x30, eanasir 25x46→16x30). tech-extra/showcase-decimation-1920.png: columns missing in the face, the hilt and the shield rim. The 1:1 title campfire Knight (1280x720__B01) is crisp and 25% larger. Every hero in 1280x720__B02/D02-D08 and 1920x1080__B03 is affected.
- The UI canvas has 1 px per art pixel and presentUI blits it up by S. Drawing a sprite there at 0.8 with smoothing off is nearest-neighbour downsampling: whole rows and columns are thrown away, and a different set on each frame. That breaks the self-coloured dark outlines and erases 1-px highlights on the most-seen screen. In play, the world goes onto the device canvas at S×0.8 device px per sheet px and loses nothing, so the menu shows the art cruder than the game does. These are committed lines; the other session's DEMO diff in hero-selection.js doesn't touch them.
- Fix: Never pass a scale under 1 to drawImage on view.uctx. Stage: draw at scale = max(1, floor(min(...))) with heroMenuScale() returning 1 for UI-canvas drawing. The 68-px stage fits the bounds. At 640x360 the stageH cap (hero-selection.js:34) can rise from 68 to about 84, which gives the hero a 2x stand. If the exact in-game size must be kept, draw the figure on the device canvas after presentUI (view.overlay exists and is unused) at max(1, round(view.S*view.zoom)) device px per sheet px. Roster: draw at 1/(spr.hires||1) only where hires is a whole number. Raise ROW_H 34→38 (ROW_STEP 40) so a 32-px crop fits at 1:1, or use a bust crop of about the top 30 rows.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): I checked this in code and in the live game, and the finding holds. Menus draw on view.uctx (main.js:124), a W×H canvas with 1 px per art pixel and smoothing off (view.js:67), which presentUI then scales up by S. heroMenuScale() returns max(1/S, view.zoom), and view.zoom is a constant 0.8 (view.js:24). Every hero sheet has hires 1, so on any display where S >= 2 the stage draws each hero at 0.8 UI px per sheet px. Phones at S=1 get scale 1 on the stage. The roster rows use min(0.8, (slot-4)/w, 30/h), which also comes out under 1 even at S=1 for any crop taller than 30. What I measured myself (scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/decimation-check.mjs and decimation-offline.mjs, fresh Chrome context at 1920x1080 on the :3017 server): - The UI canvas read back right after a real frame has S=4, scale 0.8 and a Knight rect of 64x51.2 from an 80x64 frame. - All 264 opaque hero pixels on the UI canvas match centre-sampled nearest 0.8 of the source exactly. The floor mapping matches only 33%. - Source columns 27, 32, 37, 42 and 47 never reach the screen.
