That is 5 of the Knight's 22 ink columns and 6 of his 32 ink rows, so about 35% of his ink pixels are gone. - In play the same frame goes to the device canvas at 3.2 device px per sheet px, with a minimum run of 3, so nothing is lost. - verify/menu-vs-game-1920x1080.png puts the two side by side at the same device size. The menu Knight has a narrower helm, and he loses the 1-px sword-tip row and outline columns that the in-game draw keeps. - The title campfire really is drawn at 1:1 (background.js, w = img.width/density). Note the knight sheet is high-colour, with near-opaque alpha of 252/253. Its 1-px rows are painted detail rather than strict pixel art, which softens the damage a little but does not remove it. The roster and stage lines are committed. The other session's DEMO diff in hero-selection.js does not touch lines 249-252, but that file has uncommitted changes. Severity: a visible quality loss on the most-seen menu, not broken behaviour, so 'defect' rather than 'bug'. Why the fix is not sound: 1. The main proposal (heroMenuScale returns 1, scale max(1, floor(...))) draws the stage hero 25% larger than in play. That reverses a deliberate, committed and tested invariant: commit 281bd5c, and test/hero-selection.test.js:115 'the showcase draws the selected hero at gameplay size on phone and desktop displays', which would then fail. 2. The fallback through view.overlay at round(S*zoom) does not work as a drop-in:    - overlay() reads img.naturalWidth. A canvas has none, so the result is NaN and nothing draws.    - It snaps to whole multiples of the picture's size, or downscales smoothly.    - It paints after the whole UI, so it would land on top of the Gear and Journey dialogs and the attack hint.    - round(3.2) = 3 is still about 6% off gameplay size, which would also fail that test. 3. The roster half of the fix (1:1 plus a bust crop, or taller rows) is fine. The 640x360 stageH change (68 to 84) is extra scope. Better fix: Keep the gameplay size and draw the stage hero on the device canvas at the game's own camera scale. Stage: - In HeroScreen.draw, after the roster, portrait backdrop and plinth are on view.uctx, call presentBackdrop(), the hook the title campfire already uses (screens.js:88). That blits what is drawn so far and clears uctx. - Then have HeroShowcase draw the player on view.ctx with cs = Math.max(1, view.S * view.zoom), exactly as Game.syncCam computes it, and camera { S: cs, x: -center*view.S/cs, y: -foot*view.S/cs }. - Clip to the stage box × view.S. - The locked silhouette and the h.line rim go on view.ctx the same way. - The ATTACK hint, the info panel, the buttons, any MenuDialog and screen fades keep drawing on the now-cleared uctx, so presentUI still puts them on top. - The figure then lands at exactly 3.2 (S=4), 2.4 (S=3) or 1.6 (S=2) device px per sheet px, as in play: same size, and no row or column dropped. - The gameplay-size test only needs to read the rect in device px instead of multiplying the UI rect by uiScale. Roster: - Never pass a scale under 1 to drawImage on uctx. Draw the portrait at 1 UI px per sheet px (all hero sheets are hires 1), cropping to the top ~30 rows (head and torso) so it fits ROW_H-4. - Alternatively, draw the rows on the device canvas with the same presentBackdrop approach, clipped to the scroll box. Do not change heroMenuScale to 1 for the stage. Do not use view.overlay for a canvas: it needs naturalWidth, snaps to integer multiples, and paints above dialogs.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real, and it is not something anyone chose. The UI canvas has one pixel per art pixel and imageSmoothingEnabled is false (view.js:67). heroMenuScale() is max(1/S, zoom), so it returns 0.8 on every display where S >= 2, which covers all desktops and high-dpr phones. HeroShowcase.draw then hands that 0.8 to g.drawPlayer on the UI ctx, and heroImage does a plain drawImage at that size. That is a nearest-neighbour downscale: 16 of the Knight's 80 columns and about 13 of his 64 rows are thrown away, and a different set goes on each frame. The roster rows do the same at 0.65-0.8 (hero-selection.js:249). In play, the same frame goes onto the device canvas at 3.2 device px per sheet px, which only enlarges and loses nothing. So the menu shows the art cruder than the game does. I checked this in the critic's Chrome capture 1920x1080__B03 and in showcase-decimation-1920.png: the face, hilt and shield rim are missing columns. No earlier review settles it. review-followups-2026-11-15.md even rejects a shortcut because of the same "fractional sampling" quality loss. What IS deliberate is the size. Commit 281bd5c (2026-11-21, "Rework the hero chooser") replaced the old integer upscale (max(1, floor(...)), up to 7x) with heroMenuScale. Its comment says "Keep the dais in proportion to the gameplay-sized figure", and it added the test "the showcase draws the selected hero at gameplay size on phone and desktop displays" (test/hero-selection.test.js), which checks menuW*S against the field width within one UI px. The critic's main fix makes heroMenuScale() return 1 and draws at max(1, floor(...)). That makes the figure 25% larger than in play, reverses that tested choice and fails that test, so fixSound is false. The critic's fallback keeps the size, but it names the wrong tool. view.overlay paints after the whole UI, so the hero would cover the "CLICK TO ATTACK" hint drawn on the stage. Its stretch path also turns smoothing on for non-integer scales (view.js:89), which would blur the sprite at 3.2. Rounding to an integer device scale (3 instead of 3.2) would also fail the gameplay-size test. None of this touches gameplay or Math.random. The stage code is in hero-showcase.js, which the other session has not touched. hero-selection.js does carry the other session's uncommitted DEMO hunks (lines 19, 26, 93, 127-129, 514), but not the lines this fix needs. Better fix: Keep the exact gameplay size and stop downscaling on the UI canvas. Draw the stage hero on the device canvas, the same way the title campfire screen splits its layers. 1) In HeroScreen.draw (hero-selection.js), let HeroShowcase.draw paint only the backdrop, the side edges and the plinth onto uctx. Then call presentBackdrop() from engine/view.js, which TitleScreen already uses. It blits everything drawn so far, including the roster and the heading, and clears uctx. 2) Draw the figure directly on view.ctx. Clip to the stage box times view.S. Use g.drawPlayer(view.ctx, { S: scale * view.S, x: -center / scale, y: -foot / scale }). That is exactly Game.syncCam's S * zoom device px per sheet px, with smoothing already off. Draw the locked silhouette and the h.line rim pass on view.ctx with the same device-px rect, which is their existing math times view.S. 3) The hint, identity, details, footer and any later UI keep drawing on the cleared uctx, and presentUI composites them on top, so the stacking order stays as it is now. Dialogs return before the stage is drawn, so they are unaffected. Keep this.geometry and the test's _rect comparison in UI units, or divide by view.S. The tests will need a stub view.ctx once presentBackdrop is called. Roster rows (hero-selection.js:247-250): draw at exactly 1/(spr.hires||1), which is 1 for every field sheet, never with a fractional fit. Use a top-anchored bust crop: the source rect is crop.x plus a centred min(crop.w, slot-4) wide, and crop.y with min(crop.h, ROW_H-4) rows. Alternatively, draw the portraits on view.ctx after presentBackdrop at heroMenuScale()*view.S device px, clipped to the scrolled roster box. Either way no rows or columns are dropped, the hero looks are kept, and nothing in update or the dice changes.

### menus-4 [confirmed] In-run titles use Scale2x'd 5x7 capitals: D reads as O ('RUN ENOEO'), holes and specks appear, and it is a second display voice next to the blackletter
- defect / code - public/js/ui/ui.js:284-320 logo() (font 'normal': two px.scale2x passes, bevel on every exposed top pixel); public/js/engine/pixels.js:125-145; callers gamescreen.js:634, :1354, :1437, :1984-1986, trophy.js:48, reward-reels.js:191, :334, game/hud.js:847. Found by both critics.
- Evidence: art-extra/runended.png and tech-extra/runended.png (1280x720__C08): 'RUN ENDED' reads 'RUN ENOEO'. Both D's lose their square corners and carry dark diagonal notches or diamond holes, and white bevel specks sit on each step of R and N. art-extra/trophy.png: the O in TROPHY has an X of dark spikes in its counter. tech-extra/logo-scale2x-before-after.png: the production path (red) shows D→O in DAWN, B rounded towards 8 in BREAKS, and hollow sparkles in COLLECTED GEAR; the fix renders in green (logo-fix.mjs). art-extra/type-mock-x3.png bottom: the same titles in Jacquard 24. Also visible in C03, C04 and C09.
- Scale2x removes the outer pixel of every convex corner. A second pass over a 1-px font leaves transparent diamonds where diagonals meet, and the outline ring then paints dark spikes into the counters. The 'bright rim on top edges' rule lights every stair-step white. The 5x7 capitals tell D from O and B from 8 only by those square corners. These are also the only blocky sans display titles in a blackletter UI, so results, trophy, merchant and altar look borrowed from another game.
- Fix: For the overlay titles (art lens), use the Jacquard 24 path in mixed case with the existing ramps: logo('Run Ended', cols, undefined, 'title'), and likewise for 'You Have Fallen', 'Trophy', 'Merchant', 'Altar', 'Dawn Breaks', 'Run Spoils' and 'Collected Gear'. For the fast in-play banners that keep the 5x7 voice (LEVEL UP!, ultimate names), make logo() grow-only (technical lens). After each pass, OR the result with a nearest 2x of its input, so Scale2x only fills concave steps (the green render). Alternatively use plain scaleN(m, 4) with times = 0, so corners stay square and the bevel lights only true tops.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): The defect is real. I confirmed it in code, by measurement and in the in-game pixels, though part of the mechanism the critic gave is wrong. How I checked: I ran the production mask path (textMask, then px.scale2x twice, as in ui.js:288-295) in Node. Then I copied logo() into verify/logo-variants.mjs with only the scaling step made switchable. What the measurement shows: - Every in-run title is left with enclosed pinholes. RUN ENDED has 9, DAWN BREAKS 21, TROPHY 7, MERCHANT 11, COLLECTED GEAR 25, YOU HAVE FALLEN 11, LEVEL UP! 3 and RUN SPOILS 14 (verify/holes.mjs). - Most are 6-px triangles where a top or bottom bar meets a side stroke. The rest are 1-px holes where interior diagonals meet, in W, M and K. - logo()'s outline ring paints these holes #181425, and the bevel puts a white rim under them. That makes the dark diamonds and sparkles. What the captures show: - I cropped C08 at 5x (verify/c08-ended-x5.png). Both D's carry dark diamonds at their right corners, the last one larger, and their counters are octagonal. - art-extra/trophy.png shows the dark X inside the O of TROPHY. - These come from the real game in Chrome, so they are not a capture artefact. Where the critic's mechanism is off: - In production, D keeps square left corners. Scale2x clamps at the buffer edge, so the cap and base rows read as continuing past it, and the corner pixel is never cut. - That same edge clamp is what makes the 6-px corner holes, not just "a second pass over a 1-px font". - If the mask is padded first (standard EPX), the corner holes go, but 30 1-px holes stay over the alphabet. D also then really reads as O, and B leans towards 8 (verify/variants-main-x2.png, rows marked pad). - So "D reads as O" is a partly perceptual claim about production. The pinholes are the hard, measured defect. The grow-only variant leaves 0 pinholes on every title and the whole alphabet. It keeps D and B square-cornered, keeps the same size and the same 5x7 voice, and looks clean (verify/variants-main-x2.png and variants-b-x2.png). The white specks on diagonal steps of N and R come from the bevel rule. That is taste, not a defect. The "second display voice next to the blackletter" point is also taste. The owner has not settled it, so it is a question for them, not a defect. Why fixSound is false: the proposal leads with an art-lens redesign. It moves results, trophy, merchant, altar, spoils and gear titles to Jacquard 24 in mixed case. That is not minimal: - It changes the look of every overlay. - It needs edits in gamescreen.js:1984-1986, which is another session's uncommitted work. - trophy.js uses this.title as a key (this.title === 'MERCHANT', SUB[this.title]). Writing 'Merchant' there would break the subtitle, so it would need a display-name map. The technical half of the proposal (grow-only) is correct, and on its own it removes the defect everywhere. The scaleN(m, 4) alternative also works, but it gives up the diagonal smoothing and makes every title blockier. Better fix: Change only ui.js logo(), one place, and do not touch the callers. Leave px.scale2x itself unchanged, because sprites.js:373 also uses it for sprites. Replace line 294, `for (let i = 0; i < times; i++) m = px.scale2x(m);`, with: for (let i = 0; i < times; i++) {   const s = px.scale2x(m);   // Scale2x may only fill a step, never eat a corner: keep every pixel of the nearest 2x   // (the edge clamp and meeting diagonals otherwise leave holes the outline paints dark).   for (let y = 0; y < s.h; y++) for (let x = 0; x < s.w; x++) if (m.d[(y >> 1) * m.w + (x >> 1)]) s.d[y * s.w + x] = ON;   m = s; } Every title keeps its size and its cache key. This fixes all 8 callers (results, trophy, merchant, altar, level-up, evolution, weapon names, ultimate banner, spoils, gear) without editing gamescreen.js, which is another session's uncommitted work. It measured 0 pinholes over the whole alphabet, and D and B keep their square corners. Moving the overlay titles to Jacquard 24 is a separate taste question to put to the owner, not part of this fix. If the owner wants it, trophy.js needs a display-name map, because this.title is also a lookup key.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): Confirmed by rendering. I rebuilt ui.js logo() exactly, using the real pixels.js scale2x and font.js textMask, in verify/logo-skeptic/logo-variants.mjs (renders run.png, trophy.png, dawn.png, fallen.png, levelup.png and gear.png). The production 5x7 path runs two Scale2x passes over a 1-px font. On the second pass EPX works on its own output, which leaves 1-px gaps and diamond holes where diagonals meet. An ASCII dump of D and O after both passes shows identical octagonal counters. They differ only in the outer left columns, which are square on D and notched on O, while the notch and hole on D's right side dominate. At 1280x720 (S=2), C08 really does read 'RUN ENOEO'. The outline pass then paints dark spikes into those holes: an X inside the O of TROPHY, and the U of LEVEL UP! and YOU, plus the O, C and G of COLLECTED GEAR. Nothing marks this as intended. The Scale2x smoothing dates from the first commit (84963a5). 2ae065a only added the Jacquard 'title' path with times = 0. No review in reviews/ mentions logo() or Scale2x, and no settled owner call touches it. ui.js and pixels.js have no uncommitted changes from the other session. Every in-run title runs through this path: results, trophy, merchant, altar, run spoils, collected gear, level up, evolution and ultimate names. So this is a real, visible legibility defect, not taste. One part of the finding is taste, though: the claim that the 5x7 capitals are a 'second display voice' and should become Jacquard 24. In the render, mixed-case blackletter like 'You Have Fallen' in the dark-red ramp reads worse at a glance than the fixed capitals. The swap would also need edits to gamescreen.js, which has the other session's uncommitted hunks. That part is the owner's style call, not a defect fix. Better fix: Make the fix only in ui.js logo(), which is not in progress elsewhere, and make Scale2x grow-only. Replace `for (let i = 0; i < times; i++) m = px.scale2x(m);` with: `for (let i = 0; i < times; i++) { const s = px.scale2x(m); for (let y = 0; y < s.h; y++) for (let x = 0; x < s.w; x++) if (m.d[(y >> 1) * m.w + (x >> 1)]) s.d[y * s.w + x] = ON; m = s; }`. Scale2x then only fills concave steps and never removes ink. Every 5x7 caller is fixed at once: RUN ENDED, YOU HAVE FALLEN, DAWN BREAKS, VICTORY, TROPHY, MERCHANT, ALTAR, RUN SPOILS, COLLECTED GEAR, LEVEL UP!, EVOLUTION! and the ultimate names. The size stays the same (for example 208x34 for RUN ENDED), so nothing shifts in the layout. It touches no gameplay, no Math.random call and no hero art, and the result is cached. No gothic-font caller exists, so only the 'normal' path changes. In the render, D reads as D, B as B and O is clean, and the existing 5x7 voice and colour ramps stay. Do not use plain scaleN(m, 4): the bevel rule then lights every inner step, which leaves white bars inside L, E and U and looks worse. Moving the overlay titles to Jacquard 24 in mixed case is a separate style choice for the owner, not part of this fix. It would touch gamescreen.js, which holds the other session's uncommitted work.

### menus-5 [confirmed] The 14-px focus arrow paints over the neighbouring control in tight rows (map tabs, BEGIN, Records and Codex tabs, OATHS, Options pairs)
- defect / code - public/js/ui/ui.js:238 (pointer at x-2±1); public/js/ui/skin.js:99-102 (arrow piece 14x13). Rows with gaps under 16 px: screens.js:418-432 (map tabs, gap 4), :446 (padlocked tab pointer), :516 (OATHS at checkbox +8), :522-525 (beginX = musicX + musicW + 8), :865-869 (Codex tabs, gap 4), :1003-1005 (Records tabs, gap 8), :1172/:1204 (Options half-width pairs, gap 4). Found by both critics.
- Evidence: 1280x720__B24 'OVERVIEW ▶ HEROES' (arrow at x 290-315 over OVERVIEW's right point); B26 and B27 the same; B22 'RELICS ▶ ULTIMATES'; D11 'DESERT ▶ FROZEN'. tech-extra/hell-tab.png (B11): 'FORES▶'. art-extra/chal-music.png and tech-extra/begin.png (B13): BEGIN's arrow covers the music dropdown's pointed end and caret. Arithmetic at 640 wide: the pointer spans x 363-377 while the dropdown ends at 371.
- The arrow reaches 14-17 art px to the left of the focused button, while these rows leave only 4-8 px between buttons. It damages the neighbour's silhouette, and the bob animation makes the overlap flicker. In tab rows it is redundant, because the focus rim already pulses gold.
- Fix: In button(), skip the side pointer when o.group is set (tab and choice rows), or add o.pointer:false at those call sites. Where a pointer is wanted, keep at least 16 px clear: beginX = musicX + musicW + 18 (shrink musicW by 10, or re-centre -108→-118), OATHS at x + 18. Alternatively draw a small chevron (5-7 px) inside the button's own left point or under the label.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed this in both the pixels and the code. skin.js:99-102 draws the 14x13 'arrow' piece at [X-14, X-1], and ui.js:238 sets X = buttonX - 2 + round(sin(t*8)). So the arrow covers buttonX-17 to buttonX-2 as it bobs, and any neighbour closer than about 16 px gets painted over. Because buttons are drawn left to right, the focused button's arrow always lands on top of the button to its left. Zoomed crops (nearest-neighbour, x6 to x8) are in scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/: - **b24-crop.png:** the arrow replaces OVERVIEW's right point, leaving a blunt end. - **d11-zoom.png:** the same happens between DESERT and FROZEN. - **b22-row.png:** the same between RELICS and ULTIMATES. - **b13-zoom.png:** at 640 wide, musicX = 161 and the dropdown's last column is 370, while the arrow spans 362-377. The dropdown's pointed right end is covered. - **tech-extra/hell-tab.png:** the padlocked HELL tab's own pointer (screens.js:446, gap 4) covers the 'T' of FOREST, so the arrow damages text as well as the frame. The cited arithmetic also holds for the other rows: - Map tabs: gap 4 (screens.js:426). - Codex tabs: gap 4 (:869). - Records tabs: gap 8 (:1005). - OATHS: checkbox + 8 (:516). - BEGIN: musicW + 8 (:523). - Options half-width pairs: gap 4 (:1172-1173 and :1204-1205). - The difficulty row (gap 6, :503) is not listed but has the same problem. One part is overstated: the music caret is not covered. The caret sits at x 358-362, and the arrow's first two columns are empty at the tip rows, so it only comes close. This is not a capture artefact. It follows from the code under any focus that has the button lit, from keys, pad or mouse hover. None of these lines are in another session's uncommitted hunks. screens.js does have uncommitted DEMO edits elsewhere, so the line numbers above are as on disk. ui.js and skin.js are clean. The proposed fix points the right way but is incomplete. Skipping the pointer when o.group is set only covers map, difficulty and Codex. The Records tabs have no group, the Options pairs have no group, and the padlocked tab's arrow is a direct pointer() call at screens.js:446, so none of these would change. Better fix: 1. **ui.js:238:** change the condition to `if (lit && (o.pointer ?? !o.group))`. Tab and choice rows (map, difficulty, Codex) then drop the side arrow by default. Under key navigation their focus already equals the chosen tab, and the rim pulses gold. Any button can still ask for the arrow with pointer:true. 2. **screens.js:446:** delete the padlocked tab's own pointer() call. Its padlock and name already brighten in focus, and the line below changes to the orange 'To unlock' text. 3. **screens.js:1005 (Records tabs):** add `pointer: false`. Better still, add `group: 'records_tabs', home: this.tab === id`, which also gives up/down the same return-to-shown-tab behaviour the Codex has. The focus rim stays distinct from the 'on' rim because it pulses. 4. **screens.js:1173 and :1205 (Options pairs):** pass `pointer: false` on the right-hand button of each pair (o_weapon_sounds, o_effects). The left button's arrow already falls in the margin outside the column, like the steppers' arrows. 5. **screens.js:523 (BEGIN):** keep the arrow but give it room: `musicX = Math.round((W - musicW - 118) / 2), beginX = musicX + musicW + 18`. At narrow widths, use `musicW = Math.min(210, W - 150)` so the music button's own arrow stays on screen. 6. **OATHS (screens.js:516):** use `boxX + checkboxWidth(label) + 18`. If the `W - 82` clamp can still squeeze it on narrow screens, pass `{ pointer: false }` in oath-picker.js:23 instead. The gap must be at least 17 px. The arrow's leftmost pixel reaches buttonX-17 when the bob is at -1.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed this in the code and the screenshots. The arrow piece in assets/ui/pieces.js is 14x13. pointer() in skin.js:105 puts its tip at x-2±1 (ui.js:238), so it covers roughly x-17 to x-2, about 15 px to the left of the focused button. The rows listed leave only 4-8 px between controls, so the arrow lands on the left-hand neighbour's pointed end: - map tabs, gap 4 - Codex tabs, gap 4 - difficulty row, gap 6 (the critic missed this one; it is group 'diff') - Records tabs, gap 8 - the two half-width pairs in Options, gap 4 - OATHS, 8 px after the checkbox - BEGIN, 8 px after the music dropdown At W=640 the dropdown ends at 371 while BEGIN's arrow covers about 362-377. The crops show the same thing: verify/b22-tabs.png ('RELICS ▶' covers its right point), B24 'OVERVIEW ▶', D11 'DESERT ▶', and B13/chal-music.png (BEGIN's arrow sits on the dropdown's point and caret). This is not deliberate design. The arrow came in with commit 7708271 (the Lucifer UI skin) as a general focus cue, and nothing in the code, the git log or reviews/ addresses rows this tight. The padlock comment at screens.js:446 does want the arrow on a shut map tab, but it doesn't consider the overlap either. None of the owner's settled calls covers it. The worst case is BEGIN: it is the default focus on the Challenge screen, so the collision shows on almost every run start, and the bob makes it flicker. In tab rows the arrow adds nothing, because the focused tab already pulses a gold rim (and in map/diff/Codex, focusing a tab also selects it). The fix only changes drawing: no gameplay, no Math.random, no hero art. It needs some completing: - The group rule in button() covers map, diff and Codex tabs only. hero-selection, music-menu and music-picker use ui.item() directly, so they aren't affected. - The Records tabs have no group. Pass pointer:false there. Don't add a group, because that would change how up/down navigation picks the landing tab. - screens.js:446 draws the shut map tab's arrow itself with pointer(), so the group rule won't remove it. That line has to go too, or 'FORES▶' stays. - The Options pairs can't get 16 px of room inside the 220-px column. Use pointer:false on the right-hand button of each pair (o_weapon_sounds, o_effects), or on both halves. - BEGIN and OATHS are better kept with their arrow and given room, as the critic suggests. screens.js has another session's uncommitted DEMO edits. They don't touch these lines, but stage by hunk. ui.js is clean. Better fix: The smallest complete version: (1) ui.js:238: `if (lit && o.pointer !== false && !o.group) pointer(...)`. This removes the arrow from the map, difficulty and Codex tab rows, where the pulsing rim already marks focus. (2) screens.js:446: delete the padlock tab's own pointer() call so shut map tabs match. (3) screens.js Records tabs (~:1003): add pointer: false. (4) screens.js Options (~:1172 o_weapon_sounds, ~:1204 o_effects): add pointer: false. Optionally add it to the left halves too, so each pair matches. (5) screens.js (~:521-522): musicX = Math.round((W - musicW - 118) / 2), beginX = musicX + musicW + 18. The whole row still fits at W 427 (328 px). (6) screens.js (~:516): OATHS at boxX + checkboxWidth(label) + 18, still capped by W - 82. Alternative that needs no list of call sites: in button(), skip the arrow when an item registered earlier this frame overlaps the arrow's area (x-17 to x-2 on the same rows): `ui.items.some(i => i.id !== id && i.y < y + h && i.y + i.h > y && i.x < x - 2 && i.x + i.w > x - 17)` Every tight row draws left to right, so the left neighbour is always registered first. Combined with the 18-px gaps for BEGIN and OATHS, it fixes all the rows here and any future ones with one line in ui.js. Step (2) is still needed.

### menus-6 [confirmed] Title screen: the NEXT goal line runs through the campfire heroes at 480x270, 456x256 and on phones
- defect / code - public/js/ui/screens.js:171-177 (NEXT at the last button's y + 5; it only checks y + 13 < H - 17) vs public/js/ui/background.js:645 (fy = H*0.9), :21-27 (CAMP dy -24/-16), :657-669 (heroes up to ~44 px above the fire). Found by both critics.
- Evidence: 1920x1080__B00-title-logo-crop-native.png bottom: 'NEXT · Survive 15 minutes...' over the Shadow's, Sorcerer's and Necromancer's heads ('minutes' over the Shadow's cloak). 1920x1080__B01 (y 405-420, x 330-700). sheet-01-title-menu-mid-all-resolutions.png: the same at 1366x768 (art y≈200) and on phone@1.5 (844x390@1.5-phone__B01, y≈437 device). At H=270 the buttons end at y=198, NEXT occupies 203-212, and the back-row heads sit at about 187-200.
- The goal line is placed only relative to the button stack, and the campfire only relative to H. On any screen under about 300 art px tall they overlap. That covers the most common desktop (1920x1080, S=4) and real phones. Outlined yellow text over sprites, with no backing, reads as a z-order mistake on the first screen.
- Fix: Compute campTop = round(H*0.9) - 46 (the back row's head). If y + 5 + lines*10 > campTop, choose one of these: draw NEXT in the footer centre instead of 'Progress saved automatically after each run' (screens.js:186); move it up under the motto (my + 14) and start the buttons below it; or give it a dark backing strip. Alternatively tighten the stack when H < 300 (bh 15→13, gap 4→2), or draw only the front-row heroes there.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real. I checked it in the critic's images and in my own captures of the running game (real Chrome on :3017, screenshots of the full composited screen). In the critic's 1920x1080__B01 at 3x (verify/crop-1920-next.png), the yellow NEXT line runs across the Sorcerer's beard and chest, the Shadow's body and the Necromancer's shoulders. I re-rendered the title for a new player (5 buttons, no REPLAY INTRO) and for a returning player (6 buttons, set in memory only). The results are in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/next-camp2-ret.png and next-camp2-new.png. The overlap shows in both states at: - 1920x1080 fullscreen (S=4, 480x270) - 1536x864 (S=3, 512x288; also every 2560x1440 at S=5, 288 tall) - 1440x900 (S=3, 480x300) In a maximised windowed Chrome on a 1920x1080 monitor (about 1920x945, H=315), it only grazes the Shadow's hood, and only for a returning player. The 844x390@1.5 phone shot and the 1366x768 shot in the critic's sheet show it too. The code matches the description: - screens.js:157 centres the button stack on H/2. NEXT sits at the stack bottom + 5, and line 173 checks only y+13 < H-17. - background.js:645 puts the fire base at fy = round(H*0.9), with the back row at dy -24/-16. - The Shadow's hood and the Sorcerer's lantern reach about fy-51/fy-50 in visible pixels; heads are about fy-43/-44. Measured at H=270, that puts the tops at about 192-200. NEXT ink is at 203-211. - Worked through, NEXT ink ends at about H/2+76 and the back row starts at about 0.9H-52, so the two collide whenever H is below about 320. The usual S rounding (270-330 art px tall) lands there on most desktops. This is not a capture artefact. It is the plain title screen with a profile's real nextGoal. Note: screens.js has another session's uncommitted DEMO hunks right next to this code (motto at line 110, the items list at line 154, the dialog branch at lines 135-138). Any fix must stage only its own lines. I rated fixSound false because the fix is a menu of options, and only the first one holds up: - Detection: campTop = fy-46 sits about 5-8 px too low. The Shadow's hood (fy-51) and the lantern (fy-50) are above it, plus the idle bob. - "Move it under the motto and start the buttons below": there is no slack for that. At H=256-270 the REPLAY INTRO button already ends level with the lantern and hood, so pushing the buttons down pushes them into the camp. - "Tighten the stack when H<300": the stack is re-centred on H/2, so only half the saved 24 px comes off the bottom (198 becomes 186). NEXT at 191-199 still crosses the heads. The H<300 test also misses H=300-315, where the overlap still happens. - "Backing strip": the text still covers the heroes. - "Front row only": removes three heroes from the first screen most players see. The footer-swap option is the sound one. Better fix: Use one geometric rule and change only screens.js (not the camp layout or the button stack). 1. After the button loop, compute `const campTop = Math.round(H * 0.9) - 54;`. That is the back row's highest visible pixel (the Shadow's hood and the Sorcerer's lantern, about fy-51) plus a couple of px for the idle bob. Better still, export it from background.js next to CAMP, so the two files can't drift apart. 2. Keep the NEXT line where it is when it fits: `y + 5 + lines.length*10 <= campTop` (and the existing H-17 check). Otherwise, draw it as one `fitLine(text, Math.min(320, W - 112))` in the footer centre at H-12, in place of the dim 'Progress saved automatically after each run' note. Keep `#fee761` with its outline. The footer row is free of sprites at every size: the front row's feet are at fy+6, about 9 px above the footer text at H=270. A 310 px goal clears the gold tag and the trophy count even at W=456. 3. In DEBUG, keep the debug line and skip the goal (local only). Tall screens (H at or above about 320, e.g. 1280x720) and portrait phones keep today's layout. No change to background.js, CAMP, the button sizes or the scene. Stage only these lines, because the other session's DEMO hunks sit at screens.js lines 110, 135-138 and 154.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed the bug in real Chrome with the mid-progress save. Script: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/next-camp/check.mjs, results in orig.json, images orig__*.png. The button stack is centred on H/2. The campfire (background.js:645) sits at fy = H*0.9. The NEXT guard at screens.js:173 only checks the footer (y+13 < H-17), never the camp, so below about 300 art px the line runs through the back row. Measured overlaps (NEXT top vs back-row top): - 480x270 (1920x1080 fullscreen, S=4): NEXT 203-210, back row 186. - 512x288 (1536x864): 212 vs 202. - 480x300 (1440x900): 218 vs 213. - 640x323 (a windowed 1080p browser): 230 vs 234; it touches the Shadow's hood. - 633x293 (phone@1.5): 215 vs 207. Two shorter sizes are worse than the critic said, because the buttons hit the camp too: - At 456x256 (1366x768 fullscreen), the REPLAY INTRO button (172-187) covers the Shadow's head (173) and the Sorcerer's lantern. - At 427x240 (854x480) it covers the whole back row. 640x360 and 844x390 are clean. Nothing marks this as deliberate. 8eb1bef relaxed the guard from "y + 25 < H - 40" to "y + 13 < H - 17" to show the goal on more screens, and the camp was never considered. No review in reviews/ settles it, and it touches none of the owner's calls: the five-hero ring and the hero looks stay as they are. This is a real defect on the first screen at the most common desktop size, not a matter of taste. screens.js is in the other session's uncommitted demo-mode WIP. Their hunks don't touch lines 156-186, but stage only these lines. Why the critic's fix fails: 1. The constant is wrong. campTop = round(H*0.9) - 46 is about 11 px too low. The real back-row top is fy - 57: the Shadow's hood/blade at fy-57, the Sorcerer's lantern at fy-56, the Necromancer at fy-50. I served the critic's formula through a Playwright route (fix.json / fix__*.png). NEXT still clips the heads at every short size: 188 vs 186, 204 vs 202, 215 vs 213, 175 vs 173, 209 vs 207. 2. Its options don't solve it alone:    - A dark backing strip still covers the heroes.    - Tightening the stack (bh 13, gap 2) while it stays centred gains only 12 px at 270, which lands NEXT at 191, still over the heads at 186.    - Hiding the back row cuts the owner's five-hero ring.    - It also misses the button-over-camp overlap at 256 and 240. I tested the better fix (below) with the same route: fix2.json and fix2__*.png. Results: - 480x270: NEXT at 176-183, clear of 186. - 512x288: 192-199 vs 202. - 480x300: 203-210 vs 213. - 640x323: 224-231 vs 234. - 633x293: 197-204 vs 207. - 456x256 and 427x240: the buttons clear the back row and NEXT moves to the footer. - 640x360 and 844x390 are unchanged. There were no page errors. It changes no gameplay, adds no Math.random calls, and keeps every hero look and the camp itself. Better fix: Edit only screens.js, in TitleScreen.draw. (1) Replace lines 156-157 (const bw/bh/gap and let y) with: // The campfire's back row (background.js: CAMP round the fire at H * 0.9) reaches 57 px above the fire: on a short screen the stack rises, then closes up, so it and NEXT stay above it. const goal = nextGoal(app.store.profile), camp = Math.round(H * 0.9) - 58, below = goal ? 14 : 0; const tight = my + 20 + items.length * 19 + below > camp; const bw = 104, bh = tight ? 13 : 15, gap = tight ? 2 : 4; let y = Math.round(Math.max(my + 20, Math.min(H * 0.5 - (items.length * (bh + gap)) / 2 + 6, camp - below - items.length * (bh + gap)))); (2) At 172-175, drop the later `const goal = ...` line and guard with the camp as well as the footer: const floor = Math.min(H - 17, camp), under = goal && y + 13 < floor; if (under) { ...same width and text...; const lines = y + 23 < floor ? wrapText(text, width).slice(0, 2) : [fitLine(text, width)]; ...same draw } (3) Footer, line 186: where even the tight stack leaves no room (about H < 265: the 1366x768 and 854x480 fullscreens), show the goal in place of the save note: else if (goal && !under) drawText(ctx, fitLine(`NEXT · ${goalText(goal)}`, W - 112), W / 2, H - 12, { align: 'center', color: '#fee761' }); else drawText(ctx, 'Progress saved automatically after each run', ...as now); Button height 13 is already the Options/stepper size. Optionally, export the 58 from background.js next to CAMP (e.g. CAMP_REACH) so the two files can't drift apart. If the owner would rather keep the save note than the goal on the shortest screens, leave out (3); NEXT is then simply hidden there, the way the old guard already hid it.

### menus-7 [confirmed] Tall dialogs and the battle-music dropdown cover the screen's own header ('Cho[Equipment]ero')
- defect / code - public/js/ui/menu-dialog.js:23-24 (h = min(H - 36, ...), centred); public/js/ui/music-picker.js:53 (available = max(rect.y - 8, ...)), :60 (clamp y ≥ 6), drawMenu with no dim; hero-selection.js heading() :46-50. Found by both critics.
- Evidence: 1280x720__B05 and art-extra/eq-plate.png / tech-extra/plate-equip.png: the 'Equipment' plate at y 10-26 (art) sits on 'Choose your Hero', with the dimmed 'Cho' and 'ero' ghosting on either side. 844x390@1.5-phone__B03: 'Your Journey' over 'Choose your Hero'. 1280x720__B15: the music list (y 40-365 device) cuts through 'Choose your Challenge' and the '1 DESTINATION · 2 CHALLENGE' step line, with no dim, so half-glyphs show. Maths at 640x360: available = 178 gives 7 rows, h = 166, y = 18.
- Both pop-ups size themselves to almost the full screen height and then centre or clamp, so they land on the header band every menu draws at y 7-25. The result is two blackletter titles tangled together, or a list frame cutting a heading mid-glyph. The music dropdown is also the only overlay with no dim, while the oath picker on the same screen dims at 0.55.
- Fix: MenuDialog: h = min(H - 72, ...), y = max(36, round((H-h)/2)); the dialog already scrolls. Music picker: available = max(rect.y - 40, H - below - 6) and clamp y ≥ 38 (6 rows at 360 high), or open it as a centred modal with dim(ctx, W, H, 0.55) like OathPicker. Alternatively the screens skip header() while a dialog is open.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): Confirmed in pixels and in code. The captures come from real Chrome against the :3017 server. The dialogs were opened through the screen's own equipment() and journey() calls and the dropdown by pressing Enter on combat_music, so this is not a capture artefact. MenuDialog: h = min(H-36, ...) and y = (H-h)/2 put a tall dialog at y=18. panelTitle draws its plate from y-8 to y+8, so the plate covers y 10-26. The heading's glyphs, measured in B13, cover logical y 8-24, and the trial screen's step line covers 25-32. - B05 (1280x720, H=360) shows the dimmed 'Cho' and 'ero' on either side of the 'Equipment' plate. - The same collision shows on the Journey dialog at 1920x1080 (H=270) and 844x390@1.5, and I re-rendered it on Inventory at 1366x768 (H=256). CombatMusicPicker.menuBounds at H=360: rect.y is 186, available = max(178, 150) = 178, giving 7 rows, h = 166 and y = 18. In B15 the list's top edge cuts 'Choose your Challenge' mid-glyph, the step line shows only as 'ALLENGE', and nothing is dimmed. My re-render at 1366x768 gives the same y=18. The OathPicker on the same screen dims at 0.55. The heading code in hero-selection.js (which has another session's uncommitted changes) and screens.js header() are unchanged by that work in progress. Both fixes belong in menu-dialog.js and music-picker.js, which are committed and untouched by it. I swapped the critic's fix into both modules with page.route and rendered it (verify/dialog-header/*.png). It removes the overlap but costs more than it needs to: 1. h = min(H-72) takes 36 px off every tall dialog. At 1366x768 (H=256) the 5-button Inventory shows only 2 text lines instead of 4. At 1280x720 the Equipment dialog loses about 4 rows. 2. The new music-picker limits make the list open downward at H=360, from y 204 to 352. That splits it into 3 pages instead of 2, and it covers the 'ARROWS move · ENTER select · ESC back' hint except 'ESC back', which still peeks out undimmed. That is the same half-covered look, now at the bottom of the screen. The y = max(36, ...) part is redundant once h ≤ H-72, though it does no harm. Better fix: MenuDialog (menu-dialog.js:23-24): use asymmetric margins and keep nearly all of the current text room:   const h = Math.min(H - 40, Math.max(120, rows.length * 10 + foot + 22));   const y = Math.max(34, Math.round((H - h) / 2)), ... The plate's top then sits at 26 or lower, clear of the heading glyphs, and the panel ends at H-6. I rendered this at H=360 and H=256: the heading is clear and the dialog shows the same rows as today, give or take one. If a tall dialog could ever open over the trial screen's step line, use y ≥ 42 instead. CombatMusicPicker (music-picker.js): keep the critic's available = max(rect.y - 40, H - below - 6) and the clamp of y to at least 38. Also add dim(ctx, W, H, 0.55) just before panel(...) in drawMenu, and import dim from ./ui.js, as OathPicker does. Whatever controls or keys hint the list ends up covering are then dimmed rather than half-shown. Alternatively, skip keysHint() in drawDifficulty while musicPicker.open is true. Both changes are local to the two pop-up modules. None of the screens, nor the other session's hero-selection.js and screens.js work in progress, need touching.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real, and the screenshots match the code. In B05 at 1280x720 the logical size is 640x360. The tall Equipment dialog gets h = H-36 = 324 and is centred at y = 18. panelTitle draws its plate at y-8, so the plate covers logical rows 10-26. The screen heading (header(): text at y+3 = 7, flank at 16) fills rows 7-25, which is why the dimmed 'Cho' and 'ero' show on either side of 'Equipment'. The same thing happens in B15, and that one is worse. On the challenge screen controlsY = min(H-45, 186) = 186, so available = max(186-8, ...) = 178. That gives 7 rows, h = 166 and y = 18. The list frame then cuts 'Choose your Challenge' and the '1 DESTINATION · 2 CHALLENGE' line (y 25) mid-glyph, at full brightness and with no dim. The music-list case happens at every common size: H is 360, 270 (1080p) and 256 (768p), and every one gives controlsY = 186 and y = 18. I found nothing saying this is deliberate. menu-dialog.js has a single commit (5ac70b8, 'Improve menu clarity'). The 'available = rect.y - 8' line came from bdccc2c with no comment about overlapping the header. No earlier review in reviews/ mentions it, and it does not touch any of the owner's settled calls. The fix belongs only in menu-dialog.js and music-picker.js, which are not among the other session's uncommitted files. The heading itself lives in hero-selection.js and screens.js, which are in that session's work in progress, so the 'skip header() while a dialog is open' alternative should be avoided. The fix is purely about menu layout: no gameplay, no Math.random, no change to hero looks. The critic's numbers work. Checked against the existing tests: 427x240 with rect.y 213 gives 7 rows at y 45, still above the control; 480x270 gives 5 rows at y 54; 293x633 opens below with every row. But it is not the smallest change. H-72 costs 36 px of dialog text. At 1080p (H 270) the 4-action Equipment dialog drops from about 11 visible lines to 8. Only the top needs the room, because the header is at the top. The dim on the music list is optional taste: a dropdown anchored to its button normally has no dim, and clearing the header already removes the half-glyphs. Better fix: Leave room only at the top, where the header is. public/js/ui/menu-dialog.js:23-24: `const h = Math.min(H - 42, Math.max(120, rows.length * 10 + foot + 22));` and `const y = Math.max(34, Math.round((H - h) / 2)), ...`. The title plate sits 8 px above y, so y = 34 puts it at row 26 or below. That clears the heading (7-25) and costs only 6 px of text room against today. Short dialogs stay centred as they are now. public/js/ui/music-picker.js:53: `const available = Math.max(this.rect.y - 36, H - below - 6);` and in the y clamp at :60 change the lower bound from 6 to 34. With the control at y 186 at 360/270/256 high, that gives 6 rows (3 pages for 13 tracks) at y 36. The list then clears both the heading and the step line at y 25-33. It passes the existing bounds tests: 427x240 with rect.y 213 gives 7 rows at y 45, above the control, and 293x633 opens below. Skip the extra dim(0.55) on the dropdown, which is optional taste.

### menus-8 [confirmed] In-run overlays sit on the HUD: the pause panel covers the WEAPONS label, and embedded Options puts BACK on the HP orb and the key hint on the loadout
- defect / code - public/js/ui/gamescreen.js:1526 (pause y = H/2 - ph/2 + 4), :184 (HUD drawn under options); public/js/ui/screens.js:1139 (embedded dim 0.7 only), :1217 (BACK at 14, H-20), :1239 (keysHint at H-16) (technical lens only)
- Evidence: 1280x720__C02: BACK (device 28-128, 680-706) sits on the HP orb with '114' just above it, and 'ARROWS adjust · ENTER select · ESC back' runs across the WEAPONS loadout box (555-720 × 645-712). 1366x768__C01 and 844x390@1.5-phone__C01: the pause panel's bottom frame (y≈662 at 1366, ≈478 on the phone) lands on the 'WEAPONS' label.
- These overlays are centred on the whole screen and ignore the HUD reserved at the bottom (orbs and loadout). The HUD stays drawn underneath at only 30-40% dim, so controls and HUD readouts end up stacked on each other.
- Fix: Pause: y = min(centred, g.hud.loadout(W, H).y - ph - 6), clamped ≥ 14. Embedded Options: skip g.hud.draw while this.options is open, or pass foot = loadout.y - 4 and place BACK/CONTROLS/CREDITS/keysHint above it. A stronger dim (0.9) also helps.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real. I checked it in the pixels and in the code. The captures come from real Chrome. They reach the pause through GameScreen.pause(true), the normal pause without its sound, and they open Options by pressing Enter on 'opts', which is the real path. So these are not capture artefacts. What the zoomed crops show (verify/hudov/): - 1366x768: the pause panel's bottom frame cuts off the top rows of the loadout's WEAPONS label, so it reads as "WEHPUNS". - 844x390@1.5 phone: the label is almost entirely hidden under the frame. - 1280x720 C02: BACK sits on the lower half of the HP orb, with "114" just above it. CONTROLS and CREDITS straddle the loadout's WEAPONS header. "ARROWS adjust · ENTER select · ESC back" runs across the loadout cells. What the code confirms: - gamescreen.js:184 draws g.hud.draw first, then the overlay, while paused. - gamescreen.js:1526: the panel is placed at y = round(H/2 - ph/2) + 4 and never looks at the loadout. - screens.js:1139 embedded Options only applies a 0.7 dim. BACK is fixed at (14, H-20) at screens.js:1217, CONTROLS and CREDITS at H-44, and keysHint at H-16 (screens.js:66). - The HP orb is 48 px at (4, H-52) (hud.js:654, ORB_R 21). The loadout bottom is at H-3 on keyboard and H-25 on touch (loadout.js:54). The numbers for 1366x768: - S=3, so the view is 456x256. - 4 weapons give ph=182, so the panel's y is 41 and its bottom is 223. - The loadout top is 219 (HEADER 11 + ROW 20 + 3 above 253). Phone: S=2, so the view is 633x293. The panel's bottom is 242 and the loadout top is about 234 on touch. At 1280x720 (H=360) the panel clears the belt, which matches C01. It gets worse in a full late-run build. With 6 weapons or 6 powers the panel's ph is 212. That overlaps the belt at 1366x768 by about 19 px. At 1920x1080 (view 480x270) it also overlaps, so it is not only a phone problem. Severity is defect: controls and readouts sit on top of each other on every pause at the most common laptop size and on phones. None of the other session's uncommitted hunks touch these lines. They are demo-mode edits: the demoChoice import and enter() in gamescreen.js, and DEMO strings and the dialog in screens.js. Both files still hold that work, so stage by hunk. The proposed fix works and is minimal for the captured cases. Two limits: - Clamping the pause panel to ≥14 still leaves an overlap for tall panels on a 256-high view. For example, with 6 rows at 1366x768, 219 - 212 - 6 = 1, which clamps to 14 and still overlaps by 7 px. - visiblePowers is still worked out from H, not from the room above the belt. Better fix: Pause (gamescreen.js:1520-1526): use foot = g.hud.loadout(W, H).y - 6 as the bottom limit. - Work out visiblePowers from (foot - 14 - 124 - (auto ? 18 : 0)) / 15 instead of (H - 144 - auto) / 15. - Set ph = Math.min(foot - 14, ...) and y = clamp(Math.min(Math.round(H/2 - ph/2) + 4, foot - ph), 14, H - ph - 4). - When 5-6 weapons still won't fit above the belt on a 256-high view, let the panel cover the belt completely rather than half of it. The panel already lists the same build. A simple way is a `g.hud.hideBelt = true` flag while PauseOverlay is up, so it never clips the label. Embedded Options: the one-line fix is at gamescreen.js:184. Skip the HUD while it is open: `if ((g.state !== 'over' || !this.overlay) && !this.overlay?.options) g.hud.draw(...)`. That is less intrusive than moving BACK, CONTROLS, CREDITS and the hint per caller, and the title-screen Options stays as it is. The 0.7 world dim is then enough, so the 0.9 dim isn't needed. If the owner wants the HUD kept visible there, the other route is to pass a foot into OptionsScreen.draw(…, embedded, foot) and use it in place of H for the BACK, CONTROLS/CREDITS and keysHint rows.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding holds up. I checked the captures. In 1366x768__C01 (H=256 at S=3) the PAUSED panel's bottom frame sits on the loadout's WEAPONS label. In 844x390@1.5-phone__C01 it lands on the plate as well. In 1280x720__C02 the embedded Options puts BACK at (14, H-20) right under the HP orb's "114", the key hint at H-16 crosses the loadout plate, and CONTROLS/CREDITS at H-44 cut into the top of the plate. The pause overlap is not limited to odd screens. At 1920x1080 (480x270) the belt's top is at y=234. With 5 weapons, ph=197 and centred y=41, so the panel's bottom is at 238. With 4 weapons plus the "Level-ups: always" line, or with 5-6 powers, it overlaps too. So most late-game pauses on the most common desktop size will hit it. Nothing marks this as deliberate: - The centred y (gamescreen.js:1526) and the embedded dim 0.7 (screens.js) date from the initial commit 84963a5, with no comment about the HUD. - The comment at gamescreen.js:184 ("the clock that keeps going in crypts") is about which clock is passed to the HUD, not about keeping the HUD visible under menus. - No earlier review in reviews/ covers it. It doesn't touch any of the owner's settled calls. - The same file already keeps another panel clear of the belt: drawFinish uses min(..., hud.loadout(W,H).y - bh - 47). Uncommitted work: screens.js and gamescreen.js both hold another session's uncommitted DEMO edits (imports, demoChoice, title items). Those hunks don't touch the pause or Options draw code, but they shift line numbers: BACK is now at screens.js:1223 and keysHint at 1239. The fix is draw-only. It changes no gameplay, adds no Math.random, and leaves the hero looks alone. Better fix: Make both changes in gamescreen.js only, and leave screens.js (the other session's file) alone. (1) Pause, line 1526: `const belt = g.hud.loadout(W, H); const y = Math.max(14, Math.min(Math.round(H / 2 - ph / 2) + 4, belt.y - ph - 6));`. This matches how drawFinish already avoids the belt. - Known limit: at H≈256-270 with 6 listed rows (ph=212), the panel can't fit between the top clock (drawn at y=8) and the belt. The title banner (drawn at y-8) then covers the HUD clock. - That is acceptable because the panel shows TIME LEFT itself. If the clock must stay clear, use a floor of 25 instead and accept a few pixels of overlap in that one case. (2) Embedded Options, line 184: skip the HUD while the pause overlay's Options is open, the same way the Results overlay already skips it. For example: `if ((g.state !== 'over' || !this.overlay) && !this.overlay?.options) g.hud.draw(...)`. - This also covers Options' own dialogs and its music menu. - It is better than moving BACK, CONTROLS, CREDITS and the hint above the belt. That would mean editing screens.js and squeezing rows that are already packed tightly for a 240-high embed (`foot = H - 46`). - A stronger 0.9 dim is not needed.

### menus-9 [confirmed] Results panel: the last line (the Armory line) is drawn on the panel's bottom frame
- defect / code - public/js/ui/gamescreen.js:2187 (drawText at y + ph - 11), NEXT at :2176-2186 (y + ph - 20), nearLines :2174. Found by both critics (in critic A's text-on-frames finding).
- Evidence: art-extra/results-bottom.png and tech-extra/fallen-bottom.png (1280x720__C08/C09, y≈517-525 device): 'Velocity I · 60 gold · +6% projectile speed' sits on the trim row, and the j/p/g descenders break the line. Maths: the glyph box spans y+ph-11..y+ph-3 and its outline ends at y+ph-2, while the trim line is row h-3 and the bracket arm row is h-4. The death screen also leaves an empty band under the stats (C09 y 250-320) while the bottom is crammed.
- On every results screen (quit, death and presumably victory) the closing line touches or overlaps the frame, which reads as text overflowing the panel.
- Fix: Draw the Armory line at y + ph - 14 or -15, NEXT at y + ph - 23, and the near-lock lines 3 px higher. Also add 3-4 to ph (base and tight, counted in need()) so the bottom block keeps a 2-px gap above the arm row.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): Confirmed in the pixels and in the code. I dumped the logical rows of 1280x720__C08-results-final.png (S=2) under the panel bottom with verify/results-bottom/rows.mjs. The Armory line's glyph box starts at row 225, which is y+ph-11 with y+ph=236. Its capitals run 225-231 with the outline at 232. The descenders of y, g, p and j fill rows 232-233. Row 233 is the trim line (y+ph-3), and in the dump the descender ink and its dark outline cut the orange trim at every descender. Row 234, the frame's ink line (y+ph-2), takes their outline as well. The crop verify/results-bottom/c08-velocity-x8.png shows the orange trim broken under 'y' and 'g'. C09 (the death screen) looks the same. The frame geometry matches the critic's maths. I dumped the 48x48 panel piece, which is nine-sliced 12/12/12/12. Its bottom rows are: source 47 corners only, 46 the ink line, 45 the trim across the full width, 44 the bracket arm near the corners only. In a panel that puts the trim at h-3 and the arm at h-4. Letters without descenders also sit flush: their outline at h-4 touches the trim directly. The NEXT line sits only 9 px above (y+ph-20), so its descenders (row 224) also touch the Armory line's top outline. Neither line is a capture artefact: the Armory line is drawn this way on every results screen. Victory takes the same path, only with the gold trim. The line is always 'X · N gold · ...' or 'Progress kept for this session only', so a descender always cuts the trim. History: commit bcbd3e1 placed it at ph-11 when panels still used the old one-pixel trim. Commit 7708271 (Lucifer's bracketed frame) later moved the trim up to h-3, so this is a regression from the frame swap. The results code on disk matches HEAD; the other session's uncommitted edits in gamescreen.js touch only imports and fields near the top. Line numbers on disk are about 3 higher than in HEAD. Why fixSound=false: the direction is right, but as written the fix closes another gap. It raises the near-lock lines, NEXT and Armory by 3 and grows ph by 3, but gy (y+ph-51-nNear*10) stays in place. gy anchors the gold formula, the big +N GOLD, the notes, the chips and the unsaved line. The big gold's outline ends at gy+26, which is y+ph-45 with nNear=2. The first near line's outline starts at y+ph-41. Today that leaves 3 clear rows. After the proposed change the near line's outline would start at y+ph-44 and touch the gold's outline with no gap at all. With nNear=0 the same happens between the gold and NEXT. The fix must move the whole bottom block, gy included, not just the last three lines. Better fix: Move the whole bottom-anchored block up together by 4 (3 is the minimum to clear the frame; 4 also gives NEXT and the Armory line the standard 10-px LINE_H spacing): - gy = y + ph - 55 - nNear * 10. This also carries the notes, the chips, the unsaved line, 'Counting your spoils' and SAVE AGAIN, which are all placed relative to gy. - Near lines: y + ph - 24 - (nNear - i) * 10. - ny (NEXT and its bar) = y + ph - 24. - Armory or 'Progress kept…' line: y + ph - 14. Its descender outline then ends at h-5, with one clear stone row (h-4) above the trim at h-3 and 3 clear rows under its capitals. - Add 4 to the constant in need() (… + notes.length * 10 + 59), so layouts that set ph by tight or by cap keep their room. Leave base (178) alone. In the usual layout base has about 17-27 px of slack (the empty band under the stats), so the shift comes out of that band. The panel and the buttons then don't move, and the band the critic noticed shrinks a little. If you would rather keep exactly 9 px from NEXT to the Armory line, shift everything by 3 instead: gy -54, NEXT/near -23, Armory -14, need +58.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): The finding is real and it is not deliberate. In public/js/ui/gamescreen.js:2187 the Armory line is drawn at y + ph - 11. The normal font's glyph box is 9 rows tall (font.js GLYPH_H_ = 9), so it covers ph-11..ph-3, and its outline reaches ph-2. Dumping the 48x48 'panel' piece (skin.js panelFrame, nine-sliced with 12-px corners) shows the frame's rows: h-4 is the bracket arm, h-3 is the trim line across the full width, and h-2 is the ink line. That puts the descenders of g, j, p and y on the arm and the trim, and the outline on the ink. I checked this on screen in menus/1280x720__C08 and C09 and in hud/art-extra/victory-bottom.png, so the quit, death and victory screens all have it. The git history shows how it happened. The y+ph-11 / ph-20 / ph-51 offsets date from bcbd3e1 and 58ead70, which used the old 1-px flat frame. The bracketed Lucifer panel frame arrived a day later in 7708271 (2026-10-20), and these offsets were never adjusted. So this is a regression from the reskin, not a choice. None of the earlier reviews mention it, and it doesn't touch any of the owner's settled calls. The other session's uncommitted edits in gamescreen.js are 3 inserted lines elsewhere in the file and don't touch this block. The critic's fix is the right idea but incomplete as written. It moves NEXT, the near-lock lines and the Armory line up and grows ph, but it leaves gy alone (gy = y + ph - 51 - nNear*10 at :2097). gy anchors the 2x '+N GOLD' total, the gold formula, the notes and the chips. Grow ph by 3-4 without moving gy and that whole block slides down 3-4 px. The total's outline (gy+26) would then touch the first near/NEXT line's outline (it lands at gy+27, against gy+30 today). The critic also mixes 3-px and 4-px numbers. I tested a consistent 4-px version in real Chrome on :3017, serving the patched module through page.route; the repo was not touched. Screenshots are in .../menus/verify/armory-line/: 1280x720-full-compare.png, 960x540-full-compare.png and bottom-crops.png. Afterwards the line sits clear of the frame with the same inset as the top (4 stone rows under capitals, 2 under descenders). Everything above stays where it was, and the buttons don't move. On a cap-bound screen (480x270 art) the extra 4 px can cost one near-lock line. That's the existing give-way order working as intended. The results.test.js low-screen test still holds, since its H=600 case has plenty of room. The fix changes no gameplay and no Math.random calls. The 'empty band under the stats' remark is the room the panel's minimum height (base) holds for the weapons and the unlocks; a code comment says it is kept on purpose so nothing moves, so I don't count it as a defect. Better fix: Give the bottom block one 4-px foot and shift ALL FOUR bottom anchors, including gy, by it. The tested patch, in public/js/ui/gamescreen.js: (1) need() :2034: '... + notes.length * 10 + 55' -> '+ 59'. (2) base :2056: '178 + notes.length * 10' -> '182 + ...'. (3) gy :2097: 'y + ph - 51 - nNear * 10' -> 'y + ph - 55 - nNear * 10'. (4) near lines :2173: 'y + ph - 20 - (nNear - i) * 10' -> 'y + ph - 24 - ...'. (5) ny :2174: 'ny = y + ph - 20' -> 'ny = y + ph - 24'. (6) Armory line :2187: 'y + ph - 11' -> 'y + ph - 15'. The panel grows by 4 and its contents stay where they were. The Armory line ends with the same inset as the first row's top (4 stone rows under capitals). The script is at C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/armory-line/verify.mjs. An equivalent 3-line alternative: draw the frame 4 px taller (panel(ctx, x, y, pw, ph + 4, ...)), use 'by = Math.min(H - 20, y + ph + 10)', and set 'cap = H - 56 - L.height * s'. Do not move only NEXT, the near lines and Armory while leaving gy put: the '+N GOLD' total would then touch the line below it.

### menus-10 [confirmed] Text and icons sit on 9-slice frame lines: button labels past their points, the Codex tile, the Armory row icons, the music picker label
- defect / code - public/js/ui/screens.js:211-213 and :240 (Save Unreadable/Offline button bw 124); screens.js:951-953 (Codex tile: icon centred, name at y+3); screens.js:641-642 (Armory row icon at cy+2); public/js/ui/music-picker.js:107 (art lens)
- Evidence: art-extra/unreadable-btn.png: 'START A NEW ADVENTURE' (120 px) in a 124-px button covers both pointed ends. art-extra/codex-tile.png: 'Throwing Axe' touches the top frame, and the icon sits a line lower than the text, which leaves the tile's bottom half empty. art-extra/armory-rows.png: the Might, Haste and Vitality icons cross the row's inner frame lines. B15: '10 SECOND PREVIEW' sits on the corner bracket.
- The Lucifer frame has a 2-3 px border plus 6-px corner brackets, but content is placed 2-3 px from the edge, as if the frame were 1 px thin. Text and icons collide with the frame on several menu screens.
- Fix: Save Unreadable/Offline: bw = max(124, textWidth(label) + 30). In button(), if textWidth(label) > w - 2*side - 6, widen the button or fitText the label (and warn in DEBUG). Codex tile: start the name+summary block at y + round((h-27)/2) and align the icon with its centre. Armory rows: step = 22 with the icon at cy + 3, or crop icons to 14. Music picker: label at bounds.y + PAD + 4, x + PAD + 8.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): Three of the four cited spots are real collisions, which I confirmed by dumping pixels from the native S=2 captures and by redrawing with the game's own modules in the running page. Images are in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/frame-text/ (mock-x3.png, codex-zoom.png, armory-zoom.png, armory-h22.png, music-zoom.png, btn.png). How the Lucifer frame is built (probe of skin.panelFrame): row y+1 is ink, row y+2 is the trim line, row y+3 has a trim bar near each corner, and the corner brackets are 6x6. Content therefore needs to start at y+4 or lower. (1) Save Unreadable button, screens.js:211. textWidth('START A NEW ADVENTURE') is 119 in a 124-px button whose points are 9 px a side. The label starts at x+2 and ends at x+121, so the glyphs and their outline cover both pointed rims (btn.png). Confirmed. The Offline button's labels are short ('TRY AGAIN' is 49 px, 'TRYING…' 39 px), so that half of the citation is not a problem. (2) Codex tile, screens.js:951-953. The name is drawn at y+3, so its outline row lands on y+2 and overwrites the frame's trim line. The pixel dump of B19 shows #181425 notches in the trim row above every capital and ascender, in both the focused tile (#fee761) and the unfocused one (#5a6988). The icon sits at y+14..y+29, centred on the 44-px tile, while a one-line entry's text spans only y+3..y+21. So the icon sits lower than the text and the bottom third is empty, as described. A related case: on locked tiles the progress bar at y+h-4 replaces the bottom trim and ink rows. (3) Armory rows, screens.js:641-642. These rows are 19 px tall, under PANEL_MIN (25), so they use the old fallback frame, not Lucifer's; the finding's explanation is wrong there. The collision is still real. That frame's inner ink lines are at y+2 and y+16, its second trim colour at y+17/18, and its fill covers only 13 rows (y+3..y+15). A 16-px icon at cy+2 covers y+2..y+17. Twelve of the 20 icons have ink in row 0 and nine in row 15. In the dump, Might's cuff sits on the ink line and its outline notches the trim row. Vitality's chain crosses the top line, and Phoenix, Fortune and Armor cross the bottom. (4) Music picker, music-picker.js:107. The label is not on the corner bracket. The text starts at x+9, three pixels clear of the 6-px bracket, and its outline row (y+4) sits just under the corner trim bar (y+3) without overlapping it. It is cramped, not a collision. Severity: defect. The Codex and Armory are common screens, and the frame lines are visibly broken there. Proposed fix, part by part: - Button: sound. - Codex (block at y+9, centred): sound. My mock shows it balanced and clear of the frame. - Armory (step 22, icon at cy+3): not sound. With h=20 the ink line moves to y+17 and the trim to y+18, so icon rows 14-15 still cross the frame. Might, Vitality, Armor, Phoenix and Fortune still poke through (armory-zoom.png, middle column). - Music picker (y+8): over-shifts. The label then crowds the first row's highlight at y+16. - Widening inside button(): would put the drawn button out of step with the clickable area already registered by ui.item and with the caller's centring. Ownership note: screens.js has another session's uncommitted demo-mode edits, but none of them touch these lines. music-picker.js is clean. Better fix: 1) In screens.js drawUnreadable, set `const bw = Math.max(124, textWidth('START A NEW ADVENTURE') + 30);`, which gives 149 and fits the 236-px panel. drawOffline needs no change. Optionally, in DEBUG builds only, have button() call console.warn when textWidth(label) > w - 2*side; it should not resize the button itself. 2) Codex tile(): draw the name at y + 9 (that is, y + round((h-27)/2)) and the summary lines at y + 18 + i*9. Leave the icon at y + floor((h-16)/2); it is already centred. Alternatively, draw the name at y+5, the summary at y+14 + i*9, and move codexBar to y + h - 8. That also stops the locked tile's bar from replacing the bottom frame line. 3) Armory: for a clean fit, use step = 24 so rows are h = 22, and draw the icon at cy + 3 and the name and rank text at cy + 7. The fallback frame's fill is h-6 rows tall, so only h ≥ 22 holds a 16-px icon whole (armory-h22.png). A cheaper compromise is step = 22 with the icon left at cy + 2: only the icons' outer rows (0 and 15) then rest on the inner ink lines, and none notch the trim. The proposed cy + 3 with h = 20 leaves nine icons' bottom row on the trim. 4) Music picker: this is polish only. Draw the label at bounds.y + PAD + 2 and keep x. y + PAD + 4 would press it onto the first row's highlight at y + 16.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): I checked this against the screenshots and against mock-ups drawn with the game's own panel(), button() and drawText(). No settled owner call or earlier review covers it, and git history shows it was never a deliberate choice. Three of the four parts are real. (1) Codex tile. The 'normal' font has its capitals on row 0 of the glyph box, with a 1-px outline above that. So a name drawn at y+3 puts its outline on the frame's gold trim row (y+2). In B19 and B20 the top line of every tile is broken above letters like T, h, A and G (verify/skeptic-frame/codex-b19-top-x8.png). The y+3 placement came from f98dc6b, when tiles were 33 px tall. Commit 0b4d66e later raised them to 44 px without moving the text. That is why the text now hugs the top while the icon is centred lower and the bottom of one-line tiles is empty. (2) Save Unreadable button. The label 'START A NEW ADVENTURE' is 119 px wide in a 124-px button, so its first and last letters sit on the pointed ends. You can see it even at 1x in A09. The screen is rare, but the flaw is plain. (3) Armory rows. The rows are 19 px tall, which is under PANEL_MIN (25). So they use ui.js's 1-px fallback frame, not the Lucifer frame the critic assumed. Its fill is only 13 rows tall, and a 16-px icon drawn at cy+2 cannot fit. I measured all 20 icons: 10 reach the bottom trim band and most cover the inner ink lines. It is visible on Might, Vitality and Luck (armory-rows-x6.png, armory-cmp-x7.png). (4) Music picker header. '10 SECOND PREVIEW' sits one outline row under the corner bracket's inner arm. It is cramped but does not overlap any frame pixel, so this part is polish at most. The critic's fix is not sound as written: - The button() guard. Its threshold of w - 2*side - 6 would also flag BACK, PREV and NEXT in 44-px buttons, and the arrow buttons. Those look fine today. With fitText they would be cut to 'BA…', and widening inside button() would move the drawn button away from the click area its caller laid out. - The armory fix (step 22 with the icon at cy+3) still leaves the same 10 of 20 icons in the bottom trim band. I checked this in the mock-up. - The music fix (y + PAD + 4) pushes the header's outline down against the first row's highlight at y+16. - The Codex fix leaves the switch at y+4. The switch belongs on the name's row, which the name's width reserves room for. None of these touch gameplay or Math.random. Better fix: Smallest fixes: 1. Save Unreadable, screens.js ~line 211: use `const label = 'START A NEW ADVENTURE', bw = textWidth(label) + 28;`, which gives 147 px, and pass `label` to the button. It fits easily in pw 236; checked in the mock-up. Leave button() alone. At most, add a DEBUG-only console.warn there when textWidth(label) > w - 20. Do not add fitText or auto-widening. 2. Codex tile(), screens.js ~lines 950-958: move the text block down to suit the 44-px tile. Use `const ty = y + 9`. Draw the name at ty and the summary lines at ty + 9 + i*9. Move codexSwitch to y + 10 so it stays on the name's row. The icon stays centred, and the progress bar at y+h-4 still fits below the second line. If you want only the minimum, move the name to y+4, the summary to y+13 + i*9 and the switch to y+5. That alone stops the outline from cutting the trim. 3. Armory, screens.js line 618: change step from 21 to 22 and leave the icon at cy + 2 and the text at cy + 6. With h = 20, the fallback frame's inner ink lines sit at rows 2 and 17, which exactly bound every 16-px icon: 0 of 20 reach the trim bands, against 10 of 20 now. The list is about 5% taller. Step 23 with the icon at cy+3 and text at cy+7 also works and gives more air. 4. Music picker, music-picker.js line 107: draw the label at bounds.y + PAD + 2 (one row lower) and leave x as it is. This is optional polish. screens.js has another session's uncommitted demo hunks near lines 110-160 and 255, so stage only these hunks. Mock-ups (now vs fix) are in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/skeptic-frame/mock-x4.png, armory-cmp-x7.png and armory-cmp2-x7.png.

### menus-11 [confirmed] Narrow buttons break the 9-slice: the point is truncated into a '+' cross, used as every scroll arrow
- defect / code - public/js/ui/skin.js:85-90 buttonFace (side = min(9, w/2 - 2) takes the first `side` columns of a 9-px point); callers hero-selection.js:347-348 (14x12 ↑/↓), menu-dialog.js:33-34 (18x14), reward-reels.js:451-452 (15x12) (art lens)
- Evidence: specimen-pieces-x3.png 'narrow buttons (w 14/20/30/44)': w14 is a cross and w20 a lumpy diamond (art-extra/narrow.png). art-extra/hs-info-corner.png (1920x1080 hero info): the ↑ button is a cross with the glyph sticking out, sitting on the corner bracket; ↓ overlaps the bottom frame. art-extra/eq-arrows.png (Equipment dialog).
- Slicing a pointed end narrower than its art cuts the slope off instead of redrawing it, so small buttons lose the Lucifer silhouette. The 5x7 '↑' glyph with its outline is also taller than the 12-px face. These arrows appear on hero select at 480x270, in dialogs and on the spoils screen.
- Fix: Add a dedicated arrow button: an 11x11 cap in the button palette (rim #9e5b47, face #3e2731, shade #2b1b26, ink #0d0b14) with a drawn 5x3 chevron and the same states as BUTTON. Or, in buttonFace for w < 26, rasterise a 1:1 stepped point of `side` rows instead of slicing. Place the arrows 4 px inside the frame (x + w - 22, y + 6 / y + h - 20).
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed this in code and in pixels from real Chrome. Script: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/narrow-verify.mjs. Image: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/narrow-actual-x10.png. How the shape breaks: the 'button' piece (pieces.js, 48x16) has a 9-column point that climbs 2 px per row (rows 1-13). For w=14, buttonFace (skin.js:89) sets side = min(9, 7-2) = 5. nine() then copies only source columns 0-4. Those columns are empty above row 4 and below row 10, so the cut drops the slope instead of scaling it. Meanwhile the 4-column middle runs the full height. A pixel dump of buttonFace(14x12, idle) shows a '+' cross: .....####..... / .....RRRR..... / .....ffff..... / ...##ffff##... / .##RRffffRR##. / #RRffffffffRR# / ###ffffffff### / .####ffff####. / ...##ffff##... / .....ssss..... / .....####..... / .....####..... The 15x12 reward-reels button gives the same cross. The 18x14 dialog button and the 20-px one come out as notched, lumpy diamonds. The focus state turns the cross into an orange '+' with arms. Callers: hero-selection.js:347-348 (14x12). These lines are committed, but the file has another session's uncommitted edits elsewhere. Also menu-dialog.js:33-34 (18x14) and reward-reels.js:451-452 (15x12). skin.js and ui.js are unmodified. Two smaller claims are overstated: 1. The glyph is not taller than the face. The '↑' plus its outline covers rows y+1 to y+9 of a 12-px face. It overlaps the rim only because the middle strip is 4 px wide, narrower than the 7-px outlined glyph. 2. In hero select, the ↓ button does not overlap the bottom frame. Its last row is y+h-6 and the frame row is y+h-2. Both buttons' right arms do touch the right frame line (arm ends at x+w-5, frame column at x+w-4), with no gap. The core defect stands: every narrow scroll arrow loses the Lucifer pointed silhouette. Why the proposed fix is not the best: a new 11x11 arrow cap means new art and a new glyph. Rasterising a 1:1 stepped point for w<26 works but is more code than needed. Moving the hero-select arrows to x+w-22 pushes them further over the text, and it edits a file with another session's uncommitted work. It is also not needed for the 3 other screens. Better fix: Change skin.js buttonFace only. Always take the whole 9-column point from the source and squeeze it with nearest-neighbour into `side` destination columns, instead of cutting off the first `side` columns. Do this either with an optional source-margin argument on nine() (e.g. nine(ctx, img, x, y, w, h, [side, top, side, bottom], 1, 13, [9, top, 9, bottom])) or with three direct drawImage calls in buttonFace: cols = [[0, 9, x, side], [9, img.width - 18, x + side, w - 2*side], [img.width - 9, 9, x + w - side, side]]. Buttons with w >= 22 already have side = 9, so plates, menu buttons and panel titles come out pixel-identical. Only narrow buttons change, and they become a clean lozenge in every state with the palette kept. I mocked this in real Chrome against the current code at 14x12, 15x12 (off), 18x14, 20, 30 and 14-26 bare faces. Script: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/squeeze-mock.mjs. Comparison image: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/squeeze-vs-current-x6.png. The ↑/↓ glyphs then sit fully on the face. Optional, separately: move the hero-select arrows 1-2 px left (x + w - 19 or x + w - 20) so the right arm no longer touches the frame. That line is in hero-selection.js, so coordinate with the session that has uncommitted work there.
- Intent sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed the bug in the code and in renders. The Lucifer button piece (48x16; rows 1-13 used) has a 9-column point on each side, stepping 2 px per row: the outline starts at column 9, 7, 5, 3, 1, 0 down to the tip. buttonFace sets side = min(9, floor(w/2) - 2), so any button narrower than 22 px gets a corner slice of only 5-8 columns. Those columns hold just the lower part of the slope, while the stretched middle keeps the full-height top and bottom outline. The result is a '+' cross at w 11-18 and a lumpy diamond at w 20-21. Buttons 22 px and wider are fine. Narrow callers that show it: - hero-selection.js:347-348, hero info scroll ↑/↓, 14x12. Overflows at 480x270 (1920x1080). - menu-dialog.js:33-34, Equipment and other dialogs, 18x14. - reward-reels.js:451-452, claim details, 15x12. - prologue.js:123, road pause button, 20x15 (the critic missed this one). The cross is narrower than the 5x7 arrow glyph, so the glyph and its outline stick out of it. Not deliberate: - The code comment says "a narrow button keeps them shorter", meaning shorter points, not truncated ones. - The git log has only the two original skin commits (7708271, 2ae065a). - No review in reviews/ covers it, and it touches none of the owner's settled calls. Renders in verify/narrow-btn/ come from a patched skin.js served through Playwright route interception, so the repo is untouched: - narrow-now-vs-fix-x6.png - eq-arrows-now-left-fix-right-x8.png - hero-info-arrows-now-left-fix-right-x8.png With the patch, every narrow button becomes a clean stepped hexagon or diamond with outline, rim and drop shadow, in all five states. The glyph sits inside the face, and buttons 22 px and wider stay pixel-identical. On the critic's other points: - The placement complaint does not hold up. At 480x270 the ↓ button sits about 2 px clear of the bottom frame, and with the fix both arrows clear the corner brackets. Moving the arrows would also mean editing hero-selection.js, which has another session's uncommitted work in progress. - The dedicated 11x11 chevron cap (option 1) is new art plus a new API, more than the problem needs. - Option 2 (a stepped point inside buttonFace) is the right idea. Its "w < 26" threshold is wrong, though: it would needlessly redraw 22-25 px buttons that already look correct. The fix is purely cosmetic: hit rectangles are unchanged and there is no Math.random or gameplay effect. skin.js, ui.js, menu-dialog.js and reward-reels.js have no uncommitted changes. Better fix: Change only buttonFace in public/js/ui/skin.js. Do not touch callers, add art or move the arrows. Below 22 px, draw each point 1 px per row by taking every other column of the source's 2-px slope: source columns 0,1,3,5,7 on the left and 40,42,44,46,47 on the right. Each point is then 5 px wide, with outline, rim and shade all kept.
