At 22 px and wider, keep today's [9, top, 9, bottom] nine-slice, which matches the current code exactly at those widths. export function buttonFace(ctx, x, y, w, h, state = 'idle') {   const img = skin('button', BUTTON[state] || BUTTON.idle, state);   const top = Math.min(6, h >> 1), bottom = Math.min(6, h - top);   if (w >= 22) return nine(ctx, img, x, y, w, h, [9, top, 9, bottom], 1, 13);   // Too narrow for the 9-px points: steeper ones, a pixel a row (every other column of the slope).   const rows = [[1, top, y, top], [1 + top, 13 - top - bottom, y + top, h - top - bottom], [14 - bottom, bottom, y + h - bottom, bottom]];   for (const [sy, sh, dy, dh] of rows) {     if (dh <= 0 || sh <= 0) continue;     [0, 1, 3, 5, 7].forEach((sx, i) => ctx.drawImage(img, sx, sy, 1, sh, x + i, dy, 1, dh));     ctx.drawImage(img, 9, sy, 30, sh, x + 5, dy, w - 10, dh);     [40, 42, 44, 46, 47].forEach((sx, i) => ctx.drawImage(img, sx, sy, 1, sh, x + w - 5 + i, dy, 1, dh));   } } This works down to w = 11 and was verified in verify/narrow-btn/narrow.mjs.

### menus-12 [confirmed] Backdrop clouds are flat 3-px rectangles that read as glitch lines; one grows out of the HELL tab
- defect / code - public/js/ui/background.js:553-560 (clouds: fillRect 70-100 x 3 and 40 x 2); hell theme clouds :454 (y 0.16 + i*0.07 lands on the map-tab row) (art lens)
- Evidence: art-extra/hell-tab.png: a dark bar runs from the HELL tab's right point (1280x720__D09 x 975-1000, y 266-270); it is the hell theme's fourth cloud, at y = 0.37H. White bars in D10 (210-300, 125), D11 (215-300, 108) and right of the Ranked card (980-1010, 228). Faint bars on the title B01 (130-300, 137) and (415-665, 188).
- Every other backdrop element is shaped pixel art. The clouds are bare rectangles, and in the busy UI band they look like stray rendering lines or a bug attached to a button.
- Fix: Draw clouds as 2-3 stacked bands with stepped and dithered ends (for example widths 40/70/52 on rows y-2, y, y+2). Keep their y list above 0.3H on the trial screens, or skip the cloud that would cross y 0.33-0.4H.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, defect): I confirmed this in the pixels and in the code. In public/js/ui/background.js:553-560 each cloud is two translucent rectangles: a base of 70+10i x 3 and a 40 x 2 strip on top at cy-2, set off by 10 px. Its height is cy = H*(clouds.y + i*0.07), a fraction of the screen height. The hell look sets clouds.y to 0.16, which puts the 4th cloud at 0.37H. The 1280x720 capture is S=2 with 640x360 art, so that is art row 133, drawn on rows 131-135. The map tabs sit at a fixed art y of about 129-140. A pixel dump of 1280x720__D09 shows a 3-art-px bar (#1b0d0f against the #381a12 sky) on screen rows 266-271, starting right at HELL's right point and running to x 1003. The rest of that 100-px cloud is hidden behind the HELL and FOREST tabs. A zoomed crop (verify/clouds/d09-x4.png) reads exactly as a tail growing out of the tab. The cloud drifts at 6 art px/s on a 240 s loop and is on screen about 92% of the time. For that whole stretch the dark bar sits in line with the tab row: it sticks out of a tab, shows through a gap, or carries the row on into open sky. D10 and D11 both show a light flat bar beside the Ranked card (zoom: verify/clouds/d10-card-x4.png). There, only the 3-row base shows, because the top strip is behind the card, so it looks like a tab fixed to the card. The faint bars on the title (B01) read fine as thin streaks. This is not a capture artefact: the clouds are drawn this way in the game itself. Two limits on the finding: 1. The clash with the tab row depends on resolution. It only happens when the art height is about 350-380, which means S=2 and a window 700-760 CSS px tall, such as 1280x720. At 1366x768 (S=3, H=256) and 1920x1080 (S=4, H=270) the 4th cloud lands behind the cards. I checked this in the 1366 capture and by calculation. 2. "Flat 3-px rectangles" overstates it a little: each cloud is already two stacked bands with a stepped end. background.js is not among the other session's uncommitted files. The proposed fix is in the right direction but neither minimal nor quite right. The rule "keep y above 0.3H on the trial screens" is framed wrongly. The UI rows sit at fixed art px (cards y 41-125, tabs about 129-140), while cloud height scales with H. At H=360, 0.3H is still at card height, and MenuScene has no idea which screen it is drawing. The dithered end pixels in the proposed shape show up as stray specks in a mock (verify/clouds/mock-x4.png, column B). Moving the clouds also does nothing for the bars beside the Ranked card: clouds at card height will always come out from the card sides, at every resolution. Only a real cloud shape makes those read as clouds passing behind. Better fix: 1. Placement, the actual defect. Change one number in each of two looks in background.js THEMES. Set hell clouds.y from 0.16 to 0.09, so the clouds sit at 0.09/0.16/0.23/0.30H; at H=360 the 4th lands on art row 108, behind the lower half of the cards, and still crosses the black sun at 0.28H. Set meadow from 0.14 to 0.10: its 4th cloud is now at 0.35H (art 126, rows 124-128), which fills the 3-px gap between the card row and the tab row. Do not add a per-screen rule. The desert, frozen, meadow and hell looks only appear on the trial screen anyway, and the forest look (4th cloud at 0.33H, rows 117-121) already stays clear of the tabs. 2. Shape, the polish part. Replace the two fillRects with a tapered silhouette drawn as one fillRect per scanline. Rows must never overlap, because th.clouds.col is translucent and overlapping rows would show darker seams. For example, use offsets/widths [22,14],[16,30],[6,52],[0,w],[4,0.9w],[14,0.5w] with w = 70+10i, which keeps the spread across sizes. Skip the lone dither pixels. If wanted, bake each cloud once per look in scenery() and draw it with drawImage. With this shape, the clouds that come out beside the trial cards read as clouds passing behind them, not as bars attached to the UI.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): Partly real, and smaller than the critic says. I checked it in real Chrome against :3017, setting MenuScene.t to fixed times. Images are in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/clouds/ (sheet2-hell-1280.png, zoom-hell-t10.png, sheet-hell-t10.png; scripts clouds.mjs and clouds2.mjs). What is real: at 640x360 (S=2), the hell theme puts its 4th cloud at round(0.37H) = 133, rows 131-135. That is the centre of the map-tab row (my = 41+84+4 = 129, 13 tall). So the dark bar sits level with the tabs' pointed tips. Where it crosses the black sun's orange halo, it shows as a stub growing out of the HELL tab's right tip. That happens about 1/8 of each 133 s cycle (cloud left edge at x 388-488). Elsewhere the dark cloud on the dark sky is nearly invisible. Nothing marks this as deliberate. The cloud code is unchanged since the first commit (84963a5). Hell's y 0.16 arrived with the biome themes in 116f331, and no comment mentions clearance from the UI. No earlier review mentions clouds or the backdrop, and no owner call covers them. What is overstated: - The clouds are not flat 3-px rectangles. Each is two stacked bands: 40x2 on top, offset 10, over 70-100x3. - On the title (forest, B01) they read as faint night streaks. - The white bars in D10/D11 are clouds passing behind the card panels, which is ordinary occlusion. - Reshaping the clouds is a matter of taste. - Severity is polish, not defect: it is transient, under a 0.45 dim, and mostly limited to hell. The proposed fix does not solve it. I prototyped the stepped reshape (5 non-overlapping rows, puff on top). The stub still reads as an extension of the HELL tab tip. Moving the base to 0.12 so all clouds sit above 0.33H only moves the stub to the Ranked card's bottom-right corner ornament (frames hell-yOnly / hell-protoY). Skipping a single cloud would be resolution-dependent coupling to the screen layout. The reshape would also change the title's forest backdrop, which the file header says stays "pixel for pixel" as it was. Better fix: Give each theme its own cloud spacing and leave the shape alone. In public/js/ui/background.js draw(), change `const cy = H * (th.clouds.y + i * 0.07);` to `const cy = H * (th.clouds.y + i * (th.clouds.dy || 0.07));`. In THEMES, set hell to `clouds: { y: 0.16, dy: 0.05, col: 'rgba(12,4,8,0.6)' }` and optionally meadow to `clouds: { y: 0.14, dy: 0.05, ... }`, since meadow's 4th cloud (0.35H = 126) runs through the 3-px gap between the cards and the tabs. At 640x360 the hell clouds then sit at 58/76/94/112 px. All four stay within the trial cards' height, clear of the corner ornaments and the tab row (129-142). Three still cross the black sun (79-123). The stub at the HELL tab is gone at t = 10, 93 and 127. Clouds now only pass behind card sides, which reads as occlusion. At 1366x768 (S=3, 456x256) the cards cover the band anyway. Forest, which is the title and every other menu, stays pixel for pixel, and desert and frozen are untouched. The change is two lines, has no effect on gameplay, and adds no Math.random: clouds depend only on scene time t in the menu-only MenuScene.draw.

### menus-13 [confirmed] Moons and suns have single-pixel nubs at north, east, south and west, plus a ruler-straight terminator
- polish / code - public/js/ui/background.js:353-364 orb() (`if (d > r) continue` with integer r; straight terminator x + y > r*0.4). Found by both critics.
- Evidence: art-extra/meadow-moon.png and tech-extra/moon-meadow.png (B10 moon at device 170-300, 180-310): lone pixels at the top, bottom and sides, a straight diagonal shadow line and plain circle craters. tech-extra/moon-title.png (B01): a nub on the top edge at device ≈1077,267.
- For integer r, only the four axis points satisfy d == r while their rim neighbours fall outside, so every disc orb() draws grows four pimples. This covers the title moon, the biome moons and suns, and the Abyss black sun. The terminator is also mechanical.
- Fix: Test `if (d > r - 0.5) continue;` and move the rim test to d > r - 1.7. For the shade, use a curved terminator (shade when hypot(x - r*0.45, y - r*0.45) > r*0.95), and dither the rim with one ordered row.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The nubs are real. The code and the pixels both show them. In public/js/ui/background.js:357-358, orb() skips a pixel when `d > r` and r is a whole number. So on each axis only the point at d == r survives on its outer row, and its neighbours fall at sqrt(r²+1) > r. I copied orb() exactly into a script and measured the top rows of the disc. They are 1 / 11 / 17 / 19 px wide at r=18 and 1 / 13 / 19 / 23 px at r=22. Every row the code draws has a lone pixel at north, east, south and west: verify/orb/orb-current.png shows it for the Darkwood and Bloomvale moons, the Sunscorch and Frostfell suns, and the Abyss black sun, at r = 9 to 22. Those sizes cover all the real sizes: from view.js S and THEMES, r = 18-22 at 1280x720 and 14-16 at 1920x1080. The cited crops show the same nubs: tech-extra/moon-meadow.png and art-extra/meadow-moon.png at all four axes, and tech-extra/moon-title.png at the top edge. background.js has no uncommitted changes from the other session. The straight terminator, though, is a taste item, not a defect. The comment above orb() says it is "the moon as the Darkwood's always was", and it gives a clean 45-degree staircase. The plain craters and the dithered rim are taste too. The proposed fix is not sound as written: (1) Its terminator has the sign reversed. "Shade when hypot(x - r*0.45, y - r*0.45) > r*0.95" makes the lit area a lens around the lower-right and shades the upper-left, so it swaps which side of the moon is lit (verify/orb/orb-critic.png). It also cuts the lit share from 0.69 to 0.56. (2) It moves only the cut and the outer rim. The black sun's inner ring test (`d > r - 2.2`) stays, so that ring shrinks to a 0.5 px band and breaks into red patches at the four flat sides (verify/orb/orb-literal-dark-and-fixed-terminator.png). (3) Cutting at r-0.5 shrinks every orb by 1 px of radius. Better fix: A minimal fix that keeps the look: in orb(), round the disc outward instead of inward and move the two rim tests by the same half pixel. Change `if (d > r) continue;` to `if (d > r + 0.5) continue;`, change `d > r - 1.2 ? edge` to `d > r - 0.7 ? edge`, and change the black sun's `d > r - 2.2 ? shade` to `d > r - 1.7 ? shade`. Leave the terminator `x + y > r * 0.4` alone. The orb still fills exactly its (2r+1)² canvas, because |x| and |y| never pass r. The rim keeps its 1.2 px width, the black sun keeps its inner ring, and the lit side stays upper-left. The nubs become flat runs of 7-9 px; at r=22 the top rows are 9/17/21/25 wide (verify/orb/orb-minimal.png). The halo gradient still starts at r, so nothing else has to change. If a curved terminator is wanted later as a separate, optional change, the sign must be flipped: shade when `Math.hypot(x + r*0.45, y + r*0.45) > r*0.95`. That is a change of style the owner should decide on, not a defect fix.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The nubs are a real rasterisation artifact, not an intended look. `if (d > r) continue` with an integer r keeps the four axis pixels (d == r exactly), but their neighbours (d = sqrt(r²+1)) fall outside. I reproduced it in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/orb/orb-compare.png (made by orb-compare.mjs in the same folder). The forest and meadow moons, the desert sun and the Abyss black sun all grow four one-pixel pimples at r=9 and r=18. The same pimples show in the title moon (tech-extra/moon-title.png) and the meadow moon (art-extra/meadow-moon.png). Nothing says it is deliberate: - The check came over unchanged from the first commit's title moon (84963a5) and was generalised in 116f331. - No comment or review mentions it, and no file in reviews/ talks about the menu moon. - background.js is not among the files the other session has uncommitted. The "ruler-straight terminator" half is taste. A 45° line is a clean 1:1 pixel staircase, the moon has looked that way since day one, and the comment says it keeps "the Darkwood's ... numbers it always had". That half should not be counted toward the finding. The change touches only menu art drawn once when the scene is built. It has no gameplay effect and no Math.random calls. Better fix: Change only the three thresholds in background.js orb(). On line 358, `if (d > r) continue;` becomes `if (d > r + 0.5) continue;`. On line 360, `d > r - 1.2 ? edge` becomes `d > r - 0.7 ? edge`, and the black sun's `d > r - 2.2 ? shade` becomes `d > r - 1.7 ? shade`. This keeps each orb's size, so its edge still meets the halo that starts at r, and still fits the 2r+1 canvas. The rim and the black sun's inner ring stay the same thickness, the four nubs are gone, and the moon's lighting is untouched. The 'minimal' column of the verify sheet shows the result. The critic's fix is unsound as written: - Its curved terminator `hypot(x - r*0.45, y - r*0.45) > r*0.95` flips the lighting. The lower-right becomes lit and a shaded crescent wraps the upper-left, which reverses the moon's established light side (the 'critic' column). - It moves the rim to r-1.7 but leaves the black sun's `r - 2.2` band where it was, so that ring breaks. - `r - 0.5` shrinks every orb by about a pixel. - Dithering the rim is extra taste. Keep the straight terminator. If the owner wants a curved one anyway, `Math.hypot(x + r * 0.45, y + r * 0.45) > r * 0.95 ? shade : light` keeps the lit side upper-left (the 'curvedKeepSide' column).

### menus-14 [confirmed] Scroll windows cut text and sprites mid-glyph, and the Codex row leaves 1-px slivers of clipped icons
- polish / code - public/js/ui/hero-selection.js:315 (info clip), :214 (roster clip); public/js/ui/screens.js:635 (Armory list clip), :801-813 and codexLayout :724-728 (Codex carousel scrolled to the pixel, view = gw) (technical lens)
- Evidence: 1920x1080__B02: 'WHIRLWIND' cut through its cap height at the info panel's bottom. 1280x720__D08: 'Wraith' cut under '22 RECRUITED'. 1280x720__B16 and 1366x768__B06: the last Armory row shows only its upper half. tech-extra/codexleft.png (B20 x=33, y=148-165): a 1-px sliver of the Gale Horn icon, with the tile frame cut at the clip edge.
- Clip rectangles are set to arbitrary heights and widths, and scroll offsets move by the pixel, so the visible window rarely holds whole rows or tiles. Half-letters and lone pixels at the cut read as rendering glitches rather than as 'more below'.
- Fix: Size each window to a whole number of rows: Armory height = floor(h/step)*step, roster = k*ROW_STEP, info scroll snapped to 9-px lines (clip y+4 .. y+4+9n). Codex: snap want to multiples of L.step and set L.view = n*step - gap, with scroll arrows or a 4-px fade at each end.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): I could see all four cuts in the captures, and the code explains each one. The captures came from real Chrome; the 1920 shots were only shrunk by 1/2 with nearest sampling, which keeps the pixels exact. - **Hero info panel** (1920x1080 B02, crop in verify/clip/whirl.png): only the top 4 of the 7 glyph rows of 'WHIRLWIND' are visible at the bottom clip. The clip is x+4..y+height-4 (hero-selection.js:315). Its height has nothing to do with the line positions. - **Hero roster** (1280x720 D08, crop in verify/clip/wraith.png): 'Wraith' and its sprite are cut through the middle under '22 RECRUITED'. In 1920 B02 the bottom 'Paladin' row shows only a 2-art-px strip of letter tops and sprite, which reads as stray pixels. The cause is that the scroll window `height = h - ROSTER_HEADER - 3` is not a whole number of 36-px rows. - **Armory** (1280x720 B16, crop in verify/clip/armory-bot.png): 'Reliquary' shows only its upper half. At S=2 the window is `height = H - 77` = 283, which is 13.5 rows of step 21. The bottom clamp `most = ids.length*step - height` is not a multiple of step either, so the top row gets cut when scrolled to the end. - **Codex** (B20, tech-extra/codexleft.png): I checked the arithmetic. W=640 gives gw=608 and cw=152, so step is 158 and the view holds 3.85 tiles. With 'Grappling Hook' (index 14) brought in on the right, want = 14*158+152-608 = 1756. Gale Horn (index 11) then sits at x=-2, so 18 px of it are clipped. Its icon spans x+4..x+20, which leaves a 2-art-px column at the cut, and the tile's left frame is gone. **Is it a defect?** A partly visible row at a scroll edge is a normal 'more this way' cue, and every one of these lists already has a scrollbar or arrows. So this is polish, not a bug. The 1–2 px slivers in the roster and Codex, and letters cut through their middle, do look like glitches in a pixel-perfect UI. **Repo context:** hero-selection.js and screens.js hold another session's uncommitted DEMO work. Their hunks do not touch the clip lines, but they do change the `heroSelectionLayout` signature, and the roster fix would go in that function. **Is the proposed fix sound?** It is only partly right. - **Armory:** height = floor(h/step)*step works. Keyboard focus and the wheel (2*step) both land on multiples of step. - **Roster:** k*ROW_STEP works. The keyboard clamp then gives scroll ≡ 0 or 34 (mod 36), and both keep every row whole. - **Drag (missing from the fix):** all three of these windows drag by the pixel (Armory `this.scroll = from - dy`, roster `drag.scroll + dy`, Codex `want = scroll = from - dx`). So they need to snap to a whole step when the drag is released, or the cut rows come back. - **Info panel (wrong):** snapping it to 9-px lines will not work. Its content is not on a 9-px grid: the blocks are spaced +13, a 28-px row of stat boxes, +7, +4, +6, +10, +11, and there is a 16-px icon row. The scroll steps are 27 for the wheel and 30 for the arrow buttons, and 30 is not a multiple of 9. - **Codex:** setting view = n*step - gap at the current cw would leave 140 px of the 608-px row empty at W=640. It would also pull the row out of line with the detail panel, which is gw wide at x0. Better fix: 1. **Armory (screens.js ~619):** use `height = Math.floor((H - top - 34) / step) * step`. When the drag is released, set `this.scroll = Math.round(this.scroll / step) * step`. 2. **Roster:** in heroSelectionLayout, set roster.h to ROSTER_HEADER + k*ROW_STEP + 3, with k = floor((bottom - top - ROSTER_HEADER - 3) / ROW_STEP), so `height` is k*36. In drawRoster, round rosterScroll to a multiple of ROW_STEP when the drag is released. Keyboard and wheel already keep rows whole once the height is a whole number of rows. This function is in the other session's WIP file, so coordinate with them. 3. **Info panel (hero-selection.js details):** keep the scroll steps. Wrap drawText and the stat-box and icon draws in a guard that skips any item whose box is not wholly inside y+4..y+height-4, so only whole lines are drawn. This panel only moves by wheel and buttons, so skipping a line never makes anything pop in mid-drag. 4. **Codex (codexLayout):** fit the tiles to the width instead of shrinking the view:    - `cols = Math.ceil((gw + gap) / (C.max + gap))`    - `cw = Math.floor((gw + gap) / cols) - gap`; if cw < C.min, use one column fewer    - `view = cols * step - gap`, which comes out at about gw, so x0 and the detail panel still line up        The keyboard want already lands on multiples of step, and the wheel moves by step. When a drag is released, set `want = Math.round(want / step) * step`. While the carousel is easing, tiles still slide through the edge, which is fine.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The finding is real, and nothing the owner has settled covers it. No review in reviews/ mentions cut rows. The code itself points the other way: commit 0b4d66e ("Polish menus...") swapped the old Codex grid, whose codexLayout carried the rule "Whole rows only, never one cut in half over the panel.", for today's carousel and dropped that rule along the way. So the cuts come from that rewrite and nobody chose them. I checked the screenshots and the maths: - **Codex.** At 1280x720 the logical width W is 640, so gw=608, cw=152 and step=158, which shows 3.85 tiles. Focusing tile 14 sets want = 14*158+152-608 = 1756, which puts Gale Horn at x0-18. Its icon is drawn at x+4..x+20, so only a 1-2 px sliver survives (tech-extra/codexleft.png shows it). - **Armory.** The window height H-77 is not a multiple of step=21, so the last row is cut through its text (B16, 1366 B06). - **Roster.** On entry, the focus clamp puts the selected row flush with the bottom (index*36+34-height), so the top row gets cut under "22 RECRUITED" (D08 Wraith). - **Info panel.** The clip is height-8 and the content sits at arbitrary offsets, so "WHIRLWIND" is cut through its caps (1920 B02). These are mid-glyph cuts and stray pixels in a crisp pixel-art UI. A scrollbar, the info panel's arrow buttons and the Codex's "N / M · SCROLL TO BROWSE" line already show there is more, so the cut rows add nothing. Severity is polish. Nothing touches gameplay, update paths or Math.random. Parts of the proposed fix are not sound: - **Info panel.** Snapping the clip to y+4+9n does not stop the cuts. The content does not sit on a 9-px grid: there are gaps of 4/6/7/10/13/14 px, 28-px stat boxes and a 16-px icon row. weaponY lands at relative 68, and 68-4=64 is not a multiple of 9. The wheel moves 27 px and the arrow buttons 30 px, so the scroll is not on a grid either. - **Codex.** Setting L.view = n*step - gap without resizing the tiles shrinks the row: at 1280x720 it would drop from 608 to 468 px and leave wide empty margins. - **Roster.** Shrinking the box to k*ROW_STEP would end the roster panel up to ~26 logical px above the info panel. Today both panels end on the same bottom line. - **Armory and roster drag.** Neither fix snaps the scroll after a pixel drag, so a drag would still leave the rows misaligned. - **Unfinished work in progress.** public/js/ui/hero-selection.js and screens.js both carry another session's uncommitted edits (the DEMO-mode work). They don't touch these lines, but any change there has to be staged around them. Better fix: Same goal, smaller changes, none of them gameplay: (1) **Codex** (codexLayout in screens.js). Pick the tile count so whole tiles fill the row: n = Math.max(1, Math.ceil((gw + C.gap) / (C.max + C.gap))) and cw = Math.floor((gw - (n - 1) * C.gap) / n). This gives 147 at W=640, 145 at W=480 and 137 at W=456, all within C.min..C.max. Set view = n*step - C.gap, centre x0 on view and set cols = n. Then the focus-into-view result (left + cw - view) and most both land on multiples of step automatically, and the wheel already moves one step. On drag release in scrollGrid, add `this.want = Math.round(this.want / L.step) * L.step`. No arrows or fade are needed. (2) **Armory** (draw). Set height = Math.floor((H - top - 34) / step) * step. Also round this.scroll to a multiple of step when the drag ends. (3) **Roster** (drawRoster, in a file with other in-progress edits). Keep the panel box as it is. Snap rosterScroll to multiples of ROW_STEP: use ceil in the focus clamp's lower bound, round when the drag ends, and set most = Math.ceil((ids.length*ROW_STEP - height) / ROW_STEP) * ROW_STEP. The top row is then never cut, and the only partial row is the peek at the bottom, or empty space at the end of the list. (4) **Info panel** (details). Don't snap. In the clip block, skip any text line whose 8-px glyph box is not fully inside [y+4, y+height-4], for example with a small helper wrapping drawText that checks ty >= y+4 && ty+8 <= y+height-4. The arrow buttons and scrollbar still show there is more.

### menus-16 [confirmed] Panel trims follow no rule; the hero select uses three near-identical greys and no copper
- polish / code - public/js/ui/hero-selection.js:201 ('#5a5465'), :270 ('#59505b'), :314 ('#3a4466'); copper default ui.js:180 ('#b86f50'); slate screens.js:373, :641, :950; orange music-picker.js:106; salmon oath-picker.js:49 (art lens)
- Evidence: 1280x720__B02: the roster and header frames are mauve-grey and the info frame is blue-slate, on one screen. B16 Armory and B19 Codex pair slate list rows with a copper detail panel. B13 copper summary, B14 salmon oath panel, B15 orange music panel, B33 copper music menu.
- Each screen picked its own frame colours, so hero select looks like a different skin from Armory, Codex, Records and the dialogs. Two greys 1-2 steps apart read as a mistake.
- Fix: Define four trim tokens in ui.js and use only them: CONTENT ['#b86f50','#733e39'] for detail and info panels, dialogs and summaries; ITEM ['#5a6988','#3a4466'] for list rows, tiles and cards (shut ['#3a4466','#262b44']); FOCUS ['#fee761','#be4a2f']; PICKED ['#feae34','#733e39']. On hero select, use CONTENT for the header and info panels and ITEM for the roster. hero-selection.js has the other session's uncommitted edits, so stage by hunk.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): I confirmed it in the pixels and in the code. In 1280x720__B02 the roster frame samples #5a5465, the Sir Aldric header frame #59505b and the info frame #3a4466. Those are exactly the trim[0] values at hero-selection.js:201, :270 and :314. panelFrame in skin.js draws only trim[0] on large panels, so those are the colours you see. In my zoom (verify/b02-zoom-seam.png) the header frame is mauve-grey and the info frame right under it is blue slate. Nothing on the screen uses the default copper (ui.js:179). Every other content screen does: Armory detail (B16), Codex detail (B19), Records (B23) and the Challenge summary (B13) all pair slate list items with a copper content panel. Hero select is the odd one out. Git shows the mauve greys came in with 281bd5c ("Rework the hero chooser"). Before that, the hero cards used slate #3a4466. No earlier review settles panel trims. Two parts of the finding go too far: (1) #5a5465 and #59505b differ by only (1,4,10) in RGB, so the eye reads them as one grey, not as a mistake. The defect you can actually see is grey next to slate, with no copper. (2) "Each screen picked its own" is wrong. screens.js:373/:641/:950 already follow one rule: slate for items, gold/red for focus, orange for picked. The oath picker's salmon is the oath feature's ON colour (#f6757a), so it is deliberate. The music picker's orange is the picked colour. Both are popups drawn over the copper summary panel (B14/B15), so a contrasting trim separates them from the panel behind. My mock (verify/b02-mock-full.png) applies the critic's colours to hero select: slate roster, copper header and info. It reads like Armory/Codex and looks right. The three trim lines are committed at HEAD (lines 197/266/310). None of them is in the other session's uncommitted diff to hero-selection.js, though that file does carry their edits. Better fix: Change only three values in public/js/ui/hero-selection.js and leave the other screens alone: - Roster frame (:201): trim ['#5a6988','#3a4466'], the Codex open-card slate. Use ['#3a4466','#262b44'] if a quieter frame is wanted. - Identity panel (:270) and details panel (:314): drop the trim option so they fall back to the default copper ['#b86f50','#733e39']. Keep rivets:false and the fills. Leave oath-picker.js (salmon is the oath ON colour) and music-picker.js (orange marks the picked popup) as they are: both popups sit over a copper panel and need a contrasting trim. Tokens in ui.js are optional tidying, not needed to fix this. If you add them, make PICKED/FOCUS cover the popups instead of forcing them to CONTENT. hero-selection.js has the other session's uncommitted edits elsewhere in the file. The three trim lines are unchanged from HEAD, so commit only these hunks (git diff | git apply --cached with just these hunks, or the HEAD blob plus update-index). Mock with the critic's colours: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/b02-mock-full.png
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The finding is real, but it is small. Pixel samples from the B02 capture confirm the frames: the roster is #5a5465, the identity header is #59505b and the info panel is #3a4466. On one screen, two frames are mauve-grey and the one beside them is blue slate. At 4x zoom the mismatch shows clearly next to the teal stage. A mock is in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/hero-trim-options.png. The hero select is the only place that uses either mauve value (grep across public/js). Armory (B16) and Codex (B19) both use slate item rows with a copper default panel for detail. The bottom buttons on the hero select are copper-brown too. The history shows no intent. The two greys arrived in 281bd5c ("Rework the hero chooser", 2026-11-21) with no comment, and the slate info panel dates from e9dd34d. No review in reviews/ mentions panel trims. The two greys are 1-10 steps apart per channel, which is code sloppiness rather than a visible second colour. The visible defect is the mauve-versus-slate split on one screen and a skin that differs from Armory and Codex. The proposed fix goes too far. "Use only four tokens everywhere" would recolour accents that are deliberate. The oath picker frame uses ON '#f6757a', the same heat colour as the OATHS button and checkboxes (oath-picker.js:13, 23, 54). reward-reels.js:37/219/434 and gamescreen.js:1060 trim panels in rarity or accent colours on purpose. screens.js already follows the slate-item / gold-focus / orange-picked grammar, so it needs no refactor. ui.js and the other screens can stay untouched. The other session's uncommitted hero-selection.js diff only adds DEMO roster filtering (SHOWN_HEROES, the journey dialog) and does not touch lines 201, 270 or 314. Staging by hunk is still needed. The change does not affect gameplay, Math.random calls or hero looks. Better fix: Change only hero-selection.js, three lines, and nothing in ui.js, screens.js, music-picker.js, oath-picker.js or reward-reels.js. - Line 201 (roster): trim ['#5a5465','#262b44'] becomes ['#3a4466','#262b44'], the same slate as the unfocused Armory rows. - Line 270 (identity): delete the trim option so panel() falls back to the copper default ['#b86f50','#733e39']. - Line 314 (details): delete the trim option the same way. This is option C in verify/hero-trim-options.png: a slate list beside copper content panels, like Armory and Codex, and in step with the copper buttons. If the owner prefers the cooler look, option B is the minimum: set lines 201 and 270 to the info panel's ['#3a4466','#262b44']. Stage the hunks with git diff | git apply --cached, because the other session's DEMO edits sit uncommitted in the same file. Shared trim tokens are optional. If added, they must not replace the deliberate accent trims (the oath ON salmon, the music picker orange, rarity colours in reward-reels and gamescreen).

### menus-17 [confirmed] Title campfire is drawn at 2x beside heroes at 1x (mixed pixel sizes)
- polish / art - public/js/ui/background.js:672-674 (const fs = 2; the 14x8 'campfire' prop from public/js/art/props.js:101); heroes at :658-668 drawn 1:1 (technical lens)
- Evidence: tech-extra/campfire.png (1280x720__B01 at x 470-810, y 530-665, 3x): the fire's pixels are twice the size of the Knight's, Sorcerer's and Ranger's. The same in A09 and every title capture.
- The title screen's centrepiece mixes pixel densities: a tiny world prop blown up 2x among 1:1 hero sprites, the classic mixel look the rest of the menu avoids.
- Fix: Draw a menu campfire authored at hero density (~28x16, same palette, 3 flame frames) at 1x, with fs = 1. As a stop-gap, draw the 14x8 prop at 1x and add stones and logs around it at 1x.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): Confirmed in the code, in the git history and in the pixels. At 1280x720 the menu draws onto the W x H UI canvas (view.js, S = 2, so 640x360). In background.js:659-668 each hero is drawn at img.width/density, about 1 UI pixel per sprite pixel. At :672-674 the 14x9 campfire prop (props.js:101; 9 rows, not 8 as the finding says) is drawn with fs = 2. I measured horizontal colour runs in 1280x720__B01-title-menu-mid.png. Inside the hero boxes the runs are almost all 2 screen px (1 UI px). Inside the fire they are 4 and 8 px (2 UI px). My 4x crop shows it plainly: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/campfire-skeptic/b01-fire-heroes-x4.png. The 1x embers rising off the 2x fire add a third mismatch. The history shows this was an oversight, not a choice. In the first version (84963a5) the heroes were drawn at s = round(2/hires), which matched the fire's fs = 2. Commit 21c2d53 changed the heroes to "their own sheets at one scale" and left fs = 2 behind. No earlier review in reviews/ mentions the campfire, and none of the owner's settled calls covers it. None of the files involved (background.js, props.js) are in the other session's uncommitted set. It is polish, not a bug. The fire is the title screen's centrepiece, so the mixed pixel sizes show on every launch. The main fix is sound: draw a campfire at hero density and set fs = 1. The stop-gap is weak. At 1x the flame is only about 5 px tall next to heroes about 30 px tall, so the centrepiece shrinks to a smudge. The ember spawn at fy-14 would also float well above it. Better fix: Leave the in-game 'campfire' prop alone and add a separate menu sprite, SPR.campfire_menu, in public/js/art/props.js. Make it about 28x18 with the same palette letters and the same flame() helper, so the footprint on screen stays what it is now. Build each of the 3 frames from a Scale2x/EPX upscale of the matching 14x9 frame, then refine it by hand at 1-px detail: flame tongues, inner-flame highlight, log grain, shading on the ring of stones. Keep the 3-frame loop at the current 8 fps. In public/js/ui/background.js:672-674, switch to SPR.campfire_menu and set fs = 1. Embers already spawn at fy-14, which still sits inside the 18-px-tall fire. Skip the 1x stop-gap; it shrinks the fire to a smudge. If no new art can be made yet, a Scale2x upscale baked at load time is a better interim than redrawing at 1x.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The finding is confirmed. At 1280x720 (S=2, so a 640x360 logical canvas), public/js/ui/background.js:672-674 draws SPR.campfire, a 14x9 prop, at fs = 2. The CAMP heroes at :658-668 are drawn at one logical pixel each. So each fire pixel is 2x2 logical pixels next to 1x1 hero pixels. My render shows the difference clearly (verify/campfire/compare-x3.png). The history shows this was left behind by mistake, not chosen. In the initial commit 84963a5 the old menu heroes were also drawn at about 2x (s = max(1, round(2/hires))), so fire and heroes matched. Commit 21c2d53 (2026-10-20, "every hero's own sheet at one scale") moved the heroes to 1x and left `const fs = 2` untouched. No comment calls the 2x fire intentional, and none of the earlier reviews mention the campfire or mixed pixel sizes. None of the owner's settled calls are affected. The change is menu-only, has no gameplay effect, and background.js and props.js have no uncommitted work from the other session. In game the same prop is drawn at 1x beside 1x heroes (places.js, darkwood.js, prologue.js), so one density is already how the game looks everywhere else. On the fix: I patched in fs = 1 alone and rendered it. The fire becomes a 14x9 speck between heroes about 35 px tall, and the centre of the title looks empty. That is consistent, but it weakens the composition, so the 1x fire needs to be bigger art, not just a smaller draw. The critic's main fix, a larger menu campfire drawn at 1x, is the right one. Better fix: 1. In public/js/art/props.js, add a new menu-only prop, for example campfire_menu: flame(a, b, c). Draw it at 1x, about 28x18, in the same palette letters ('oyYfF' flames, b/B/m logs, N/g stones) and with 3 frames. It needs a full stone ring, a crossed log pile and a taller flame. Leave the in-world 'campfire' prop unchanged, because places.js, darkwood.js and prologue.js use it. 2. In background.js drawCamp, set fire = SPR.campfire_menu.frames[...] and fs = 1. 3. Move the ember spawn (currently y: fy - 14, which assumes the 2x fire) to the new flame tip, about fy - (flame height - 2). Keep the same rand() calls in the same order. They are menu draw-time only, so seeded tests are unaffected either way. 4. Check that the Shadow at dy = -24 still clears the taller flame. If the flame is taller than about 22 px, nudge her up. If new art is not wanted now, the stop-gap is fs = 1 plus a 1x ring of stones and logs, which amounts to the same new sprite. fs = 1 on its own leaves the centrepiece too small (see verify/campfire/compare-x3.png).

### menus-18 [confirmed] Trial cards: the win stars and 'BEST 10:00' are printed over the scene thumbnail's top edge
- polish / code - public/js/ui/screens.js:378-385 (SCENE 64x44 at sx0 = x+23, sy0 = y+5) vs :395 (BEST at x+6, y+5) and :398-401 (stars from x+6 every 7 px at y+5) (technical lens)
- Evidence: tech-extra/cards-top.png (1280x720__D09, y 84-114): Survival's 3rd and 4th stars sit on the Abyss sky, the Dungeon's stars clip its arch, and '10:00' runs across the Ranked scene's top rows. B10: the Survival stars cover the Bloomvale scene's top-left corner (tech-extra/card.png).
- The badge row and the picture both start at y+5. With the scene at x+23, only two 7-px stars fit to its left, so the third star and the Ranked time overlap the art.
- Fix: Stack the stars down the 17-px left margin (x+6, y+6+i*8; 4 fit beside the 44-px scene). Move the Ranked best time into the subtitle ('Endless · best 10:00') or the right margin, or shift the scene 9 px down on cards that carry a badge row.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The finding is real, and both the code and the pixels show it. In public/js/ui/screens.js, ModeScreen.draw, every landscape desktop screen has a logical W of at least about 372, so cw = 110 and ch = 84, which makes k = 1. That puts the scene at sx0 = x+23 and sy0 = y+5, with its #0d0b14 backing starting at x+22 and y+4 (lines 377-382). The badges are drawn at y+5 too. Ranked's text is at x+6 (line 395) and the stars start at x+6 and step 7 px (lines 398-401). The star glyph (fontdata) is 5 px of ink, 7 px with its outline, so the stars' ink covers x+6..10, x+13..17, x+20..24 and x+27..31. The 3rd star therefore sits across the scene's frame edge and the 4th sits wholly on the picture. 'BEST 10:00' measures 51 px, so it runs from x+6 to x+56, covering 34 px of the Ranked scene's top 7 rows. I cropped the real 1280x720 capture to 8x logical (verify/d09-card-tops-x8.png) and it shows exactly this. Survival's silver star straddles the Abyss frame and its red star sits red-on-red on the sky. The Dungeon's 3rd star clips the brick and arch edge, and 'BEST 10:00' crosses the blood-moon sky. B10's card.png shows the same thing on the Bloomvale. This is not a capture artefact. Because the first two stars sit neatly in the margin and the rest spill onto the art, it reads as accidental, not as a deliberate badge overlay. Nothing breaks and the badges stay readable, so it is polish, not a defect. The stars-in-the-margin part of the proposed fix is sound: x+6 with y+6+i*8 keeps the stars in x+5..x+11 and y+5..y+38, inside the 17-px margin beside the 44-px scene. The Ranked part is a menu of three options, and two of them don't work. (a) The right margin is too narrow: it runs only from about x+87 to x+106, and even '10:00' is 23 px wide. (b) Moving the scene 9 px down would make it end at y+58, on top of the trial name at y+52, unless ch grows by about 9 px for every card. Only the subtitle option fits: 'Endless · best 10:00' is 90 px against cw-6 = 104. Cited line numbers are off by about one (on disk the scene is at 377-382). Caveat: screens.js holds another session's uncommitted DEMO work, and line 390 (the subtitle line with 'FULL GAME') is one of its hunks. The badge lines 393-402 are untouched by that work. Better fix: Stars: keep them in the top-left corner but fit them inside the 17-px margin, either as a 2x2 grid or as a column. For the grid, use sx = x+6 + (i%2)*7 and sy = y+5 + (i>>1)*8, which keeps the ink inside x+5..x+18 and y+4..y+20, clear of the backing at x+22. For the column, use x+6 and y+5+i*8. Draw every slot (i counts all four, not only the stars earned) so a star stays in the same place from hero to hero. Ranked: drop the top-left text and put the best time on the subtitle line in its amber, using drawRich or two drawText calls. 'Endless · 10:00' is 67 px and 'Endless · best 10:00' is 90 px, both within cw-6 = 104; run it through fitLine on narrow screens. Keep the 'Endless' wording the owner chose. Line 390 is in the other session's uncommitted hunk, so stage this by hand or coordinate with that session. Do not shift the scene down for cards with badges: without making ch taller for all three cards it runs into the trial name at y+52.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The finding is real. tech-extra/cards-top.png and card.png show it: the 3rd win star sits half on the scene's frame line and the 4th sits on the art. On Ranked, "BEST 10:00" (51 px wide by textWidth) starts at x+6, runs across the frame at x+22 and onto the blood-moon sky. The numbers match the code. In landscape every resolution gives cw=110 and k=1, so sx0 = x+23 and sy0 = y+5. The star glyph is 5 px wide and steps 7 px from x+6, so stars 3 and 4 (x+20, x+27) overlap the scene. This is not deliberate. The star row and the BEST text go back to the first commit (84963a5), when the cards held small oak/stairs/grave sprites. f416b0e swapped those for 64x44 code-drawn scenes and nobody moved the badge row, so this is a leftover from that change, not a choice. No earlier review in reviews/ raises or settles it, and it goes against none of the owner's settled calls. The fix only changes menu drawing: no gameplay, no Math.random, no hero looks. Severity is polish: it shows on the main trial screen every time, but only once the hero has wins or a Ranked best. Two of the critic's three options work. Stacking the stars in the left margin works: x+6..x+11 with outline is clear of the frame at x+22, and four 8-px rows end at about y+37, inside the scene's y+5..y+49. Moving the Ranked best into the subtitle also fits: "Endless · best 10:00" measures 90 px against cw-6 = 104. The third option, shifting the scene 9 px down, does not work. The scene would end at y+58 and run into the trial name at y+52 and the subtitle at y+61, all inside the fixed ch=84 card. Note for whoever edits: screens.js has another session's uncommitted DEMO changes, and line ~390 (the `sub` line a subtitle fix would touch) is one of their changed hunks. Stage around it. Better fix: This is the smallest version, in ModeScreen.draw (public/js/ui/screens.js, about lines 392-402), and it only changes drawing. (1) Stars: keep the tier slots (the slot advances even when a star is not earned) but stack them in the left margin, centred between the panel edge and the scene's frame: `const sx = Math.round((x + 2 + sx0 - 1) / 2 - 2.5); stars.forEach(([, col, got], i) => { if (got) drawText(ctx, '★', sx, y + 6 + i * 8, { color: col }); });`. A 2x2 block at x+6/x+13, y+6/y+14 also fits before the frame. Placing them relative to sx0 rather than at a fixed x+6 keeps them correct if the card ever gets narrower. (2) Ranked best: drop the overlay and put it in the subtitle. For ranked with a best, draw `fitLine('Endless · best ' + fmtTime(best), cw - 6)` in place of M.sub. To keep the orange, draw 'Endless' in #8b9bb4 and the time in #feae34 as two pieces centred together. That keeps the D10 'Endless' label. Leave out the 'shift the scene 9 px down' option because it collides with the name and subtitle rows. The `sub` line is in the other session's uncommitted DEMO hunk ('FULL GAME'), so stage the edit by hunk.

### menus-19 [confirmed] The prologue's band, hint box and arrival card use a one-off flat style below the rest of the UI
- polish / code - public/js/ui/prologue.js:106-108 (top band rgba(25,34,27,.85)), :129-131 (hint box W-40 wide), :85-91 (arrival card: 1x Pixtura title on pure black from g.fade) (art lens)
- Evidence: 1280x720__A05: an unframed translucent green band in '#fff0c4' / '#c7d4b0' / '#354937' / '#e1c581', colours used nowhere else in the menus, and a hint box that stops 35 px short of the right edge to dodge the 'II' button. A08: the arrival chapter card is a 12-px gothic line on #000, smaller than a dialog plate.
- This is the first screen a new player sees after the title, and the only one without the Lucifer frames, the palette inks (#0d0b14 / #15131f) or the display type. It looks like a placeholder.
- Fix: Band: a Lucifer panel strip (fill '#15131f', CONTENT trim) with the title on a plate, and HP/GOLD in #ead4aa / #8b9bb4. Hint: a framed panel spanning W - 44 with the 'II' button inside its right end. Arrival card: background '#0d0b14', title via logo('The Sunscorch', ramp, undefined, 'title') with flank() dividers as on the title screen.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): Confirmed in the code and in the pixels. public/js/ui/prologue.js (another session has not touched this file) draws the top band as a bare fillRect 'rgba(25,34,27,0.85)', 0,0,W,49. It has no frame and no edge line. The pixels sample #2d3022 and #2f3123, and the cacti show through. The text uses '#fff0c4' and '#c7d4b0', and the bar uses '#354937' and '#e1c581'. A grep of public/js finds these five colours only in prologue.js, so the claim that they appear nowhere else holds. Every menu screen titles itself with the gradient heading between flank() dividers ('Choose your Hero' in A03), and its panels use the Lucifer frame with #15131f fill. This band uses neither. The hint box is fillRect(5, …, W-40, …). In the zoomed crop it ends at screen x 1210, which is logical 605 = W-35, so it stops 35 px short of the right edge to dodge the II button, as reported. The critic missed something worse: the text wraps at Math.min(300, W-55) while the box is W-40 wide. At 640 logical width, the two lines ("…Your sword attacks / automatically.") fill only the left half of a 600-px box. The arrival card (A08) is the 1x gothic drawText on the world canvas, which g.fade fills with pure '#000000' (game.js:3409-3412). Each step from the title to the road is confirmed: screens.js:260 sends a first-time player through needsPrologue to 'prologue'. The claim that the arrival title is "smaller than a dialog plate" is slightly overstated, because plate titles use the same 1x gothic, only on a bronze plate. A07 shows a further related flaw: the arrival text is drawn at full opacity from frame 0 while the world is still fading. For about 1.2 s it sits over the half-dimmed desert with no backing. Severity: this is polish, not a defect. Everything works and reads, but it is the one screen a new player sees that has none of the menu's skin. The proposed fix is not sound as written. (1) The "CONTENT trim" it names does not exist anywhere in public/js; the critic made up the constant. (2) "Title on a plate" uses panelTitle, which draws the plate at y-8, so on a band at y=0 the plate is clipped off the top of the screen. (3) A full Lucifer panelFrame across the band has 12-px corner brackets that collide with the text at x=9 (title at y=7, CAMP at y=39). The whole layout would have to move. (4) Putting the II button inside the hint's frame ties a permanent control to a hint that disappears after 7 s. The button would sit in an orphaned spot once the hint goes, and the box would still be mis-sized against its 300-px wrap. (5) logo(…, 'title') is the Jacquard-24 face used for the game logo. Screen titles use textCanvas(…, 'heading') plus flank (screens.js header(), hero-selection.js:47), so the fix would add another one-off instead of reusing the menu style. (6) Swapping the arrival background from #000 to #0d0b14 is a nearly invisible change. Doing it at the source means editing the g.fade fill in game.js, which every fade shares (faint, descend, travel). Better fix: All changes are in public/js/ui/prologue.js only. Band: keep it a flat strip, so the text layout and touch regions stay as they are, but switch to the palette's colours. - Fill 'rgba(13,11,20,0.85)' (#0d0b14) over 0,0,W,48. - Add a bronze rule under it, echoing the panel trim: fillRect(0,47,W,1) in '#733e39' and fillRect(0,48,W,1) in '#b86f50'. - Draw the title with the gold ramp the plates use: color ['#fff6c2','#fee761','#feae34','#d77643'], font 'gothic'. - HP/GOLD in '#ead4aa'; CAMP/SUNSCORCH in '#8b9bb4'. - Bar track '#262b44' and fill '#feae34', with a 1-px '#fee761' highlight on top. Hint: size the box to its text instead of the screen. With ww = Math.min(300, W-55), call panel(ctx, 5, H-17-lines.length*11, ww+12, lines.length*11+12, { fill: 'rgba(21,19,31,0.9)', rivets: false }). If lines.length*11+12 < 25, panel() falls back to its own small trim, which is fine. Keep the text in '#ead4aa' at x=11. The box then never reaches the II button, so the two are not coupled. Arrival card: reuse the screens' heading idiom:   const c = textCanvas('The Sunscorch', ['#fff6c2','#fee761','#feae34','#f77622'], '#181425', '#0d0b14', 'heading');   ctx.drawImage(c, …centred…);   flank(ctx, W/2 - c.width/2 - 4, W/2 + c.width/2 + 4, y + 9, Math.min(48, W/2 - c.width/2 - 10)); Then do ctx.globalAlpha = g.fade for the whole card, so the text fades in with the dark instead of sitting over the half-faded desert (A07). Optionally fill uctx with '#0d0b14' at g.fade to tint the black, without touching the g.fade fill that game.js shares with every other fade.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): This is a real leftover, not a deliberate choice. git show 6937fe4 (24 Oct, "Add a daylight Knight prologue") shows the band and hint colours rgba(25,34,27,.85), #fff0c4, #c7d4b0, #354937 and #e1c581 were written for "The road to Darkwood", a forest road. When the road moved to the desert in 116f331 and d2981ae, only the words changed. The forest-green tint now sits over yellow sand, and none of those colours appear anywhere else in the UI. The Lucifer skin (7708271, 20 Oct) already existed when the prologue was written. Every other in-run card uses it: the prologue's own pause panel (A06), Level Complete and the HUD slot frames. So the band and hint box are the only flat, unframed pieces in the game. The arrival card is a 12-px Pixtura line on the shared #000 g.fade. Every other titled screen uses screens.js header(): Jacquard 12 in a gold ramp between Kenney flank() dividers. No earlier review in reviews/ mentions how the prologue looks. Nothing conflicts with the owner's settled calls. prologue.js is not among the other session's uncommitted files. I mocked both options in Chrome by patching the page only, nothing in the repo; the images are in scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/prologue-band/. The critic's fix is not sound as written. "CONTENT trim" does not exist (grep finds nothing). panel(o.title) draws its plate at y-8, so on a band at y=0 the plate would be cut off. A real Lucifer panel strip (mock 1280x720-panel.png) puts its corner brackets over "CAMP" and over the start of the hint text unless the layout is re-padded. logo(...,'title') is the 24-px Jacquard of the game's own logo, heavier than the menus' screen titles. Putting the always-on II button inside the hint frame ties a lasting button to a hint that is gone after 7 s. The 35-px gap is just room left for that button and is not a defect. The flat-palette mock (1280x720-flat.png) and the header-style arrival mock (1280x720-arrivalAfter.png) both fit the rest of the UI with no layout change. Better fix: Keep the layout and change only the inks, all inside public/js/ui/prologue.js. No gameplay change, no Math.random, no change to touch regions. (1) drawHud band, :106-117: fill rgba(21,19,31,0.88) (#15131f) over 0..48, then a 1-px #733e39 trim at y=48 with #0d0b14 below it. Title colour: the panel-title gold ramp ['#fff6c2','#fee761','#feae34','#d77643'] in the gothic font. HP/GOLD in #ead4aa; CAMP and SUNSCORCH in #8b9bb4. Bar track #262b44, fill #feae34. (2) Hint box, :131-132: the same rgba(21,19,31,0.88) fill with a 1-px #733e39 line along its top. Text in #ead4aa. Keep W-40 so the II button stays clear. (3) Arrival card, :84-88: before the text, fill uctx with rgba(13,11,20,g.fade) so the fade ends on #0d0b14 rather than #000. Draw the title the way screens.js header() does: textCanvas('The Sunscorch', ['#fff6c2','#fee761','#feae34','#f77622'], '#181425', '#0d0b14', 'heading'), centred about 14 px above the story lines, with flank(ctx, W/2-hw-4, W/2+hw+4, ty+9, Math.min(48, W/2-hw-10)) from skin.js. Leave the story and lesson lines as they are. game.js's shared fade stays untouched.

### menus-20 [confirmed] Codex and Armory detail panels are mostly empty text boxes in two different styles
- polish / code - public/js/ui/screens.js:654-660 (Armory detail: plain 5x7 yellow name), :915 (Codex detail with a gothic plate), codexLayout detail height (art lens)
- Evidence: 1280x720__B19 and B22: one or two text lines in a roughly 600x175 art-px panel, about 75% empty, while the entry's icon appears only at 16 px in the tile row. B16: the Armory detail is 5 short lines in a 335x165 panel, with 'Vitality' in plain 5x7 while the Codex uses a plate title.
- At 640x360 the detail areas look unfinished, and the two sibling screens present 'the selected thing' differently: one with a plate, one as plain text.
- Fix: In both detail panels, show the entry's icon at 3x on the left, plus the evolution recipe as weapon icon, '+', relic icon, '→', evolved icon at 2x (codexIcon at an integer scale). Give the Armory detail the same plate title as the Codex (panel {title}), and size both panels to their content when W > 480.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The finding is real. I checked it in the captures and in the code. **Codex (B19, B21, B22).** At 640x360, codexLayout gives `gw = min(620, W-32) = 608` and `detail = min(180, H-54-44-30-34) = 180`. That makes the panel 608x180 art px. In B19, the text ends at about art y 178, while the panel runs from y 134 to 314. Only about 44 of 176 rows are used, so roughly 75% is empty. B21 (2 lines) and B22 (1 line) are emptier still. The code says the same: `room = floor((180-16)/9) = 18` rows, but codexDetail returns at most about 6-7 wrapped rows, even for the exclusion-slot feature entry. The entry's icon appears only at 1x (16 px) in the tile (`codexIcon` at `x+4` in `tile()`), and the panel itself has no icon. **Armory (B16).** `detailW = 540-190-10 = 340`, and `detailH = 170`. The name is drawn in plain 5x7 `#fee761` text inside the box (screens.js:656). The Codex instead puts the name on a gothic plate via `panel({title})` (screens.js:915). That inconsistency is visible in B16 next to B19. The Armory box is less empty than the Codex: the text ends near top+90 and the BUY button sits at top+146, so about a third is empty. **Not a capture artefact.** Both heights are fixed by layout code and do not depend on the profile. The 13 Nov commit 0b4d66e ("Polish menus") replaced the Codex's multi-row grid and 44 px panel with one row of tiles and this fixed ~180 px panel. **Other session's work.** The other session's uncommitted diffs in screens.js and ladder.js touch only DEMO code (title, mode screen, lockReason). They do not touch these lines, but both files are on that session's WIP list. **Why the proposed fix is not sound as written:** 1. **It would reveal hidden relics.** An icon recipe of weapon + relic -> evolved would break the owner's settled call of 27 Oct 2026. Per menu-copy.js:73-79, a recipe's relic stays unnamed until a run has made that evolution (`knownEvos`). Today the text deliberately says "unknown relic". Also, `evo.random` (the copper evolution) has no single evolved icon. 2. **"Size both panels to their content" points the wrong way.** The Codex block comment records the owner's 27 Oct aim: "the screen filled, easy to find one's way about". Shrinking the panel would leave the lower half of the screen as bare dimmed background. The space should be filled with content instead. 3. **The plate would crowd the Armory subtitle.** With `top = 43`, a plate on the Armory panel would span art y 35-50. The subtitle "Permanent upgrades · all heroes" spans y 26-34, and its tail overlaps the plate's span horizontally. The two would touch at a 1 px gap unless `top` moves down. Better fix: Keep both panels at their current size and fill them. **Codex (`drawDetail`):** - Draw `codexIcon` at an integer 3x (48 px) in a left column: `ctx.save()`, translate to the rounded x/y, `scale(3,3)`, `imageSmoothingEnabled=false`, then restore. Start the text at x+62 instead of x+10, with wrap width w-72. - For weapons with an evo, add a 2x icon row under the text: weapon icon + relic icon + chest -> evolved icon. - When the evolution is not in `knownEvos`, draw the relic and the result as a padlocked ghost or "?" (`codexIcon` with `shut`, or `padlock`), as the "unknown relic" wording does today. When `evo.random`, show the copper/chest icon with "?". - Keep the existing recipe text line as the caption. **Armory:** - Draw the upgrade's `UP_ICONS` sprite at 2x or 3x beside the name. Either set the name in the gothic font or use `panel({title})` for the same plate as the Codex. If you use the plate, move `top` from 43 to about 49 so the plate clears the subtitle at y 26-34. - Use the empty band above the BUY button for a row of rank pips (rank/max). Neither panel changes size, so both screens stay filled as the owner asked.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The Codex half is real. codexLayout (screens.js:728) makes the detail panel Math.max(60, Math.min(180, ...)) tall, which is 180 art px at 640x360 and 683x384. I counted the wrapped codexDetail rows for every entry, on a fresh profile and a fully opened one (verify/codex-lines.mjs). At those sizes the longest entry, the exclusion slots, needs 4 rows (52 px), and most entries need 1 or 2. So each panel is at least 70% empty and usually more, as B19/B21/B22 show. Nothing marks the 180 as deliberate. It arrived in 0b4d66e "Polish menus" (13 Nov), not from an owner call. That commit even left CODEX_TILE.detail = 88 defined and unused. No test pins the panel height, and no earlier review covers it. The Armory half is weak. Its detail panel holds 5 lines plus the BUY button, about 35% empty, and the selected list row already shows the name highlighted. Whether the name sits in plain text or on a plate is mostly taste. The proposed fix does not hold up as written: (1) Recipe as weapon + relic -> evolved icons: evolutionRecipe (menu-copy.js:79) deliberately hides the partner relic and the result ("unknown relic") until the evolution is discovered. The 11-07 review calls this "concealing undiscovered recipes". Showing the icons would give the recipe away. Random copper evolutions also have no single result icon. The text already carries the recipe. (2) Armory plate title: I rendered it at the panel's top (verify/armory-plate-640x360.png and -480x270.png). The plate sits right against the "Permanent upgrades · all heroes" subtitle, 1 px apart at 480x270. The plain name line would also have to go and the text move up. (3) "Size both panels to their content": the comment says the Codex has "a fixed detail panel". Sizing to content would make the panel jump between 1-row and 4-row entries as the focus moves. The 3x icon is the good part. Drawn with ctx.translate/scale(3,3) around the existing codexIcon, it looks right for weapons and for the ult hero crops (verify/codex-proto-640x360.png and codex-proto-ult-640x360.png). None of this touches gameplay or Math.random. Note: screens.js has another session's uncommitted DEMO edits (title and mode screen). They don't overlap these regions, but stage by hunk. Better fix: Codex only: (1) In codexLayout (screens.js:728), change `Math.min(180, H - C.top - C.h - C.foot - 34)` to `Math.min(C.detail, H - C.top - C.h - C.foot - 34)`. This reuses the unused CODEX_TILE.detail = 88. The height stays fixed, so nothing jumps, but the panel loses its empty bottom half. 88 px holds 8 rows, which is exactly the worst case on the 293x633 portrait. With the icon in place, 640x360 needs at most 4 rows and 480x270 at most 6. (2) In drawDetail (screens.js:914), when w >= 300 && h >= 64 && !e.hidden, draw the icon at 3x: ctx.save(); ctx.translate(x + 10, y + ((h - 48) >> 1) + 2); ctx.scale(3, 3); codexIcon(ctx, e, 0, 0); ctx.restore(). Then wrap and draw the text at x + 68 with width w - 78 instead of x + 10 / w - 20. Otherwise keep today's layout, so short phone screens don't cut more text. Keep the recipe as the existing text so undiscovered recipes stay hidden. Leave the Armory detail as it is: the list row already names the selection, and a plate there runs into the subtitle.

### menus-21 [confirmed] Oath picker ribbons are as wide as their labels, leaving a ragged column
- polish / code - public/js/ui/ui.js:263-265 (checkboxWidth = textWidth(label) + 30); public/js/ui/oath-picker.js:53 (art lens)
- Evidence: 1280x720__B14: ten checkbox ribbons whose right ends range from x 525 to 600, while the heat pips sit fixed at x 900-920, so each ribbon floats a different distance from its pips.
- In a list of parallel options, unequal widths read as misalignment. Every other list in the game uses one width.
- Fix: Give checkbox() an optional width (o.w ?? checkboxWidth(label)) and pass w - 2*PAD - 30 from OathPicker, so every ribbon ends at the same x just before its pips.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): The finding is real. I confirmed it in the pixels and in the code. Pixels: I measured 1280x720__B14-trial-oath-picker-open.png at each row's mid-height. The ribbons' right tips are at x = 525, 535, 581, 573, 583, 599, 533, 545, 549 and 563. The heat pips start at a fixed x of 898 (two pips) or 912 (one pip), so the gap from ribbon to pips runs from 313 to 373 px. Code: checkbox() in ui.js:263-265 sizes the ribbon as textWidth(label) + 30, with no way to set a width. oath-picker.js:54 calls it with no width, while the pips sit at the panel's right edge (x + w - PAD - 6 - k*7). So this is what the game itself draws, not something the capture added. The claim that other lists use one width holds where I checked: the music picker's rows are bounds.w - 2*PAD, steppers use a fixed w, and the only other checkbox (Nightmare) stands alone. Testing the fix: I re-rendered the picker in real Chrome against :3017, serving patched copies of ui.js and oath-picker.js through page.route (the repo was not touched). With the proposed fix (o.w ?? checkboxWidth(label), and w: w - 2*PAD - 30 passed from OathPicker), all ten ribbons end at the same x. The pips sit about 17 logical px after the tips, still on the dark panel, and the list reads as a tidy column like the music picker. There were no console errors. The change is safe elsewhere. The Nightmare box in screens.js:511-516 passes no w, so it keeps its centred, label-wide ribbon and the OATHS button beside it stays where it is. As a side benefit, the click area now covers the whole row. On a narrow screen (panel min(300, W-12)) the longest label, "Oath of the Restless", still fits. I also tried an alternative: full-row ribbons with the pips drawn inside. It is worse, because the grey pips (#5d4a52) almost vanish on the iron and focused-gold ribbon faces. So the proposed placement, with pips outside the ribbon, is the right one. Neither file is in the other session's uncommitted set. Images: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/oath/stack.png (before / fix / alternative), plus 1280-before.png, 1280-fix.png and 1280-alt.png in the same folder. Scripts: measure.mjs and oath-fix.mjs, also in that folder. Better fix: Same two-line fix, with one small refinement. Replace the bare 30 with space reserved for the pip column, so the gap holds if an oath ever weighs 3: const PIPS = Math.max(...OATHS.map(o => o.heat)) * 7 + 16; then pass w: w - 2 * PAD - PIPS, which equals 30 for today's maximum heat of 2. Keep the pips outside the ribbon. Drawing them inside makes the #5d4a52 pips nearly invisible on the iron and focused faces.
- Intent sceptic (real, polish): The ragged column is real, and I found nothing showing it was meant. In B14 the ten ribbons end at different x positions, while the heat pips sit in a fixed column at the panel's right edge. checkbox() was written for the one Nightmare box on the trial screen (screens.js:511-516). There it sizes itself to its label, and the Nightmare box and OATHS button are centred on that width. The oath picker (5b4ab62, G4 of the 2026-11-21 review) is the only place that stacks checkboxes into a list. Neither the commit message, the code comments nor any file in reviews/ says the widths are meant to differ. The other lists in the game do use one width: the battle-music dropdown in B15 has full-width rows with the durations aligned on the right. Nothing here touches the owner's settled calls. ui.js and oath-picker.js are not among the other session's uncommitted files. The Nightmare caller in screens.js, which is one of those files, needs no change because the new width is optional. I tested the fix without touching the repo: Playwright served patched copies of ui.js and oath-picker.js in place of the originals (scripts and images in C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/oath-ribbon/, see current.png, proposed.png and fullrow.png). With the proposed width every ribbon ends at the same x, about 17 px short of the widest (2-pip) column, and the list reads as one tidy column. Making the ribbon span the full row, pips included, makes the pips collide with the ribbon's pointed end cap, so the critic's width is the better choice. Side effect: the whole width of each row becomes clickable, which is a small usability gain. No gameplay or Math.random involvement and no hero art affected. It is pure UI drawing, so polish is the right severity. Better fix: Same as proposed, two lines. In ui.js checkbox(), change the button width to `o.w ?? checkboxWidth(label)` (also update the comment "as wide as checkboxWidth makes it" to "as wide as checkboxWidth makes it, or o.w"). In oath-picker.js:54, pass `{ color: ON, w: w - PAD * 2 - 30 }`. Optionally guard it with Math.max(checkboxWidth(o.name), w - PAD * 2 - 30) for very narrow panels. Don't stretch the ribbon to w - PAD * 2: in the mock the pips then sit on the ribbon's pointed end cap.

### menus-2 [unchecked] Lucifer panel 9-slice insets are off by one, so the corner brackets' arms stretch with the panel's width
- defect / code - public/js/ui/skin.js:75 nine(ctx, img, x, y, w, h, [12,12,12,12]) and :66 PANEL_MIN; asset public/assets/ui/pieces.js 'panel' 48x48 (technical lens only)
- Evidence: Piece dump: in rows 3 and 44 the inner bracket arm runs over x 3..12 and 35..44. Columns 12 and 35 fall in the stretched middle slice. Measured arm lengths on screen: trophy card (C03, ~128 wide) ≈13 px, Records panel (B24, 377 wide) ≈24 px, Codex detail (B19, 608 wide) ≈33 px. See tech-extra/panel-corner.png: a thick bronze arm from x 34 to 67 device.
- One decorative pixel lies in the slice that gets stretched, so the length of each bracket arm depends on the panel's width rather than on the art. Panels on the same screen show brackets of different lengths. This affects every panel, dialog and card, and the pause and results panels.
- Fix: Use insets [13, 12, 13, 12] (columns 0-12 and 35-47 stay 1:1) and raise PANEL_MIN to 27. Check the bottom rows 36-47 the same way; they mirror the top and are already covered by 12.

### menus-3 [unchecked] Pixtura12 plate capitals misread: 'Save Vnreadable', 'Nert Run · knight', 'Throwing Are', 'paused', 'The koad Can Wait'
- defect / code - public/js/ui/ui.js:218-226 panelTitle (font 'gothic'); public/js/engine/font.js:33, :38-49 (gothic uses the PIXTURA glyphs unchanged); public/assets/ui/pixtura12.js glyphs U/V, N/R, K, H, P, x; also prologue.js:86,110 and the title motto screens.js:110-111. Found by both critics.
- Evidence: specimen-fonts-x3.png 'gothic' rows 4-6. Plates: 1280x720__A09 'Save Vnreadable', B08 'Nert Run · knight', B19 'Throwing Are', C01 'paused' (the P has a descender), A06 'The koad Can Wait', B33 'llusic', and the title motto 'Survive the llight' (B01 y 125). Glyph dump: U and V are the same shape (they differ by one pixel at row 7). N and R are identical in rows 0-2 and 6-8. x ('##.##./.#####/.##..#/####../.##.../###.../..###.') is an r with a tail. art-extra/type-mock-x3.png left column (now) against the right column (Jacquard 12).
- font.js already notes that the blackletter capitals 'alone don't read', yet every panel plate (dialogs, pause, Codex detail, music, prologue pause) starts with a Pixtura capital. At 12 px, U becomes V, N and R become 'll' or 'k', K, H and P look like minuscules, and x reads as r. These are words the player has to parse: an error panel, the stats dialog, codex names.
- Fix: Option A (art lens): set the plates in 'heading' (Jacquard 12, already bundled, OFL), which reads correctly in the mock. In panelTitle use buttonFace(ctx, tx, y-10, tw, 20, 'on') with tw = textWidth(text,'heading') + 26 and the text at y-9. Keep Pixtura for lowercase-led lines (motto, prologue band). Option B (technical lens): add a GOTHIC_FIX override table in font.js for the gothic font. U gets the right stem down to the baseline with a foot, like Pixtura's own u. x becomes a true crossed x, '##..##/.####./..##../..##../.####./##..##', at rows 2-8. Optionally P and H get a closed bowl and a bar. The OFL file declares no Reserved Font Name, so the glyph edits are allowed.

### menus-15 [unchecked] Button labels sit on a solid blue-black slab that eats the button's top rim
- polish / code - public/js/ui/ui.js:236-237 (button label drawText, default outline '#181425'), :246-247 (stepper); public/js/engine/font.js:79, :88-104 (8-neighbour ring) (art lens)
- Evidence: art-extra/options-rows.png (Options, 1280x720 x3): 'MUSIC VOLUME', 'CHOOSE MUSIC' and 'SOUND EFFECTS' each sit in a dark rectangle covering the 13-px button's top highlight row. art-extra/hell-tab.png: 'FOREST' and 'HELL' on dark plates. specimen-pieces-x3.png: 'IDLE', 'FOCUS', 'START RUN'.
- With 1-px letter spacing, the 8-neighbour outline fills every gap between letters, so each label becomes a near-black block pasted on the bronze, hiding the Lucifer bevel. The cool #181425 is also foreign to the warm button ramp, against the project's self-coloured dark-edge standard.
- Fix: Label buttons and steppers without the ring: drawText(..., { outline: null, shadow: '#2b1b26' }) for idle and off, and shadow '#4f2a2e' for focus, on and pulse (each state's shade from skin.js BUTTON). Keep the full outline only for text over the world or over backdrops.

### menus-22 [rejected] View scale: 1366x768 lands at 256 art px tall (below the 270 floor view.js targets) with a 1368-px blit; 1080p menus run at a cramped 480x270
- taste / owner - public/js/engine/view.js:57-59 (S = round(min/300); W = ceil(pxW/S)) and presentUI :78 (draws ui.width*S) (technical lens)
- Evidence: Measured: 1366x768 gives S=3, so 456x256 art and a 1368-px UI blit that is 2 device px wider than the canvas (the last art column is a third shown). 1920x1080 gives S=4, so 480x270, a smaller layout than 1280x720's 640x360. Consequences: the Armory drops its hint and shows a half row (1366x768__B06), the pause panel hits the loadout (1366x768__C01), the hero-select info needs scroll arrows and cuts WHIRLWIND (1920x1080__B02), and NEXT collides with the camp (sheet-01).
- The two most common PC resolutions get the most cramped menus, and the comment's own 270-330 band is broken at 1366x768. Many layout collisions above are worst at these sizes. Changing S also changes how much of the world the player sees, so this is an owner decision, not a silent fix.
- Fix: Option A: S = max(1, floor(min(pxW, pxH)/300)), giving 1080→3 (640x360), 768→2 (683x384), 720→2, and a 1170-px phone→3. Option B (minimal): after rounding, `while (S > 1 && min(pxW, pxH)/S < 270) S--;`. Either way use W = floor(pxW/S) and centre the blit (offset (pxW - W*S)/2) so no art column is cut.
- Reproduce sceptic (real, polish): Confirmed in code and by measuring the live game. I opened real Chrome on :3017 with the viewport set to each size and read `__fs.view`. Script: C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/menus/verify/viewscale/probe.mjs. Measurements: - 1366x768: S=3, UI 456x256, UI blit 1368 px wide on a 1366-px canvas. The last art column shows 1 of its 3 device px, as the finding says. - 1920x1080, and 1536x864 at 125% scaling: S=4, UI 480x270. - 1280x720: S=2, UI 640x360. The code comment's 270-330 band is broken. round(m/300) gives short sides of 250-350 at S=3, so 768 lands at 256. 1280x800 also lands below the floor, at 266. Consequences, checked: - Armory hint: screens.js:663 draws it only when `top + detailH + 20 < H - 34`. That is 233 < 236 at H=270 but 233 < 222 at H=256, so the hint disappears at 1366x768. It shows at 1280x720 and 1920x1080. - Pause panel: an x4 crop (verify/viewscale/pause-1366-bottom-x4.png) shows the panel's bottom border covering the top edge of the HUD WEAPONS tray and the top row of its label. At 1920x1080 there is a clear gap. - NEXT line: at 480x270 and 456x256 it sits on the campfire figures' heads (sheet-01). - Hero select at 1080p: the info panel needs its scroll arrows and WHIRLWIND is cut. Two corrections to the finding: 1. These sizes only occur in fullscreen (F11, the F key or the Options FULLSCREEN button). A normal Chrome window on the same screens gets 1366x657, so S=2 and 683x329, and 1920x969, so S=3 and 640x323. Going fullscreen at 1080p therefore switches S from 3 to 4. The UI grows 33%, and the visible world shrinks from 800x404 to 600x338 world px (world scale is S*0.8, game.js:2787). That makes the "the two most common resolutions" claim an overstatement, but the fullscreen case is real and supported. 2. The half Armory row is not specific to 1366: the list is a clipped scroller, and 1280x720 cuts a row too. The 1080p half of the finding is an owner/taste call, because 480x270 is the default design size. The 1366x768 and 1280x800 fullscreen half is a real polish defect: a hint is lost and the pause panel overlaps the HUD tray. Better fix: Change only the sub-270 case, guarded so it steps down by at most one and never reaches S=1. In view.js resize(), after line 57 add: `if (view.S > 2 && Math.min(pxW, pxH) / view.S < 270) view.S--;` This affects short sides of 750-809 px (S 3 to 2, so 768 gives 683x384 and 800 gives 640x400) and 1050-1079 px (S 4 to 3). 1080p, 720p, phones and windowed play are unchanged. 1366x768 fullscreen then matches what the same laptop already shows in a window (S=2). The player sees more world (854x480 instead of 569x320), so the owner should sign off. Note that the world scale can also be changed separately from the UI scale. Problems with the proposed fix: - Unguarded Option B, and Option A's floor(m/300), would drop windows 450-539 (B) or 450-599 (A) px tall from S=2 to S=1. That gives a 1:1-pixel UI with tiny text on desktop. - Option A also re-scales every 1080p fullscreen player (world view 600x338 to 800x450), which is an owner decision. - Keep the ceil() W/H and the zero-offset blit. The overhang is at most S-1 device px, and no UI is drawn in the outermost art column. The suggested `W = floor(pxW/S)` plus centred blit is not minimal: input.js:178 maps the pointer as `clientX*sx/view.S` with no offset, and overlay() positions (presentUI :81) also assume origin 0,0. Centring would put mouse hit-testing and framed pictures off by up to (S-1)/2 device px, and leave strips that menus never repaint. - Separately, the NEXT line over the campfire at 480x270 is a title-screen layout bug that S=4 screens (1050-1349 px tall) will still hit. Fix it in the title layout, not with S.
- Intent sceptic (not real, taste): The numbers are right. At 1366x768 the scale is S=3, giving a 456x256 view and a 1368-px UI blit. At 1080p it is S=4, giving 480x270, while 720p gives 640x360. But both view sizes were chosen on purpose and the project supports them. 1) 480x270 at 1080p is the reference size. view.js starts at S:4, W:480, H:270. tools/headless.mjs says "The camera is 600×338 art pixels, as on a 1080p screen: the HUD's 480×270 at the world zoom of 0.8". The horde cap's far-recycle comment in data/waves.js tunes its distances to "a 1080p screen's 600×338". Several tests (hud, frame-garbage, hazards, leftovers' chestLayout(480, 270)) draw at 1080p. Option A would make 1080p 640x360 and show an 800x450 world. That changes the camera the balance was tuned on, so it is a gameplay change. 2) 456x256 is a supported, tested size, not a floor violation. test/trial-maps.test.js says "a 1366 x 768 laptop 456 x 256" and checks layouts at [456,256] and even [427,240], the 854x480 itch embed. The review of 28 October 2026 fixed the 1366x768 problem in the layout (BEGIN hopping), not by changing S. screens.js shortens rows for 427x240. The view.js comment only says "roughly" 270-330. 3) The fallout listed is mostly adaptive layout already accepted. The hero-select scroll arrows are expected: the 2026-11-14 hero reviews accept that "Long descriptions require the existing scroll controls at small sizes" at 480x270.
