The Armory drops its hint where there is no room and shows a cut row to signal scrolling. 4) Any change to S changes gameplay at that resolution. spawner.js sets the spawn ring from hypot(cam.W, cam.H). The game.js horde recycling, lighting and forest culling also read cam.W and cam.H. Option B turns the 1366x768 world view from 569x320 into 854x480, where sprites draw at 1.6 device px per art pixel. 5) The blit fix is not the smallest change and would break things. Centring the blit needs a matching offset in input.js (pointer = client / view.S) and in every hud.js world-to-HUD overlay (z = cam.S / view.S). Without them, clicks and markers shift by up to one art pixel. The ceil overflow is at most S-1 device px (2 px here) and harmless. Using floor would leave an uncovered strip on menus. The one real trace in the captures is the pause panel's bottom frame just covering the loadout belt's WEAPONS label at 456x256. That is a layout item in gamescreen.js, which another session is editing, not a reason to change the scale. Better fix: Leave the view.js scale rule as it is, since 1080p's 480x270 / 600x338 is the reference camera and 456x256 and 427x240 are tested sizes. Fix only layouts that truly collide at 456x256 on their own screen. For example, in public/js/ui/gamescreen.js (another session's uncommitted file, so coordinate), keep the pause panel's bottom above the loadout belt's top when H is small: shrink the panel's gaps or move the belt. Add [456,256] and [427,240] cases to that screen's layout test, the way test/trial-maps.test.js already does.

## pipeline

No, the game is not pixel-perfect, though both critics (art lens and tech lens) agree its foundations are better than most browser pixel games. Keep these parts as they are. Image smoothing is off everywhere except a few deliberate soft layers. The camera snaps to whole device pixels, so ground and props scroll rigidly: in all 76 frame pairs the best whole-pixel shift equals the camera step, with no shimmer. Sprites never crawl internally. Arrows and orbit blades are pre-rotated at art resolution. The HUD is composited at an integer S with bitmap fonts. The pixelated CSS stretch was measured as true nearest (0% new colours). The title campfire heroes are a clean 1:1, and the Ranger, Berserker and Ea-nasir sheets are clean palette art with 16-52 colours. The core flaw is the world scale. S x 0.8 is fractional in 17 of 21 measured setups, including the three most common sizes: art pixels come out 1/2 wide at 720p, 2/3 at 768p and 3/4 at 1080p. On 4K, Retina and 150% screens the canvas pixel cap adds a second, non-integer browser stretch that breaks even the HUD. Several concrete bugs sit on that base. The hero selection showcase and roster, the ult cut-in and the death wisp drop about 36% of a hero's pixels because they are drawn at 0.8 UI px per art px. Eight regenerated hero sheets carry an alpha 1-14 halo plus alpha 240-254 bodies. Drawn normally, the halo is a ghost outline against the owner's no-outline rule. Every alpha>0 consumer treats it as solid, so each hit flash and the locked-hero silhouette grow a 21-28% blob, including a detached block next to the Knight. Several layers also run at other densities: the six reforged bosses at half density (about 20% of columns dropped at 720p, a 1/2 split at 1080p), damage numbers at continuous sizes (glyph columns dropped at 720p/768p), the Cultist at 1.5x, champions at 1.35x and the hit stretch, and the Scythe and telegraphs as anti-aliased vectors. The hero also twitches 1 device px while walking; a hero-anchored camera snap removes that completely in replay. Smaller items: HUD bars slide against their owners, a few HUD chevrons and markers are anti-aliased, the CRT scanlines beat against the world, and the soft light and fog layers sit on their own grids. The cheapest high-value fixes are small code changes in committed files: an integer DPR divisor for the cap, device-space or integer-scale hero blits in the menus, cut-in and wisp, alpha snapping when hero sheets are decoded, quantised damage-number scale, the hero-anchored camera snap, and a 2:1 reduced variant for the reforged bosses below 1 px per source px. An integer world scale (round(minSide/345-360)) needs the owner's OK because it reframes the field of view. The current FOV already spans 320-450 rows; the new one would span about 288-432, and 720p would lose about 20%. Art passes come after that: native-density reforged bosses, natively drawn 1.5x Cultist-type sprites, champion sizing, and an owner-approved colour consolidation of the continuous-tone hero sheets (the Knight idle frame has about 420 colours in 438 px and boils 6% per frame). Some findings touch files holding another session's uncommitted work: hero-selection.js:249, tools/art-v4/bake.mjs, and the uncommitted public/assets/heroes/*.js. The game does not load those; it loads the committed heroes-approved/*.js. Evidence lives under C:/Users/I/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/015cd6aa7d087c29/bb69f979-5b65-4b71-a433-ff8642911dab/scratchpad/gfx-review/pipeline/, with the art critic's extras in art-extra/ and the tech critic's in tech-extra/. Gaps: only the hit flash was checked in a real headed Chrome window. The CRT option and the cut-in were checked in code only.

### pipeline-1 [unchecked] Hero selection showcase, roster, ult cut-in and death wisp are drawn at a non-integer scale on the UI canvas (0.8 UI px per art px), so about 36% of the hero's pixels are never shown
- bug / code - public/js/ui/hero-showcase.js:27-29 (heroMenuScale = max(1/S, zoom) = 0.8), :84-88, :103 (g.drawPlayer on the W x H UI ctx), :113 (Shadow rim at the same scale); public/js/ui/hero-selection.js:249 (roster thumbnails min(0.8, fit); uncommitted edits by another session's demo work); public/js/game/hud.js:845-846 (ult cut-in pose ps = (bh-6)/pf.height); hud.js:1246-1277 (departing-soul wisp at c.S/view.S = 0.8)
- Evidence: Both lenses. pipeline/10-hero-select-showcase-dropped-pixels.png: the Knight is missing art columns 27 32 37 42 47 and rows 27 32 37 42 47 52 (5/22 columns, 6/32 rows), so 156 of 430 opaque px (36.3%) are never drawn. The Paladin loses 6/27 columns and 6/32 rows (35.7%). pipeline/16-menu-title-vs-hero-select.png: the 1:1 campfire Knight is clean, while the roster and showcase Knights lose face and shield detail. Ult cut-in scales (art-extra/posebox.mjs): Knight 1.939 at 1080p, 1.848 at 1366x768, 2.667 at 720p; Mage 1.600 and 1.525. Rows are dropped or doubled at every common size.
- The hero screen is where players look at the hero art most closely, and it shows the worst version of it. Nearest sampling at 0.8 deletes every fifth art row and column, and the Shadow's 1-px menu rim loses every fifth pixel too. The ult cut-in and the death wisp have the same root cause: a non-integer sprite blit on the low-res UI canvas, which then gets an integer blow-up.
- Fix: Preferred: draw these figures at an integer scale. heroMenuScale() returns 1, as the title campfire does. In the showcase draw(), keep scale = 1 when the figure fits, and use Math.floor of the fit when it is 2 or more. Where in-game size is wanted, or the box is smaller than 1:1, draw on the device canvas: extend the unused view.js:107-110 overlay() queue with a draw callback that presentUI runs after the UI blit, with ctx = view.ctx and a clip to box*S. Then call g.drawPlayer(view.ctx, {S: Math.max(1, Math.floor(view.S*scale)), ...}). Roster thumbnails (coordinate with the session editing hero-selection.js): draw the crop at 1:1 and clip it to the slot instead of scaling down; only the Shadow's 40-px pose overflows the 35-px slot. Cut-in: use the integer device scale floor(S*ps), or as a stopgap ps = Math.max(1, Math.round((bh-6)/pf.height)) with bh grown to fit. Wisp: use the same device-space path. Never use the smoothing branch at view.js:89 for sprites.

### pipeline-2 [unchecked] Regenerated hero sheets carry an alpha 1-14 halo and alpha 240-254 bodies: a ghost outline, ground bleed, and a fattened, detached hit flash and lock silhouette
- bug / code - Root: public/assets/heroes-approved/{knight,mage,rogue,necromancer,paladin,druid,witchhunter}.js and recruits (footman, templar, marksman, ...) palettes, decoded unchanged by public/js/art/approved-heroes.js:7-17 (unpack, line 14). Consumers: public/js/engine/pixels.js:74-78 (silhouette: alpha>0 becomes opaque), sprites.js:153-155 Frame.white(), game.js:3477 (p.flash > 0 ? fr.white(flip)), hero-showcase.js:107 (lock map c>>>24 ? 0xff201b29 : 0), and outline()/rimRing()/bounds()/heroCrop(). Build: tools/animation-round/build.mjs (tools/art-v4/bake.mjs has another session's uncommitted edits; the uncommitted public/assets/heroes/*.js are not what the game loads).
- Evidence: Both lenses. tech-extra/T2-hit-flash-real-chrome.png (real Chrome at 1920x1080 and 1280x720, devHold, p.flash=1): the flashing Knight becomes a blob with a detached white rectangle above his right shoulder, and the Druid's staff and leaves merge into one mass. tech-extra/T1-hit-flash-faint-halo.png: the flash and lock add +106 px to the Knight's 435 (+24.4%), +160/565 to the Druid (+28.3%) and +115/534 to the Shadow (+21.5%). pipeline/14-hero-alpha-fringes.png (red ring = alpha 1-14). Knight idle frame: alpha 255 = 2 px, 240-254 = 419, 15-239 = 17, 1-14 = 103. Knight palette (art-extra/palalpha.mjs): 6,349 entries, of which 945 are at 255, 4,987 at 240-254, 314 at 15-239 and 102 at 1-14. Druid: 21.8% of inked px are alpha 1-14. Ranger, Berserker and Ea-nasir have 0 (converted to opaque on 2026-11-15, reviews/hero-style-match-2026-11-15.md).
- This is a bake artefact, not a design choice. Drawn normally, the 1-14 ring is a faint dark halo (up to 5.5% of the edge colour, about 9 levels on sand), which amounts to a ghost outline against the owner's no-outline call. The 240-254 bodies let 0.4-6% of the ground show through, and the 15-239 band tints the self-coloured dark edges with whatever ground the hero stands on. Every alpha-thresholding consumer treats alpha>0 as solid. So the hit flash (0.12 s on every hit taken, which in a crowd is nearly constant), the locked-hero silhouette and the Shadow's rim paint a silhouette one art px fatter all round, plus detached blocks from stray faint clumps.
- Fix: Snap alpha once at decode in approved-heroes.js unpack(). Build a per-sheet palette once, for example pal = data.palette.map(c => { const a = c>>>24; return a < 15 ? 0 : a >= 240 ? (c|0xff000000)>>>0 : c; }), and use pal[color]. This change is invisible apart from removing the halo and the bleed, and it already fixes the flash blob. Then settle the 15-239 band: threshold at 128 (a >= 128 opaque, else 0), either at decode or in the sheet build. Preview it first, since 4-8% of edge pixels become crisp, and check the Rogue and Necromancer for intended translucent wisps. The minimal alternative, which changes no art: pixels.silhouette keeps the source alpha (out.d[i] = ((color & 0xffffff) | (buf.d[i] & 0xff000000)) >>> 0), and hero-showcase.js:107 uses (c >>> 24) >= 128. Binary-alpha monsters are unaffected.

### pipeline-3 [unchecked] The world is drawn at a fractional scale (S x 0.8) in 17 of 21 measured setups, so art pixels come out 1/2 wide at 720p, 2/3 at 768p and 3/4 at 1080p
- defect / owner - public/js/engine/view.js:24 (zoom 0.8), view.js:57 (S = round(minSide/300)); public/js/game/game.js:2787 (cam.S = max(1, view.S*view.zoom)); knock-on: enemies.js:993 per-sprite Math.round(w/h) (skeleton 54/17 = 3.176 vs 3.2), forest.js:343 ground chunks 819/820 px
- Evidence: Both lenses. pipeline/03-scale-chain-table.png: only 4 of 21 configs are even, all at S=5 (c2560x1440@1, c1920x1080@1.25, p2560x1440@1.25, p2560x1440@1.5). pipeline/01-knight-art-pixel-widths-dpr1.png rulers: 1280x720 gives 1px x32 / 2px x48; 1366x768 gives 2px x48 / 3px x32; 1920x1080 gives 3px x64 / 4px x16; 2560x1440 gives an even 4px x80. pipeline/15-hud-vs-world-1920.png: HUD runs of 4 make up 70%, beside sand runs of 3 and 4. A windowed 1080p Chrome (innerHeight 953) gets S=3 and world 2.4, against 3.2 fullscreen, so the hero changes size by 33% on F11. Today's FOV is already inconsistent: 720p sees 800x450, 1366x768 sees 569x320, 1080p sees 600x338, and windowed 1080p sees 800x397. art-extra/scales.mjs: for short sides of 600-1600 the visible field is 320-450 art px today; an integer round(minSide/345) gives 288-400.
- The art pixel is the unit the whole game is drawn in, but the world multiplies it by a fractional device scale in every common setup. Alternate art columns and rows therefore get different widths. The one-pixel features the plumed-Knight style depends on (self-coloured dark edges, eye pixels, hue-shifted ramps) change thickness across the body, and change again as sprites move through the phase. The even 4-px HUD beside the world makes the mismatch obvious. This is the single biggest reason the game is not pixel-perfect: only about one player in five (S=5, short side 1350-1649) sees clean pixels.
- Fix: Choose an integer world scale directly in syncCam, decoupled from the HUD's S: c.S = Math.max(1, Math.round(Math.min(view.pxW, view.pxH) / K)) with K around 345-360 (or Math.max(1, Math.round(view.S*view.zoom))). Keep c.W/c.H as computed; hud.js z = cam.S/view.S still works, and the spawner already sizes rings from cam.W/H (spawner.js:418,443). With K=345: 720p -> 2 (360 tall vs 450), 1366x768 -> 2 (384 vs 320), windowed 1080p -> 3 (318 vs 397), 1080p -> 3 (360 vs 338), 1440p -> 4 (360, unchanged). Windowed and fullscreen 1080p then match. The owner must OK the per-resolution FOV change, since 720p loses about 20% each way. Once the world is integer, the enemies.js:993 rounding and the 819/820 forest chunks become exact. Even world scales (2 or 4) also land the half-density reforged bosses exactly.

### pipeline-4 [unchecked] The canvas pixel cap makes the browser stretch the frame by x1.0675-x1.4233 on 4K, Retina and 150% screens, so even the HUD goes uneven
- defect / code - public/js/engine/view.js:28 (MAX_PIXELS = 2560*1600), view.js:47-50 (dpr = sqrt(MAX/(cssW*cssH))); public/index.html:18 (100% CSS size, image-rendering: pixelated)
- Evidence: Both lenses. pipeline/06-4k-canvas-cap-stretch-hud-and-ground.png: any 4K screen (100/150/200%) gets a 2698x1518 canvas stretched x1.4233, so HUD UI px go from 5 canvas px to 7/8 screen px (7: 63.7%, 8: 4.5-8.5%) and art px from 4 to 5/6 (5px x24 / 6px x56). pipeline/02-knight-dpr-and-canvas-cap.png panels 3-5: CSS 1920x1080@150% -> 2698x1518 x1.0675 (art 4px 73% / 5px 27%); Retina 1440x900@2 x1.125 (4/5 50/50); CSS 2560x1440@1.25 x1.186. Seven of 21 configs in 03-scale-chain-table.png carry a browser stretch.
- The DPR is cut by a continuous square root, so the backing store is not an integer fraction of the screen. The pixelated CSS stretch then repeats every 2nd to 15th screen row and column, adding a second uneven resampling on top of the world's. This is the only setup where the integer HUD breaks, and it hits the high-DPI screens that have the most pixels to spare. Fractional CSS x DPR sizes, such as 1366x768@1.25 = 1707.5, also leave the last 2-5 columns stretched.
- Fix: When capping, divide by the smallest integer that fits. const d0 = Math.min(devicePixelRatio || 1, 3); const devW = Math.round(cssW*d0), devH = Math.round(cssH*d0); const div = Math.max(1, Math.ceil(Math.sqrt(devW*devH/MAX_PIXELS) - 1e-9)); pxW = Math.round(devW/div); pxH = Math.round(devH/div); dpr = d0/div. Results: 4K -> 1920x1080 x2 exact (HUD 8 screen px per UI px; world 6 per art px once the world is integer), Retina 2880x1800 -> 1440x900 x2, CSS 1920x1080@1.5 -> 1440x810 x2, CSS 2560x1440@1.25 -> 1600x900 x2. Fill cost also drops (2.07 M px instead of 4.1 M on 4K). For exact device sizes, read ResizeObserver devicePixelContentBoxSize where available. Raising MAX_PIXELS to 3840*2160 is the alternative, if fill rate allows.

### pipeline-5 [unchecked] The six reforged bosses are drawn at half density (0.5 world px per source px): source columns are dropped at world 1.6 and widths split 1/2 at 2.4/3.2
- defect / code - public/assets/art/bosses-reforged/{butcher,lich,pharaoh,frostgiant,thornqueen,pitlord}.js (hires 4.07-4.56); public/js/data/enemies.js:89,97,172,176,180,184 (scale 57/26..57/28); public/js/game/enemies.js:214-216 (drawScale = scale/hires = 0.5: Butcher 2.1923/4.3846, Lich 2.28/4.56, others 2.0357/4.0714), :966-967 (w = img.width*S*sx), :993 (drawImage)
- Evidence: Both lenses (the art lens says the lasting fix is a re-bake, the tech lens gives the code fallback). pipeline/12-mixed-density-lineup.png: the Butcher at 1280x720 is drawn at 0.80 device px per source px, with 45/224 columns and 38/192 rows drawn 0 px wide (red ticks). At 1080p it is 1.598 (a 1/2 split, the worst possible ratio) beside the Bone King at 3.198; only world 4.0 gives an even 2.0. pipeline/13-boss-half-density-detail.png: brow, eye, strap and hood-edge pixels break up at 1.6 and come out lumpy at 2.4/3.2. The Bone King and Demon Lord stay 1:1, so two boss densities coexist.
- The most-watched sprites in a run are authored at twice the world's pixel density, which is textbook mixels. On S=2 screens (720p, 1366x768@1.25), nearest minification throws away a fifth of each boss's columns and rows, and which ones vanish changes from frame to frame as the boss moves. At 2.4 and 3.2, source pixels 1 and 2 device px wide sit side by side, so the reforged detail reads as uneven noise next to the 3-4 px art pixels. The pixel drop below 1 device px per source px is a plain rendering error. The mixed density is an art-direction matter, separate from the 2026-10-27 pixel pass, which left the big bosses' art (not their density) alone.
- Fix: Code (now): in Enemy.draw, when S*this.drawScale < 2 (at minimum when < 1), draw a 2:1 reduced variant: img = fr.variant('half', halve, flip) with the per-source scale doubled. halve takes the most frequent opaque colour of each 2x2 block (dark-ink priority), opaque when 2 or more of the 4 px are opaque, and Frame.variant caches it per frame. The same rect feeds _rect and the night cut, and the full-detail frames stay for world 4 and above. One-line stopgap: imageSmoothingEnabled = true only for draws below 1 device px per source px. Art (lasting): bake native world-density sheets with the same 2:1 reduction plus a hand cleanup of faces, eyes and outlines, and set hires = scale so that drawScale = 1. With an integer world, prefer even scales (2 or 4).

### pipeline-6 [unchecked] The Scythe (plus the Lantern cone, Ward Bubble, rotated telegraphs and weather strokes) is runtime anti-aliased vector drawing at device resolution, a non-pixel layer among the sprites
- defect / code - public/js/game/weapons-light.js:289-352 (Scythe wake arcs lineWidth (7 - i*0.45)*S, drawBlade rotated strokes, quadraticCurveTo blade, 1.5*S edge); weapons-light.js:77-96 (Lantern radial-gradient cone); public/js/game/game.js:3420-3445 (Ward Bubble gradient + arc strokes); effects.js:1228-1256 and boss-mechanics.js:202,246 (fillRect under ctx.rotate); weather.js:365-455 strokes
- Evidence: Both lenses. pipeline/19-vector-and-scaled-text-vs-pixels.png, middle panel (x4): the Scythe's haft and blade have smooth anti-aliased edges 1 device px wide with grey AA fringes, and the wake is a smooth arc, directly against the 3-4 px sprite and ground pixels. Left panel (1:1): the arc is the only element on screen without a pixel grid and reads like a Flash-game overlay. The Lantern cone is a continuous gradient.
- Every other rotating weapon (arrows, orbit blades) is pre-rotated at art resolution with nearest sampling (sprites.js:184), so it stays on the pixel grid. The Scythe alone is drawn entirely as canvas paths at device resolution, so it looks below every other weapon. The rotated telegraph bars show the same AA edges at every angle except 0 and 90 degrees, and the other vector effects share the problem on a smaller scale.
- Fix: Rasterise at art resolution, then upscale with nearest. Quick path: draw the existing Scythe path code into a small offscreen canvas at scale 1 (line widths in art px), sized ceil(2R+16) and centred on the hero. Optionally threshold or quantise its alpha to 0/255 or 3-4 steps with getImageData. Blit with smoothing off: drawImage(off, Math.round(sx - ox*S), Math.round(sy - oy*S), off.width*S, off.height*S). General path: one world-aligned art-res FX layer of (ceil(cam.W)+2) x (ceil(cam.H)+2) px with origin floor(cam.x/y), blitted at cam.S with offset (floor(cam.x) - cam.x)*S, for all vector FX. Best for the Scythe specifically: an authored blade sprite pre-rotated 32 ways (rotations()) plus pixel arc frames for the wake. Build the telegraph bars once with px.rotate. The Lantern cone may stay soft as light, but posterising it into 3-4 bands at art resolution would match the rest.

### pipeline-7 [unchecked] The hero twitches 1 device px on screen while walking, from rounding p.x*S and the damped camera separately
- defect / code - public/js/game/game.js:3034-3035 (camera damp rate 9), :3233-3234 (c.x = round(cx*S)/S), :3484 (X = Math.round((p.x - c.x)*S - w/2), Y = round((p.y - c.y - anchorY/density + bob - hop)*S)), :3530-3536 (drawHeroRing)
- Evidence: Both lenses (the art lens rated it polish, the tech lens defect). pipeline/05-motion-camera-steps-and-hero-twitch.png right column (60 Hz): hero X toggles 586/587 on 22 of 49 frames at world 1.6, 603/604 on 15/49 at 2.4, 852/853 on 5/49 at 3.2 and 1146/1147 on 6/49 at 4.0; pipeline/04b-motion-filmstrip-1280-60hz.png labels 586/587. Replays: art-extra/twitch-sim.mjs (199 settled frames) gives 122/123/33/60 twitches today against 0 with the anchor; tech-extra/twitch-sim.mjs (production damp, 1.058 art px/frame) gives 73 X changes in 119 frames at 1.6 and 18 at 3.2, against 0 at every scale. Ground steps are unchanged (1/2, 3/4, 4/5; e.g. 3px x123 / 4px x76 at 3.2).
- The camera trails the hero at a constant lag, and the two rounding errors (camera to device px, then hero offset) beat against each other. The eye tracks the hero, so a sideways shimmer of 1 device px at up to about 27 Hz reads as vibration. It is most visible at 720p/768p, where 1 device px is about 0.63 art px. Vertical motion has the same issue, because anchorY*S is fractional (56*3.2 = 179.2). Ground and props stay rigid, with 0 shimmer over 76 frame pairs, and that should be kept.
- Fix: Derive the camera's pixel position from the hero's rounded position in draw(). Keep the same two Math.random() calls in the same order (seeded-tests rule): const shx = (Math.random()-0.5)*9*sh, shy = (Math.random()-0.5)*9*sh; const hx = Math.round(p.x*S), hy = Math.round(p.y*S); c.x = (hx - Math.round((p.x - bx - shx)*S))/S; c.y = (hy - Math.round((p.y - by - shy)*S))/S. In drawPlayer, and for the hero's ring and shadow, compose the position from integers: X = hx - Math.round(c.x*S) - Math.round(w/2); Y = hy - Math.round(c.y*S) - Math.round((spr.anchorY/density - bob + p.hop)*S) + Math.round(sink*S). c.x*S stays an integer, so the world stays rigid.

### pipeline-8 [unchecked] Damage numbers scale continuously, so font pixels are dropped at 720p/768p, strokes are uneven elsewhere, the shine band is misregistered, and the crit spin rotates bitmap text
- defect / code - public/js/game/numbers.js:256-282 (scale = st.scale*n.boost*k*S at :265; drawImage at Math.round(w/h) :281-282; crit spin ctx.rotate :275-279), :290 (shine band at X + x0*scale with unrounded scale and h), boostFor :321-324
- Evidence: Both lenses (the art lens rated it polish, the tech lens defect). tech-extra/T3-damage-number-font-pixels.png (production textCanvas, Chrome nearest mapping): at 720p a value-3 number is drawn at 0.6*0.95*1.6 = 0.91 device px per font px, dropping 1/7 columns and 1/12 rows. Fading (k=0.82) it is 0.75, dropping 2/7 columns and 3/12 rows, and the '3' becomes unreadable. At 1080p '48' is 2.34, so strokes are 2 or 3 px. tech-extra/numbers-scale.mjs: bleed numbers at 720p are 0.88 steady (3/23 columns dropped), and every style drops 4-6/23 columns as it fades. pipeline/19-vector-and-scaled-text-vs-pixels.png right panel (x3): '44', '43', '19' and '9' mid-pop each use a different glyph-pixel size. pipeline/17-night-forest-effects.png: '51' fading at t=0.81.
- The bitmap font is crisp only at whole device px per font px, but the pop, the boost-by-value and the fade all scale it continuously. Below 1 (small hits at 1280x720 or on S=2 screens), whole glyph columns and rows vanish. Between 1 and 3, the same digit looks different in neighbouring numbers. Numbers are the most numerous foreground element, and their glyph grid matches neither the HUD's nor the world's. The settled flash-on-every-hit behaviour is fine and stays.
- Fix: Quantise the scale: const unit = Math.max(1, Math.round(st.scale*n.boost*k*S)); w = c.width*unit; h = c.height*unit. Use unit for the main draw, the crit flash and the shine band (X + x0*unit, (x1 - x0)*unit, h). The pop becomes a stepped pixel pop through 2-3 sizes (at 3.2: 1 -> 3 -> 2 px), and the steady size is unchanged at 1080p (normal = 2). Replace the crit spin's ctx.rotate with a 1-2 px jiggle, or with a pre-rotated glyph canvas at glyph resolution (sprites.js rotations path).

### pipeline-9 [unchecked] Non-integer per-sprite multipliers keep mixing pixel sizes within one frame (Cultist/Frost Witch/Thorn Witch 1.5, champion 1.35, hit-flash stretch, area-scaled blades, meteor and projectile scales)
- polish / owner - public/js/data/enemies.js:32 (Cultist scale 1.5) and the two witches; public/js/game/enemies.js:214 (champion x1.35), :148-156 renderScale (flash 1.12x0.9, squash, hopper 1.12/0.92), :993 (Math.round(w), Math.round(h) per sprite); effects.js:734 (orbit blades S*min(1.3, area)); effects.js:1060 (meteor 1.2/1.6); projectiles.js:374 (S*this.scale: 1.25/1.5 plus upgrades)
- Evidence: Both lenses (the art lens says art, the tech lens says owner). pipeline/12-mixed-density-lineup.png: the Cultist is 4.79 device px per source px at 1080p (4/5 ruler) and 6.0 at 1440p. The champion skeleton is 4.29 at 1080p and 5.41 at the integer 4.0 world (5/6 ruler, uneven even there). A plain skeleton is 3.176 and the Bone King 3.198, against a 3.2 world. pipeline/17-night-forest-effects.png: the Ranger arrow is 4.0 device px per arrow px over a 3.2 world; x1.25 lands on integers (2/3/4/5), so it is crisp but a second density.
- Even with an integer world, these sprites would keep uneven or mismatched pixel sizes, and champions stay uneven at every resolution. The hit-flash stretch fires on every hit across the horde, briefly dropping or doubling a row or column on dozens of sprites at once. The owner's 1.5x Cultist-type size is settled and not questioned here, only how it is rendered.
- Fix: Cultist, Frost Witch and Thorn Witch: author the frames at 1.5x and draw them at drawScale 1; the hitbox keeps this.scale. Champions (owner's choice): decouple drawScale from the 1.35 AI scale and draw at 1 with authored champion bulk or variants, keeping the gold treatment, or make it x1.5 (whole px at world 2 and 4) or x2. Quantise drawn sizes generally: u = Math.max(1, Math.round(S*sx)), w = img.width*u. Replace the 1.12x0.9 flash stretch with a 1-art-px widen or up-pop of the white frame, unless the owner prefers the smooth squash. Quantise orbit-blade, meteor and projectile scales to {1, 1.5, 2}, or pre-render the scaled rotations at art resolution.

### pipeline-10 [unchecked] Eight hero sheets are continuous-tone images rather than palette pixel art (Knight idle: about 420 colours in 438 px), and the colour noise boils between frames
- polish / owner - public/assets/heroes-approved/{knight,mage,rogue,necromancer,paladin,druid,witchhunter}.js and recruit sheets (footman, templar, marksman, ...); palettes Knight 6,349 entries, Rogue 7,056, Paladin 5,046 vs archer.js 52, berserker.js 53 (matched to native art in aa98685); built by tools/animation-round/build.mjs
- Evidence: Both lenses (the art lens rated it polish, the tech lens taste). art-extra/21-hero-sheet-colour-noise.png (magenta = a pixel next to a near-twin within 1-8 levels): Knight 420 colours in 438 px, 39% noisy; Necromancer 454/539, 57%; Paladin 384/461, 23%; Ranger 51/400, 1%. tech-extra/colours.mjs, idle frame 0 (visible px / unique colours / colours with a twin within 6 levels): Knight 435/417/378, Druid 565/535/478, Paladin 445/368/254, Shadow 534/478/450; against Ranger 400/51/15, Berserker 421/52/14, Ea-nasir 732/16/2, Skeleton 196/7/0, Butcher 6857/31/0. Whole sheets (art-extra/sheetstats.mjs): Knight 5,464 distinct RGB, Rogue 5,509, Footman 4,941, Paladin 4,550. Boil per frame step (art-extra/boil.mjs): Knight walk 6.0%, Necromancer walk 8.3%, Rogue idle 8.9%; Ranger and Berserker 0.1%. A greedy merge within 8 levels takes the Knight from 5,327 to 253 colours (91 within 16).
- Even the style-reference Knight is not clean pixel art. Flat planes (armour plates, cloth) carry ±6-8-level jitter, the fingerprint of a resampled painting, and the noise shimmers frame to frame in idle and walk. Clusters read slightly muddy at 3-4 px per art px. The roster is split in two: the Ranger, Berserker and Ea-nasir are on clean palettes (2026-11-15) and the rest are continuous-tone. The soft alpha in the halo finding comes from the same source. The looks and the hue-shifted multi-colour style are settled; this is only about near-identical duplicates.
- Fix: Needs owner sign-off with a before/after at x6, side by side with the current sheets, since the Knight is the style reference. In the sheet build (tools/animation-round/build.mjs), consolidate each hero's colours. Merge colours within about 6-8 levels (perceptual), which is invisible one by one and keeps every hue-shifted ramp, into one shared per-hero palette of roughly 48-64 colours. Map every frame through that palette so flat areas stay flat from frame to frame. This is the same native-art match pass the Ranger, Berserker and Ea-nasir received.

### pipeline-11 [unchecked] World-anchored HUD bars and names snap to UI pixels (S device px) while sprites move by 1 device px, so they slide against their owner
- polish / code - public/js/game/hud.js:1002-1011 (hero health bar at Math.round((p.x - cam.x)*z) in UI px); hud.js:976-982 (elite/boss names and bars); hud.js:1015-1036 (crypt strike label); hud.js:361/369 (coin and fury comet starts)
- Evidence: Art lens only. In code, z = cam.S/view.S = 0.8 puts the bar's device x at S*round(0.8*d), while the hero's centre is at round(3.2*d). The offset wanders across S device px (±2 at 1080p, ±2.5 at 1440p, about 1 art px) whenever the hero moves on screen. The 144 Hz trace in pipeline/05-motion-camera-steps-and-hero-twitch.png shows this for dozens of frames (hero X 850-854 while the camera settles), and it is constant for every walking elite or boss.
- The bar under the hero and the bars over elites and bosses lag behind smoothly moving sprites in coarse steps. It is subtle but reads as imprecise, especially on bosses.
- Fix: Draw these few elements on the device canvas, anchored to the sprite's device rect (p._rect / e._rect) and keeping UI-sized pixels, e.g. ctx.fillRect(cx - 9*view.S, top, 18*view.S, 4*view.S), where cx is the sprite's rounded device centre. Text can use the same device-space overlay hook proposed for the hero showcase.

### pipeline-12 [unchecked] HUD chevrons, edge markers and touch circles are anti-aliased vector paths on the UI canvas, so blended pixels get blown up into S x S smudges
- polish / code - public/js/game/hud.js:1042-1054 (crypt strike chevron: stroke lineWidth 3 and 1.5 under ctx.rotate), hud.js:1205-1215 (rotated edge-marker triangle), hud.js:1322-1331 (touch stick arcs)
- Evidence: Art lens only. art-extra/20-hud-aim-chevron-aa.png renders the chevron exactly as hud.js draws it: 41-56 blended colours from 2 inks at 0-75 degrees, and each blended UI px becomes a 4x4 block at 1080p. Every other HUD element is crisp (pipeline/15-hud-vs-world-1920.png: weapons-panel runs of 4 are 70%).
- These are the only muddy elements in an otherwise integer, bitmap-font HUD.
- Fix: Replace them with pixel sprites: an 8x8 chevron or triangle buffer pre-rotated 16 or 32 ways with px.rotate (as sprites.js rotations does), drawn at integer UI coordinates. Use pixelEllipse (ui/scenes.js) rings for the touch stick.

### pipeline-13 [unchecked] The optional CRT scanlines use the HUD pixel period (view.S), which beats against the world's 3.2-px rows and turns uneven under the canvas-cap stretch
- polish / code - public/js/main.js:90-101 (scanlines: period view.S, dark rows floor(S/3))
- Evidence: Art lens only, from code (the CRT option was not captured). At 1080p a 4-px period over 3.2-px art rows puts the dark sub-row at a different height in each art row, with a 16-px (5 art rows) beat. On 4K the 1-canvas-px lines become 1 or 2 screen px after the x1.4233 stretch.
- The CRT option should darken the same sub-row of every art pixel. Instead it adds a moire band.
- Fix: With an integer world, set the pattern period to cam.S and the dark band to Math.max(1, Math.floor(cam.S/3)). With the integer cap divisor, the lines stay even on screen. Optionally keep a separate HUD-period pattern clipped to the HUD.

### pipeline-14 [unchecked] Soft and coarse screen layers ignore the art grid: smooth light map, smoothed fog of war, night/duststorm holes, 3S fog texels, and a screen-fixed 256x144 vignette
- taste / owner - public/js/game/lighting.js:57 (LS=2), :140-146 (bilinear multiply; softness is by design per the header, lines 1-3); dungeon.js:1784-1786 (fog of war, one texel per tile, smoothing on); enemies.js:1199-1203 (night hole); hazards/haze.js:52-55; game.js:3346-3350, 3394, 3400 + fxsprites.js:222 (vignette 256x144 stretched to pxW x pxH with smoothing off); forest fog texture at 3S
- Evidence: Both lenses (the art lens says code, the tech lens owner). pipeline/18-dungeon-fog-of-war-edge.png: a soft blurred gradient about one tile wide over crisp wall tiles. pipeline/17-night-forest-effects.png bottom: dark-ground row runs are 1 px 21.2% and 2 px 15.0% where art pixels are 3-4; the contrast-stretched twin shows the 9.6-px fog grid and 7.5-px vignette blocks (alternating 7/8), neither aligned to the 3.2-px art grid. pipeline/15-hud-vs-world-1920.png: bare-sand runs are 1 px 15.4% and 2 px 14.2%, from the light and the vignette. On 16:10 the vignette blocks become non-square (5.625 x 6.25 at 1440x900).
- Soft light is a stated, defensible design choice and not a bug. But three grids overlay the art: device-res smooth gradients, the fog's 3S texels and the vignette's 7.5-px blocks. They match neither the world pixels nor each other. The dungeon's fog-of-war edge stands out most: it reads as a blur filter laid over pixel art.
- Fix: Owner's taste. For a fully pixel-consistent look, resolve each layer at one texel per world px, aligned to the world grid. Accumulate lights as now, draw them smoothed into a ceil(cam.W) x ceil(cam.H) art-px canvas, then multiply onto the screen with smoothing off at cam.S, offset (floor(cam.x) - cam.x)*S. Render fog of war, the night hole and the haze hole into the same canvas, with edges stepped per art px or 4x4 Bayer-dithered. Build the vignette at cam.W x cam.H and blit it at cam.S. Minimal change: draw the vignette with imageSmoothingEnabled = true (it is a soft gradient anyway), which removes its block grid.
